===Original===
To

Arthur Marwood

===Chinese===
献给

阿瑟·马伍德（Arthur Marwood）

===Original===
> “Da habt Ihr schon das End vom Lied”

===Chinese===
> “Da habt Ihr schon das End vom Lied”（德语题词，大意：你们这就听到了歌之终章）

## Part I. The Major Chord / 第一部 主和弦

### I

===Original===
“The Bishop of Rome—”

Thomas Cranmer began a hesitating speech. In the pause after the words the King himself hesitated, as if he poised between a heavy rage and a sardonic humour. He deemed, however, that the humour could the more terrify the Archbishop—and, indeed, he was so much upon the joyous side in those summer days that he had forgotten how to browbeat.

“Our holy father,” he corrected the Archbishop. “Or I will say my holy father, since thou art a heretic—”

Cranmer’s eyes had always the expression of a man’s who looked at approaching calamity, but at the King’s words his whole face, his closed lips, his brows, the lines from his round nose, all drooped suddenly downwards.

“Your Grace will have me write a letter to the—to his—to him—”

The downward lines fixed themselves, and from amongst them the panic-stricken eyes made a dumb appeal to the griffins and crowns of his dark green hangings, for they were afraid to turn to the King. Henry retained his heavy look of jocularity: he jumped at a weighty gibe—

===Chinese===
「罗马主教——」

托马斯·克兰默开口，说出一句迟疑未定的话。这话之后的停顿里，国王自己也迟疑了，仿佛在一团沉怒与一种讥诮的谐谑之间掂量。然而他认定，谐谑更能震慑大主教——的确，那些夏日里他的心绪是那样偏在欢愉一侧，竟已忘了如何疾言厉色。

「我们的圣父，」他纠正大主教道，「或者朕该说，我的圣父，既然汝是个异端——」

克兰默的眼中向来带着一种望着灾祸步步逼近之人的神色，可一听国王这话，他的整张脸——紧闭的嘴唇、眉眼、圆鼻两侧的纹路——忽然一齐往下垂落。

「陛下要臣写信给——给他的——给他——」

那下垂的纹路就此凝定，其间一双惊惶的眼睛默默向深绿帷幔上的狮鹫与王冠求告，因为它们不敢转向国王。亨利仍端着他那副沉重的打趣神情：他一把抓住一句分量十足的讥诮——

===Original===
“My Grace will have thy Grace write a letter to his Holiness.”

He dropped into a heavy impassivity, rolled his eyes, fluttered his swollen fingers on the red and gilded table, and then said clearly, “My. Thy. His.”

When he was in that mood he spoke with a singular distinctness that came up from his husky and ordinary joviality like something dire and terrible—like that something that upon a clear smooth day will suggest to you suddenly the cruelty that lies always hidden in the limpid sea.

“To Caesar—egomet, I mineself—that which is Caesar’s: to him—that is to say to his Holiness, our lord of Rome—the things which are of God! But to thee, Archbishop, I know not what belongs.”

He paused and then struck his hand upon the table: “Cold porridge is thy portion! Cold porridge!” he laughed; “for they say: Cold porridge to the devil! And, since thou art neither God’s nor the King’s, what may I call thee but the devil’s self’s man?”

A heavy and minatory silence seemed to descend upon him; the Archbishop’s thin hands opened suddenly as if he were letting something fall to the ground. The King scowled heavily, but rather as if he were remembering past heavinesses than for any present griefs.

===Chinese===
「朕这陛下要汝这大人写信给教皇陛下。」

他沉入一种滞重的漠然，转着眼珠，肿胀的手指在红漆描金的桌面上扑扑轻颤，随后清清楚楚地说：「朕的。汝的。他的。」

每逢这种心境，他说话便带着一种异样的明晰，自他那嘶哑而寻常的快活腔调里升起来，如同某种凶险可怖之物——恰似晴明无风的日子，忽然使你想起那永远潜藏在澄澈海水之下的残忍。

「归恺撒——egomet，朕躬自身——归恺撒之物；归他——就是说，归教皇陛下、我们罗马之主——归上帝之物！至于汝，大主教，朕不知还有什么当归于汝。」

他顿了顿，一掌拍在桌上：「冷粥便是汝的分！冷粥！」他笑道，「常言道：冷粥给魔鬼！汝既非上帝的人，亦非国王的人，朕若不称汝是魔鬼本人的仆役，还能称汝什么？」

一阵沉重的、含着威吓的沉默仿佛降落在他身上；大主教瘦削的双手蓦地摊开，仿佛把什么东西撒落地上。国王沉沉地皱起眉，却不像是为了眼前的烦忧，倒像是在追想往昔的重负。

===Original===
“Why,” he said, “I am growing an old man. It is time I redded up my house.”

It was as if he thought he could take his time, for his heavily pursed eyes looked down at the square tips of his fingers where they drummed on the table. He was such a weighty man that the old chair in which he sat creaked at the movement of his limbs. It was his affectation of courtesy that he would not sit in the Archbishop’s own new gilded and great chair that had been brought from Lambeth on a mule’s back along with the hangings. But the other furnishings of that Castle of Pontefract were as old as the days of Edward IV—even the scarlet wood of the table had upon it the arms of Edward IV’s Queen Elizabeth, side by side with that King’s. Henry noted it and said—

“It is time these arms were changed. See that you have here fairly painted the arms of my Queen and me—Howard and Tudor—in token that we have passed this way and sojourned in this Castle of Pontefract.”

He was dallying with time as if it were a luxury to dally: he looked curiously round the room.

“Why, they have not housed you very well,” he said, and, as the Archbishop shivered suddenly, he added, “there should be glass in the windows. This is a foul old kennel.”

“I have made a complaint to the Earl Marshal,” Cranmer said dismally, “but ’a said there was overmuch room needed above ground.”

===Chinese===
「唉，」他说，「朕渐渐成了个老人。也该是朕收拾门庭的时候了。」

他仿佛觉得尽可从从容容，因为他那双厚厚眯起的眼垂视着敲击桌面的方头指尖。他是个如此沉重的男人，稍一动弹肢体，所坐的那把旧椅子便咯吱作响。他故作谦恭之态，不肯坐大主教自己那把崭新的镀金大椅——那椅连同帷幔，是从兰贝斯用骡子驮来的。但这庞蒂弗拉克特城堡里的其余陈设却古老得仍如爱德华四世的时代——连这张朱红木桌的桌面上，还镌着爱德华四世王后伊丽莎白的纹章，与那位国王的并排而列。亨利注意到了，便道——

「这些纹章早该换过了。务必在此处端端正正绘上朕与朕王后的纹章——霍华德与都铎——以志朕等曾取道此地，驻跸于这座庞蒂弗拉克特城堡。」

他消磨着光阴，仿佛消磨光阴本身就是一桩奢侈的事：他饶有兴味地环顾这间屋子。

「唉，他们真没把大人安顿好，」他说；见大主教蓦地一颤，他又添一句，「窗上本该镶玻璃。这是个腌臜的老狗窝。」

「臣已向王室典礼官申诉过，」克兰默黯然道，「可他说，地面上要用的屋子太多。」

===Original===
This room was indeed below ground and very old, strong, and damp. The Archbishop’s own hangings covered the walls, but the windows shot upwards through the stones to the light; there was upon the ground of stone not a carpet but only rushes; being early in the year, no provision was made for firing, and the soot of the chimney back was damp, and sparkled with the track of a snail that had lived there undisturbed for many years, and neither increasing, because it had no mate, nor dying, because it was well fed by the ferns that, behind the present hangings, grew in the joints of the stones. In that low-ceiled and dark place the Archbishop was aware that above his head were fair and sunlit rooms, newly painted and hung, with the bosses on the ceilings fresh silvered or gilt, all these fair places having been given over to kinsmen of the yellow Earl Marshal from the Norfolk Queen downwards. And the temporal and material neglect angered him and filled him with a querulous bitterness that gnawed up even through his dread of a future—still shadowy—fall and ruin.

===Chinese===
这间屋子确在地下，古老、坚固而潮湿。四壁覆着大主教自家的帷幔，窗子却穿透石墙，笔直向上通到天光；石铺的地面上没有地毯，只有灯芯草；时值岁初，柴火尚未备置，烟囱后壁的烟灰潮乎乎的，闪着一线蜗牛爬过的亮痕——那蜗牛在此安居多年，无人惊扰，既不曾繁衍，因为没有配偶；也不曾死去，因为帷幔之后石缝里滋生的蕨叶把它喂养得很好。在这低顶幽暗之处，大主教明知头顶上便是明朗向阳的厅室，新髹新悬，天花板上的凸饰新镀了银或金；而这些华美所在，尽数已赐给那位枯黄脸色的王室典礼官的亲族——自诺福克家的那位王后而下，一概如此。这世俗物质上的怠慢激怒了他，使他满腹怨怼的苦涩，那苦涩甚至啮蚀透了他对未来——仍然影影绰绰的——倾覆败亡的恐惧。

===Original===
The King looked sardonically at the line of the ceiling. He had known that Norfolk, who was the Earl Marshal, had the mean mind to make him set these indignities upon the Archbishop, and loftily he considered this result as if the Archbishop were a cat mauled by his own dog whose nature it was to maul cats.

The Archbishop had been standing with one hand on the arm of his heavy chair, about to haul it back from the table to sit himself down. He had been standing thus when the King had entered with the brusque words—

“Make you ready to write a letter to Rome.”

And he still stood there, the cold feet among the damp rushes, the cold hand still upon the arm of the chair, the cap pulled forward over his eyes, the long black gown hanging motionless to the boot tops that were furred around the ankles.

“I have made a plaint to the Earl Marshal,” he said; “it is not fitting that a lord of the Church should be so housed.”

Henry eyed him sardonically.

===Chinese===
国王讥诮地望着天花板的线条。他早知道身为王室典礼官的诺福克怀着卑劣的心思，撺掇他把这等折辱加到大主教身上；而他高高地品评这一结果，仿佛大主教是一只被自家恶犬撕咬的猫，而那犬的天性本来就是要撕咬猫。

大主教一直站着，一只手搭在那把沉重座椅的扶手上，正要把它从桌边拖开坐下。国王进来时他便是这样站着，进门便撂下一句突兀的话——

「汝且预备着，写信去罗马。」

而他仍旧站在那里，冰冷的脚没在潮湿的灯芯草间，冰冷的手仍搭在椅臂上，帽子往前压得遮住了眼睛，黑色长袍纹丝不动地垂到靴筒——靴口在脚踝处围着一圈毛。

「臣已向王室典礼官诉过苦，」他说，「教会的领主遭此安置，实非所宜。」

亨利讥诮地打量着他。

===Original===
“Sir,” he said, “I am being brought round to think that ye are only a false lord of the Church. And I am minded to think that ye are being brought round to trow even the like to mine own self.”

His eyes rested, little and twinkling like a pig’s, upon the opening of the Archbishop’s cloak above his breastbone, and the Archbishop’s right hand nervously sought that spot.

“I was always of the thought,” he said, “that the prohibition of the wearing of crucifixes was against your Highness’ will and the teachings of the Church.”

A great crucifix of silver, the Man of Sorrows depending dolorously from its arms and backed up by a plaque of silver so that it resembled a porter’s badge, depended over the black buttons of his undercoat. He had put it on upon the day when secretly he had married Henry to the papist Lady Katharine Howard. On the same day he had put on a hair shirt, and he had never since removed either the one or the other. He had known very well that this news would reach the Queen’s ears, as also that he had fasted thrice weekly and had taken a Benedictine sub-prior out of chains in the tower to be his second chaplain.

“Holy Church! Holy Church!” the King muttered amusedly into the stiff hair of his chin and lips. The Archbishop was driven into one of his fits of panic-stricken boldness.

“Your Grace,” he said, “if ye write a letter to Rome you will—for I see not how ye may avoid it—reverse all your acts of this last twenty years.”

===Chinese===
「先生，」他说，「朕渐渐被引得相信，汝不过是教会里一个僭冒的领主。朕也有意相信，汝也正渐渐被引得相信，朕自己也是同样的货色。」

他的目光——细小，像猪眼似的晶晶发亮——落在大主教胸骨上方法袍敞开的地方，大主教的右手便不安地探向那处。

「臣素来以为，」他说，「禁佩十字架之令，既有违陛下圣意，亦悖于教会的训导。」

一面巨大的银十字架，忧患之子凄然悬在架臂之间，背后衬着一方银牌，倒像一枚门房的号牌——就这样悬在他内袍的黑纽扣前。他戴上它，正是在他秘密为亨利与教皇派贵女凯瑟琳·霍华德主持婚配的那一日。同一天，他还贴身穿上了一件苦衣，此后两样都不曾卸除。他心里十分明白，这消息必会传入王后耳中，一如他每周斋戒三次、又从塔中的锁链里提擢一名本笃会副院长神父充任自己的第二随行司铎，也必会传入一样。

「圣教会！圣教会！」国王冲着自己下巴和嘴唇上硬扎扎的短须，有滋有味地低声念叨。大主教被逼出了他那种惊惶中生出的胆气。

「陛下，」他说，「汝若写信去罗马——臣看不出汝如何避得开——便要把这二十年来汝的全部作为尽数推翻。」

===Original===
“Your Grace,” the King mocked him, “by your setting on of chains, crucifixes, phylacteries, and by your aping of monkish ways, ye have reversed—well ye know it—all my and thy acts of a long time gone.”

He cast himself back from the table into the leathern shoulder-straps of the chair.

“And if,” he continued with sardonic good-humour, “my fellow and servant may reverse my acts—videlicet, the King’s—wherefore shall not I—videlicet, the King—reverse what acts I will? It is to set me below my servants!”

“I am minded to redd up my house!” he repeated after a moment.

“Please it, your Grace—” the Archbishop muttered. His eyes were upon the door.

The King said, “Anan?” He could not turn his bulky head, he would not move his bulky body.

===Chinese===
「大人，」国王讥笑他道，「凭着汝佩上的锁链、十字架、经匣，凭着汝对僧侣做派的亦步亦趋，汝早已推翻了——汝自己心里明白——朕与汝许久以来的一切作为。」

他把身子往桌后一仰，靠进椅子的皮肩带里。

「再说，」他带着讥诮的好脾气继续道，「倘若朕的同侪与仆役都能推翻朕的作为——videlicet（就是说），国王的作为——那朕为何不——videlicet，国王——要翻什么便翻什么？这岂不是把朕放在仆役之下了！」

「朕有心要收拾朕的门庭了！」过了片刻他又说了一遍。

「伏乞陛下——」大主教喃喃道。他的眼睛望着门。

国王道：「啊？」他那样沉重的头转不动，也不肯挪动那样沉重的身子。

===Original===
“My gentleman!” the Archbishop whispered.

The King looked at the opposite wall and cried out—

“Come in, Lascelles. I am about cleaning out some stables of mine.”

The door moved noiselessly and heavily back, taking the hangings with it; as if with the furtive eyes and feathery grace of a blonde fox Cranmer’s spy came round the great boards.

“Ay! I am doing some cleansing,” the King said again. “Come hither and mend thy pen to write.”

Against the King’s huge bulk—Henry was wearing purple and black upon that day—and against the Archbishop’s black and pillar-like form, Lascelles, in his scarlet, with his blonde and tender beard had an air of being quill-like. The bones of his knees through his tight and thin silken stockings showed almost as those of a skeleton; where the King had great chains of gilt and green jewels round his neck, and where the Archbishop had a heavy chain of silver, he had a thin chain of fine gold and a tiny badge of silver-gilt. He dragged one of his legs a little when he walked. That was the fashion of that day, because the King himself dragged his right leg, though the ulcer in it had been cured.

===Chinese===
「臣的侍从！」大主教低声道。

国王望着对面的墙，扬声唤道——

「进来吧，拉塞尔斯。朕正要清扫自家的几座马厩。」

门无声而沉重地向后滑开，带得帷幔一齐移动；克兰默的密探绕过大案板走来，仿佛一头金毛狐狸，眼色鬼祟，步态轻盈如羽。

「是了！朕正做些洒扫，」国王又说，「过来削好汝的笔，预备誊写。」

衬着国王魁伟的身躯——那天亨利穿的是紫与黑——又衬着大主教黑黢黢、柱子般的身形，一身绯衣、蓄着金色软须的拉塞尔斯，便显出羽毛笔也似的轻飘。紧薄的丝袜之下，他的膝骨几乎如骷髅般凸现；国王颈间垂着镀金绿宝的粗链，大主教垂着沉甸甸的银链，他却只挂一条纤细的纯金细链，缀一枚小小的镀金银徽。他走路时一条腿微微拖曳。那是当时的时兴做派，因为国王自己便拖着右腿，虽说腿上的溃疡早已治愈。

===Original===
Sitting askew in his chair at the table, the King did not look at this gentleman, but moved the fingers of his outstretched hand in token that his crook of the leg was kneeling enough for him.

“Take your tablets and write,” Henry said; “nay, take a great sheet of parchment and write—”

“Your Grace,” he added to the Archbishop, “ye are the greatest penner of solemn sentences that I have in my realm. What I shall say roughly to Lascelles you shall ponder upon and set down nobly, at first in the vulgar tongue and then in fine Latin.” He paused and added—

“Nay; ye shall write it in the vulgar tongue, and the Magister Udal shall set it into Latin. He is the best Latinist we have—better than myself, for I have no time—”

Lascelles was going between a great cabinet with iron hinges and the table. He fetched an inkhorn set into a tripod, a sandarach, and a roll of clean parchment that was tied around with a green ribbon.

Upon the gold and red of the table he stretched out the parchment as if it had been a map. He mended his pen with a little knife and kneeled down upon the rushes beside the table, his chin level with the edge. His whole mind appeared to be upon keeping the yellowish sheet straight and true upon the red and gold, and he raised his eyes neither to the Archbishop’s white face nor yet to the King’s red one.

===Chinese===
国王歪坐在桌旁的椅子里，并不看这位侍从，只把伸出的一只手动了动手指，示意他那屈腿一躬，已算得跪拜如仪。

「取汝的笺板来写，」亨利说，「不，取一大张羊皮纸来写——」

「大人，」他又对大主教说，「汝是朕全境之内最善缀写庄重文句的圣手。朕粗粗说给拉塞尔斯的话，汝要细细推敲，庄严录下——先用本国白话，再润成典雅的拉丁文。」他顿了顿，又补上一句——

「不必了；汝便用白话写，尤达尔先生自会把它翻成拉丁文。他是我们手头最好的拉丁文家——比朕还强，因为朕没有工夫——」

拉塞尔斯在一只带铁铰链的大柜与桌子之间来回。他取来一架装在三脚架上的墨壶、一筒吸墨砂，还有一卷以绿丝带束着的干净羊皮纸。

他在金红的桌面上摊开羊皮纸，仿佛摊开一幅地图。他用一柄小刀修好笔，跪在桌旁的灯芯草上，下巴与桌沿齐平。他的全副心神似乎都放在让那张微黄的纸在金红桌面上铺得端正平直，一双眼既不抬向大主教的白脸，也不抬向国王的红脸。

===Original===
Henry stroked the short hairs of his neck below the square grey beard. He was reflecting that very soon all the people in that castle, and very soon after, most of the people in that land would know what he was about to say.

“Write now,” he said. “ ‘Henry—by the grace of God—Defender of the Faith—King, Lord Paramount.’ ” He stirred in his chair.

“Set down all my styles and titles: ‘Duke Palatine—Earl—Baron—Knight’—leave out nothing, for I will show how mighty I am.” He hummed, considered, set his head on one side and then began to speak swiftly—

“Set it down thus: ‘We, Henry, and the rest, being a very mighty King, such as few have been, are become a very humble man. A man broken by years, having suffered much. A man humbled to the dust, crawling to kiss the wounds of his Redeemer. A Lord of many miles both of sea and land.’ Why, say—

“ ‘Guide and Leader of many legions, yet comes he to thee for guidance.’ Say, too, ‘He who was proud cometh to thee to regain his pride. He who was proud in things temporal cometh to thee that he may once more have the pride of a champion in Christendom—’ ”

He had been speaking as if with a malicious glee, for his words seemed to strike, each one, into the face of the pallid figure, darkly standing before him. And he was aware that each word increased the stiff and watchful constraint of the figure that knelt beside the table to write. But suddenly his glee left him; he scowled at the Archbishop as if Cranmer had caused him to sin. He pulled at the collar around his throat.

===Chinese===
亨利抚着方正灰须之下颈后的短毛。他心中盘算着：过不多久，这城堡里的所有人，再过不多久，这国土上的大多数人，都将知道他要说的话。

「现在写，」他说。「『亨利——托上帝洪恩——信仰的捍卫者——国王、至尊领主。』」他在椅子里挪动了一下身子。

「把朕的尊称头衔尽数写下：『王领公爵——伯爵——男爵——骑士』——一样也不许漏，朕要显一显朕是何等威赫。」他哼了一声，思量片刻，把头一偏，随即急促地说道——

「这样写：『朕亨利，暨其余诸号，本是威权无匹之王，旷世少有，今成至谦之人。为岁月所摧之人，饱经苦楚之人。谦卑至尘土之人，匍匐亲吻救赎主的圣伤。统御海陆广袤疆土之主。』喏，再写——

「『万千军团之引导者与统帅，却来至汝座前求引导。』再写：『曾骄矜者，来至汝前，求复其骄矜。曾骄矜于尘世之物者，来至汝前，惟愿重得基督教世界卫道之士的骄傲——』」

他方才说这些话时一直带着一种恶意的快意，因为他的字字句句仿佛都掼在那张苍白的脸上——那身影正幽幽地立在他面前。他也觉察到，每添一字，便叫跪在桌边誊写的那身影愈加僵硬戒备。可他的快意忽然离他而去；他向大主教怒目而视，仿佛是克兰默引他犯了罪。他扯了扯喉前的衣领。

===Original===
“No,” he cried out, “write down in simple words that I am a very sinful man. Set it down that I grow old! That I am filled with fears for my poor soul! That I have sinned much! That I recall all that I have done! An old man, I come to my Saviour’s Regent upon earth. A man aware of error, I will make restitution tenfold! Say I am broken and aged and afraid! I kneel down on the ground—”

He cast his inert mass suddenly a little forward as if indeed he were about to come on to his knees in the rushes.

“Say—” he muttered—“say—”

But his face and his eyes became suffused with blood.

“It is a very difficult thing,” he uttered huskily, “to meddle in these sacred matters.”

He fell heavily back into his chair-straps once more.

===Chinese===
「不，」他扬声道，「用浅白的话写下，朕是个罪孽极重的人。写下朕老了！写下朕为朕这可怜的灵魂满心惧怖！写下朕罪愆深重！写下朕追省平生一切作为！朕已是老朽之人，来至朕救主在世的摄政座前。朕自知有过，愿十倍偿还！就说朕衰颓、老迈、惶恐！朕跪伏于地——」

他那沉重的身躯蓦地向前一倾，仿佛当真就要跪到灯芯草上去。

「说——」他喃喃道——「说——」

可他的脸和双眼涨满了血色。

「这等神圣之事，」他嗓音嘶哑地说，「插手起来，实在为难。」

他又沉重地向后跌回椅子的肩带里。

===Original===
“I do not know what I will have you to say,” he said.

He looked broodingly at the floor.

“I do not know,” he muttered.

He rolled his eyes, first to the face of the Archbishop, then to Lascelles—

===Chinese===
「朕不知要你们说些什么。」他说。

他郁郁地望着地板。

「朕不知道。」他喃喃道。

他转着眼珠，先望向大主教的脸，再望向拉塞尔斯——

===Original===
“Body of God—what carved turnips!” he said, for in the one face there was only panic, and in the other nothing at all. He rolled on to his feet, catching at the table to steady himself.

“Write what you will,” he called, “to these intents and purposes. Or stay to write—I will send you a letter much more good from the upper rooms.”

Cranmer suddenly stretched out, with a timid pitifulness, his white hands. But, rolling his huge shoulders, like a hastening bear, the King went over the rushes. He pulled the heavy door to with such a vast force that the latch came again out of the hasp, and the door, falling slowly back and quivering as if with passion, showed them his huge legs mounting the little staircase.

===Chinese===
「上帝的圣体——好一对雕出来的芜菁！」他说，因为一张脸上唯有惊惶，另一张脸上则什么都没有。他撑住桌子稳住身子，滚动着站了起来。

「你们想写什么便写什么，」他喊道，「只要合乎这层宗旨就行。要么且慢写——朕自会从楼上的屋子，给你们送一封好得多的信下来。」

克兰默蓦地伸出一双白手，带着怯懦的哀恳。可国王滚动着巨大的双肩，像一头赶路的熊，一路踏过灯芯草去了。他关门用那样一股巨力，门闩又从搭扣里崩脱出来，那扇门缓缓荡回，仿佛激愤难抑似的颤动着，让他们望见他粗大的双腿正登上那道小小的楼梯。

---

===Original===
A long silence fell in that dim room. The Archbishop’s lips moved silently, the spy’s glance went, level, along his parchment. Suddenly he grinned mirthlessly and as if at a shameless thought.

“The Queen will write the letter his Grace shall send us,” he said.

Then their eyes met. The one glance, panic-stricken, seeing no issue, hopeless and without resource, met the other—crafty, alert, fox-like, with a dance in it. The glances transfused and mingled. Lascelles remained upon his knees as if, stretching out his right knee behind him, he were taking a long rest.

===Chinese===
长久的沉默落进那间昏暗的屋子。大主教的嘴唇无声翕动，密探的视线平平地沿他的羊皮纸扫过。忽然他咧嘴一笑，毫无欢意，仿佛起了一个无耻的念头。

「国王陛下要颁给我们的那封信，自有王后来写。」他说。

随后他们的目光相遇了。一道目光惊惶失措，望不见出路，绝望而无计可施；另一道——狡黠、警醒、狐狸一般，里面跃动着一点活气。两道目光交融互渗，混而为一。拉塞尔斯仍跪在原地，右腿向后舒展着，仿佛要就此长歇一番。

### II

===Original===
It was almost within earshot of these two men in their dim cell that the Queen walked from the sunlight into shadow and out again. This great terrace looked to the north and west, and, from the little hillock, dominating miles of gently rising ground, she had a great view over rolling and very green country. The original builders of the Castle of Pontefract had meant this terrace to be flagged with stone: but the work had never been carried so far forward. There was only a path of stone along the bowshot and a half of stone balustrade; the rest had once been gravel, but the grass had grown over it; that had been scythed, and nearly the whole space was covered with many carpets of blue and red and other very bright colours. In the left corner when you faced inwards there was a great pavilion of black cloth, embroidered very closely with gold and held up by ropes of red and white. Though forty people could sit in it round the table, it appeared very small, the walls of the castle towered up so high. They towered up so high, so square, and so straight that from the terrace below you could hardly hear the flutter of the huge banner of St. George, all red and white against the blue sky, though sometimes in a gust it cracked like a huge whip, and its shadow, where it fell upon the terrace, was sufficient to cover four men.

To take away from the grimness of the flat walls many little banners had been suspended from loopholes and beneath windows. Swallow-tailed, long, or square, they hung motionless in the shelter, or, since the dying away of the great gale three days before, had looped themselves over their staffs. These were all painted green, because that was the Queen’s favourite colour, being the emblem of Hope.

A little pavilion, all of green silk, at the very edge of the platform, had all its green curtains looped up, so that only the green roof showed; and, within, two chairs, a great leathern one for the King, a little one of red and white wood for the Queen, stood side by side as if they conversed with each other. At the top of it was a golden image of a lion, and above the peak of the entrance another, golden too, of the Goddess Flora, carrying a cornucopia of flowers, to symbolise that this tent was a summer abode for pleasantness.

===Chinese===
那两人蛰居的幽暗囚室几乎近在声闻可及之处，王后便在那上头的露台上来回走着，从阳光里步入阴影，又从阴影里步出。这座大露台朝向北与西，从小丘之上俯瞰绵延数英里的缓坡，一片起伏青翠的原野尽收她眼底。庞蒂弗拉克特城堡最初的建造者原打算给这露台满铺石板，但工程从未推进到那一步。只有一条石径，沿着一箭半射程长的石栏延伸；其余之处从前铺的是砾石，青草早已蔓生其上；草刈去之后，几乎整个场地都覆上了许许多多的地毯，蓝的、红的，还有别样极鲜亮的颜色。面朝堡内而立，左手角落里张着一座巨大的黑呢帷帐，绣满细密的金线，由红白二色的绳索绷起。围着桌子坐进四十个人绰绰有余，它却显得极小，因为城堡的墙垣耸得太高了。墙耸得那样高、那样方正、那样笔直，人在其下的露台上，几乎听不见圣乔治（St. George）巨旗的猎猎声——红白二色衬着蓝天——虽然疾风乍起时，它会像一根巨鞭般炸响，而它落在露台上的影子，足以罩住四个人。

为冲淡平直墙面的森然之气，枪眼旁、窗下悬起了许多小旗。燕尾形的、长条的、方形的，在背风处纹丝不动地垂着；三日之前那场大风息止以来，有几面已软软翻卷在旗杆上。这些旗子一律绘成绿色，因为那是王后最钟爱的颜色——望德的象征。

平台尽端有一座通体绿绸的小亭，绿色的帷幔尽数挽起，只露出绿顶；亭内两把椅子并肩而设，仿佛在彼此交谈——一把宽大的皮椅是国王的，一把红白木的小椅是王后的。亭顶立着一尊金狮像，入口尖顶之上另有一尊金像，是花神弗洛拉（Flora），捧着满盛鲜花的丰饶角，以象征此帐乃怡情悦性的夏日居所。

===Original===
Here the King and Queen, for the four days that they had been in the castle, had delighted much to sit, resting after their long ride up from the south country. For it pleased Henry to let his eyes rest upon a great view of this realm that was his, and to think nothing; and it pleased Katharine Howard to think that now she swayed this land, and that soon she would alter its face.

They looked out, over the tops of the elm trees that grew right up against the terrace wall; but the land itself was too green, the fields too empty of dwellings. There was no one but sheep between all the hedgerows: there was, in all the wide view, but one church tower, and where, in place and place, there stood clusters of trees as if to shelter homesteads—nearly always the homesteads had fallen to ruin beneath the boughs. Upon one ridge one could see the long walls of an unroofed abbey. But, to the keenest eye no men were visible, save now and then a shepherd leaning on his crook. There was no ploughland at all. Now and then companies of men in helmets and armour rode up to or away from the castle. Once she had seen the courtyard within the keep filled with cattle that lowed uneasily. But these, she had learned, had been taken from cattle thieves by the men of the Council of the Northern Borders. They were destined for the provisioning of that castle during her stay there, they being forfeit, whether Scotch or English.

“Ah,” she said, “whilst his Grace rides north to meet the King’s Scots I will ride east and west and south each day.”

===Chinese===
进驻城堡的四天里，国王与王后极爱坐在此处，自南国长途鞍马而来，正好在此歇息。因为亨利乐于让目光栖息于这片属于他的国土的壮阔景象之上，什么也不想；而凯瑟琳·霍华德乐于想的是：如今执掌这片土地的是她，而且不久她便要改换它的容颜。

两人凭眺，视线越过紧贴露台墙根生长的榆树梢；然而这片土地本身太绿了，田野间屋舍太稀。一道道树篱之间除了羊便空无一人：纵目所及的广阔天地里，只有一座教堂尖塔；处处有树丛矗立，仿佛荫蔽着农庄宅院——而那些宅院几乎总已在枝叶之下倾颓。一道山脊上，望得见一座没了屋顶的修道院的长墙。然而任凭眼力再尖，也见不着人影，唯有偶尔一个牧人拄着曲柄杖。全然没有耕地。时有顶盔贯甲的队伍驰近城堡，或自城堡驰离。有一回，她望见堡楼内院的庭院里挤满了牛，哞哞地叫得不安。但她后来得知，这些牛是北境枢密院的人马从盗牛贼手里夺来的。它们注定要在她驻跸期间充作城堡的供应——横竖是充公之物，无论是苏格兰的还是英格兰的。

「啊，」她说，「陛下北上去会苏格兰国王的日子，我便每日向东、向西、向南巡游。」

---

===Original===
At that moment, whilst the King had left Cranmer and his spy and, to regain his composure, was walking up and down in her chamber, she was standing beside the Duke of Norfolk about midway between the end of the terrace and the little green pavilion.

She was all in a dark purple dress, to please the King whose mood that colour suited; and the Duke’s yellow face looked out above a suit all of black. He wore that to please the King too, for the King was of opinion that no gathering looked gay in its colours that had not many men in black amongst the number.

He said—

“You do not ride north with his Grace?”

He leaned upon his two staves, one long and of silver, the other shorter and gilt; his gown fell down to his ankles, his dark and half-closed eyes looked out at a tree that, struck lately by lightning, stretched up half its boughs all naked from a little hillock beside a pond a mile away.

===Chinese===
此刻，国王已把克兰默与他的密探撇下，为恢复镇定，正在她的寝室里来回踱步；她则站在诺福克公爵身旁，约在露台尽头与那座绿色小亭的中途。

她通身一件深紫裙裳，为的是取悦国王，那种颜色正合国王的心境；公爵那张蜡黄的脸，从一套通黑的衣装上方探出来。他穿黑衣也是为了取悦国王，因为国王素持一议：任何聚会，人群之中若无许多穿黑衣的人，色彩的喜气便显不出来。

他说——

「陛下北上，您不随驾同行么？」

他倚着两根手杖，一根长而银制，另一根较短、镀金；长袍垂至脚踝；他半阖的暗色眼睛望着一株树——那树新近遭了雷击，从一英里外一方池畔的小丘上，伸着半树光秃秃的枝桠。

===Original===
“So it is settled between his Grace and me,” she said. She did not much like her uncle, for she had little cause. But, the King being away, she walked with him rather than with another man.

“I ask, perforce,” he said, “for I have much work in the ordering of your progresses.”

“We meant that you should have that news this day,” she said.

He shot one glance at her face, then turned his eyes again upon the stricken tree. Her face was absolutely calm and without expression, as it had been always when she had directed him what she would have done. He could trace no dejection in it: on the other hand, he gave her credit for a great command over her features. That he had himself. And, in the niece’s eyes, as they moved from the backs of a flock of sheep to the dismantled abbey on the ridge, there was something of the enigmatic self-containment that was in the uncle’s steady glance. He could observe no dejection, and at that he humbled himself a little more.

===Chinese===
「陛下与我之间已经这样定了，」她说。她不大喜欢这位叔祖，因为她实在没有什么缘由喜欢他。但国王既不在，她宁可与他同行，也胜过与别的男人作伴。

「我不得不问，」他说，「因为打点您的巡幸，有许许多多的操劳在我身上。」

「我们本就有意让您今日得知这消息，」她说。

他朝她的脸迅疾瞥了一眼，随即又把目光转回那株遭雷击的树。她的脸绝然平静，毫无表情，一如她向来指示他办什么事时的神色。他从中窥不出半点沮丧：转念之间，他倒称许她对自己的面目有绝大的把持。这份本事，他自己也有。而在侄孙女的眼睛里——当那目光从一群羊的背脊移向山脊上那座拆毁了的修道院时——有着叔祖凝定一瞥中那种谜一般的自持。他窥不出沮丧，为此他愈发谦卑了几分。

===Original===
“Ay,” he said, “the ordering of your progresses is a heavy burden. I would have you commend what I have done here.”

She looked at him, at that, as if with a swift jealousy. His eyes were roving upon the gay carpets, the pavilions, and the flags against the grim walls, depending in motionless streaks of colour.

“The King’s Grace’s self,” she said, “did tell me that all these things he ordered and thought out for my pleasuring.”

Norfolk dropped his eyes to the ground.

“Aye,” he said, “his Grace ordered them and their placing. There is no man to equal his Grace for such things; but I had the work of setting them where they are. I would have your favour for that.”

===Chinese===
「是，」他说，「打点您的巡幸是副重担。我望您称许我在此间的操办。」

她闻言看他，神情仿佛闪过一丝迅疾的妒意。他的目光正逡巡于那些鲜丽的地毯、帷亭，以及森然壁上凝然垂落的彩旗。

「国王陛下本人，」她说，「亲口告诉我，这一切都是他吩咐下来、为博我欢心而亲自擘画的。」

诺福克垂下眼，望着地面。

「是，」他说，「是陛下吩咐了这些物事并其陈设。这类事上，无人能及陛下；然而把它们一一安放在如今位置上的，是我。这一点，我望您眷顾。」

===Original===
She appeared appeased and gave him her hand to kiss. There was a little dark mole upon the third finger.

“The last niece that I had for Queen,” he said, “would not suffer me to kiss her hand.”

She looked at him a little absently, for, because since she had been Queen—and before—she had been a lonely woman, she was given to thinking her own thoughts whilst others talked.

She was troubled by the condition of her chief maid Margot Poins. Margot Poins was usually tranquil, modest, submissive in a cheerful manner and ready to converse. But of late she had been moody, and sunk in a dull silence. And that morning she had suddenly burst out into a smouldering, heavy passion, and had torn Katharine’s hair whilst she dressed it.

===Chinese===
她似乎已然息怒，伸出手去让他亲吻。第三根手指上有一颗小小的黑痣。

「我上一位身为王后的侄孙女，」他说，「是不容我吻她的手的。」

她略带恍惚地望了他一眼，因为——自她为王后以来，乃至为王后之前——她一向是个孤寂的女人，旁人说话时，她惯于想自己的心事。

她为首席侍女玛戈·普因斯的境况忧着心。玛戈·普因斯素来沉静、谦逊、温顺而爽利，乐于与人交谈。但近来她心绪无常，沉在一种滞闷的缄默里。而那天早晨，她突然迸出一阵郁结的盛怒，替凯瑟琳梳发时，扯痛了凯瑟琳的头发。

===Original===
“Ay,” Margot had said, “you are Queen: you can do what you will. It is well to be Queen. But we who are dirt underfoot, we cannot do one single thing.”

And, because she was lonely, with only Lady Rochford, who was foolish, and this girl to talk to, it had grieved the Queen to find this girl growing so lumpish and dull. At that time, whilst her hair was being dressed, she had answered only—

“Yea; it is good to be a Queen. But you will find it in Seneca—” and she had translated for Margot the passage which says that eagles are as much tied by weighty ropes as are finches caught in tiny fillets.

“Oh, your Latin,” Margot had said. “I would I had never heard the sound of it, but had stuck to clean English.”

Katharine imagined then that it was some new flame of the Magister Udal’s that was troubling the girl, and this troubled her too, for she did not like that her maids should be played with by men, and she loved Margot for her past loyalties, readiness, and companionship.

===Chinese===
「是，」玛戈当时说，「您是王后：您想做什么便做什么。做王后是好事。可我们这些踩在脚下的泥土，连一件事也做不成。」

而她终究孤寂，身边除了愚蠢的罗奇福德夫人，便只有这姑娘可以说话，于是眼见这姑娘变得这般滞钝阴沉，王后深感伤心。当时正梳着发，她只答了一句——

「是；做王后是好的。不过你在塞内加（Seneca）的书里找得到——」说着，她为玛戈译了那一段：鹰隼被沉重的绳索拴系，与雀鸟被细小的丝带缚住，同是羁缚。

「哦，您那拉丁文，」玛戈当时说，「我但愿从没听过那声音，只守着干干净净的英文便好了。」

凯瑟琳当时猜想，搅扰这姑娘的，是尤达尔先生的哪一段新欢；这教她也不安，因为她不愿自己的侍女被男人们玩弄，况且她爱玛戈——为她往日的忠心、爽利与相伴。

---

===Original===
She came out of her thoughts to say to her uncle, remembering his speech about her hands—

“Aye; I have heard that Anne Boleyn had six fingers upon her right hand.”

“She had six upon each, but she concealed it,” he answered. “It was her greatest grief.”

Katharine realised that his sardonic tone, his bitter yellow face, the croak in his voice, and his stiff gait—all these things were signs of his hostility to her. And his mention of Anne Boleyn, who had been Queen, much as she was, and of her bitter fate, this mention, if it could not be a threat, was, at least, a reminder meant to give her fears and misgiving. When she had been a child—and afterwards, until the very day when she had been shown for Queen—her uncle had always treated her with a black disdain, as he treated all the rest of the world. When he had—and it was rarely enough—come to visit her grandmother, the old Duchess of Norfolk, he had always been like that. Through the old woman’s huge, lonely, and ugly halls he had always stridden, halting a little over the rushes, and all creatures must keep out of his way. Once he had kicked her little dog, once he had pushed her aside; but probably, then, when she had been no more than a child, he had not known who she was, for she had lived with the servants and played with the servants’ children, much like one of them, and her grandmother had known little of the household or its ways.

===Chinese===
她从自己的思绪里出来，想起方才他那番关于吻手的话，对叔祖说道——

「是了；我可听说，安妮·博林（Anne Boleyn）的右手生着六根手指。」

「她两只手上各有六根，只是掩着不使人知，」他答道，「那是她平生最大的伤心事。」

凯瑟琳明白了：他那讥诮的口气、那张刻毒的蜡黄脸、他嗓音里的嘎响、他僵直的步态——这一切都是他对她怀敌意的表征。而他提起安妮·博林——那位于她之前同样为后的女人——又提起她惨烈的结局，这一提，纵然算不得威胁，至少也是存心教她心生畏惧与疑虑的提点。在她还是个孩子的时候——以及其后，直到她被册立示众为王后的那一天为止——她叔祖待她向来是一种阴黑的轻蔑，一如他对待世上其余众人。他偶尔——那是极难得的——来探望她的祖母、老诺福克公爵夫人时，也总是那副样子。穿过老妇人那巨大、空寂而丑陋的一进进厅堂，他从来大步直闯，在灯芯草铺地上略略滞着步子，一切生灵都须给他让路。有一次他踢过她的小狗，有一次他把她推搡到一旁；不过多半那时——她不过是个孩子——他并不知道她是谁，因为她与仆役同住、与仆役的孩子们玩耍，同他们中间的一个没什么两样，而她的祖母对这宅子里的人事规矩，也所知寥寥。

===Original===
She answered him sharply—

“I have heard that you were no good friend to your niece, Anne Boleyn, when she was in her troubles.”

He swallowed in his throat and gazed impassively at the distant oak tree, nevertheless his knee trembled with fury. And Katharine knew very well that if, more than another, he took pleasure in giving pain with his words, he bore the pain of other’s words less well than most men.

“The Queen Anne,” he said, “was a heretic. No better was she than a Protestant. She battened upon the goods of our Church. Why should I defend her?”

===Chinese===
她尖利地回敬他——

「我听说，你的侄孙女安妮·博林身陷困厄之时，你可算不得她的好朋友。」

他喉头一哽，木然凝望远处那株橡树，膝头却因狂怒而发颤。凯瑟琳深知：他若比旁人更乐于用言语予人痛楚，那么他承受他人言语之痛的能耐，也远不如寻常男人。

「安妮王后，」他说，「是个异端。她比新教徒好不到哪里去。她靠我们教会的产业养肥了自己。我为何要卫护她？」

===Original===
“Uncle,” she said, “where got you the jewel in your bonnet?”

He started a little back at that, and the small veins in his yellow eye-whites grew inflamed with blood.

“Queen—” he brought out between rage and astonishment that she should dare the taunt.

“I think it came from the great chalice of the Abbey of Rising,” she said. “We are valiant defenders of the Church, who wear its spoils upon our very brows.”

===Chinese===
「叔祖，」她说，「你软帽上那颗宝石，是从哪里得来的？」

他闻言微微一缩，眼白上细小的血管涨满了血丝。

「王后——」他迸出这一声，怒气与惊愕交攻——惊的是她竟敢出此讥刺。

「我想那是从里辛修道院（the Abbey of Rising）的大圣杯上来的，」她说，「我们真是教会勇毅的卫士，竟把教会的掠获物戴在自己的额前。」

===Original===
It was as if she had thrown down a glove to him and to a great many that were behind him.

She knew very well where she stood, and she knew very well what her uncle and his friends awaited for her, for Margot, her maid, brought her alike the gossip of the Court and the loudly voiced threats and aspirations of the city. For the Protestants—she knew them and cared little for them. She did not believe there were very many in the King’s and her realm, and mostly they were foreign merchants and poor men who cared little as long as their stomachs were filled. If these had their farms again they would surely return to the old faith, and she was minded to do away with the sheep. For it was the sheep that had brought discontent to England. To make way for these fleeces the ploughmen had been dispossessed.

It was natural that Protestants should hate her; but with Norfolk and his like it was different. She knew very well that Norfolk came there that day and waited every day, watching anxiously for the first sign that the King’s love for her should cool. She knew very well that they said in the Court that with the King it was only possession and then satiety. And she knew very well that when Norfolk’s eyes searched her face it was for signs of dismay and of discouragement. And when Norfolk had said that he himself had placed the banners, the tents, the pavilions and carpets that made gay all that grim terrace of the air, he was essaying to make her think that the King was abandoning the task of doing her honour. This had made her angry, for it was such folly. Her uncle should have known that the King had discussed all these things with her, asking her what she liked, and that all these bright colours and these plaisaunces were what her man had gallantly thought out for her. She carried her challenge still further.

===Chinese===
这话仿佛是向他、也向他背后那一大批人掷下的一只挑战的手套。

她深知自己的处境，也深知她叔祖及其党羽在盼着她的什么，因为她的侍女玛戈既给她带来宫廷的流言，也带来城中高声鼓噪的威胁与愿望。至于新教徒——她了解他们，也不甚把他们放在心上。她不相信在国王与她的国土上他们能有多少，多半是外国商人和穷人，只要肚皮填得饱便别无所求。这些人若能重得田庄，必定回归旧教；她便有心把羊废去。因为给英格兰带来怨望的，正是羊。为了给这些羊毛让路，庄稼人才被夺去了土地。

新教徒恨她，是自然之理；但诺福克之流却是另一回事。她深知诺福克那天到那里去、又日日守候在那里，焦灼地窥伺国王对她的爱降温的头一个征兆。她深知宫廷里人人都说，对国王而言唯有占有，占有过了便是餍足。她也深知，诺福克的眼睛搜索她的脸时，找的是惊惶与气馁的迹象。而当诺福克说，把那高踞半空的森然露台装点得喜气洋洋的旗帜、营帐、帷亭与地毯都出自他亲手安放时，他是想让她以为，国王已倦于再操办荣宠她的差事。这教她动了怒，因为这是何等的愚妄。她叔祖本该知道，这一切国王都同她商议过，问过她喜欢什么；这一切鲜亮的色彩、这一切怡情的布置，都是她的夫君满怀殷勤为她筹思出来的。她把她的挑战又推进了一步。

===Original===
“It ill becomes us Howards and all like us,” she said, “to talk of how we will defend the Church of God—”

“I am a swordsman only,” he said. “Give me that—”

She was not minded to listen to him.

“It becomes us ill,” she said; “and I take shame in it. For, a very few years agone we Howards were very poor. Now we are very rich—though it is true that my father is still a very poor man, and your stepmother, my grandmother, has known hard shifts. But we Howards, through you who are our head, became amongst the richest in the land. And how?”

“I have done services—” the Duke began.

“Why, there has been no new wealth made in this realm,” she said; “it came from the Church. Consider what you have had of this Abbey of Risings that I speak of, because I knew it well as a child, and saw many times then, sparkling in that which held the blood of my Saviour, the jewel that is now in your cap.”

===Chinese===
「我们霍华德家，以及一切同我们一样的人家，」她说，「侈谈要如何捍卫上帝的教会——终归不相宜——」

「我不过是个使剑之人，」他说，「给我那个——」

她无意听他说下去。

「这确乎不相宜，」她说，「我且引以为耻。因为不过寥寥数年之前，我们霍华德家还穷得很。如今我们富极——虽然千真万确，我父亲至今仍是一贫如洗，您的继母、我的祖母也尝过艰辛的日子。但我们霍华德家，借着你——我们一族之长——成了这国土上数一数二的富室。怎么来的？」

「我立过功劳——」公爵开口道。

「不然，这王国里并没有生出什么新的财富，」她说，「钱财都出自教会。想想你从我所说的这座里辛修道院得了多少吧——我幼时对它再熟悉不过，多少次望见如今缀在你帽上的那颗宝石，在盛着我救主之血的器皿上灿灿生光。」

===Original===
The Abbey of Risings, after the visitors had been to it and the monks had been driven out, had fallen to the Duke of Norfolk. And his men had stripped the lead from the roofs, the glass from the windows, the very tiles from the floor. And this little abbey was only one of many, large and small, that had fallen to the Duke, so that it was true enough that, through him, the Howards had become a very rich family.

Norfolk burst into a sudden speech—

“I hold these things only as a trust,” he said. “I am ready to restore.”

“Why, that is very well,” Katharine said; “and I have hopes that soon you will be called to make that restoration to your God.”

Norfolk looked at the square toes of his shoes for a long time.

===Chinese===
巡察专员到过、修士被逐之后，里辛修道院便落到了诺福克公爵手里。他的人剥下屋顶的铅皮，起走窗上的玻璃，连地上的铺砖都掀了去。这座小小的修道院，不过是大小不一、尽归公爵的许多座中的一座；所以说，正是通过他，霍华德家才成了豪富之族，这话再真不过。

诺福克突然迸出话来——

「我持有这些，不过代为保管，」他说，「我随时可以归还。」

「哦，那便很好，」凯瑟琳说，「我且盼着您不久便蒙受感召，向你的上帝交还这一切。」

诺福克久久望着自己鞋子的方头。

===Original===
“Will you have *all* things to be given back?” he said at last after he had thought much.

“The King will have all things be as they were before the Queen Katharine, my namesake of Aragon, was undone,” Katharine answered. “And me he will have to take her place so that all things shall be as before they were.”

The Duke, leaning on his silver and gold staves, shrugged his shoulders very slowly.

“This will make a very great confusion,” he said.

===Chinese===
「你要把*一切*都归还么？」他思忖良久，终于开口。

「国王要一切复归旧观——回到阿拉贡的凯瑟琳、我那位同名的王后被废之前，」凯瑟琳答道，「而他要我坐她的位子，好教万事如旧。」

公爵倚着银杖与金杖，极缓慢地耸了耸肩。

「这将惹出天大的乱子，」他说。

===Original===
“Ay,” Katharine answered, “there will a very many be confounded, and a great number of hundreds be much annoyed.”

She broke in again upon his slow meditations—

“Sir,” she said, “this is a very pitiful thing! Privy Seal that is dead and done with worked with a very great cunning. Well he knew that for most men the heart resideth in the pocket. Therefore, though ye said all that he rode this land with a bridle of iron, he was very careful to stop all your mouths alike with pieces of gold. It was not only to his friends that he gave what had been taken from God, but he was very careful that much also should fall into the greedy mouths of those that cried out. If he had not done this, do you think that he would have remained so long above the earth that he made weary? No. But since he made all rich alike with this plunder, so there was no man, either Catholic or Lutheran, very anxious to have him away. And, now that he is dead he worketh still. For who among you lords that do call yourselves sons of the Church, but holdeth of the Church’s goods? Oh, bethink you! bethink you! The moment is at hand when ye may work restoration. See that ye do it willingly and with good hearts, smoothing and making plain the way by which the bruised feet of our Saviour shall come across this, His land.”

===Chinese===
「是，」凯瑟琳答道，「会有许许多多人陷于困窘，成百上千的人大为恼恨。」

她再度打断他迟缓的沉思——

「爵爷，」她说，「这是顶可怜见的事！那位已死已了的御玺大臣，行事有绝大的狡黠。他深知，对大多数人而言，心是居于钱袋之中的。所以，尽管汝等众口一词，说他以铁缰驾驭这片国土，他却万分小心，用金块把你们众人的嘴一律堵住。从上帝那里夺来的东西，他不仅分给他的朋友，还格外留神，教大宗的财物也落进那些高声叫嚷者的贪婪之口。倘若没有这一手，你想他能在自己搅得困乏的人间存身这么久么？断乎不能。只因他用这掠获教人人一律致富，便无论旧教徒还是路德派，谁也不急于去他而后快。而如今他虽已死，其道犹行。因为你们这些自称教会之子的勋贵，又有哪一个不占着教会的产业？啊，深思吧！深思吧！时候就在眼前，汝等正可行归还之工。务要甘心情愿、怀着一副好心肠去做，将那道路铺平修直——好教我们救主带伤的双足，得以走过这属于祂的土地。」

===Original===
Norfolk kept his eyes upon the ground.

“Why, for me,” he said, “I am very willing. This day I will send to set clerks at work discovering that which is mine and that which came from the Church; but I think you will find some that will not do it so eagerly.”

She believed him very little; and she said—

“Why, if you will do this thing I think there will not many be behindhand.”

===Chinese===
诺福克垂着眼，望着地面。

「至于我嘛，」他说，「我十二分情愿。今日我就遣书吏们动手，查清哪些是我的、哪些来自教会；不过我想，你会碰上几位，做事没有这般起劲的。」

她几乎不信他；她说——

「这么说，若你肯行此事，我想落于人后的也不会有几个。」

===Original===
He did what he could to conceal his wincing, and her voice changed its tone.

“Sir,” she said, and she was eager and pleading, “you have many men that take counsel with you, for I trow that you and my Lord of Winchester do lead such lords as be Catholic in this realm. I know very well that you and my Lord Bishop of Winchester and such Catholic lords would have me to be your puppet and so work as you would have me, giving back to the Church such things as have fallen to Protestants or to men that ye mislike. But that may not be, for, since I owe mine advancement not to you, nor to mine own efforts, but to God alone, so to God alone do I owe fealty.”

She stretched out towards him the hand that he had kissed. The tail of her coif fell almost to her feet; her body in the fresh sunlight was all cased in purple velvet, only the lawn of her undershirt showed, white and tremulous at her wrists and her neck; and, fair and contrasted with the gold of her hair, her face came out of its abstraction, to take on a pitiful and mournful earnestness.

===Chinese===
他竭力掩饰面上的畏缩，而她的声调转了。

「爵爷，」她说，语气恳切而近乎哀求，「有许多人向你问计，因为我确知，你与温彻斯特主教大人在引领着此国中信奉旧教的勋贵们。我也深知，你与温彻斯特主教大人、与这些旧教勋贵，是要我做你们的傀儡，照你们的意旨行事——把落入新教徒之手、或落入你们厌憎之人手中的产业归还教会。但这断然不行：我的进位既不缘于你，亦不缘于我自身的经营，而唯独出于上帝，故我所欠的忠忱，也唯独向上帝奉上。」

她朝他伸出那只被他吻过的手。头巾的垂带几乎直落脚边；清新明澈的日光里，她的身躯通体裹在紫色天鹅绒中，只有内衫的细麻纱在腕间与颈际露出，白得微微颤动；衬着一头金发之明媚，那张脸从出神中回转，带上一种哀婉而悲戚的诚挚。

===Original===
“Sir,” she said, “if you shall speak for God in the councils that you will hold, believe that your rewards shall be very great. I think that you have been a man of a very troubled mind, for you have thought only or mostly of the affairs of this world. But do now this one good stroke for God His piteous sake, and such a peace shall descend upon you as you have never yet known. You shall have no more griefs; you shall have no more fears. And that is better than the jewels of chalices, and than much lead from the roofs of abbeys. Speak you thus in these councils that you shall hold, give you such advice to them that come to you seeking it, and this I promise you—for it is too little a thing to promise you the love of a Queen and a King’s favour, though that too ye shall not lack—but this I promise you, that there shall descend upon your heart that most blessed miracle and precious wealth, the peace of God.”

===Chinese===
「爵爷，」她说，「若你在将主持的会议上为上帝发言，请相信你的报偿必极其丰厚。我想你素来是个心绪烦乱的人，因为你所思所虑，只在此世、或多半在此世的事务上。但如今看在上帝悲悯的份上，为祂做成这一桩善举，便会有一种你从未识得的安宁降临于你。你将不再有忧伤；你将不再有恐惧。这比圣杯上的宝石、比修道院屋顶的大宗铅皮都要贵重。你在将主持的会议里这样陈说，向那些来向你求教的人这样进言——这个我应许你——因为应许你一位王后的爱恋与一位国王的恩眷，未免太微末了，虽然那些你也决不会缺少——但我应许你这个：必有一种至福的奇迹、极宝贵的财富，降临于你的心——那便是上帝的平安。」

### III

===Original===
When Henry was calmed by his pacing in her chamber he came out to her in the sunlight, rolling and bear-like, and so huge that the terrace seemed to grow smaller.

“Chuck,” he said to her, “I ha’ done a thing to pleasure thee.” He moved two fingers upwards to save the Duke of Norfolk from falling to his knees, caught Katharine by the elbow, and, turning upon himself as on a huge pivot, swung her round him so that they faced the pavilion. “Sha’t not talk with a citron-faced uncle,” he said; “sha’t save sweet words for me. I will tell thee what I ha’ done to pleasure thee.”

“Save it a while and do another ere ye tell me,” she said.

“Now, what is your reasoning about that, wise one?” he asked.

She laughed at him, for she took pleasure in his society and, except when she was earnest to beg things of him, she was mostly gay at his side.

“It takes a woman to teach kings,” she said.

===Chinese===
亨利在内室里踱步踱平了怒气，便出到日光下她跟前，脚步滚重如熊，身躯又那样魁伟，露台也仿佛被他衬得小了一圈。

「心肝儿，」他对她说，「俺做了一件事，好教汝欢喜。」他抬起两根手指向上一扬，免了诺福克公爵这一跪，随即扣住凯瑟琳的臂肘，以自身为轴，像一座巨大的转枢似的把她抡着转了半圈，两人便面朝凉亭。「柠檬脸的叔祖，汝休得与他多谈，」他说，「甜言蜜语，留着给俺。俺这就告诉汝，俺做了什么好事讨汝欢心。」

「先存着，再去另做一件，再来告诉我。」她说。

「唔，这里头汝有什么道理，聪明人儿？」他问。

她朝他笑了。与他为伴她自有欢趣，除非正经求恳什么事情，她在他身旁多半是快活的。

「调教国王，非得女人不可。」她说。

===Original===
He answered that it took a Queen to teach him.

“Why,” she said, “listen! I know that each day ye do things to pleasure me, things prodigal or such little things as giving me pouncet boxes. But you will find—and a woman, quean or queen, knows it well—that to take the full pleasure of her lover’s surprises well, she must have an easy mind. And to have an easy mind she must have granted her the little, little boons she asketh.”

He reflected ponderously upon this point and at last, with a sort of peasant’s gravity, nodded his head.

“For,” she said, “if a woman is to take pleasure she must guess at what you men have done for her. And if she be to guess pleasurably, she must have a clear mind. And if I am to have a clear mind I must have a maiden consoled with a husband.”

===Chinese===
他答道，调教他，非得一位王后不可。

「哟，」她说，「听我说！我知道汝天天都做事讨我欢心，或是一掷千金的大手笔，或是送我香粉盒这等小物事。可汝终会明白——女人嘛，婆娘也好，王后也好，无不知此理——要把情人惊喜里的甜头足足受用，她必得心境安闲。而要心境安闲，她所恳求的那些小而又小的恩典，必得桩桩都准了。」

这个道理他沉甸甸地思量了一番，末了，带着几分庄稼人的郑重，点了点头。

「因为，」她说，「女人要受用欢喜，就得猜猜你们男人为她做了什么。要猜得开心，她得心思澄净。我要心思澄净，就得先有一位得了夫婿慰藉的侍女。」

===Original===
Henry seated himself carefully in the great chair of the small pavilion. He spread out his knees, blinked at the view and when, having cast a look round to see that Norfolk was gone—for it did not suit her that he should see on what terms she was with the King—she seated herself on a little foot-pillow at his feet, he set a great hand upon her head. She leaned her arms across over his knees, and looked up at him appealingly.

“I do take it,” he said, “that I must make some man rich to wed some poor maid.”

“Oh, Solomon!” she said.

“And I do take it,” he continued with gravity, “that this maid is thy maid Margot.”

===Chinese===
亨利在小凉亭的大椅里小心落座，岔开双膝，对着景致眨眼。她环顾一眼，确知诺福克已经走远——她不愿让他瞧见自己与国王亲狎到何等地步——便在他脚边一方小踏垫上坐下；他于是一只大手覆上她的头顶。她把双臂横搭过他的膝头，仰起脸，满目恳求地望着他。

「俺琢磨着，」他说，「俺必得让哪个男人富起来，好娶哪位贫寒的少女。」

「哦，所罗门王！」她说。

「俺还琢磨着，」他郑重其事地续道，「这位少女，便是汝的侍女玛戈。」

===Original===
“How know you that?” she said.

“I have observed her,” he maintained gravely.

“Why, you could not well miss her,” she answered. “She is as big as a plough-ox.”

“I have observed,” he said—and he blinked his little eyes as if, pleasurably, she were, with her words, whispering around his head. “I have observed that ye affected her.”

“Why, she likes me well. She is a good wench—and today she tore my hair.”

“Then that is along of a man?” he asked. “Didst not stick thy needle in her arm? Or wilto be quit of her?”

===Chinese===
「汝怎生知道？」她说。

「俺留意过她。」他板着面孔咬定说。

「哟，想看不见她也难，」她答道，「她壮得赛过拉犁的公牛。」

「俺留意到，」他说——他眨着小眼睛，仿佛她的话语正绕着他的脑袋窃窃私语，令他受用——「俺留意到，汝是钟爱她的。」

「哟，她也着实喜欢我。她是个好姑娘——就是今日，她扯了我的头发。」

「那可是为了一个男人？」他问，「可是汝把针戳进了她的胳膊？还是想甩脱她？」

===Original===
She rubbed her chin.

“Why, if she wed, I mun be quit of her,” she said, as if she had never thought of that thing.

He answered—

“Assuredly; for ye may not part man and lawful wife were you seven times Queen.”

“Why,” she said, “I have little pleasure in Margot as she is.”

“Then let her go,” he answered.

===Chinese===
她摩挲着下巴。

「哟，她若嫁了人，我就得放她走。」她说，倒像从未想过这一节似的。

他答道——

「那是自然；夫妻名分既定，任汝做七重王后，也拆散不得。」

「哟，」她说，「玛戈照现在这样，我实在没多少乐趣。」

「那就放她走。」他答道。

===Original===
“But I am a very lonely Queen,” she said, “for you are much absent.”

He reflected pleasurably.

“Thee wouldst have about thee a little company of well-wishers?”

“So that they be those thou lovest well,” she said.

“Why, thy maid contents me,” he answered. He reflected slowly. “We must give her man a post about thee,” he uttered triumphantly.

“Why, trust thee to pleasure me,” she said. “You will find out a way always.”

===Chinese===
「可我这王后，实在孤单得很，」她说，「汝时常不在跟前。」

他惬意地寻思着。

「汝想在身边聚一小伙心愿汝好的人儿？」

「只要他们都是汝所深爱的人。」她说。

「哟，汝那侍女，俺看中意。」他答道。他又慢吞吞地想了想。「咱们得给她的男人在汝跟前安个差事。」他得意洋洋地说。

「哟，指望得上汝讨我欢心，」她说，「汝总能想出法子来。」

===Original===
He scrubbed her nose gently with his heavy finger.

“Who is the man?” he said. “What ruffler?”

“I think it is the Magister Udal,” she answered.

Henry said—

===Chinese===
他用粗大的手指，轻轻摩挲她的鼻尖。

「那男人是谁？」他说，「哪一路招摇的浪荡子？」

「我想，是尤达尔先生。」她答道。

亨利道——

===Original===
“Oh ho! oh ho!” And after a moment he slapped his thigh and laughed like a child. She laughed with him, silverly upon a little sound between “ah’ and ’e.” He stopped his laugh to listen to hers, and then he said gravely—

“I think your laugh is the prettiest sound I ever heard. I would give thy maid Margot a score of husbands to make thee laugh.”

“One is enough to make her weep,” she said; “and I may laugh at thee.”

He said—

“Let us finish this business within the hour. Sit you upon your chair that I may call one to send this ruffler here.”

===Chinese===
「哦嗬！哦嗬！」过了一会儿，他一巴掌拍在大腿上，笑得像个孩子。她陪着他笑，银铃也似，落在一个介于「啊」与「欸」之间的细小声息上。他收了自己的笑去听她的，然后正色道——

「我想，汝的笑声是我此生听过的最好听的声响。为博汝一笑，我甘愿把汝的侍女玛戈嫁上二十回。」

「嫁一回就够教她哭的了，」她说，「我呢，只消笑您就是。」

他说——

「咱们一个钟头内把这事办完。汝且坐上您的椅子，好教俺唤个人来，把那浪荡子传到此地。」

===Original===
She rose, with one sinuous motion that pleased him well, half to her feet and, feeling behind her with one hand for the chair, aided herself with the other upon his shoulder because she knew that it gave him joy to be her prop.

“Call the maid, too,” she said, “for I would come to the secret soon.”

That pleased him too, and, having shouted for a knave he once more shook with laughter.

“Oh ho,” he said, “you will net this old fox, will you?”

And, having sent his messenger off to summon the Magister from the Lady Mary’s room, and the maid from the Queen’s, he continued for a while to soliloquise as to Udal’s predicament. For he had heard the Magister rail against matrimony in Latin hexameters and doggerel Greek. He knew that the Magister was an incorrigible fumbler after petticoats. And now, he said, this old fox was to be bagged and tied up.

===Chinese===
她起身，一个蜿蜒的动作让他好生欢喜——半支起身子，一手往身后摸索椅背，另一手借他的肩膀使力，因为她知道，做她的支柱，于他是一桩乐事。

「把那侍女也叫来，」她说，「我想快些听到那个秘密。」

这也中了他的意。他喊来一个小厮，接着又笑得浑身直颤。

「哦嗬，」他说，「汝要把这只老狐狸一网套住，是不是？」

打发信使去玛丽公主房里传尤达尔先生、去王后房里传那侍女之后，他又自言自语了好一阵，品咂尤达尔的窘境。他听见过这位先生用拉丁六音步和蹩脚希腊诗痛骂婚姻。他知道这位先生见了裙钗便忍不住伸手，无可救药。如今，他说，这只老狐狸就要落袋扎口了。

===Original===
He said—

“Well, well, well; well, well!”

For, if a Queen commanded a marriage, a marriage there must be; there was no more hope for the Magister than for any slave of Cato’s. He was cabined, ginned, trapped, shut in from the herd of bachelors. It pleased the King very well.

The King grasped the gilded arms of his great chair, Katharine sat beside him, her hands laid one within another upon her lap. She did not say one single word during the King’s interview with Magister Udal.

===Chinese===
他说——

「好好好；好好！」

王后金口既令成婚，婚便非成不可；尤达尔先生的指望，不比加图名下任何一个奴隶更多。他被圈住了、夹住了、套牢了，从此隔绝于光棍汉的群落之外。国王对此深觉快意。

国王紧攥着大椅的镀金扶手，凯瑟琳坐在他身旁，双手在裙兜上一只叠着一只。国王接见尤达尔先生的问答之间，她一言未发。

===Original===
The Magister fell upon his knees before them and, seeing the laughing wrinkles round the King’s little eyes, made sure that he was sent for—as had often been the case—to turn into Latin some jest the King had made. His gown fell about his kneeling shins, his cap was at his side, his lean, brown, and sly face, with the long nose and crafty eyes, was like a woodpecker’s.

“Goodman Magister,” Henry said. “Stand up. We have sent for thee to advance thee.” Without moving his head he rolled his eyes to one side. He loved his dramatic effects and wished to await the coming of the Queen’s maid, Margot, before he gave the weight of his message.

Udal picked up his cap and came up to his feet before them; he had beneath his gown a little book, and one long finger between its leaves to keep his place where he had been reading. For he had forgotten a saying of Thales, and was reading through Caesar’s Commentaries to find it.

“As Seneca said,” he uttered in his throat, “advancement is doubly sweet to them that deserve it not.”

===Chinese===
尤达尔先生拜倒在他们面前。他一见国王小眼睛四周含笑的皱纹，便断定自己又是被召来——此为常例——把国王说的什么俏皮话译成拉丁文。长袍散在跪着的胫骨四周，帽子搁在手边，那张瘦削、棕黄、狡黠的脸，长鼻配一双精明眼，活脱一只啄木鸟。

「教书先生，」亨利说，「起来。朕召汝来，为的是擢升汝。」他头也不动，眼珠向旁一滚。他素来讲究戏剧效果，定要等王后的侍女玛戈到了，才肯把口谕的分量放出来。

尤达尔拾起帽子，起身立在他们跟前；袍子底下藏着一本小书，一根长指头夹在书页间，留住方才读到的所在。原来他把泰勒斯的一句格言忘了，正通读恺撒的《战记》，想把它寻出来。

「诚如塞内加所言，」他在喉咙里发声道，「擢升之于不配得之人，甘美加倍。」

===Original===
“Why,” the King said, “we advance thee on the deserts of one that finds thee sweet, and is sweet to one doubly sweet to us, Henry of Windsor that speak sweet words to thee.”

The lines on Udal’s face drooped all a little downwards.

“Y’are reader in Latin to the Lady Mary,” the King said.

“I have little deserved in that office,” Udal answered; “the lady reads Latin better than even I.”

===Chinese===
「如此，」国王说，「朕擢升汝，凭的是一位觉得汝甘甜之人的情分；而她，于朕所加倍甘甜之人也甚甘甜——朕，温莎的亨利，正把甘甜话说与汝听。」

尤达尔脸上的皱纹，一齐微微往下垂。

「汝现为玛丽公主的拉丁文侍读。」国王说。

「此职我实在无功可居，」尤达尔答道，「这位公主读拉丁文，比我还强。」

===Original===
“Why, you lie in that,” Henry said, “’a readeth well for she’s my daughter; but not so well as thee.”

Udal ducked his head; he was not minded to carry modesty further than in reason.

“The Lady Mary—the Lady Mary of England—” the King said weightily—and these last two words of his had a weight all their own, so that he added, “of England” again, and then, “will have little longer need of thee. She shall wed with a puissant Prince.”

“I hail, I felicitate, I bless the day I hear those words,” the Magister said.

“Therefore,” the King said—and his ears had caught the rustle of Margot’s grey gown—“we will let thee no more be reader to that my daughter.”

===Chinese===
「哟，这话便是虚言了，」亨利说，「她读得好，因为她是朕的女儿；却不及汝。」

尤达尔低下头去；谦逊过了本分，他可不干。

「玛丽公主——英格兰的玛丽公主——」国王字字千钧地说——末后这个字眼自有其千钧之重，于是他又补了一遍「英格兰」，然后道，「不久便用不着汝了。她要许配一位威势煊赫的亲王。」

「我欢呼，我称贺，我祝福听闻此言的这一天。」尤达尔先生说。

「故而，」国王说——他的耳朵已捕捉到玛戈灰裙的窸窣——「朕再不许汝做朕这女儿的侍读了。」

===Original===
Margot came round the green silk curtains that were looped on the corner posts of the pavilion. When she saw the Magister her great, fair face became slowly of a fiery red; slowly and silently she fell, with motions as if bovine, to her knees at the Queen’s side. Her gown was all grey, but it had roses of red and white silk round the upper edges of the square neck-place, and white lawn showed beneath her grey cap.

“We advance thee,” Henry said, “to be Chancellier de la Royne, with an hundred pounds by the year from my purse. Do homage for thine office.”

Udal fell upon one knee before Katharine, and dropping both cap and book, took her hand to raise to his lips. But Margot caught her hand when he had done with it and set upon it a huge pressure.

“But, Sir Chancellor,” the King said, “it is evident that so grave an office must have a grave fulfiller. And, to ballast thee the better, the Queen of her graciousness hath found thee a weighty helpmeet. So that, before you shall touch the duties and emoluments of this charge you shall, and that even tonight, wed this Madam Margot that here kneels.”

===Chinese===
玛戈绕过凉亭角柱上挂拢的绿丝帘走了进来。一见到尤达尔先生，她那张白净的大脸慢慢烧起一片通红；她缓缓地、无声地跪倒在王后身侧，动作迟缓如牛。她的裙衫通体灰色，只是方形领口的上缘镶着一圈红白二色的丝绢玫瑰，灰色便帽底下露出一圈细白麻纱。

「朕擢升汝，」亨利说，「为王后府宰相（Chancellier de la Royne），岁俸一百镑，由朕的私囊支给。为汝的职衔行效忠之礼吧。」

尤达尔在凯瑟琳面前单膝跪下，帽子和书一并脱手落地，捧起她的手要送到唇边。他一放手，玛戈便把那只手接了过去，紧紧攥住，一股大力直压下去。

「只是，宰相大人，」国王说，「如此庄重之职，显然须得一位庄重之人来充任。再者，为给汝压舱配重，王后已大发慈恩，为汝觅得一位分量十足的贤内助。故而，在汝沾手此项职守之责与俸之前，汝须——就在今夜——娶了跪在此处的这位玛戈夫人。」

===Original===
Udal’s face had been of a coppery green pallor ever since he had heard the title of Chancellor.

“Eheu!” he said, “this is the torture of Tantalus that might never drink.”

In its turn the face of Margot Poins grew pale, pushed forward towards him; but her eyes appeared to blaze, for all they were a mild blue, and the Queen felt the pressure upon her hand grow so hard that it pained her.

The King uttered the one word, “Magister!”

Udal’s fingers picked at the fur of his moth-eaten gown.

===Chinese===
自听得『宰相』这头衔那一刻起，尤达尔的脸就一直是铜绿一般的灰败。

「呜呼！」他说，「这是坦塔罗斯的酷刑——近着水，却永世喝不得。」

轮到玛戈·普因斯的脸色发白了，一张脸朝他探过去；那双眼虽是温和的蓝色，却似在冒火；王后觉出手上那一握越来越紧，紧得发疼。

国王只吐出一个词：「先生！」

尤达尔的指头抠弄着虫蛀长袍上的毛皮。

===Original===
“God be favourable to me,” he said. “If it were anything but Chancellor!”

The King grew more rigid.

“Body of God,” he said, “will you wed with this maid?”

“Ahí!” the Magister wailed; and his perturbation had in it something comic and scarecrowlike, as if a wind shook him from within. “If you will make me anything but a Chancellor, I will. But a Chancellor, I dare not.”

The King cast himself back in his chair. The suggested gibe rose furiously to his lips; the Magister quailed and bent before him, throwing out his hands.

===Chinese===
「愿天主眷顾我，」他说，「若是宰相之外的任何官职都好说！」

国王愈发端凝。

「天主的圣体，」他说，「汝究竟娶不娶这位少女？」

「哎呀！」尤达尔先生哀声叫起来。他那副狼狈里透着几分滑稽，活像个稻草人，仿佛有风在他身体里摇晃他。「陛下若封我宰相之外的任何官职，我都敢领。唯独宰相，我不敢。」

国王猛地朝椅背上一仰。一句讥诮涌到唇边，气势汹汹；尤达尔在他面前吓矮了半截，弯着身子，双手直往外摊。

===Original===
“Sire,” he said, “if—which God forbid—this were a Protestant realm I might do it. But oh, pardon and give ear. Pardon and give ear—”

He waved one hand furiously at the silken canopy above them.

“It is agreed with one of mine in Paris that she shall come hither—God forgive me, I must make avowal, though God knows I would not—she shall come hither to me if she do hear that I have risen to be a Chancellor.”

The King said, “Body of God!” as if it were an earthquake.

“If it were anything else but Chancellor she might not come, and I would wed Margot Poins more willingly than any other. But—God knows I do not willingly make this avowal, but am in a corner, *sicut vulpis in lucubris*, like a fox in the coils—this Paris woman is my wife.”

===Chinese===
「陛下，」他说，「倘若——天主不容——这是个新教之邦，我或许敢。可是，唉，恕罪，且听我说。恕罪，且听我说——」

他朝头顶上方的丝幔狂乱地挥着一只手。

「我与巴黎的一位相好有约在先——天主饶恕我，我须得招认，虽则天主知道我百般不愿——只消她听闻我荣升了宰相，她便要到我这里来。」

国王说了声「天主的圣体！」，地动山摇一般。

「倘是宰相之外的任何官职，她便不会来，我也情愿娶玛戈·普因斯，胜过娶天下任何女子。可是——天主知道，这招认我并非甘愿，只是被逼进了死角，*sicut vulpis in lucubris*，如堕网罗的狐狸——这个巴黎女人，是我的妻。」

===Original===
Henry gave a great shout of laughter, but slowly Margot Poins fell across the Queen’s knees. She uttered no sound, but lay there motionless. The sight affected Udal to an epileptic fury.

“Jove be propitious to me!” he stuttered out. “I know not what I can do.” He began to tear the fur of his cloak and toss it over the battlements. “The woman is my wife—wed by a friar. If this were a Protestant realm now—or if I pleaded pre-contract—and God knows I ha’ promised marriage to twenty women before I, in an evil day, married one—eheu!—to this one—”

He began to sob and to wring his thin hands.

“*Quod faciam? Me miser! Utinam. Utinam—*”

He recovered a little coherence.

===Chinese===
亨利爆出一声大笑，而玛戈·普因斯缓缓地瘫倒进王后的膝间。她一声不吭，直挺挺卧着。这景象激得尤达尔一阵羊痫风似的狂怒。

「朱庇特垂怜于我！」他结结巴巴地说，「我不知如何是好。」他动手去撕斗篷上的毛皮，一把把抛过雉堞。「那女人是我的妻——由一个修士证的婚。倘这如今是个新教之邦——又或者我申辩预先婚约——天主知道，在我于一个倒霉日子娶下一个之前，我已向二十个女人许过婚——呜呼！——偏娶了这一个——」

他开始呜咽，绞着自己一双瘦手。

「*Quod faciam? Me miser! Utinam. Utinam—*（教我如何是好？苦命的我！但愿如此，但愿如此——）」

他稍稍复了点条理。

===Original===
“If this were a Protestant land ye might say this wedding was no wedding, for that a friar did it; but I know ye will not suffer that—” His eyes appealed piteously to the Queen.

“Why, then,” he said, “it is not upon my head that I do not wed this wench. You be my witness that I would wed; it gores my heart to see her look so pale. It tears my vitals to see any woman look pale. As Lucretius says, ‘Better the sunshine of smiles—’”

A little outputting of impatient breath from Katharine made him stop.

“It is you, your Grace,” he said, “that make me thus tied. If you would let us be Protestant, or, again, if I could plead pre-contract to void this Paris marriage it would let me wed with this wench—eheu—eheu. Her brother will break my bones—”

He began to cry out so lamentably, invoking Pluto to bear him to the underworld, that the King roared out upon him—

===Chinese===
「倘这是个新教的国，二位陛下或许可以说这婚礼算不得婚礼，因它出于修士之手；可我知道二位陛下断然容不得——」他的眼睛凄凄切切地哀告着王后。

「那么，如此说来，」他说，「我不娶这位姑娘，罪不在我头上。二位陛下可为我作证，我是肯娶的；见她面色这样惨白，我心如刀绞。见任何女人面色惨白，我都肝肠寸断。诚如卢克莱修所言：『微笑的阳光更胜——』」

凯瑟琳从鼻息里透出一点不耐，他便住了口。

「正是您，陛下，」他说，「害我落到这般受缚。陛下若肯让咱们改宗新教，再或者，我若能申辩预先婚约、废了这桩巴黎婚事，我便能娶这位姑娘——呜呼——呜呼。她兄弟会打断我的骨头——」

他开始嚎得凄凄惨惨，呼求普路托把他摄往冥府，国王朝他咆哮起来——

===Original===
“Why, get you gone, fool.”

The Magister threw himself suddenly upon his knees, his hands clasped, his gown drooping over them down to his wrists. He turned his face to the Queen.

“Before God,” he said, “before high and omnipotent Jove, I swear that when I made this marriage I thought it was no marriage!” He reflected for a breath and added, at the recollection of the cook’s spits that had been turned against him when he had by woman’s guile been forced into marriage with the widow in Paris, “I was driven into it by force, with sharp points at my throat. Is that not enow to void a marriage? Is that not enow? Is that not enow?”

Katharine looked out over the great levels of the view. Her face was rigid, and she swallowed in her throat, her eye being glazed and hard. The King took his cue from a glance at her face.

“Get you gone, Goodman Rogue Magister,” he said, and he adopted a canonical tone that went heavily with his rustic pose. “A marriage made and consummated and properly blessed by holy friar there is no undoing. You are learned enough to know that. Rogue that you be, I am very glad that you are trapped by this marriage. Well I know that you have dangled too much with petticoats, to the great scandal of this my Court. Now you have lost your preferment, and I am glad of it. Another and a better than thou shall be the Queen’s Chancellor, for another and a better than thou shall wed this wench. We will get her such a goodly husband—”

===Chinese===
「罢了，去你的，蠢货。」

尤达尔先生猛然扑跪下去，双手合十，长袍垂覆过手，直垂到腕。他把脸转向王后。

「在天主面前，」他说，「在高高在上、全能的朱庇特面前，我起誓：我结这门亲时，只当它算不得婚姻！」他寻思了一息，又想起当年他中了女人的奸计、被逼与巴黎那寡妇成婚时，一杆杆转向他的烤肉铁叉，便又添道：「我是被逼成婚的，喉咙上抵着锋利的尖叉。这还不足以废一桩婚么？这还不足以么？这还不足以么？」

凯瑟琳纵目望向视野中开阔的平野。她面容僵冷，喉间咽了一口气，目光呆滞而坚硬。国王瞥了一眼她的脸，便得了提点。

「去，滚吧，好个流氓教书先生。」他说着，换上一副教规的腔调，同他那庄稼汉做派搁在一处，滞重难移。「凡经圣洁修士主持、既已完婚、又正经祝圣过的婚姻，无可解除。以汝之博学，尽知此理。汝这无赖，朕见汝被这桩婚事套住，欢喜得很。朕深知汝流连裙裾太久，大损朕这座宫廷的体面。如今汝失了这份美差，朕心甚慰。自有比汝更好的人来做王后的宰相，因为自有比汝更好的人来娶这位姑娘。朕会给她寻一位好端端的夫婿——」

===Original===
A low, melancholy wail from Margot Poins’ agonised face—a sound such as might have been made by an ox in pain—brought him to a stop. It wrung the Magister, who could not bear to see a woman pained, up to a pitch of ecstatic courage.

“*Quid fecit Caesar*,” he stuttered; “what Caesar hath done, Caesar can do again. It was not till very lately since this canon of wedding and consummating and blessing by a holy friar hath been derided and contemned in this realm. And so it might be again—”

Katharine Howard cried out, “Ah!” Her features grew rigid and as ashen as cold steel. And, at her cry, the King—who could less bear than Udal to hear a woman in pain—the King sprang up from his chair. It was as amazing to all them as to hunters it is to see a great wild bull charge with a monstrous velocity. Udal was rigid with fear, and the King had him by the throat. He shook him backwards and forwards so that his book fell upon the Queen’s feet, bursting out of his ragged gown, and his cap, flying from his opened hand, fell down over the battlement into an elm top. The King guttered out unintelligible sounds of fury from his vast chest and, planted on his huge feet, he swung the Magister round him till, backwards and staggering, the eyes growing fixed in his brown and rigid face, he was pushed, jerking at each step of the King, out of sight behind the green silk curtains.

===Chinese===
玛戈·普因斯那张痛楚的脸发出一声低低的、凄惶的哀号——像一头受苦的牛会发出的声音——教他把话收住了。这声音绞着尤达尔先生的心——他见不得女人受苦——竟把他拧到了一种忘乎所以的勇气顶点。

「*Quid fecit Caesar*，」他结结巴巴地说，「恺撒做过的，恺撒还能再做。迟至晚近，这条由圣洁修士主持婚礼、完婚与祝圣的教规，才在本国遭人讥诮鄙弃。是故它未必不能再遭一回——」

凯瑟琳·霍华德喊出一声「啊！」她五官僵住，灰败如冷钢。这一声喊里，国王——比起尤达尔，他更容不得听见女人受苦——国王从椅中一跃而起。众人眼里的骇异，不亚于猎人望见一头巨野公牛挟着骇人的势头冲来。尤达尔吓得僵在原地，国王已扼住他的咽喉，前后摇晃他，摇得那本书挣出褴褛的长袍，落在王后脚上；帽子从他松开的手里飞出去，翻过雉堞，落进一株榆树的树梢。国王从宽阔的胸膛里迸溅出一串含混的怒吼，如残烛噼啪；他双足如桩钉地，抡着尤达尔先生绕身打转，直抡得他踉跄倒退，棕黄僵板的脸上一双眼珠渐渐定住，随着国王每一步被推得一颠一颠，终于推出绿丝帘后，不见了。

===Original===
The Queen sat motionless in her purple velvet. She twisted one hand into the chain of the medallion about her throat, and one hand lay open and pale by her side. Margot Poins knelt at her side, her face hidden in the Queen’s lap, her two arms stretched out beyond her grey coifed head. For a minute she was silent. Then great sobs shook her so that Katharine swayed upon her seat. From her hidden face there came muffled and indistinguishable words, and at last Katharine said dully—

“What, child? What, child?”

Margot moved her face sideways so that her mouth was towards Katharine.

“You can unmake it! You can unmake the marriage,” she brought out in huge sobs.

===Chinese===
王后一身紫天鹅绒，端坐不动。她一只手绞进喉间大奖章的链子里，另一只手摊在身侧，苍白。玛戈·普因斯跪在她旁边，脸埋在王后裙兜里，两条手臂伸展开去，越过她那戴灰帽的头。有一分钟她无声无息。随即大颗的抽噎撼动她，撼得凯瑟琳在座上摇晃。那埋着的脸里透出闷住的、听不真切的话语，末了，凯瑟琳木然道——

「什么，孩子？什么，孩子？」

玛戈把脸侧过来，让嘴对着凯瑟琳。

「您能拆散它！您能解除这桩婚事。」她夹着大颗大颗的抽噎，一字一顿地说。

===Original===
Katharine said—

“No! No!”

“You unmade a King’s marriage,” Margot wailed.

Katharine said—

“No! No!” She started and uttered the words loudly; she added pitifully, “You do not understand! You do not understand!”

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳道——

「不！不！」

「您解除过国王的婚事呢。」玛戈哀声哭道。

凯瑟琳道——

「不！不！」她一惊，高声说出这几个字；又凄然添道：「您不明白！您不明白！」

===Original===
It was the more pitiful in that Margot understood very well. She hid her face again and only sobbed heavily and at long intervals, and then with many sobs at once. The Queen laid her white hand upon the girl’s head. Her other still played with the chain.

“Christ be piteous to me,” she said. “I think it had been better if I had never married the King.”

Margot uttered an indistinguishable sound.

“I think it had been better,” the Queen said; “though I had jeoparded my immortal part.”

Margot moved her head up to cry out in her turn—

“No! No! You may not say it!”

===Chinese===
愈发堪怜的是，玛戈其实全然明白。她重新埋起脸，只沉沉地抽噎，一阵隔得老远，一阵又密密连成片。王后把白皙的手搁在少女头上。另一只手仍在拨弄那条链子。

「愿基督怜悯我，」她说，「我想，我当初若不曾嫁给国王，倒好些。」

玛戈发出一声听不真切的声响。

「我想倒好些，」王后说，「纵使我曾陷自己不朽的灵魂于危殆。」

玛戈抬起头来，轮到她喊出声——

「不！不！您不可这样讲！」

===Original===
Then she dropped her face again. When she heard the King coming back and breathing heavily, she stood up, and with huge tears on her red and crumpled face she looked out upon the fields as if she had never seen them before. An immense sob shook her. The King stamped his foot with rage, and then, because he was softhearted to them that he saw in sorrow, he put his hand upon her shoulder.

“Sha’t have a better mate,” he uttered. “Sha’t be a knight’s dame! There! there!” and he fondled her great back with his hand. Her eyes screwed tightly up, she opened her mouth wide, but no words came out, and suddenly she shook her head as if she had been an enraged child. Her loud cries, shaken out of her with her tears, died away as she went across the terrace, a loud one and then a little echo, a loud one and then two more.

“Before God!” the King said, “that knave shall eat ten years of prison bread.”

His wife looked still over the wooded enclosures, the little stone walls, and the copses. A small cloud had come before the sun, and its shadow was moving leisurely across the ridge where stood the roofless abbey.

“The maid shall have the best man I can give her,” the King said.

===Chinese===
随后她又把脸埋了下去。听得国王回来、喘息沉重，她站起身，大红皱作一团的脸上挂着大颗大颗的泪，望向田野，仿佛从未见过它们似的。一声巨大的抽噎撼过她全身。国王怒得跺脚，可他见了愁苦之人便心软，于是把手放上她的肩头。

「汝会有一位更好的夫君，」他说，「汝要做骑士夫人了！好啦！好啦！」他用手抚着她宽厚的脊背。她双眼紧紧挤作一团，嘴张得老大，吐不出一个字，忽然像恼怒的孩子般连连摇头。她穿过露台而去，哭喊随着泪水一齐从她身上抖落，渐次消逝——一声高，跟着一点回响；又一声高，跟着又是两声。

「天主在上！」国王说，「那厮得吃上十年牢饭。」

他这位王后仍望着林木围场、矮矮的石墙和一丛丛小树林。一小片云遮到日头前面，云影正悠悠地掠过那道山脊——无顶的修道院便立在脊上。

「这少女自会有朕能给的最好的男人。」国王说。

===Original===
“Why, no good man would wed her!” Katharine answered dully.

Henry said—

“Anan?” Then he fingered the dagger on the chain before his chest.

“Why,” he added slowly, “then the Magister shall die by the rope. It is an offence that can be quitted with death. It is time such a thing were done.”

Katharine’s dull silence spurred him; he shrugged his shoulders and heaved a deep breath out.

===Chinese===
「哟，正经的好男人谁肯娶她！」凯瑟琳木然答道。

亨利道——

「嗯？」说着，他拨弄起胸前链上坠着的那柄匕首。

「如此，」他慢条斯理地补上一句，「那尤达尔先生就得拿绞索抵命。这是一项足以拿死来清偿的罪名。这样的事，也该有人办上一回了。」

凯瑟琳木然的沉默刺激着他；他耸耸肩，深深吐出一口气。

===Original===
“Why,” he said, “a man can be found to wed the wench.”

She moved one hand and uttered—

“I would not wed her to such a man!” as if it were a matter that was not much in her thoughts.

“Then she may go into a nunnery,” the King said; “for before three months are out we will have many nunneries in this realm.”

She looked upon him a little absently, but she smiled at him to give him pleasure. She was thinking that she wished she had not wedded him; but she smiled because, things being as they were, she thought that she had all the authorities of the noble Greeks and Romans to bid her do what a good wife should.

===Chinese===
「得，」他说，「总能寻出一个男人来娶这姑娘。」

她动了动一只手，说道——

「我不愿把她嫁给那样一个男人！」仿佛这事在她心上本没多少分量。

「那她便入女修道院去，」国王说，「不出三个月，朕要教这国中立起许多女修道院。」

她略带惘然地望着他，却仍朝他微笑，好教他欢喜。她心里想的是，但愿自己不曾嫁他；可她仍旧微笑，因为事已至此，她自认古来高贵的希腊人、罗马人的一切权威，都命她尽贤妻的本分。

===Original===
He laughed at her griefs, thinking that they were all about Margot Poins. He uttered jolly grossnesses; he said that she little knew the way of courts if she thought that a man, and a very good man, might not be found to wed the wench.

She was troubled that he could not better read what was upon her mind, for she was thinking that her having consented to his making null his marriage with the Princess of Cleves that he might wed her would render her work always the more difficult. It would render her more the target for evil tongues, it would set a sterner and a more stubborn opposition against her task of restoring the Kingdom of God within that realm.

Henry said—

===Chinese===
他拿她的愁烦打趣，只当那全是为了玛戈·普因斯。他说了几句乐呵呵的粗话；又说，她若以为寻不出一个男人——还是一个好好先生——来娶这姑娘，那她可太不谙宫廷的门道了。

她因他参不透她的心事而烦恼。她想的是：当日她既曾应允国王废除他与克莱沃公主（the Princess of Cleves）的婚姻、好娶她为妻，此事便要教她的功业永远加倍艰难——教她更成谗口之的，教更严峻、更顽梗的对立面，拦在她于这国度之内重建天主之国的功业跟前。

亨利道——

===Original===
“Ye hannot guessed what my secret was? What have I done for thee this day?”

She still looked away over the lands. She made her face smile—

“Nay, I know not. Ha’ ye brought me the musk I love well?”

He shook his head.

“It is more than that!” he said.

===Chinese===
「汝还没猜着俺的秘密是什么？俺今日为汝做下了什么？」

她仍望着远处的田畴。她摆出一脸笑——

「不，我猜不着。汝可给我带来了我所深爱的麝香？」

他摇了摇头。

「比那要紧得多！」他说。

===Original===
She still smiled—

“Ha’ ye—ha’ ye—made make for me a new crown?”

She feared a little that that was what he had done. For he had been urgent with her, many months, to be crowned. It was his way to love these things. And her heart was a little gladder when he shook his head once again and uttered—

“It is more than that!”

She dreaded his having made ready in secret a great pageant in her honour, for she was afraid of all aggrandisements, and thought still it had been better that she had remained his sweet friend ever and not the Queen. For in that way she would have had as much empire over him, and there would have been much less clamour against her—much less clamour against the Church of her Saviour.

===Chinese===
她仍旧笑着——

「汝可——汝可——命人为我制了一顶新王冠？」

她有点怕，怕他做的正是这一桩。数月以来，他一直催她受加冕。这般排场，本是他心口所爱。待他又一次摇头、说道——她心里才轻快了些。

「比那要紧得多！」

她怕的是他暗中备下了一场为她增光的大型庆典，因为她畏惧一切荣显，而且始终觉得，她若永远做他的甜心爱侣、而非王后，倒更好些。因为那样，她对他的辖制不减分毫，而攻讦她的喧嚣会少得多——攻讦她救主之教会的喧嚣，也会少得多。

===Original===
She forced her mind to run upon all the things that she could wish for. When she said it must be that he had ordered for her enough French taffetas to make twelve gowns, he laughed and said that he had said that it was more than a crown. When she guessed that he had made ready such a huge cavalcade that she might with great comfort and safety ride with him into Scotland, he laughed, contented that she should think of going with him upon that long journey. He stood looking at her, his little eyes blinking, his face full of pride and joy, and suddenly he uttered—

“The Church of God is come back again.” He touched his cap at the sacred name. “I ha’ made submission to the Pope.”

He looked her full in the face to get all the delight he might from her looks and her movements.

Her blue eyes grew large; she leaned forward in her chair; her mouth opened a little; her sleeves fell down to the ground. “Now am I indeed crowned!” she said, and closed her eyes. “*Benedicta sit mater dei!*” she uttered, and her hand went over her heart place; “*deo clamavi nocte atque dië.*”

===Chinese===
她强逼着自己的心念，把心愿一件件想过来。当她说，想必是他给她订下了够做十二件袍裙的法兰西塔夫绸，他笑道，方才不是说了么，这桩事比一顶王冠还要紧。当她猜，他是备下了一支浩荡的仪仗马队，好教她舒舒服服、安安稳稳地随他骑行入苏格兰，他又笑了，见她想着随他同赴那趟长途，心下满足。他站着端详她，小眼睛眨呀眨，满脸得意与欢欣，忽然说道——

「天主的教会回来了。」道出这神圣名号，他抬手触帽为敬。「朕已向教皇臣服。」

他直直望定她的脸，要从她的神色举止里，把能得的欢悦尽数收进眼底。

她的蓝眼睛睁大了；她在椅中前倾；朱唇微启；双袖垂落在地。「如今我这才是真受了加冕！」她说着阖上双眼。「*Benedicta sit mater dei!*（福哉，天主之母！）」她低声诵道，一只手覆上心口；「*deo clamavi nocte atque dië.*（昼夜我向天主呼求。）」

===Original===
She was silent again, and she leaned more forward.

“*Sit benedicta dies haec; sit benedicta hora haec benedictaque, saeculum saeculûm, castra haec.*”

She looked out upon the great view: she aspired the air.

“*Ad colles*,” she breathed, “*levavi oculos meos; unde venit salvatio nostra!*”

“Body of God,” Henry said, “all things grow plain. All things grow plain. This is the best day that ever I knew.”

===Chinese===
她重又静默，身子愈发前倾。

「*Sit benedicta dies haec; sit benedicta hora haec benedictaque, saeculum saeculûm, castra haec.*（愿此日蒙福；愿此时辰蒙福，且蒙福至于世世无尽，并及这座营垒。）」

她纵目望向那片辽阔景致，深深吸进长风。

「*Ad colles*，」她轻轻吐息，「*levavi oculos meos; unde venit salvatio nostra!*（我向群山举目；我们的救恩，正从那里来！）」

「天主的圣体，」亨利说，「万事都豁然开朗。万事都豁然开朗。这是朕平生所知的最好的一天。」

### IV

===Original===
The Lady Mary of England sat alone in a fair room with little arched windows that gave high up on to the terrace. It was the best room that ever she had had since her mother, the Queen Katharine of Aragon, had been divorced.

Dressed in black she sat writing at a large table before one window. Her paper was fitted on to a wooden pulpit that rose before her; one book stood open upon it, three others lay open too upon the red and blue and green pattern of the Saracen rug that covered her table. At her right hand was a three-tiered inkstand of pewter, set about with the white feathers of pens; and the snakelike pattern of the table-rug serpentined in and out beneath seals of parcel gilt, a platter of bread, a sandarach of pewter, books bound in wooden covers and locked with chains, books in red velvet covers, sewn with silver wire and tied with ribbons. It ran beneath a huge globe of the world, blue and pink, that had a golden pin in it to mark the city of Rome. There were little wooden racks stuck full with written papers and parchments along the wainscoting between the arched windows, but all the hangings of the other walls were of tinted and dyed silks, not any with dark colours, because Katharine Howard had deemed that that room with its deep windows in the thick walls would be otherwise dark. The room was ten paces deep by twenty long, and the wood of the floor was polished. Against the wall, behind the Lady Mary's back, there stood a high chair upon a platform. Upon the platform a carpet began that ran up the wall and, overhead, depended from the gilded rafters of the ceiling so that it formed a dais and a canopy.

The Lady Mary sat grimly amongst all these things as if none of them belonged to her. She looked in her book, she made a note upon her paper, she stretched out her hand and took a piece of bread, putting it in her mouth, swallowing it quickly, writing again, and then once more eating, for the great and ceaseless hunger that afflicted her gnawed always at her vitals.

A little boy with a fair poll was reaching on tiptoe to smell at a pink that depended from a vase of very thin glass standing in the deep window. The shield of the coloured pane cast a little patch of red and purple on to his callow head. He was dressed all in purple, very square, and with little chains and medallions, and a little dagger with a golden sheath was about his neck. In one hand he had a piece of paper, in the other a pencil. The Lady Mary wrote; the child moved on tiptoe, with a sedulous expression of silence about his lips, near to her elbow. He watched her writing for a long time with attentive eyes.

===Chinese===
英格兰的玛丽公主独自坐在一间雅致的房间里，小小的拱窗高高地开向露台。自她的母亲阿拉贡的王后凯瑟琳被休弃以来，这是她所曾拥有过的最好的房间。

她一身黑衣，坐在一扇窗前的大书案边书写。稿纸铺在她面前立起的一座斜面木架上；架上摊开一本书，另有三本也摊开着，放在罩住整张书案的撒拉森地毯的红蓝绿花纹上。她右手边是一方白镴三层墨水架，四周插满白色的鹅毛笔；地毯上蛇形的纹样，蜿蜒穿行于局部鎏金的印章、一盘面包、一只白镴砂粉盒、几部木板封面加链锁扣的书、几部红天鹅绒封面银线装订缎带系扎的书之间。它还蜿蜒到一只蓝粉二色的硕大地球仪底下——仪上插着一枚金针，标出罗马城。拱窗之间的护壁板上，一排排小木架插满了写满字迹的纸页和羊皮卷；可其余几面墙上挂的都是浅染色绸，没有一幅深色的，因为凯瑟琳·霍华德认为，这房间窗洞开在厚墙深处，再用深色便要显暗了。房间进深十步，长二十步，地板的木料打磨得光亮。玛丽公主背后靠墙，一座高椅立在台座上；台座上铺开一张地毯，沿墙直上，又在头顶从镀金的椽木间垂落下来，合成一座高台华盖。

玛丽公主冷硬地坐在这一切中间，仿佛没有一样东西是她的。她查书，在稿纸上记一笔，伸手拿一块面包，塞进嘴里，匆匆咽下，再写，然后又吃——因为折磨着她的那巨大而无休止的饥饿，总在啃啮她的脏腑。

一个淡金头发的小男孩踮起脚尖，去嗅深窗里一只极薄的玻璃瓶中垂下的一枝石竹。彩色玻璃的盾形纹样，在他雏发未丰的头顶投下一小块红紫光斑。他一身紫衣，方方正正，缀着细小的链子和圆章，颈间挂一柄金鞘小匕首。一只手拿一张纸，另一只手拿一支笔。玛丽公主写着；那孩子踮着脚尖，唇边挂着刻意缄默的神情，挪到她的臂肘旁，用专注的眼睛看了她写字很久。

===Original===
Once he said, "Sister, I﻿—" but she paid him no heed.

After a time she looked coldly at his face and then he moved along the table, fingered the globe very gently, touched the books and returned to her side. He stood with his little legs wide apart. Then he sighed, then he said﻿—

"Sister, the Queen did bid me ask you a question."

She looked round upon him.

"This was the Queen's question," he said bravely: " '﻿*Cur*﻿—why﻿—*nunquam*﻿—never﻿—*rides*﻿—dost thou smile﻿—*cum*﻿—when﻿—*ego, frater tuus*﻿—I, thy little brother﻿—*ludo*﻿—play﻿—*in camerâ tuâ*﻿—in thy chamber?' "

===Chinese===
有一回他开口：「姐姐，我——」她却不理他。

过了一阵，她冷淡地看了他的脸一眼，他便沿着书案挪动，轻轻碰了碰地球仪，摸了摸那些书，又回到她身边。他两条小腿分开站着。他叹了口气，然后说道——

「姐姐，王后叫我来问您一个问题。」

她转过脸来看他。

「王后的问题是这个，」他鼓着勇气说：「『*Cur*——为何——*nunquam*——从不——*rides*——汝方展颜——*cum*——当——*ego, frater tuus*——我，汝的小兄弟——*ludo*——嬉戏——*in camerâ tuâ*——于汝的闺房之时？』」

===Original===
"Little Prince," she said, "art not afeared of me?"

"Aye, am I," he answered.

"Say then to the Queen," she said, " '﻿*Domina Maria*﻿—the Lady Mary﻿—*ridet nunquam*﻿—smileth never﻿—*quod*﻿—because﻿—*timoris ratio*﻿—the reason of my fear﻿—*bona et satis*﻿—is good and sufficient.' "

He held his little head upon one side.

"The Queen did bid me say," he uttered with his brave little voice, " 'Holy Writ hath it: *Ecce quam bonum et dignum est fratres﻿—fratres﻿—*' " He faltered without embarrassment and added, "I ha' forgot the words."

===Chinese===
「小王子，」她说，「汝不怕我么？」

「怕，怕的。」他答道。

「那么告诉王后，」她说：「『*Domina Maria*——玛丽小姐——*ridet nunquam*——从不展颜——*quod*——因为——*timoris ratio*——我恐惧的缘由——*bona et satis*——充分而足够。』」

他歪着小脑袋。

「王后还叫我说，」他用他那勇敢的小嗓音说：「『圣经有言：*Ecce quam bonum et dignum est fratres—fratres*（看哪，弟兄们同居一处，是何等善、何等美——弟兄们——）』」他不慌不忙地打住，又添道：「后头的词儿我忘了。」

===Original===
"Aye!" she said, "they ha' been long forgotten in these places; I deem it is overlate to call them to mind."

She looked upon him coldly for a long time. Then she stretched out her hand for his paper.

"Your Highness, I will set you a copy."

She took his paper and wrote﻿—

"Malo malo malâ."

He held it in his chubby fist, his head on one side.

===Chinese===
「是么！」她说，「这些地方早把那话忘了多年；我想，如今要再想起，也太迟了。」

她冷冷地看了他许久，然后伸手去接他的纸。

「殿下，我来给汝写个字帖。」

她接过他的纸，写道——

「*Malo malo malâ.*（宁取一只苹果，不取一个恶妇。）」

他用胖乎乎的拳头攥着纸，头歪在一边。

===Original===
"I cannot conster it," he said.

"Why, think upon it," she answered. "When I was thy age I knew it already two years. But I was better beaten than thou."

He rubbed his little arm.

"I am beaten enow," he said.

===Chinese===
「我解析不来。」他说。

「哟，想想去，」她答道，「我在汝这年纪，已经会了它两年了。不过我挨的打比汝狠。」

他揉了揉自己的小胳膊。

「我挨的打够多了。」他说。

===Original===
"Knowest not what a swingeing is," she answered.

"Then thou hadst a bitter childhood," he brought out.

"I had a good mother," she cut him short.
