She turned her face to her writing again; it was bitter and set. The little prince climbed slowly into the chair on the dais. He moved sturdily and curled himself up on the cushion, studying the words on the paper all the while with a little frown upon his brows. Then, shrugging his shoulders, he set the paper upon his knee and began to write.

===Chinese===
「汝还不知道什么叫狠抽一顿。」她答道。

「那您的童年可真苦。」他冒出一句。

「我有过一位好母亲。」她截住他的话。

她把脸转回自己的书写上；那张脸又苦又僵。小王子慢慢爬上高台上的那把椅子。他动作稳实，蜷在坐垫上，一直皱着小眉头研读纸上的字。然后，他耸耸肩，把纸放到膝上，写了起来。

===Original===
At that date the Lady Mary was still called a bastard, though most men thought that that hardship would soon be reversed. It was said that great honours had been shown her, and that was apparent in the furnishing of her rooms, the fineness of her gear, the increase in the number of the women that waited on her, and the store of sweet things that was provided for her to eat. A great many men noted the chair with a dais that was set up always where she might be, in her principal room, and though her ladies said that she never sat in it, most men believed that she had made a pact with the King to do him honour and so to be reinstated in the estate in which she held her own. It was considered, too, that she no longer plotted with the King's enemies inside or out of the realm; it was at least certain that she no longer had men set to spy upon her, though it was noted that the Archbishop's gentleman, Lascelles, nosed about her quarters and her maids. But he was always spying somewhere and, as the Archbishop's days were thought to be numbered, he was accounted of little weight. Indeed, since the fall of Thomas Cromwell there seemed to be few spies about the Court, or almost none at all. It was known that gentlemen wrote accounts of what passed to Gardiner, the Bishop of Winchester. But Gardiner was gone back into his see and appeared to have little favour, though it was claimed for him that he had done much to advance the new Queen. So that, upon the whole, men breathed much more freely﻿—and women too﻿—than in the days before the fall of Privy Seal. The Queen had made little change, and seemed to have it in mind to make little more. Her relatives had, nearly none of them, been advanced. There were few Protestants oppressed, though many Catholics had been loosed from the gaols, most notably him whom the Archbishop Cranmer had taken to be his chaplain and confessor, and others that other lords had taken out of prison to be about them.

===Chinese===
就在那时节，玛丽公主仍被唤作私生女，不过多数人认为这重屈辱不久便会翻转过来。据说她已备受尊荣，这从她房间的陈设、衣饰的精美、侍女数目的增加、备给她吃的那许多甜食上，都看得出来。许多人留意到，凡她所居之处，她的正房里总设着一张带高台的座椅；虽说她的侍女们讲她从不坐它，多数人却相信她已与国王有过约定：尊他敬他，好恢复她本该享有的名分。人们还认为，她不再同国王的敌人内外勾结了；至少有一点确凿：不再有人被派来窥伺她——虽说大主教手下那位绅士拉塞尔斯，仍在她的住处和她的侍女们中间嗅来嗅去。可他本就在各处刺探，况且众人都认为大主教的日子屈指可数，人们便不把他看得很重。的确，自托马斯·克伦威尔倒台以来，宫廷里似乎没有几个密探了，或者说几乎没有。人们知道，有几位绅士把宫廷中事写信报知温彻斯特主教加德纳。可加德纳已回他的教区去了，圣眷看来寥寥，虽有人替他表功，说新王后的上位他出力甚多。因此，总的说来，比起御玺大臣倒台之前的那些日子，人们呼吸得畅快多了——女人也一样。王后几乎没有做什么变更，看那意思，也不打算再做什么。她的亲眷，几乎无人得到擢升。受压迫的新教徒寥寥，倒有许多旧教徒放出牢来，其中最显赫的一位，是克兰默大主教收为随行司铎兼听告解神父的那人，另有别的勋贵从狱中提出、安置在自己身边的那些。

===Original===
All in all the months that had passed since Cromwell's fall had gone quietly. The King and Queen had gone very often to mass since Katharine had been shown for Queen in the gardens at Hampton Court, and saints' days and the feasts of the life of our Lady had been very carefully observed, along with fasts such as had used to be observed. The King, however, was mightily fond with his new Queen, and those that knew her well, or knew her servants well, expected great changes. Some were much encouraged, some feared very much, but nearly all were heartily glad of that summer of breathing space; and the weather was mostly good, so that the corn ripened well and there was little plague or ague abroad.

Thus most men had been heartily glad to see the new Queen upon her journey there to the north parts. She had ridden upon a white horse with the King at her side; she had asked the names of several that had come to see her; she had been fair to look at; and the King had pardoned many felons, so that men's wives and mothers had been made glad; and most old men said that the good times were come again, with the price of malt fallen and twenty-six to the score of herrings. It was reported, too, that a cider press in Herefordshire had let down a dozen firkins of cider without any apples being set in it, and this was accounted an omen of great plenty, whilst many sheep had died, so that men who had set their fields down in grass talked of giving them to the plough again, and upon St. Swithin's Day no rain had fallen. All these things gave a great contentment, and many that in the hard days had thought to become Lutheran in search of betterment, now looked in byres and hidden valleys to find priests of the old faith. For if a man could plough he might eat, and if he might eat he could praise God after his father's manner as well as in a new way.

Thus, around the Lady Mary, whilst she wrote, the people of the land breathed more peace. And even she could not but be conscious of a new softness, if it was only in the warmth that came from having her window-leads properly mended. She had hardly ever before known what it was to have warm hands when she wrote, and in most days of the year she had worn fur next her skin, indoors as well as out. But now the sun beat on her new windows, and in that warmth she could wear fine lawn, so that, in spite of herself, she took pleasure and was softened, though, since she spoke to no man save the Magister Udal, and to him only about the works of Plautus or the game of cards that they played together, few knew of any change in her.

===Chinese===
总而言之，克伦威尔倒台以来这几个月，光阴过得平静。自凯瑟琳在汉普顿宫的花园里册立示众为王后以来，国王与王后常去望弥撒，诸圣徒的节期与圣母生平的庆典都谨严地守着，往常用守的斋戒也一并恢复了。只是国王对新王后宠爱得火热，深知她为人的人，或深识她仆从的人，都指望着大变。有的大受鼓舞，有的深怀恐惧，可几乎人人都由衷庆幸那个喘息的夏天；天气又多半晴好，谷物熟得饱满，乡间少有瘟疫疟疾。

因此，新王后启程北巡，人们见了都由衷欢喜。她骑一匹白马，国王随侍在侧；她问过几个前来观见者的姓名；她容色照人；国王又赦免了许多重犯，教那些人的妻室母亲皆大欢喜；许多老人都说好日子回来了——麦芽落了价，鲱鱼买二十条还饶六条。又有传闻，说赫里福德郡（Herefordshire）一架榨酒机不曾放进一只苹果，竟淌出一打小桶苹果酒，众人便把这当作大丰年的吉兆；又有许多羊死了，那些把田地改作草场的人便谈起要重新犁耕；圣斯威辛节（St. Swithin's Day）那天也滴雨未落。这些事都令人大大知足，许多在苦日子里为寻出路而想改宗路德派的人，如今都到牛棚里、隐谷中去寻访旧教的神父了。因为人若能耕作便能得食，既能得食，便可以照他父辈的老规矩赞美天主，同新花样一样。

就这样，玛丽公主伏案书写之际，这国土上的人们呼吸得愈发安宁。连她也不能不觉出一种新的温软，哪怕那温软只来自窗上铅条修葺妥当后透进的暖意。从前，写字时双手是暖的算什么滋味，她几乎不曾知道；一年里多数日子，无论屋里屋外，她都贴身穿着毛皮。可如今阳光照在她的新窗上，在那份暖意里她穿得起细麻纱了，于是不由自主地，她也生出欢喜，人也软了些——只是，除了尤达尔先生，她不与任何男人说话，同他也只谈普劳图斯（Plautus）的著作，或两人一起玩的纸牌，所以很少有人察觉她有什么变化。

===Original===
Nevertheless, on that day she had one of her more ill moods and, presently, having written a little more, she rang a small silver bell that was shaped like a Dutch woman with wide skirts.

"The Prince annoys me," she said to her woman; "send for his lady governess."

The woman, dressed all in black, like her mistress, and with a little frill of white cambric over her temples as if she were a nun, stood in the open doorway that was just level with the Lady Mary's chair, so that the stone wall of the passage caught the light from the window. She folded her hands before her.

"Alack, Madam," she said, "your Madamship knows that at this hour his Highness' lady governess taketh ever the air."

The little boy in the chair looked over his paper at his sister.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，那一天她偏偏犯上了比往常更坏的心绪；不多时，又写了几笔，她便摇响一只小银铃——铃身铸成一个宽裙荷兰妇人的模样。

「这王子搅扰我，」她对待女说，「去把他的女傅请来。」

那侍女一身黑衣，同她女主子一样，鬓角覆着一圈细白麻纱的小褶边，活像位修女。她站在敞开的门口——那门正与玛丽公主的座椅齐平——走廊的石墙承着窗光。她双手交叠在身前。

「哎呀，夫人，」她说，「夫人您知道的，这个时辰，殿下的女傅总要到外头透气。」

椅中的小男孩从纸页上方望着姐姐。

===Original===
"Send for his physician then," Mary said.

"Alack, sister," the little Prince said before the woman could move, "my physician is ill. *Jacet*﻿—He lieth﻿—*in cubiculo*﻿—in his bed."

The Lady Mary would not look round on him.

"Get thee, then," she uttered coldly, "to thine own apartments, Prince."

"Alack, sister," he answered, "thou knowest that I may not walk along the corridors alone for fear some slay me. Nor yet may I be anywhere save with the Queen, or thee, or with my uncles, or my lady governess, or my physicians, for fear some poison me."

He spoke with a clear and shrill voice, and the woman cast down her eyes, trembling a little, partly to hear such a small, weary child speak such a long speech as if by wizardry﻿—for it was reported among the serving maids that he had been overlooked﻿—and partly for fear of the black humour that she perceived to be upon her mistress.

===Chinese===
「那便去请他的医师。」玛丽说。

「哎呀，姐姐，」侍女还未及动身，小王子便抢先说，「我的医师病了。*Jacet*——他卧着——*in cubiculo*——在他床上。」

玛丽公主不肯回头看他。

「那么，汝自己去，」她冷冷地说，「回汝自己的住处去，王子。」

「哎呀，姐姐，」他答道，「您知道我不敢独自走过那些走廊，怕有人杀我。我也不得到别处去，只能同王后、同您，或同我的叔伯们、我的女傅、我的医师们在一处，怕有人毒我。」

他嗓音清亮尖细。侍女垂下眼帘，微微发抖——一半因为听这样一个瘦小倦怠的孩子像着了魔法似的一口气说出这样长一串话（侍女间都传言，他被人施过邪眼），一半因为害怕她察觉女主人身上正罩着的那股阴郁。

===Original===
"Send me then my Magister to lay out cards with me," the Lady Mary said. "I cannot make my studies with this Prince in my rooms."

"Alack, Madam," the girl said. She was high coloured and with dark eyes, but when she faltered then the colour died from her cheeks. The Lady Mary surveyed her coldly, for she was in the mood to give pain. She uttered no words.

"Alack, alack﻿—" the maid whimpered. She was full of fear lest the Lady Mary should order her to receive short rations or many stripes; she was filled with consternation and grief since her sweetheart, a server, had told her that he must leave her. For it was rumoured that the Magister had been cast into gaol for sweethearting, and that the King had said that all sweethearts should be gaoled from thenceforth. "The Magister is gaoled," she said.

"Wherefore?" the Lady uttered the one expressionless word.

"I do not know," the maid wailed; "I do not know."

===Chinese===
「那就把我的先生请来，陪我摆纸牌，」玛丽公主说，「这王子在我房里，我做不成功课。」

「哎呀，夫人。」侍女说。她面色红润，眼睛乌黑，可一迟疑，血色便从双颊褪尽了。玛丽公主冷冷打量她，因为她正存着给人苦头吃的心绪。她一言不发。

「哎呀，哎呀——」侍女呜咽起来。她满心恐惧，生怕玛丽公主罚她减食或挨鞭子；她又满怀惊惶与悲伤，因为她的情郎——一位弥撒辅祭——告诉她说，他必须离她而去了。因为有传言说，尤达尔先生因私结情缘被扔进了监狱，还说国王发了话，从今往后凡谈情的一律下狱。「先生下狱了。」她说。

「为何？」公主吐出这一句不带表情的话。

「我不知道，」侍女哀声道，「我不知道。」

===Original===
The form of the Archbishop's gentleman glided noiselessly behind her back. His eyes shot one sharp, sideways glance in at the door, and, like a russet fox, he was gone. He was so like a fox that the Lady Mary, when she spoke, used the words﻿—

"Catch me that gentleman."

He was brought to the doorsill by the panting maid, for he had walked away very fast. He stood there, blinking his eyes and stroking his fox-coloured beard. When the Lady Mary beckoned him into the room he pulled off his cap and fell to his thin knees. He expected her to bid him rise, but she left him there.

"Wherefore is my secretary gaoled?" she asked cruelly.

He ran his finger round the rim of his cap where it lay on the floor beside him.

"That he is gaoled, I know," he said; "but the wherefore of it, not."

===Chinese===
大主教手下那位绅士的身影，无声无息地滑过她背后。他朝门里飞快地斜瞥了一记，便像只黄毛狐狸似的去了。他那样像只狐狸，以致玛丽公主开口时用了这样的话——

「替我捉住那位绅士。」

气喘吁吁的侍女把他追了回来——他方才走得飞快。他站在门槛上，眨着眼，捋着狐狸色的胡须。玛丽公主招手让他进屋，他摘下帽子，跪到瘦削的膝上。他只等她吩咐起身，她却由他跪着。

「我的秘书为何下狱？」她狠声问道。

他用手指沿帽子边沿画了一圈——帽子就躺在他身旁的地上。

「他下了狱，这我知道，」他说，「至于缘故，却不知道。」

===Original===
He looked down at the floor and she down at his drooped eyelids.

"God help you," she uttered scornfully. "You are a spy and yet know no more than a Queen's daughter."

"God help me," he repeated gravely and touched his eyelid with one finger. "What passed, passed between the King and him. I know no more than common report."

"Common report?" she said. "I warrant thee thou wast slinking around the terrace. I warrant thee thou heardst words of the King's mouth. I warrant thee thou followedst here to hear at my doorhole how I might take this adventure."

One of his eyelids moved delicately, but he said no word. The Lady Mary turned her back on him and he expected her order to be gone. But she turned again﻿—

===Chinese===
他垂眼看向地板，她垂眼看向他低垂的眼睑。

「天主帮帮您，」她讥诮道，「您做奸细，知道的却不多过一位王后的女儿。」

「天主帮帮我，」他一本正经地应道，一根指头碰了碰自己的眼皮。「出了什么事，只在国王与他之间。我知道的，不比市井传闻更多。」

「市井传闻？」她说，「我敢担保，汝方才正在露台一带鬼祟逡巡。我敢担保，汝听见了国王口中的话。我敢担保，汝一路跟到此地，贴着我的门洞偷听我会怎样领受这场变故。」

他一只眼皮细巧地动了一动，却一言不发。玛丽公主转身背对着他，他只等她下逐客令。可她又转了回来——

===Original===
"Common report?" she uttered once more. "I do bid you give me the common report upon this, that the Queen sends to me every day this little Prince to be alone with me two hours."

He winced with his eyebrows again.

"Out with the common report," she said.

"Madam," he uttered, "it is usually commended that the Queen should seek to bring sister and Prince-brother together."

She shrugged her stiff shoulders up to her ears.

"What a poor liar for a spy," she said. "It is more usually reported"﻿—and she turned upon the little Prince﻿—"that the Queen sends thee here that I may work thee a mischief so that thou die and her child reign after the King thy father."

===Chinese===
「市井传闻？」她又说了一遍，「我命您把这件事的市井传闻讲给我听——王后每日打发这小王子来，与我独处两个钟头。」

他的眉心又一缩。

「把那传闻吐出来。」她说。

「夫人，」他说，「世人通常都称许王后设法让姐弟亲睦。」

她把僵硬的双肩耸到了耳根。

「做奸细的，谎竟撒得这样拙劣，」她说，「更通常的说法是——」她转身向着小王子——「王后把汝打发到此地，好教我对汝下毒手，教汝死了，她的孩子好接汝父王的位。」

===Original===
The little Prince looked at her with pensive eyes. At that moment Katharine Howard came to the room door and looked in.

"Body of God," the Lady Mary said; "here you spy out a spy committing treason. For it is still treason to kneel to me. I am of illegal birth and not of the blood royal."

Katharine essayed her smile upon the black-avised girl.

"Give me leave," she said.

"Your Grace's poor room," Mary said, "is open ever to your Grace's entry. *Ubi venis ibi tibi.*"

The Queen bade her waiting women go. She entered the room and looked at Lascelles.

===Chinese===
小王子用一双沉思的眼睛望着她。正在此时，凯瑟琳·霍华德行至房门口，朝里张望。

「天主的圣体，」玛丽公主说，「您正好侦破一个奸细正在行叛逆。因为跪我，至今仍算叛逆。我生于非法，不属王家血胤。」

凯瑟琳试着朝那面色黧黑的姑娘展露笑容。

「容我进来。」她说。

「殿下的寒室，」玛丽说，「永远敞开，恭迎殿下莅临。*Ubi venis ibi tibi*（汝所临之处，即为汝设）。」

王后吩咐侍女们退下。她走进屋来，看着拉塞尔斯。

===Original===
"I think I know thy face," she said.

"I am the Archbishop's poor gentleman," he answered. "I think you have seen me."

"No. It is not that," she said. "It was long ago."

She crossed the room to smell at the pinks in the window.

"How late the flowers grow," she said. "It is August, yet here are still vernal perfumes."

She was unwilling to bid the gentleman rise and go, because this was the Lady Mary's room.

===Chinese===
「我想我认得汝这张脸。」她说。

「我是大主教手下清寒的绅士，」他答道，「您想必见过我。」

「不，不是这个，」她说，「是很久以前的事。」

她穿过房间，去嗅窗台上的石竹。

「花开得多迟啊，」她说，「都已经是八月，此处竟还有春日的芬芳。」

她不愿叫那位绅士起身离去，因为这是玛丽公主的房间。

===Original===
"Where your Grace is, there the spring abideth," Mary said sardonically. "*Ecce miraculum sicut erat, Joshuâ rege.*"

The little Prince came timidly down to beg a flower from the Queen and they all had their backs upon the spy. He ran his hands down his beard and considered the Queen's words. Then swiftly he was on his feet and through the door. He was more ready to brave the Lady Mary's after-wrath than let the Queen see him upon his knees. For actually it was a treason to kneel to the Lady Mary. It had been proclaimed so in the old days when the King's daughter was always subject to new debasements. And who knew whether now the penalty of treason might not still be enacted? It was certain that the Queen had no liking for the Archbishop. Then, what use might she not make of the fact that the Archbishop's man knelt, seeming to curry favour, though in these days all men knelt to her, even when the King was by? He cursed himself as he hastened away.

The Queen looked over her shoulder and caught the glint of his red heel as it went past the doorpost.

"In our north parts," she said, and she was glad that Lascelles had fled, "the seasons come ever tardily."

"Well, your Grace has not delayed to blossom," Mary said.

It was part of her humour when she was in a taunting mood to call the Queen always "your Grace" or "your Majesty" at every turn of the phrase.

===Chinese===
「殿下所在之处，春天便常驻，」玛丽讥诮道，「*Ecce miraculum sicut erat, Joshuâ rege.*（看哪，奇迹恍如约书亚为王之时——日月亦为之停驻。）」

小王子怯生生地下来，向王后讨一朵花，他们便都把背冲着那奸细。他捋着胡须，思量王后的话。随即他倏地立起，穿门而去。他宁可回头领受玛丽公主的事后之怒，也不肯让王后看见自己跪着。因为跪玛丽公主，实实在在仍是一项叛逆。当年国王这女儿不断遭受新的贬辱时，曾明诏如此。如今叛逆之刑是否仍会照办，谁又知道？王后不喜欢大主教，这是笃定的。那么——虽说这些日子人人见了王后都跪，连国王在场也跪——大主教的人跪在此地、形同邀宠，这个把柄，她还有什么用场使不出来？他一面疾走，一面咒骂自己。

王后回头一望，捕捉到他那红鞋跟掠过门柱时的一闪光。

「在我们北方，」她说——拉塞尔斯逃了，她正高兴——「节令总是来得迟。」

「倒是，殿下自己开花，可不曾迟。」玛丽说。

她讥诮人的时候惯有一样做派：句句都把王后称做「殿下」或「陛下」。

===Original===
Katharine looked at the pink intently. Her face had no expression, she was determined at once to have a cheerful patience and not to show it in her face.

The little Prince stole his hand into hers.

"Wherefore did my father﻿—*rex pater meus*﻿—pummel the man in the long cloak?" he asked.

"You knew it then?" Katharine asked of her stepdaughter.

"I knew it not," the Lady Mary answered.

"I saw it from this window, but my sister would not look," the Prince said.

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳凝神望着那枝石竹。她脸上毫无表情——她已拿定主意，既要存一副快活的耐性，又不能在脸上显露出来。

小王子把手悄悄塞进她的手里。

「我父王——*rex pater meus*（我的父王）——为什么痛打那个穿长斗篷的人？」他问。

「这么说您知道了？」凯瑟琳问她的继女。

「我并不知道。」玛丽公主答道。

「我从这扇窗口看见了，可我姐姐不肯看。」王子说。

===Original===
The Queen was going to shut, with her own hand, the door, the little boy trotting behind her, but, purple-clothed and huge, the King was there.

"Well, I will not be shut out in mine own castle," he said pleasantly.

In those, the quiet days of his realm when most things were going well, his face beneath his beard had taken a rounder and a smoother outline. He moved with motions less hasty than those he had had two years before, and when he had cast a task off it was done with and went out of his mind, so that he appeared a very busy man with, between whiles, the leisure to saunter.

"In a half hour," he said, "I go north to meet the King o' Scots. I would I had not the long journey to make but could stay with ye. It is pleasant here; the air is livening." He caught his little son by the armpits and hoisted him on to his purple shoulders. "Hey, princekin," he said, "what news ha' you o' the day?"

===Chinese===
王后正要亲手去关门，小男孩跟在她身后小步跑着——可一身紫衣、身躯魁伟的国王已经立在那里。

「嘿，朕可不愿在自己的城堡里吃闭门羹。」他和颜悦色地说。

在他王国那段诸事顺遂的太平岁月里，他胡须底下的脸庞轮廓变得圆润平滑了些。他举手投足，已不似两年前那样急促；一桩差事脱手，便算交代清楚，从心里放下了，所以他看上去像个极忙的人，忙里又偷得出闲步的空当。

「半个钟头后，」他说，「朕便北上，去会苏格兰国王。朕巴不得不必赶这趟远路，能同你们守在一处。此地宜人，空气提神。」他一把夹住小儿子的腋下，把他举上自己的紫衣肩头。「嘿，小王子，」他说，「今日汝这里有什么新闻？」

===Original===
The little Edward pulled his father's bonnet off that he might the better see the huge brows and the little eyes.

"I told my sister that you did pummel a man in a long gown. What is even 'long gown' in the learned tongue?" He played daintily and languidly with the hair of the King's temples, and when the King had said that he might call it "*doctorum toga*," he added, "But my sister would not come to look."

"Well, thy sister is a monstrous learned wench," the King said with a heavy benignity. "She could not leave her book."

The Lady Mary stood rigid, with a mock humility. She had her hands clasped before her, the folds of her black skirt fell stiffly just to the ground. She pursed her lips and strove with herself to speak, for she was minded to exhibit disdain, but her black mood was too strong for her.

"I did not read in my book, because I could not," she said numbly. "Your son disturbed my reading. But I did not come to look, because I would not."

With one arm round the boy's little waist as he sat on high, and one hand on the little feet, the King looked at his daughter in a sudden hot rage; for to speak contemptuously of his son was a thing that filled him with anger and surprise. He opened his mouth to shout. Katharine Howard was gently turning a brass sphere with the constellations upon it that stood upon the table. She moved her fair face round towards the King and set her finger upon her lips. He shrugged his shoulders, prince and all moving up together, and his face took on the expression, half abashed and half resigned, of a man who is reminded by his womankind that he is near to a passionate folly.

===Chinese===
小爱德华摘下父亲的软帽，好把那两道浓眉和小眼睛看得更真切。

「我告诉姐姐了，说您痛打过一个穿长袍的人。『长袍』在学问人的话里又该怎么说？」他优雅而慵懒地拨弄着国王鬓角的头发，国王说他不妨称之为「*doctorum toga*（博士们的长袍）」，他便又添道：「可我姐姐不肯来看。」

「嘿，汝姐姐是个学问大得吓人的姑娘，」国王带着沉甸甸的慈和说，「她的书放不下。」

玛丽公主僵直地站着，一副佯装的谦卑。她双手交握身前，黑裙的褶裥僵僵地直垂到地。她抿紧嘴唇，挣扎着要开口——本想露一露轻蔑，可那阴郁的心绪压倒了她。

「我没有读书，因为我读不下去，」她木然说，「您的儿子搅扰了我的读书。但我不去看，是因为我不愿意。」

小男孩高坐在上，国王一臂环着他的小腰，一手托着那双小脚，猛然一股狂怒，瞪视着女儿——因为轻贱他的儿子，这件事叫他既怒且惊。他张口要吼。凯瑟琳·霍华德正轻轻拨转着桌上一只铸着星座的黄铜天球仪。她把姣好的脸转向国王，一根手指竖在唇上。他耸了耸肩，小王子连人一起跟着耸动，脸上换上半是赧然、半是无可奈何的神情——一个男人被他的女眷提醒他险些要犯下暴躁的蠢行时所有的神情。

===Original===
Katharine by that time had schooled him how to act when Mary was in that humour, and he let out no word.

"I do not like that this Prince should play in my room," the Lady Mary pursued him relentlessly, and he was so well lessoned that he answered only﻿—

"Ye must fight that cock with Kat. It is Kat that sends him, not I."

Nevertheless he was too masterful a man to keep his silence altogether; he was, besides, so content upon the whole that he was sure he could hold his temper in check, and the better to take breath for a long speech, he took the little boy from his shoulder and planted his feet abroad on the carpet.

"See now, Moll," he said, "make friends!" and he stretched out a large hand. She shrugged her shoulders half invisibly.

===Chinese===
到那时为止，凯瑟琳已调教得他知道玛丽犯这心绪时该当如何，他便一声不出。

「我不喜欢这王子在我房里玩，」玛丽公主对他穷追不舍。他受教受得那样到家，只答了一句——

「这只斗鸡，汝得去同凯特斗。是凯特打发他来的，不是朕。」

话虽如此，他终究是个太专断的人，做不到全然缄默；再说，他心里总的来说那样舒泰，自信按得住性子；为缓一口气好作长谈，他把小男孩从肩上抱下来，让他两脚分开在地毯上站定。

「你看，莫尔，」他说，「交个朋友！」说着伸出一只大手。她几不可察地耸了耸肩。

===Original===
"I will kneel down to the King of this country and to the Supreme Head of the Church as it is here set up by law. What more would you have of me?"

"See now, Moll!" he said.

He fingered the medal upon his chest and cast about for words.

"Let us have peace in this realm," he said. "We are very near it."

She raised her eyelids with a tiny contempt.

"It hangs much around you," he went on. "Listen! I will tell ye the whole matter."

===Chinese===
「我肯跪此邦的国王，也肯跪此间依法律立起的教会最高领袖。您还要我怎样？」

「你看，莫尔！」他说。

他摩挲着胸前的圆章，搜肠刮肚地找话。

「让这国度享太平吧，」他说，「我们已经离它很近了。」

她掀起眼皮，带着一丝微微的轻蔑。

「这事多半系在汝身上，」他接着说，「听着！朕把整个情由讲给汝听。」

===Original===
Slowly and sagaciously he disentangled all his coil of policies. His letter to the Holy Father was all drafted and ready to be put into fine words. But, before he sent it, he must be sure of peace abroad. It was like this﻿—

"Ye know," he said, "though great wrangles have been in the past betwixt him and thee and mine own self, how my heart has ever been well inclined to my nephew, thy cousin the Emperor. There are in Christendom now only he and France that are anyways strong to stand against me or to invade me. But France I ha' never loved, and him much."

"Ye are grown gentle then," Mary said, "and forgiving in your old age, for ye know I ha' plotted against you with my cousin and my cousin with me."

"It is a very ancient tale," the King said. "Forget it, as do I and he."

"Why, you live in the sun where the dial face moves. I in the shadow where Time stays still. To me it is every day a new tale," the Lady Mary answered.

===Chinese===
他慢条斯理、老谋深算地，把他那一团乱麻似的方略一一解开。他致圣父的信已拟就草稿，只待润成雅驯的文辞。可在发出之前，他必须先稳住外邦的和平。事情是这样——

「汝知道，」他说，「虽说往昔在他与汝、与朕本人之间起过多少大争执，朕的心却一向向着朕的外甥——汝的表兄皇帝。如今基督世界中，唯他与法兰西还勉强有力量与朕相抗，或兴兵犯朕。可法兰西，朕从未爱过；他，却爱得深。」

「这么说您变得温和了，」玛丽说，「上了年纪，也肯饶恕了——您知道的，我曾同我表兄算计过您，我表兄也曾同我算计过您。」

「那是老掉牙的旧话了，」国王说，「忘掉它吧，朕和他都已忘了。」

「哟，您住在太阳底下，日晷的盘面还在走动；我住在阴影里，光阴在那里凝驻不动。对我来说，它天天都是一桩新闻。」玛丽公主答道。

===Original===
His face took on an expression of patience and resignation that angered her, for she knew that when her father looked so it was always very difficult to move him.

"Why, all the world forgets," he said.

"Save only I," she answered. "I had only one parent﻿—a mother. She is dead: she was done to death."

"I have pardoned your cousin that he plotted against me," he stuck to his tale, "and he me what I did against your mother."

"Well, he was ever a popinjay," the Lady Mary said.

"Lately," Henry continued, "as ye wiz he had grown very thick with Francis of France. He went across the French country into the Netherlands, so strict was their alliance. It is more than I would do to trust myself to France's word. All Holland marvelled."

===Chinese===
他脸上现出耐心与听天由命的神情。这惹恼了她，因为她知道，父亲这副神色一摆出来，便再难说动他。

「哟，全世界都忘了。」他说。

「唯独我忘不了，」她答道，「我只有一个亲人——母亲。她死了：她是被害死的。」

「汝表兄算计过朕，朕已饶恕了他，」他咬定自己的话头，「朕对汝母亲做过的事，他也饶恕了朕。」

「哼，他向来是只绣花枕头似的鹦鹉公子。」玛丽公主说。

「晚近，」亨利接着说，「汝也知道，他同法兰西的法兰西斯打得火热。他们盟好紧密，他竟横穿法兰西国土去了尼德兰。要朕把自己托付给法兰西的一句空话，朕是万万不肯的。全荷兰都为之咋舌。」

===Original===
"What is this to me?" the Lady Mary said. "Will you send me across France to the Netherlands?"

He left her gibe alone.

"But in these latter months," he said, "Kat and I ha' weakened with true messages and loyal conceits this unholy alliance."

"Why, I ha' heard," Mary said, "ye did send the Duke of Norfolk to tell the King o' France that my cousin had said in private that he was the greater King of the twain. These be princely princes!"

"An unholy alliance it was," Henry went on his way, "for the Emperor is a very good Christian and a loyal son of the Church. But Francis worships the devil﻿—I have heard it said and I believe it﻿—or, at least, he believes not in God and our Saviour; and he pays allegiance to the Church only when it serves his turn, now holding on, now letting go. I am glad this alliance is dissolving."

===Chinese===
「这与我何干？」玛丽公主说，「您是要把我取道法兰西送去尼德兰么？」

他不理会她的讥刺。

「可在近几个月，」他说，「凯特和朕用诚恳的传话与忠信的计谋，把这桩不神圣的同盟拆得松动了。」

「哟，我可听说了，」玛丽说，「您派诺福克公爵去告诉法兰西国王，说我表兄私下里讲过，他们两位当中数他更是伟大的国王。这可真是王侯气派的王侯！」

「这同盟本就不神圣，」亨利顺着自己的话头说下去，「因为皇帝是个很好的基督徒，圣教会忠顺的儿子。可法兰西斯崇拜魔鬼——朕听人这样讲，朕也信——或者至少，他不信天主与我们的救主；他对教会的效忠，只在对他有用时才有，时而抓紧，时而撒手。这同盟正在散伙，朕很高兴。」

===Original===
"Why, I am glad to hear you speak like this," Mary said bitterly. "You are a goodly son to Mother Church."

The King took her scorn with a shrug of the shoulders.

"I am glad this alliance is dissolved or dissolving," he said, "for when it is fully dissolved I will make my peace with Rome. And I long for that day, for I am weary of errors."

"Well, this is a very goodly tale," Mary said. "I am glad you are minded to escape hell-flame. What is it all to me?"

"The burden of it rests with thee," he answered, "for thou alone canst make thy cousin believe in my true mind."

"God help me," Mary said.

===Chinese===
「哟，听您这样讲我真高兴，」玛丽刻毒地说，「您真是圣教会的好儿子。」

国王耸了耸肩，把她的讥诮受了。

「这同盟已经散了，或正在散，朕很高兴，」他说，「等它散尽，朕便同罗马修好。朕盼着那一天，因为种种谬误，朕早已厌倦了。」

「好啊，这真是个动听的故事，」玛丽说，「您有心逃脱地狱之火，我很高兴。可这一切与我何干？」

「担子却落在汝身上，」他答道，「因为唯有汝，能让汝表兄相信朕的真心。」

「天主助我。」玛丽说。

===Original===
"See you, Moll," the King broke in on her eagerly, "if you will marry the Infant of Spain﻿—"

"God's sakes," she said lightly, "my cousin's son will wed no bastard as I be."

He brushed her jest aside with one hand.

"See you," he said, "now I ride to the north to meet the King o' Scots. That nephew of mine has always been too thick with Francis. But I will be so friendly with him. And see you, with the Scots cut away and the Emperor unloyal, the teeth of Francis are drawn. I might not send my letter to the Pope with all Christendom arrayed together against me. But when they are set by the ears I am strong enow."

"Oh, good!" the Lady Mary said. "Strong enow to be humble!"

Her eyes sparkled so much and her bosom so heaved, that Katharine moved solicitously and swiftly to come between them.

===Chinese===
「汝听着，莫尔，」国王急切地插进来说，「倘若汝肯嫁西班牙王子（the Infant of Spain）——」

「看在天主份上，」她轻飘飘地说，「我表兄的儿子才不肯娶我这样的私生女。」

他抬起一只手，把她的玩话拂到一边。

「汝看，」他说，「朕此刻北上，去会苏格兰国王。朕那外甥一向同法兰西斯过从太密。可朕要同他好得亲密无间。汝再看，苏格兰一隔断，皇帝一不再向法兰西效忠，法兰西斯的牙齿便拔光了。倘若整个基督世界联成一气对付朕，朕便送不得这封给教皇的信。可等他们互相咬斗起来，朕便足够强了。」

「哦，好极了！」玛丽公主说，「强到足够谦卑了！」

她两眼放光，胸脯剧烈起伏，凯瑟琳赶紧关切而敏捷地插身到两人中间。

===Original===
"See you, Moll," the King said, "forgive the ill I wrought thee, and so shall golden days come again. Once more there shall be a deep peace with contented husbandmen and the spreading of the vines abroad upon the stakes. And once more *venite creator spiritus* shall be sung in this land. And once more you shall be much honoured; nay, you shall be as one that saved this realm﻿—"

She screamed out﻿—

"Stay your tongue!" with such a shrill voice that the King's words were drowned. Katharine Howard ran in between them, but she pushed her aside, speaking over her shoulder.

"Before God," she said, "you gar me forget that you are the King that begot me illegally."

Katharine turned upon the King and sought to move him from the room. But he was still of opinion that he could convince his daughter and stood his ground, looking over her shoulder as Mary had done.

===Chinese===
「汝听着，莫尔，」国王说，「饶恕朕对汝造下的罪愆，黄金岁月便会重来。这国度将再度享有深深的太平，庄稼人心满意足，葡萄藤重新爬满支架。*venite creator spiritus*（造物主圣神，求君临）将再度在这片土地上唱起。汝也将再度备受尊崇；不，汝将被视同拯救这国度的人——」

她尖声喊道——

「收住您的舌头！」那嗓音尖利得把国王的话全淹没了。凯瑟琳·霍华德奔到两人中间，她却被她一把推开，只顾越过她的肩头说话。

「天主在上，」她说，「您害我忘了，您本是那非法生养了我的国王。」

凯瑟琳转向国王，想劝他离开房间。可他仍自信说得服女儿，钉在原地不动，目光越过她的肩头朝玛丽望去——正如玛丽方才越过凯瑟琳的肩头说话一般。

===Original===
"Body of God!" Mary said. "Body of God! That a man could deem me so base!" She looked, convulsed, into Henry's eyes. "Can you bring my mother alive by the truckling and cajoling and setting lying prince against lying prince? You slew my mother by lies, or your man slew her by poison. It is all one. And will you come to me that you have decreed misbegotten, to help you save your soul!"

There was such a violent hatred in her tone that the King could bring no word out, and she swept on﻿—

"Could even a man be such a dull villain? To creep into heaven by bribing his daughter! To creep into heaven by strengthening himself with lies about one prince to another till he be strong enow to be humble! This is a king! This is even a man! I would be ashamed of such manhood!"

She took a deep breath.

===Chinese===
「天主的圣体！」玛丽喊道，「天主的圣体！一个男人竟能把我看得这样下贱！」她浑身抽搐地直视亨利的眼睛。「凭这些卑躬屈节、连哄带骗，凭让撒谎的君王去斗撒谎的君王，您能让我母亲活转来么？你们用谎言杀了我母亲，或者您的走狗用毒药杀了她。都是一回事。您颁布敕令，说我是私生，如今却来找我——帮您救您自己的灵魂！」

她话音里的恨意那样狂暴，国王竟吐不出一个字，她便乘势倾泻下去——

「一个男人竟能是这样一个冥顽的无赖？靠贿赂亲生女儿爬进天国！靠拿这个君王的谎话去攻那个君王、把自己养壮，壮到足够谦卑，再爬进天国！这就是国王！这也就是个男人！这样的男子气概，我都替它害臊！」

她深深吸了一口气。

===Original===
"What can you bribe me with? A marriage with my cousin's son? Why, he has deserted my mother's cause. I had rather wed a falconer than that prince. You will have me no longer called bastard? Why, I had rather be called bastard than the acknowledged child of such a royal King. You will cover me with brocades and set me on high? By God, the sun in the heaven has looked upon such basenesses that I seek only a patch of shade. God help me; you will recall the decree that said my mother was not a Queen! God help us! God help us all! You will ennoble my mother's memory. With a decree! Can all the decrees you can make render my mother more sacred? When you decreed her not a Queen, did a soul believe it? If now you decree that a Queen she was, who will believe you? I think I had rather you left it alone, it is such a foul thing to have been thy wife!"

===Chinese===
「您能拿什么贿赂我？同我表兄之子的婚事？哟，他早背弃了我母亲的事业。我宁肯嫁一个养鹰人，也不嫁那位王子。您要不再叫我私生女？哟，我宁肯顶着私生女的名分，也不做这样一位堂堂国王名下得了公认的孩子。您要给我披上织锦、把我捧上高位？天主在上，天上的太阳见过的这等下贱事太多了，我只求一小片荫凉。天主助我；您要收回说我母亲不是王后的那道敕令！天主助我们！天主助我们大家！您要给我母亲的亡灵添光彩。用一道敕令！您立得出的所有敕令，能让我母亲更神圣一分么？您敕令她不是王后时，可有一个灵魂信了？如今您敕令她曾是王后，又会有谁信您？我想您还是罢手的好——做过您的妻子，就是这么一桩肮脏事！」

===Original===
The saying of these things had pleased her so much that she gained control of her tongue.

"You cannot bribe me," she said calmly. "You have naught to give that I have need of."

But the King was so used to his daughter's speeches that, though he had seldom seen her so mutinous, he could still ignore them.

"Well," he said, "I think you are angered with me for having set the Magister in gaol﻿—"

"And in addition," the Lady Mary pursued her own speech, for she deemed that she had thought of a thing to pain both him and the Queen, "how might I with a good conscience tell my cousin that you have a true inclination to him? I do believe you have; it is this lady that has given it you. But how much longer will this lady sway you? No doubt the King o' Scots hath a new lady for you﻿—and she will be on the French side, for the King o' Scots is the French King's man."

The King opened his mouth convulsively, but Katharine Howard laid her hand right across it.

===Chinese===
这些话说得她痛快至极，舌头反倒收住了缰。

「您贿赂不了我，」她平静地说，「您拿得出的东西，没有一样是我需要的。」

可国王对女儿的这些话早已听惯，虽说很少见她这样桀骜不驯，他还是能充耳不闻。

「好了，」他说，「朕想，汝是在气朕把尤达尔先生下了狱——」

「还有一层，」玛丽公主顺着自己的话头说下去——她自觉想出了一句能同时刺痛国王与王后的话——「我又怎能凭一副干净的良心，去告诉我表兄说您对他是真心亲近？我确实信您是；这番心意，是这位夫人给您的。可这位夫人还能左右您多久？想必苏格兰国王已为您备下了一位新的夫人——而她准站在法国一边，因为苏格兰国王是法兰西国王的人。」

国王痉挛着张开嘴，凯瑟琳·霍华德却把一只手横过来，整个按住了它。

===Original===
"You must be riding soon," she said. "I have had a collation set in my chamber." She was so used by now to the violent humours of these Tudors. "You have still to direct me," she added, "what is to be done with these rived cattle."

As they went through the door, the little Prince holding his father's hand and she moving him gently by the shoulder, the child said﻿—

"I thought ye wad ha' little profit speaking to my sister in her then mood."

The King, in the gallery, looked with a gentle apprehension at his wife.

===Chinese===
「您该快些上路了，」她说，「我已叫人在我房里备下一桌点心。」都铎家这些人的火爆心性，她如今早已见惯。「这起子被生生扯散的可怜人儿该怎样发落，」她添道，「您还得给我个示下。」

他们出门而去，小王子牵着父亲的手，她轻轻扶着他的肩引他前行，孩子说道——

「我原想着，姐姐那时正犯着那性子，您同她说不上什么好处。」

长廊里，国王带着温和的忐忑望了妻子一眼。

===Original===
"I trow ye think I ha' done wrong," he said.

She answered﻿—

"Oh nay; she must come to know one day what your Grace had to tell her. Now it is over. But I would not have had you heated. For it is ill to start riding in a sweat. You shall not go for an hour yet."

That pleased him, for it made him think she was unwilling he should go.

===Chinese===
「俺想，汝是觉得俺做错了。」他说。

她答道——

「哦不；您要告诉她的话，她总有一天得知道。如今总算说完了。只是我不想让您动这样大的肝火。一身汗气上马赶路，最伤身体。您还有一个钟头才动身呢。」

这话中了他的意，因为这让他觉得，她舍不得他走。

===Original===
In her own room the Lady Mary sat back in her chair and smiled grimly at the ceiling.

"Body of God," she said, "I wish he had married this wench or ever he saw my mother." Nevertheless, upon reflection, she got pleasure from the thought that her mother, with her Aragonia pride, had given the King some ill hours before he had put her away to her death. Katharine of Aragon had been no Katharine Howard to study her lord's ways and twist him about her finger; and Mary took her rosary from a nail beside her and told her beads for a quarter hour to calm herself.

===Chinese===
玛丽公主回到自己房里，仰靠椅中，对着天花板冷冷地笑。

「天主的圣体，」她说，「我但愿他在见我母亲之前，早娶了这女人。」话虽如此，转念一想，她又生出快意：她母亲凭着她阿拉贡人的傲气，在被他休弃致死之前，总算也给过国王一些难熬的时辰。阿拉贡的凯瑟琳终究不是凯瑟琳·霍华德，不肯去细察夫君的脾性、把他绕着指头转。玛丽从身旁一枚钉子上取下念珠，数着珠子诵了一刻钟经文，好让自己平静下来。

### V

===Original===
There fell upon the castle a deep peace when the King and most of the men were gone. The Queen had the ordering of all things in the castle and of most in the realm. Beneath her she had the Archbishop and some few of the lords of the council who met most days round a long table in the largest hall, and afterwards brought her many papers to sign or to approve. But they were mostly papers of accounts for the castles that were then building, and some few letters from the King’s envoys in foreign courts. Upon the whole, there was little stirring, though the Emperor Charles V was then about harrying the Protestant Princes of Almain and Germany. That was good enough news, and though the great castle had well-nigh seven hundred souls, for the most part women, in it, yet it appeared to be empty. High up upon the upper battlements the guards kept a lazy watch. Sometimes the Queen rode a-hawking with her ladies and several lords; when it rained she held readings from the learned writers amongst her ladies, to teach them Latin better. For she had set a fashion of good learning among women that did not for many years die out of the land. In that pursuit she missed the Magister Udal, for the ladies listened to him more willingly than to another. They were reading the *True History of Lucian*, which had been translated into Latin from the Greek about that time.

What occupied her most was the writing of the King’s letter to the Pope. Down in their cellar the Archbishop and Lascelles wrought many days at this very long piece of writing. But they made it too humble to suit her, for she would not have her lord to crawl, as if in the dust upon his belly, so she told the Archbishop. Henry was to show contrition and repentance, desire for pardon and the promise of amendment. But he was a very great King and had wrought greatly. And, having got the draft of it in the vulgar tongue, she set about herself to turn it into Latin, for she esteemed herself the best Latinist that they had there.

===Chinese===
国王与大部分男人离去之后，一层深深的安宁落在了城堡上。王后总揽城堡中的一切，兼摄国中大半事务。她之下，有大主教与枢密院诸勋中的寥寥数位，多数日子在大厅里围着一张长桌议事，事后捧来许多文书请她签署或核准。但那多半是当时正在兴建的各城堡的账目，另有国王派驻外国宫廷的使节寄来的寥寥几封信。大体而言，并无多少动静，虽说皇帝卡尔五世其时正着手蹂躏阿尔迈因与德意志的新教诸侯。那也算是够好的消息了。这座大城堡里虽则住着将近七百口人，且大半是女眷，却显得空空荡荡。高处的上层雉堞上，卫兵们懒懒地守望。王后有时携贵妇与几位勋贵骑马出游放鹰；逢雨，她便在贵妇们中间主持博学作家作品的诵读，好教她们的拉丁文更精进。因为她在妇人中间树立了好学问的风尚，多年之后才在这片国土上消歇。在这桩事业里她惦念着尤达尔先生，因为贵妇们听他讲学，比听旁任何人都甘心。她们诵读的是琉善（Lucian）的*《信史》*，其时刚由希腊文译成拉丁文。

最占她心神的，是国王致教皇的信的起草。在楼下他们的地窖里，大主教与拉塞尔斯为这篇极长的文章熬了许多天。可他们写得太卑屈，不合她的心意，因为她不愿她的夫君匍匐爬行、仿佛肚皮贴着尘土——这话她是告诉过大主教的。亨利要显露的是悔罪与痛改，是求赦之愿与改过之诺。但他是一位至为伟大的君王，且建树至伟。于是，拿到俗语写就的底稿之后，她便亲自动手把它翻成拉丁文，因为她自许为他们中间最好的拉丁文家。

---

===Original===
But in that again she missed the Magister at last, and in the end she sent for him up from his prison to her antechamber where it pleased her to sit. It was a tall, narrow room, with much such a chair and dais as were in the room of the Lady Mary. It gave on to her bedchamber that was larger, and it had little, bright, deep windows in the thick walls. From them there could be seen nothing but the blue sky, it was so high up. Here she sat, most often with the Lady Rochford, upon a little stool writing, with the parchments upon her knee or setting a maid to sew. The King had lately made her a gift of twenty-four satin quilts. Most of her maids sat in her painted gallery, carding and spinning wool, but usually she did not sit with them, since she was of opinion that they spoke more freely and took more pleasure when she was not there. She had brought many maids with her into Yorkshire for this spinning, for she believed that this northern wool was the best that could be had. Margot Poins sat always with these maids to keep them to their tasks, and her brother had been advanced to keep the Queen’s door when she was in her private rooms, being always without the chamber in which she sat.

When the Magister came to her, she had with her in the little room the Lady Rochford and the Lady Cicely Rochford that had married the old knight when she was Cicely Elliott. Udal had light chains on his wrists and on his ankles, and the Queen sent her guards to await him at her outer door. The Lady Cicely set back her head and laughed at the ceiling.

“Why, here are the bonds of holy matrimony!” she said to his chains. “I ha’ never seen them so plain before.”

The Magister had straws on his cloak, and he limped a little, being stiff with the damp of his cell.

“*Ave, Regina!*” he said. “*Moriturus te saluto!*” He sought to kneel, but he could not bend his joints; he smiled with a humorous and rueful countenance at his own plight.

The Queen said she had brought him there to read the Latin of her letter. He ducked his brown, lean head.

===Chinese===
可在这一层上，她终究又惦念起尤达尔先生来，末了她派人把他从牢里提到她的前厅——她素来喜欢坐在那里。那是一间高而窄的屋子，屋里的座椅与矮台，同玛丽公主房中的大致相仿。它通向更大的寝宫，厚墙上开着小小的、明亮的深窗。凭窗除了蓝天什么也望不见，那屋子实在太高。她在这里坐着，多半有罗奇福德夫人作陪，坐在一张小凳上书写，羊皮纸摊在膝头，或是派一名侍女做针线。国王新近赠了她二十四床缎被。她的大多数侍女坐在彩绘长廊里梳毛纺线，她通常不同她们同坐，因为她以为她不在跟前时，她们说话更自在，也更快活。为着纺线，她带了许多侍女到约克郡（Yorkshire）来，因为她信这北地的羊毛是世上能得的最好的羊毛。玛戈·普因斯总是同这些侍女坐在一起，督着她们做活计；她弟弟则已蒙擢升，替王后看门——王后在私室时，他总守在她所坐那间屋子之外。

尤达尔先生来到她跟前时，那间小屋里陪着她的是罗奇福德夫人，与嫁那老骑士时还叫西西莉·埃利奥特（Cicely Elliott）的西西莉·罗奇福德夫人。尤达尔腕上踝上都带着轻链，王后派了卫兵在外门候他。西西莉夫人仰起头，冲着天花板大笑。

「哟，这可是神圣婚姻的锁链了！」她冲着他的镣铐说。「我可从没见过它们这样明摆在人跟前过。」

尤达尔先生的斗篷上沾着草屑，走路微微跛着——牢里的潮气叫他浑身僵了。

「*Ave, Regina!*（万福，王后！）」他说，「*Moriturus te saluto!*（将死之人向您致敬！）」他想要跪下，关节却弯不过来；他冲着自己的狼狈处境，露出一副又诙谐又酸楚的神色。

王后说，她把他带到那里，是要他读一读她那封信的拉丁文。他棕色的瘦脑袋一俯。

===Original===
“*Ha*,” he said, “*sine cane pastor*—without his dog, as Lucretius hath it, the shepherd watches in vain. Wolves—videlicet, errors—shall creep into your marshalled words.”

Katharine kept to him a cold face and, a little abashed, he muttered under his breath—

“I ha’ played with many maids, but this is the worst pickle that ever I was in.”

He took her parchment and read, but, because she was the Queen, he would not say aloud that he found solecisms in her words.

“Give me,” he said, “your best pen, and let me sit upon a stool!”

He sat down upon the stool, set the writing on his knee, and groaned with his stiffness. He took up his task, but when those ladies began to talk—the Lady Cicely principally about a hawk that her old knight had training for the Queen, a white sea hawk from Norway—he winced and hissed a little because they disturbed him.

===Chinese===
「哈，」他说，「*sine cane pastor*——正如卢克莱修所言，牧人没了狗，看守也是徒劳。豺狼——videlicet，即是说，种种谬误——将潜入您排布齐整的字句。」

凯瑟琳对他端着一副冷脸，他有些讪讪的，压着嗓子咕哝——

「我玩弄过的姑娘多了，可从没陷进过这样窘的境地。」

他接过她的羊皮纸读下去，但因为她是王后，他不肯出声说自己看出她的文句里有语病。

「给我，」他说，「您最好的一支笔，再让我坐上一张凳子！」

他在凳上坐定，把稿子搁在膝头，僵痛得哼出声来。他着手做他的活计，可那几位夫人攀谈起来了——西西莉夫人谈的主要是她的老骑士正替王后驯的一只鹰，一只挪威（Norway）来的白海鹰——他便皱眉咝气，嫌她们搅扰。

===Original===
“Misery!” he said; “I remember the days when no mouse dared creak if I sat to my task in the learned tongues.”

The Queen then remembered very well how she had been a little girl with the Magister for tutor in her father’s great and bare house. It was after Udal had been turned out of his mastership at Eton. He had been in vile humour in most of those days, and had beaten her very often and fiercely with his bundle of twigs. It was only afterwards that he had called her his best pupil.

Remembering these things, she dropped her voice and sat still, thinking. Cicely Elliott, who could not keep still, blew a feather into the air and caught it again and again. The old Lady Rochford, her joints swollen with rheumatism, played with her beads in her lap. From time to time she sighed heavily and, whilst the Magister wrote, he sighed after her. Katharine would not send her ladies away, because she would not be alone with him to have him plague her with entreaties. She would not go herself, because it would have been to show him too much honour then, though a few days before she would have gone willingly because his vocation and his knowledge of the learned tongues made him a man that it was right to respect.

But when she read what he had written for her, his lean, brown face turning eagerly and with a ferreting motion from place to place on the parchment, she was filled with pity and with admiration for the man’s talent. It was as if Seneca were writing to his master, or Pliny to the Emperor Trajan. And, being a very tender woman at bottom—

===Chinese===
「苦也！」他说，「我记得往昔的日子，只要我坐下来做博学语言的功课，连老鼠也不敢吱一声响。」

于是王后清清楚楚地记起，她自己曾是怎样一个小姑娘，在父亲那座又大又空落的宅子里，由尤达尔先生授课。那是尤达尔被革去伊顿（Eton）教职之后的事了。那些日子他多半心境恶劣，常用他那束细枝狠狠抽她。也是到后来，他才称她为他最好的学生。

想着这些，她放低了声音，静坐沉思。西西莉·埃利奥特闲不住，把一根羽毛吹上半空，又一遍一遍去接。老罗奇福德夫人关节叫风湿胀得粗大，在膝头拨弄她的念珠。她时时沉沉地叹气；尤达尔先生一边写，一边应和着她的叹声。凯瑟琳不肯把贵妇们遣开，因为她不愿同他独处，受他纠缠哀求。她自己也不肯走到他跟前去，因为那便是在此时给他太大的体面了——虽则几天之前，她还会欣然前去，因为他的圣职与他对博学语言的学识，使他是个理应敬重的人。

可当她读到他为她写下的文字——他那棕色的瘦脸在羊皮纸上一处一处热切地、像嗅探似地转来转去——她心里便涌起怜悯，也涌起对这人才华的钦佩。那文墨，仿佛塞内加在给他的主人写信，普林尼（Pliny）在给皇帝图拉真（Trajan）上书。而她终究是个心底极温柔的女人——

===Original===
“Magister,” she said, “though you have wrought me the greatest grief I think ye could, by so injuring one I like well, yet this is to me so great a service that I will entreat the King to remit some of your pains.”

He stumbled up from his stool and this time managed to kneel.

“Oh, Queen,” he said, “*Doctissima fuisti*; you were the best pupil that ever I had—” She tried to silence him with a motion of her hand. But he twined his lean hands together with the little chains hanging from them. “I call this to your pitiful mind,” he brought out, “not because I would have you grateful, but to make you mindful of what I suffer—*non quia grata sed ut clemens sis*. For, for advancement I have no stomach, since by advancing me you will advance my wife from Paris, and for liberty I have no use since you may never make me free of her. Leave me to rot in my cell, but, if it be but the tractate of Diodorus Siculus, a very dull piece, let me be given some book in a learned tongue. I faint, I starve, I die for lack of good letters. I that no day in my life have passed—*nulla die sine*—no day without reading five hours in goodly books since I was six and breeched. Bethink you, you that love learning—”

===Chinese===
「先生，」她说，「你给了我我以为你所能给的最深的伤痛——如此糟践一个我甚喜欢的人——然而这桩事于我是这样大的功劳，我要恳求国王，减免你的几分苦楚。」

他从凳子上踉跄爬起，这一回总算跪成了。

「哦，王后，」他说，「*Doctissima fuisti*（您曾是最有学问的）；您是我有过的最好的学生——」她摆手示意他住口。他却把两只瘦手绞在一起，小链子从腕上垂下来。「我把这个提到您悲悯的心头，」他迸出话来，「不是要您感念，是要您记挂我受的苦——*non quia grata sed ut clemens sis*（非为邀恩，但求垂怜）。因为，进位于我毫无胃口：您提拔了我，便是提拔了我巴黎（Paris）的妻子；自由于我也毫无用处：您断不能叫我摆脱她。就让我烂在牢里吧，可是，哪怕只是西西里的狄奥多罗斯（Diodorus Siculus）的那部论著——顶顶枯燥的一部书——也求您让人给我一本博学语言的书。我昏晕，我饥渴，我因缺乏美好的文墨而枯死。我这辈子没有一天虚度——*nulla die sine*（无日或缺）——自六岁穿上裤子以来，没有一天不读足五小时的好书。请您想想，您这钟爱学问的人——」

===Original===
“Now tell me,” Cicely Elliott cried out, “which would you rather in your cell—the Letters of Cicero or a kitchen wench?”

The Queen bade her hold her peace, and to the Magister she uttered—

“Books I will have sent you, for I think it well that you should be so well employed. And, for your future, I will have you set down in a monastery where there shall be for you much learning and none of my sex. You have done harm enow! Now, get you gone!”

He sighed that she had grown so stern, and she was glad to be rid of him. But he had not been gone a minute into the other room when there arose such a clamour of harsh voices and shrieks and laughter that she threw her door open, coming to it herself before the other ladies could close their mouths, which had opened in amazement.

===Chinese===
「倒是说说看，」西西莉·埃利奥特扬声说，「在您那牢里，您宁可要哪一样——西塞罗（Cicero）的书信，还是一个厨房丫头？」

王后命她住口，对尤达尔先生说道——

「书，我会叫人给你送去，因为我想，你能这样有所事事是好事。至于你的前程，我要把你安置进一座修道院，那里于你有的是学问，却再无一个女人。你闯下的祸够多了！去！」

见她变得这般严厉，他叹了口气；她也乐得摆脱他。可他进那边屋里还不到一分钟，便腾起了一片沙嗓的叫骂、尖叫与大笑，她猛地推开门，赶在几位夫人惊得张开的嘴合拢之前，亲自到了门口。

---

===Original===
The young Poins was beating the Magister, so that the fur gown made a greyish whirl about his scarlet suit in the midst of a tangle of spun wool; spinning wheels were overset, Margot Poins crashed around upon them, wailing; the girls with their distaffs were crouching against the window-places and in corners, crying out each one of them.

The Queen had a single little gesture of the hand with which she dismissed all her waiting-women. She stood alone in the inner doorway with the Lady Cicely and the Lady Rochford behind her. The Lady Rochford wrung her gouty hands; the Lady Cicely set back her head and laughed.

The Queen spoke no word, but in the new silence it was as if the Magister fell out of the boy’s hands. He staggered amidst the trails of wool, nearly fell, and then made stiff zigzags towards the open outer door, where his prison guards awaited him, since they had no warrant to enter the antechamber. He dragged after him a little trail of fragments of spinning wheels and spindles.

“Well, there’s a fine roister-doister!” the Lady Cicely laughed behind the Queen’s back. The Queen stood very still and frowned. To her the disturbance was monstrous and distasteful, for she was minded to have things very orderly and quiet. The boy, in his scarlet, pulled off his bonnet and panted, but he was not still more than a second, and suddenly he called out to the Queen—

“Make that pynot to marry my sister!”

===Chinese===
小普因斯正在殴打尤达尔先生，那件皮袍围着少年猩红的衣裳，在乱作一团的毛线中间搅成一团灰影；纺车掀翻在地，玛戈·普因斯扑在车堆里跌撞打转，嚎哭着；执纺杆的姑娘们缩在窗龛里、角落里，一个个尖声叫嚷。

王后只轻轻一挥手，便把侍女们尽数遣散。她独自站在内门口，西西莉夫人和罗奇福德夫人立在她身后。罗奇福德夫人绞着她痛风的手；西西莉夫人仰起头大笑。

王后一言不发，可在这新的静默里，尤达尔先生仿佛从少年的手里脱落了下来。他在缠绊的毛线间踉跄，险些跌倒，随即僵直地拐着之字，朝敞开的外门走去——他的狱卒们在那里候着他，因为他们没有进入前厅的手令。他身后拖着一小串纺车和纺锤的残片。

「嗬，好一场大闹腾！」西西莉夫人在王后背后笑道。王后立着不动，蹙起了眉。在她看来，这场骚乱可怖又可憎，因为她素来要诸事井然、清静。那猩红衣裳的少年摘下软帽喘气，却连一秒钟也静不下来，忽然朝王后喊道——

「叫那花喜鹊娶我姐姐！」

===Original===
Margot Poins hung round him and cried out—

“Oh no! Oh no!”

He shook her roughly loose.

“An’ you do not wed with him how shall I get advancement?” he said. “’A promised me that when ’a should come to be Chancellor ’a would advance me.”

He pushed her from him again with his elbow when she came near.

“Y’ve grown over familiar,” the Queen said, “with being too much near me. Y’are grown over familiar. For seven days you shall no longer keep my door.”

Margot Poins raised her arms over her head, then she leant against a windowpane and sobbed into the crook of her elbow. The boy’s slender face was convulsed with rage; his blue eyes started from his head; his callow hair was crushed up.

===Chinese===
玛戈·普因斯攀住他哭喊——

「哦不行！哦不行！」

他粗暴地把她甩脱。

「你若不嫁给他，我怎能得进身？」他说。「他应承过我，等他做到宰相，便会提拔我。」

她一凑近，他又用胳膊肘把她推开。

「你仗着在我跟前侍候得太久，变得过分放肆了，」王后说，「你变得过分放肆了。此后七天，你不得再守我的门。」

玛戈·普因斯双臂举过头顶，随即倚住一块窗玻璃，伏在臂弯里啜泣。少年那张瘦脸气得抽搐；蓝眼睛从头颅里暴突出来；一头稚软的头发揉得乱蓬蓬。

===Original===
“Shall a man—” he began to protest.

“I say nothing against that you did beat this Magister,” the Queen said. “Such passions cannot be controlled, and I pass it by.”

“But will ye not make this man to wed with my sister?” the boy said harshly.

“I cannot. He hath a wedded wife!”

He dropped his hands to his side.

“Alack; then my father’s house is down,” he cried out.

===Chinese===
「难道一个男人——」他开口抗议。

「你打了这位尤达尔先生，我并没有说什么，」王后说。「那样的血气是约束不住的，我不予追究。」

「可您难道不肯叫这人娶我姐姐么？」少年狠狠地说。

「我不能。他已有明媒正娶的妻子！」

他垂下双手，落在身侧。

「唉，罢了；我父亲的门第这下塌了，」他叫道。

===Original===
“Gentleman Guard,” Katharine said, “get you for seven days away from my door. I will have another sentry whilst you bethink you of a worthier way to advancement.”

He gazed at her stupidly.

“You will not make this wedding?” he asked.

“Gentleman Guard,” Katharine said, “you have your answer. Get you gone.”

A sudden rage came into his eyes; he swallowed in his throat and made a gesture of despair with his hand. The Queen turned back into her room and busied herself with her task, which was the writing into a little vellum book of seven prayers to the Virgin that the Lady Elizabeth, Queen Anne Boleyn’s daughter, a child then in London, was to turn each one into seven languages, written fair in the volume as a gift, against Christmas, for the King.

===Chinese===
「卫士，」凯瑟琳说，「你且离开我的门七天。在你想出一条更值得称道的进身之路以前，我另派一名哨兵。」

他呆呆地望着她。

「您不肯促成这桩婚事？」他问。

「卫士，」凯瑟琳说，「你的答复已经有了。去。」

一阵狂怒涌进他眼里；他喉头一哽，用手做了个绝望的姿势。王后转身回到自己房中，专心做她的功课——往一本小羊皮册子里，写上献给圣母的七篇祷文：安妮·博林王后之女、其时还在伦敦的孩子伊丽莎白公主，须将每一篇转成七种文字，端端正正誊在册中，赶在圣诞前，作为献给国王的礼物。

---

===Original===
“I would not have that boy to guard my door,” the Lady Cicely said to the Queen.

“Why, ’tis a good boy,” Katharine answered; “and his sister loves me very well.”

“Get your Highness another,” the Lady Cicely persisted. “I do not like his looks.”

The Queen gazed up from her writing to where the dark girl, her figure raked very much back in her stiff bodice, played daintily with the tassels of the curtain next the window.

===Chinese===
「我不愿叫那小子守您的门，」西西莉夫人对王后说。

「哎，他是个好孩子，」凯瑟琳答道，「他姐姐又非常爱我。」

「殿下还是另换一个吧，」西西莉夫人执意道，「我不喜欢他那副长相。」

王后从笔下的书写里抬起目光，望向那肤色深暗的少女——僵挺的紧身胸衣把她身姿绷得极度后仰，人正娇俏地拨弄窗边帷幔的流苏。

===Original===
“My Lady,” Katharine said, “my Highness must get me a new maid in place of Margot Poins, that shall away into a nunnery. Is not that grief enough for poor Margot? Shall she think in truth that she has undone her father’s house?”

“Then advance the springald to some post away from you,” the Lady Cicely said.

“Nay,” the Queen answered; “he hath done nothing to merit advancement.”

She continued, with her head bent down over the writing on her knee, her lips moving a little as, sedulously, she drew large and plain letters with her pen.

===Chinese===
「夫人，」凯瑟琳说，「我这殿下总得给自己另寻一名侍女，补玛戈·普因斯的缺——她就要进修道院去了。对可怜的玛戈，这伤心还不够么？难道还要她当真以为，自己断送了父亲的门第？」

「那就把这愣小子提拔到离您远远的什么职位上去，」西西莉夫人说。

「不，」王后答道，「他没有立下配得进身的功劳。」

她继续写下去，头低在膝上的字纸上，嘴唇微微翕动，一笔一笔，兢兢业业地描着又大又端正的字母。

===Original===
“By Heaven,” the Lady Cicely said, “you have too tickle a conscience to be a Queen of this world and day. In the time of Caesar you might have lived more easily.”

The Queen looked up at her from her writing; her clear eyes were untroubled.

“Aye,” she said. “*Lucio Domitio, Appio Claudio consulibus*—”

Cicely Rochford set back her head and laughed at the ceiling.

“Aye, your Highness is a Roman,” she tittered like a magpie.

===Chinese===
「凭上天，」西西莉夫人说，「您这良心太娇嫩，做不得当今之世的王后。生在恺撒的时代，您本可以活得轻省些。」

王后从书写中抬眼看她；那双清澈的眼睛波澜不惊。

「是，」她说。「*Lucio Domitio, Appio Claudio consulibus*（在卢基乌斯·多米提乌斯与阿庇乌斯·克劳狄乌斯出任执政官之年）——」

西西莉·罗奇福德仰起头，冲着天花板笑。

「是了，殿下是位罗马人，」她像喜鹊似的喳喳笑。

===Original===
“In the day of Caesar it was simple to do well,” the Queen said.

“Why, I do not believe it,” Cicely answered her.

“Cousin! Cousin!” The old Lady Rochford warned her that this was the Queen, not her old playmate.

“But now,” the Queen said, “with such a coming together and a concourse of peoples about us; with such holes and corners in a great Court—” She paused and sighed.

“Well, if I may not speak my mind,” Cicely Rochford said to the old lady, “what good am I?”

===Chinese===
「在恺撒的时代，行善是件简单的事，」王后说。

「嗳，我才不信呢，」西西莉答她。

「堂妹！堂妹！」老罗奇福德夫人提醒她：这是王后，不是她旧日的游伴。

「可如今，」王后说，「这样多的族类与人群在我们周遭聚拢交汇；这样一座大宫廷里，又有这样多的幽隅暗角——」她顿住，叹了口气。

「唉，我若连心里的话都说不成，」西西莉·罗奇福德对老夫人说，「那我还有什么用处？」

===Original===
“I did even what I might to keep this lamb Margot from the teeth of that wolf Magister,” the Queen said. “I take shame to myself that I did no more. I will do a penance for it. But still I think that these be degenerate days.”

“Oh, Queen of dreams and fancies,” Cicely Rochford said. “I am very certain that in the days of your noble Romans it was as it is now. Tell me, if you can, that in all your readings of hic and hoc you lit not upon such basenesses? You will not lay your hand upon your heart and say that never a man of Rome bartered his sister for the hope of advancement, or that never a learned doctor was a corrupter of youth? I have seen the like in the plays of Plautus that here have been played at Court.”

“Why,” the Queen said, “the days of Plautus were days degenerated and fallen already from the ancient nobleness.”

“You should have Queened it before Goodman Adam fell,” Cicely Rochford mocked her. “If you go back before Plautus, go back all the way.”

===Chinese===
「我尽了力所能及，不叫这羔羊玛戈落进那恶狼尤达尔的齿间，」王后说。「我引以为耻，自己没有做得更多。我要为此行一桩补赎。可我仍以为，这年月是颓堕的年月。」

「哦，满是梦幻与奇想的王后啊，」西西莉·罗奇福德说。「我十二分地确信，在您那高贵的罗马人的年月里，世道同如今一般无二。您若能，便告诉我：在您读*hic*读*hoc*的全部光阴里，就没撞上过这等卑污事么？您总不肯把手按在心口起誓，说罗马从来没有一个男人，为着进身的指望贩卖过自己的妹妹；也从来没有一位博学的博士，做过戕害少年的败德者吧？这般光景，我在宫里上演过的普劳图斯（Plautus）的戏文里都见识过。」

「话虽如此，」王后说，「普劳图斯的时代，也已是颓坏堕落的了，早从上古的高贵里跌落了下来。」

「您该趁好人亚当（Goodman Adam）堕落之前便君临做王后才是，」西西莉·罗奇福德讥笑她。「您既要追溯到普劳图斯以前，索性一路追溯到底吧。」

===Original===
She shrugged her shoulders up to her ears and uttered a little sound like “*Pfui!*” Then she said quickly—

“Give me leave to be gone, your Highness, that I may not grow over familiar like the boy with the pikestaff, for if it do not gall you it shall wring the withers of this my old husband’s cousin!”

The old Lady Rochford, who was always thinking of what had been said two speeches ago, because she was so slow-witted, raised her gouty hands in the air and opened her mouth. But the Queen smiled faintly at Cicely.

“When I ask you to mince matters in my little room you shall do it. It was Lucius the Praetor that went always accompanied by a carping Stoic to keep him from being puffed up, and it was a good custom.”

===Chinese===
她把肩膀耸到耳根，发出一声「*Pfui!*（呸！）」似的轻响。随即她急急说道——

「请殿下恩准告退，免得我也变得像那扛长枪的小子一样过分放肆——这话若磨不着您，可要绞痛我这位老夫家堂姊的肩脊了！」

老罗奇福德夫人素来迟钝，总在琢磨两句之前说过的话，这时把痛风的双手举到半空，张开了嘴。王后却只朝西西莉淡淡一笑。

「当我请你在我这小屋里说话检点些时，你便照办。昔日的裁判官卢基乌斯（Lucius）出行，总有一名好挑剔的斯多噶哲人随侍在侧，免得他自高自大，那是个好习俗。」

===Original===
“Before Heaven,” Cicely Rochford said in the midst of her curtsey at the door, “shall I have the office of such a one as Diogenes who derided Alexander the Emperor? Then must my old husband live with me in a tub!”

“Pray you,” the Queen said after her through the door, “look you around and spy me out a maid to be my tiring-woman and ward my spinsters. For nowadays I see few maids to choose from.”

When she was gone the old Lady Rochford timorously berated the Queen. She would have her be more distant with knights’ wives and the like. For it was fitting for a Queen to be feared and deemed awful.

“I had rather be loved and deemed pitiful,” Katharine answered. “For I was once such a one—no more—than she or thou, or very little more. Before the people I bear myself proudly for my lord his high honour. But I do lead a very cloistered life, and have leisure to reflect upon for what a little space authority endureth, and how that friendship and true love between friends are things that bear the weather better.” She did not say her Latin text, for the old lady had no Latin.

===Chinese===
「在上天面前，」西西莉·罗奇福德在门边行着屈膝礼说道，「莫非要派我出任那讥诮过亚历山大皇帝（Alexander）的第欧根尼（Diogenes）一般的职司？那我那老丈夫可就得陪我住进木桶里去了！」

「有劳，」王后隔着门在她身后说，「替我四下留意，寻访一名侍女来做我的梳妆侍女，替我管着这些纺线的姑娘。如今我可见不着多少可供拣选的侍女了。」

她去后，老罗奇福德夫人怯生生地数说王后。她要王后对骑士之妻之流的人物更疏远些，因为王后合该受人畏惮，被人视作威严可畏。

「我宁可被人爱戴、被人视作悲悯，」凯瑟琳答道。「因为我从前也就是那样一个人——不多什么——同她或汝一样，或至多多那么一点。在人前，为我夫君的尊荣，我自持高傲。可我过的实在是深门幽居的日子，有闲暇细细思量：权势所能持存的时日何其短促；而朋友之间的情谊与真爱，才是更经得起风雨的东西。」她没有说出她那句拉丁文的经文，因为老夫人不通拉丁文。

### VI

===Original===
In the underground cell, above the red and gold table that afternoon, Lascelles wrought at a fair copy of the King’s letter to the Pope, amended as it had been by Udal’s hand. The Archbishop had come into the room reading a book as he came from his prayers, and sate him down in his chair at the tablehead without glancing at his gentleman.

“Prithee, your Grace,” Lascelles said, “suffer me to carry this letter mine own self to the Queen.”

The Archbishop looked up at him; his mournful eyes started wide; he leaned forward.

“Art thou Lascelles?” he asked.

“Aye, Lascelles I am,” the gentleman answered; “but I have cut off my beard.”

===Chinese===
那天下午，在地下的囚室里，拉塞尔斯伏在那张金红的桌案上，誊写国王致教皇书信的清稿——那信已经过尤达尔的手笔改削。大主教自祈祷处归来，一路捧着一本书走进屋来，在上首自己的椅中坐定，一眼也不曾望他那位侍从。

「求大人，」拉塞尔斯说，「容我亲身把这封信呈送王后。」

大主教抬眼看他；那双哀戚的眼睛蓦地睁大；他把身子向前倾去。

「汝是拉塞尔斯？」他问。

「是，拉塞尔斯正是在下，」那侍从答道，「只是我把胡须剪去了。」

===Original===
The Archbishop was very weak and startled; he fell into an anger.

“Is this a time for vanities?” he said. “Will you be after the wenches? You look a foolish boy! I do not like this prank.”

Lascelles put up his hand to stroke his vanished beard. His risible lips writhed in a foxy smile; his chin was fuller than you would have expected, round and sensuous with a dimple in the peak of it.

“Please it, your Grace,” he said, “this is no vanity, but a scheme that I will try.”

===Chinese===
大主教身子虚弱，又猛吃一惊；他动了怒。

「这是贪慕虚荣的时候么？」他说。「你又去追那些娘儿们了？你看上去像个傻小子！我不喜欢这种恶作剧。」

拉塞尔斯抬手去抚那已经不存的胡须。他那两片爱笑的嘴唇扭出一个狐狸似的笑；他的下巴比你料想的更丰腴，滚圆而肉感，颏尖上还凹着一个小酒窝。

「伏乞大人，」他说，「这不是虚荣，而是一桩我要施行的计策。」

===Original===
“What scheme? What scheme?” the Archbishop said. “Here have been too many schemes.” He was very shaken and afraid, because this world was beyond his control.

“Please it, your Grace,” Lascelles answered, “ask me not what this scheme is.”

The Archbishop shook his head and pursed his lips feebly.

“Please it, your Grace,” Lascelles urged, “if this scheme miscarry, your Grace shall hear no more of it. If this scheme succeed I trow it shall help some things forward that your Grace would much have forwarded. Please it, your Grace, to ask me no more, and to send me with this letter to the Queen’s Highness.”

The Archbishop opened his nerveless hands before him; they were pale and wrinkled as if they had been much soddened in water. Since the King had bidden him compose that letter to the Pope of Rome, his hands had grown so. Lascelles wrote on at the new draft of the letter, his lips following the motions of his pen. Still writing, and with his eyes down, he said—

===Chinese===
「什么计策？什么计策？」大主教说。「这里的计策已经太多了。」他大为震动，心下害怕，因为这个世界的走向全然不在他的掌控之中。

「伏乞大人，」拉塞尔斯答道，「莫问我这计策是什么。」

大主教摇了摇头，无力地抿住嘴唇。

「伏乞大人，」拉塞尔斯劝道，「倘若这计策失手，大人便再听不到它的半点风声。倘若这计策得成，我确信它能推动几桩大人极愿促成的事。伏乞大人，莫再问我，且遣我带着这封信去见王后陛下。」

大主教在身前摊开那双绵软无力的手；双手苍白起皱，仿佛久浸水中一般。自国王命他撰写那封致罗马教皇的信以来，他的手就变成了这样。拉塞尔斯继续誊写那封信的新稿，嘴唇随笔锋的起落而翕动。他一面写，一面垂着眼说——

===Original===
“The Queen’s Highness will put from her her tirewoman in a week from now.”

The Archbishop moved his fingers as who should say—

“What is that to me!” His eyes gazed into the space above his book that lay before him on the table.

“This Margot Poins is a niece of the master-printer Badge, a Lutheran, of the Austin Friars.” Lascelles pursued his writing for a line further. Then he added—

“This putting away and the occasion of it shall make a great noise in the town of London. It will be said amongst the Lutherans that the Queen is answerable therefor. It will be said that the Queen hath a very lewd Court and companionship.”

===Chinese===
「不出一周，王后陛下便要把她的梳妆侍女打发了。」

大主教动了动手指，那样子仿佛在说——

「这与我何干！」他的双眼凝望着桌上摊在他面前那本书上方的虚空。

「这位玛戈·普因斯，是印书坊主巴奇（Badge）的侄女——那巴奇是个路德派，住在奥斯汀修士堂（the Austin Friars）。」拉塞尔斯又续写了一行。然后他补上一句——

「这一番打发，连同其中的缘由，必将在伦敦城里掀起大哗。路德派中人会传说，此事王后难辞其咎。会传说王后的宫廷与侍从淫逸不堪。」

===Original===
The Archbishop muttered wearily—

“It hath been said already.”

“But not,” Lascelles said, “since she came to be Queen.”

The Archbishop directed upon him his hangdog eyes, and his voice was the voice of a man that would not be disturbed from woeful musings.

“What use?” he said bitterly; and then again, “What use?”

===Chinese===
大主教疲惫地喃喃道——

「这话早就有人说过。」

「然而，」拉塞尔斯说，「自她登上后位以来，还不曾有人说。」

大主教把那双愁苦卑怯的眼睛转向他，那嗓音，是不愿被人从悲戚的沉思里搅起之人的嗓音。

「有何用处？」他辛酸地说；随即又是一句，「有何用处？」

===Original===
Lascelles wrote on sedulously. He used his sandarach to the end of the page, blew off the sand, eyed the sheet sideways, laid it down, and set another on his writing-board.

“Why,” he brought out quietly, “it may be brought to the King’s Highness’ ears.”

“What way?” the Archbishop said heavily, as if the thing were impossible. His gentleman answered—

“This way and that!” The King’s Highness had a trick of wandering about among his faithful lieges unbeknown; foreign ambassadors wrote abroad such rumours which might be re-reported from the foreign by the King’s servants.

“Such a report,” Lascelles said, “hath gone up already to London town by a swift carrier.”

===Chinese===
拉塞尔斯孜孜不辍地写着。他在页末撒上吸墨砂，吹去砂粒，侧眼端详那一页，放下，又在写字板上铺开新的一页。

「哦，」他轻声吐出一句，「这事或许会传到国王陛下的耳中。」

「怎么个传法？」大主教沉沉地说，仿佛那是全然不可能的事。他的侍从答道——

「这样传，那样传！」国王陛下有个惯技，会隐去身份，混迹于他忠实的臣民中间；外邦使节又惯把这类风声写寄国外，而国王的仆役们还可能把这些话从国外再奏回来。

「这样一封报信，」拉塞尔斯说，「早已由一名急递信使送进伦敦城去了。」

===Original===
The Archbishop brought out wearily and distastefully—

“How know you? Was it you that wrote it?”

“Please it, your Grace,” his gentleman answered him, “it was in this wise. As I was passing by the Queen’s chamber wall I heard a great outcry—”

He laid down his pen beside his writing-board the more leisurely to speak.

He had seen Udal, beaten and shaking, stagger out from the Queen’s door to where his guards waited to set him back in prison. From Udal he had learned of this new draft of the letter; of Udal’s trouble he knew before. Udal gone, he had waited a little, hearing the Queen’s voice and what she said very plainly, for the castle was very great and quiet. Then out had come the young Poins, breathing like a volcano through his nostrils, and like to be stricken with palsy, boy though he was. Him Lascelles had followed at a convenient distance, where he staggered and snorted. And, coming upon the boy in an empty guardroom near the great gate, he had found him aflame with passion against the Queen’s Highness.

===Chinese===
大主教疲惫而厌烦地迸出一句——

「你怎么知道？写它的莫不是你？」

「伏乞大人，」他的侍从答道，「事情的原委是这样：我打王后寝宫的墙外经过，听得里面一场大闹——」

他把笔搁在写字板旁，好更加从容地讲述。

他看见尤达尔挨了打、浑身发抖，跌跌撞撞从王后的门里出来，走向等着押他回牢的看守那里。从尤达尔口中，他得知了这封信的新稿；尤达尔的祸事，他先前就已知晓。尤达尔去后，他又稍待片刻，王后的声音、她说的话，他听得字字分明，因为城堡极大，四下静悄。随后小普因斯冲了出来，鼻孔里喷着气，活像一座火山，虽是个少年，却险些中了风瘫。拉塞尔斯便隔着一段合宜的距离尾随他，任他一路踉跄、一路呼哧喘气。及至在大门附近一间空着的卫兵室里赶上这少年，他发现他对王后陛下正燃着一腔怒火。

===Original===
“I,” the boy had cried out, “I that by my carrying of letters set this Howard where she sits! I!—and this is my advancement. My sister cast down, and I cast out, and another maid to take my sister’s place.”

And Lascelles, in the guard-chamber, had shown him sympathy and reminded him that there was gospel for saying that princes had short memories.

“But I did not calm him!” Lascelles said.

On the contrary, upon Lascelles’ suggestion that the boy had but to hold his tongue and pocket his wrongs, the young Poins had burst out that he would shout it all abroad at every street corner. And suddenly it had come into his head to write such a letter to his Uncle Badge the printer as, printed in a broadside, would make the Queen’s name to stink, until the last generation was of men, in men’s nostrils.

Lascelles rubbed his hands gently and sinuously together. He cast one sly glance at the Archbishop.

“Well, the letter was written,” he said. “Be sure the broadside shall be printed.”

===Chinese===
「我，」那少年当时高声嚷道，「我靠着一趟趟送信，才把这个霍华德家的女人扶上她如今坐的位子！我啊！——这便是我得的升赏。我姐姐被撵倒，我被逐出，另换一个侍女来顶我姐姐的缺。」

而拉塞尔斯在卫兵室里对他表露了同情，还提醒他：福音书里原有话可援——君王们的记性素来短浅。

「可我并没有劝他平静下来！」拉塞尔斯说。

恰恰相反，当拉塞尔斯暗示这少年只须闭紧嘴巴、把冤屈咽下肚去时，小普因斯顿时发作起来，嚷着要把这一切嚷遍每一个街角。忽然间他又起了一个念头：给他的叔父、印书匠巴奇写一封信，教它印成单页传单，好教王后的名声发臭——直臭到世上最后一辈人的鼻孔里。

拉塞尔斯温文而油滑地搓着双手。他朝大主教狡黠地瞥了一眼。

「喏，那封信已经写下了，」他说。「你只管放心，那传单一准会印出来。」

===Original===
Cranmer’s head was sunk over his book.

“This lad,” Lascelles said softly, “who in seven days’ time again shall keep the Queen’s door (for it is not true that the Queen’s Highness is an ingrate, well sure am I), this lad shall be a very useful confidant; a very serviceable guide to help us to a knowledge of who goes in to the Queen and who cometh out.”

The Archbishop did not appear to be listening to his gentleman’s soft voice and, resuming his pen, Lascelles finished his tale with—

“For I have made this lad my friend. It shall cost me some money, but I do not doubt that your Grace shall repay.”

The Archbishop raised his head.

“No, before God in heaven on His throne!” he said. His voice was shrill and high; he agitated his hands in their fine, tied sleeves. “I will have no part in these Cromwell tricks. All is lost; let it be lost. I must say my prayers.”

===Chinese===
克兰默的头垂在了书上。

「这少年，」拉塞尔斯柔声说，「再过七日便又会回王后宫门当值（因为王后陛下绝非忘恩负义之人，这一点我确信不疑）；这少年会是一个极得用的心腹，一个极得力的向导，帮我们弄清谁走进了王后的门，谁又从门里走出来。」

大主教似乎并没有在听他侍从那轻柔的语声；拉塞尔斯重新提笔，用这样一句话收束了他的故事——

「因为我已交结下这个少年做朋友。这要花去我一些钱财，但我毫不怀疑，大人定会如数偿还。」

大主教抬起了头。

「不，凭天上宝座上的天主起誓！」他说。他的嗓音尖厉而高亢；他在精工束袖的袖管里激动地抖着双手。「这些克伦威尔式的伎俩，我决计不再沾手。一切都完了；由它去吧。我得去做我的祈祷。」

===Original===
“Has it been by saying of your Grace’s prayers that your Grace has lived through these months?” Lascelles asked softly.

“Aye,” the Archbishop wrung his hands; “you girded me and moved me when Cromwell lay at death, to write a letter to the King’s Highness. To write such a letter as should appear brave and faithful and true to Privy Seal’s cause.”

“Such a letter your Grace wrote,” Lascelles said; “and it was the best writing that ever your Grace made.”

The Archbishop gazed at the table.

“How do I know that?” he said in a whisper. “You say so, who bade me write it.”

“For that your Grace lives yet,” Lascelles said softly; “though in those days a warrant was written for your capture. For, sure it is, and your Grace has heard it from the King’s lips, that your letter sounded so faithful and piteous and true to him your late leader, that the King could not but believe that you, so loyal in such a time to a man disgraced and cast down beyond hope, could not but be faithful and loyal in the future to him, the King, with so many bounties to bestow.”

===Chinese===
「这几个月来，大人靠的可是诵念这些祈祷，才捱了过来？」拉塞尔斯轻声问。

「是，」大主教绞着双手，「克伦威尔命在旦夕之时，是你讥刺我、撺掇我给国王陛下写信。写一封显得义勇忠贞、矢忠于御玺大臣之业的信。」

「这样一封信，大人写了，」拉塞尔斯说，「那是大人生平写得最好的一篇文字。」

大主教凝望着桌面。

「这我如何得知？」他低声道。「是你说的——是你叫我去写的。」

「凭的便是大人至今还活着，」拉塞尔斯柔声道，「虽说那些日子里，缉拿大人的拘票都已写就。因为千真万确——大人也亲耳听国王说过——大人的那封信，听来是那样忠贞、那样哀婉、那样忠于大人昔日的主上，以致国王不能不信：一个在如此时节仍对一位身败名裂、万难翻身之人如此尽忠的人，日后对他——这位有无数恩赏可颁赐的国王——也势必忠贞不贰。」

===Original===
“Aye,” the Archbishop said, “but how do I know what of a truth was in the King’s mind who casteth down today one, tomorrow another, till none are left?”

And again Cranmer dropped his anguished eyes to the table.

===Chinese===
「是，」大主教说，「可国王今日贬黜一个、明日贬黜另一个、直到一个不剩，他心里打的究竟是什么真主意，我又如何得知？」

克兰默又一次把那双深陷痛苦的眼睛垂向了桌面。

---

===Original===
In those days still—and he slept still worse since the King had bidden him write this letter to Rome—the Archbishop could not sleep on any night without startings and sweats and cryings out in his sleep. And he gave orders that, when he so cried out, the page at his bedside should wake him.

For then he was seeing the dreadful face of his great master, Privy Seal, when the day of his ruin had come. Cromwell had been standing in a window of the council chamber at Westminster looking out upon a courtyard. In behind him had come the other lords of the council, Norfolk with his yellow face, the High Admiral, and many others; and each, seating himself at the table, had kept his bonnet on his head. So Cromwell, turning, had seen them and had asked with his hard insolence and embittered eyes of hatred, how they dared be covered before he who was their president sat down. Then, up against him in the window-place there had sprung Norfolk at the chain of the George round his neck, and Suffolk at the Garter on his knee; and Norfolk had cried out that Thomas Cromwell was no longer Privy Seal of that kingdom, nor president of that council, but a traitor that must die. Then such rage and despair had come into Thomas Cromwell’s terrible face that Cranmer’s senses had reeled. He had seen Norfolk and the Admiral fall back before this passion; he had seen Thomas Cromwell tear off his cap and cast it on the floor; he had heard him bark and snarl out certain words into the face of the yellow dog of Norfolk.

“*Upon your life you dare not call me traitor!” and Norfolk had fallen back abashed.

Then the chamber had seemed to fill with an awful gloom and darkness; men showed only like shadows against the window lights; the constable of the Tower had come in with the warrants, and in that gloom the earth had appeared to tremble and quake beneath the Archbishop’s feet.

===Chinese===
那些日子里，大主教依旧夜夜不得安睡——自国王命他写这封寄往罗马的信以来，睡得愈发坏了——每夜总免不了惊悸、盗汗，在睡梦里喊出声来。他因此吩咐下去：每当他这样喊叫，侍立榻旁的小侍童便须唤醒他。

因为那时他便看见他那位可畏的主上、御玺大臣败亡之日的那张脸。克伦威尔当时正站在威斯敏斯特枢密院会议厅的一扇窗前，望着楼下的一座庭院。他身后络绎走进其余的枢密院诸勋——诺福克带着他那张蜡黄的脸，还有海军大臣（the High Admiral），以及许多别的人；人人各自在桌旁落座，头上却兀自戴着帽子。克伦威尔转过身来望见了，便带着冷硬的傲慢和饱含怨毒的双眼质问：他这位议长尚且未曾落座，他们怎敢冠戴俨然。于是，在那窗龛里，诺福克当胸扑上去，揪住他颈间圣乔治项章的链子，萨福克抓住他膝上的嘉德带；诺福克高声宣布：托马斯·克伦威尔已不再是这王国的御玺大臣，不再是那枢密院的议长，而是一个必死无疑的叛贼。霎时间，托马斯·克伦威尔那张可怕的脸上腾起那样的狂怒与绝望，克兰默的神志都为之晕眩。他看见诺福克与海军大臣在这盛怒面前踉跄后退；他看见托马斯·克伦威尔扯下帽子掼在地上；他听见他冲着诺福克那条黄狗的脸，嗥着、狺着吠出几个字眼——

「*拿你的性命打赌，你也不敢叫我叛贼！*」而诺福克羞惭地退了下去。

随后，那间大厅仿佛注满了骇人的阴沉与黑暗；人影只映着窗光，恍如一道道鬼影；伦敦塔总管（the Constable of the Tower）拿着拘票走了进来，而在那片昏暗里，大地仿佛就在大主教的脚下战栗、震颤。

---

===Original===
He crossed himself at the recollection, and, coming out of his stupor, saw that Lascelles was finishing his writings. And he was glad that he was here now and not there then.

“Prithee, your Grace,” the gentleman’s soft voice said, “let me bear, myself, this letter to the Queen.”

The Archbishop shivered frostily in his robes.

“I will have no more Cromwell tricks,” he said. “I have said it”; and he affected an obdurate tone.

“Then, indeed, all is lost,” Lascelles answered; “for this Queen is very resolved.”

===Chinese===
想起这些，他画了个十字；从失神中回过魂来，看见拉塞尔斯已快誊写完毕。他庆幸自己如今身在此间，而不是当日身在场中。

「求大人，」那侍从轻柔的语声说，「容我亲身把这封信呈送王后。」

大主教在法袍里寒凛凛地打了个颤。

「克伦威尔式的伎俩，我再也不沾了，」他说，「我已经说过了」；他做出一副执拗的口吻。

「那么，一切当真都完了，」拉塞尔斯答道，「因为这位王后心意极坚。」

===Original===
The Archbishop cast his eyes up to the cold stone ceiling above him. He crossed himself.

“You are a very devil,” he said, and panic came into his eyes, so that he turned them all round him as if he sought an issue at which to run out.

“The Papist lords in this castle met on Saturday night,” Lascelles said; “their meeting was very secret, and Norfolk was their head. But I have heard it said that not one of them was for the Queen.”

The Archbishop shrank within himself.

“I am not minded to hear this,” he said.

“Not one of them was for the Queen altogether; for she will render all lands and goods back to the Church, and there is no one of them but is rich with the lands and goods of the Church. That they that followed Cromwell are not for the Queen well your Grace knoweth,” his gentleman continued.

===Chinese===
大主教抬眼望向头顶冰冷的石砌穹顶。他又画了个十字。

「你真是个魔鬼，」他说，惊惶窜进他的眼中，于是他把眼睛四下里乱转，仿佛在寻一处可以夺路而逃的出口。

「这座城堡里的教皇派勋贵们，星期六夜里聚了一会，」拉塞尔斯说，「聚会极为隐秘，为首的是诺福克。但我听说，他们中间没有一个人是站在王后一边的。」

大主教整个人往内里一缩。

「我不愿听这些，」他说。

「他们当中，没有一个人是全然站在王后一边的；因为她要把一切土地财货归还教会，而他们之中没有一个不是靠教会的土地财货发家的。至于克伦威尔的旧党不站在王后一边，大人知之甚明，」他的侍从接着说。

===Original===
“I will not hear this; this is treason,” the Archbishop muttered.

“So that who standeth for the Queen?” Lascelles whispered. “Only a few of the baser sort that have no lands to lose.”

“The King,” the Archbishop cried out in a terrible voice; “the King standeth for her!”

He sprang up in his chair and then sank down again, covering his mouth with his hands, as if he would have intercepted the uttered words. For who knew who listened at what doors in these days. He whispered horribly—

“What a folly is this. Who shall move the King? Will reports of his ambassadors that Cleves, or Charles, or Francis miscall the Queen? You know they will not, for the King is aware of how these princes batten on carrion. Will broad sheets of the Lutheran? You know they will not, for the King is aware of how those coggers come by their tales. Will the King go abroad among the people any more to hear what they say? You know he will not. For he is grown too old, and his fireside is made too sweet—”

He wavered, and he could not work himself up with a longer show of anger.

===Chinese===
「我不要听；这是叛逆之言，」大主教喃喃道。

「那么，究竟还有谁站在王后一边？」拉塞尔斯低声道。「只剩几个没有土地可失的微贱之人罢了。」

「国王，」大主教用一种可怖的声音喊道，「国王是站在她一边的！」

他在椅中霍然直起身，随即又颓然坐倒，双手掩住嘴，仿佛想把已经出口的话拦回去。因为这些日子里，谁知道有什么人正贴在哪扇门后窃听。他声音骇人地低语道——

「这是何等的愚妄。谁能撼得动国王？外邦使臣回禀说克莱沃、皇帝卡尔或法兰西斯辱骂了王后，便撼得动么？你知道不能，因为国王深知这些君王是怎样靠腐肉养肥自己的。路德派的传单便撼得动么？你知道不能，因为国王深知那些老千的谣诼是何等来路。国王还会再到百姓中间去，听他们说些什么么？你知道他不会了。因为他已老得太甚，炉边又被安置得太甜蜜——」

他动摇了，再也支撑不起更久的怒容。

===Original===
“Prithee,” Lascelles said, “let me bear this letter myself to the Queen.” His voice was patient and calm.

The Archbishop lay back, impotent, in his chair. His arms were along the arms of it: he had dropped his book upon the table. His long gown was draped all over him down to his feet; his head remained motionless; his eyes did not wink, and gazed at despair; his hands drooped, open and impotent.

Suddenly he moved one of them a very little.

===Chinese===
「求大人，」拉塞尔斯说，「容我亲身把这封信呈送王后。」他的语声耐心而平静。

大主教瘫靠在椅中，全然无力。两臂摊在扶手上：书已跌落在桌上。长袍从身上一直披垂到脚；头一动不动；眼睛一眨不眨，凝望着绝望本身；双手垂着，摊开，绵软无力。

忽然，他把其中一只微微动了一动。

### VII

===Original===
It was the Queen’s habit to go every night, when the business of the day was done, to pray, along with the Lady Mary, in the small chapel that was in the roof of the castle. To vespers she went with all the Court to the big chapel in the courtyard that the King had builded especially for her. But to this little chapel, that was of Edward IV’s time, small and round-arched, all stone and dark and bare, she went with the Lady Mary alone. Her ladies and her doorguards they left at the stair foot, on a level with the sleeping rooms of the poorer sort, but up the little stairway they climbed by themselves, in darkness, to pray privately for the conversion of England. For this little place was so small and so forgotten that it had never been desecrated by Privy Seal’s men. It had had no vessels worth the taking, and only very old vestments and a few ill-painted pictures on the stone walls that were half hidden in the dust.

Katharine had found this little place when, on her first day at Pontefract, she had gone a-wandering over the castle with the King. For she was curious to know how men had lived in the old times; to see their rooms and to mark what old things were there still in use. And she had climbed thus high because she was minded to gaze upon the huge expanse of country and of moors that from the upper leads of the castle was to be seen. But this little chapel had seemed to her to be all the more sacred because it had been undesecrated and forgotten. She thought that you could not find such another in the King’s realm at that time; she was very assured that not one was to be found in any house of the King’s and hers.

And, making inquiries, she had found that there was also an old priest there served the chapel, doing it rather secretly for the well-disposed of the castle’s own guards. This old man had fled, at the approach of the King’s many, into the hidden valleys of that countryside, where still the faith lingered and lingers now. For, so barbarous and remote those north parts were, that a great many people had never heard that the King was married again, and fewer still, or none, knew that he and his wife were well inclined again towards Rome.

This old priest she had had brought to her. And he was so well loved that along with him came a cluster of weather-battered moorsmen, right with him into her presence. They kneeled down, being clothed with skins, and several of them having bows of a great size, to beg her not to harm this old man, for he was reputed a saint. The Queen could not understand their jargon but, when their suit was interpreted to her by the Lord Dacre of the North, and when she had had a little converse with the old priest, she answered that, so touched was her heart by his simplicity and gentleness, that she would pray the good King, her lord and master, to let this priest be made her confessor whilst there they stayed. And afterwards, if it were convenient, in reward for his faithfulness, he should be made a prior or a bishop in those parts. So the moorsmen, blessing her uncouthly for her fairness and kind words, went back with their furs and bows into their fastnesses. One of them was a great lord of that countryside, and each day he sent into the castle bucks and moor fowl, and once or twice a wolf. His name was Sir John Peel, and Sir John Peel, too, the priest was called.

So the priest served that little altar, and of a night, when the Queen was minded next day to partake of the host, he heard her confession. On other nights he left them there alone to say their prayers. It was always very dark with the little red light burning before the altar and two tapers that they lit beneath a statue of the Virgin, old and black and ill-carved by antique hands centuries before. And, in that blackness, they knelt, invisible almost, and still in the black gowns that they put on for prayers, beside a low pillar that gloomed out at their sides and vanished up into the darkness of the roof.

Having done their prayers, sometimes they stayed to converse and to meditate, for there they could be very private. On the night when the letter to Rome was redrafted, the Queen prayed much longer than the Lady Mary, who sat back upon a stool, silently, to await her finishing—for it seemed that the Queen was more zealous for the converting of those realms again to the old faith than was ever the Lady Mary. The tapers burned with a steady, invisible glow in the little side chapel behind the pillar; the altar gleamed duskily before them, and it was so still that through the unglassed windows they could hear, from far below in the black countryside, a tenuous bleating of late-dropped lambs. Katharine Howard’s beads clicked and her dress rustled as she came up from her knees.

===Chinese===
每当一日的事务办完，王后有个习惯：同玛丽公主一起到城堡顶层的小礼拜堂里去祈祷。行晚祷时，她与整个宫廷同往院子里那座国王特为她建造的大礼拜堂。但这座小礼拜堂——爱德华四世时代的旧物，狭小而圆拱，通体石造、幽暗而素朴——她只同玛丽公主单独前去。王后的女官与门卫都被留在楼梯脚下，与微贱者们的寝处同一层；她们两人则独自攀上小小的楼梯，摸着黑，去私下为英格兰的皈依祈祷。因为这小小的地方实在太小、太被人遗忘，从未遭御玺大臣的人马亵渎。它没有一件值得拿走的器皿，只有几件极旧的祭衣，和石壁上几幅画工拙劣的画像，半掩在尘埃里。

凯瑟琳初到庞蒂弗拉克特的第一天，同国王一起在城堡里信步漫游，发现了这个小地方。因为她素来好奇古时的人们怎样生活；想看看他们的房间，留意哪些旧物什至今还在使用。她攀到这般高处，是因为有心眺望自城堡顶上的铅顶平台望得见的辽阔原野与荒原。而这小礼拜堂，正因为未遭亵渎又被人遗忘，在她眼里反倒愈发神圣。她想，那时在国王的疆土上，你再也寻不出第二处这样的所在；她十分确信，在国王与她的任何一座宅邸里，一处也寻不出来。

一经查问，她又发现原来还有一位老神父在那里侍奉这礼拜堂，做得颇为隐秘，专为城堡自家卫卒中那些心向旧教的人。国王的大队扈从临近时，这位老人逃进了那一带乡野的隐秘山谷——信仰在那里当时犹自流连，至今犹自流连。因为北方诸地竟是那般蛮荒偏远，许许多多人从未听说国王又娶了亲；至于国王与他王后二人又重新倾心于罗马，知道的更是寥寥，或竟无一人。

她命人把这位老神父带到自己面前。他是如此受人爱戴，随着他来了一小群饱经风霜的荒原汉子，同他一道直入她的驾前。他们跪了下来——身上披着兽皮，其中几人还携着偌大的弓——恳求她不要伤害这位老人，因为他被尊为圣者。王后听不懂他们的土话，但等北方达克雷勋爵把他们的恳求转译给她，等她与老神父略谈了几句，她便答道：老人的淳朴与温良如此触动了她的心，她要祈求善良的国王、她的夫君与主上，在他们驻跸期间让这位神父做她的告解神父。往后倘若方便，为酬他的忠信，还应让他在那一带荣任修道院院长或主教。于是荒原汉子们因她的美貌与温言，用粗拙的言语为她祝祷，带着毛皮与弓，回他们的山寨僻壤去了。其中一人是那一带乡野的一位大领主，每天往城堡里送雄鹿与荒原雷鸟，有一两回还送来一只狼。他名叫约翰·皮尔爵士（Sir John Peel），而那位神父，也叫约翰·皮尔爵士。
