于是神父便侍奉那座小祭坛；夜里，当王后有意在次日领受圣体，他便听她告解。其余的夜晚，他便把她们单独留在那里祈祷。那里总是极暗的，只有祭坛前燃着的一盏小小红灯，和她们在圣母像下点起的两支细烛——那圣像年代久远、通体黢黑，出自几百年前古人之手，雕工拙劣。在那片黑暗里，她们跪着，身影几乎不可见，仍穿着为祈祷披上的黑袍，跪在一根低矮的柱子旁；柱子在她们身侧幽然显出轮廓，向上隐没进屋顶的黑暗。

祈祷既毕，她们有时还留下交谈、默想，因为在那里可以极尽私密。致罗马的信重新拟稿的那一夜，王后祈祷的时间比玛丽公主长得多；公主退坐在一只小凳上，静默地等她祷毕——因为要使这些邦国重归旧教，王后的热忱似乎比玛丽公主素来的还要炽烈。细烛在柱后的小侧堂里燃着，光晕沉静而隐约；祭坛在她们面前幽幽泛着微光；四下静极，静得她们隔着无玻璃的窗洞，听得见下方远远黑色乡野里传来晚产羊羔细弱的咩咩声。凯瑟琳·霍华德的念珠咔嗒轻响，她从跪姿起身时，裙裳窸窣作响。

===Original===
“It rests more with thee than with any other in this land,” her voice reverberated amongst the distant shadows. A bat that had been drawn in by the light flittered invisibly near them.

“Even what?” the Lady Mary asked.

“Well you know,” the Queen answered; “and may the God to whom you have prayed, that softened the heart of Paul, soften thine in this hour!”

The Lady Mary maintained a long silence. The bat flittered, with a leathern rustle, invisible, between their very faces. At last Mary uttered, and her voice was taunting and malicious—

“If you will soften my heart much you must beseech me.”

“Why, I will kneel to you,” the Queen said.

“Aye, you shall,” Mary answered. “Tell me what you would have of me.”

“Well you know!” Katharine said again.

In the darkness the lady’s voice maintained its bitter mirth, as it were the broken laughter of a soul in anguish.

===Chinese===
「这事系于汝身，重于此土之上任何旁人，」她的声音在远处的阴影间回荡。一只被烛光引进来的蝙蝠，在她们近旁无形地翻飞。

「究竟是何事？」玛丽公主问。

「汝分明知晓，」王后答道，「愿汝素来祈祷的那位上帝——那位曾软化保罗（Paul）之心的上帝——在此刻软化汝的心！」

玛丽公主久久缄默。蝙蝠带着革质的窸窣声，无形地在她们两张脸之间翻飞。玛丽终于开口，声音里满是讥刺与怨毒——

「你若要把我的心软化得彻底，就须得恳求我。」

「那么，我便跪求于你，」王后说。

「是，你跪定了，」玛丽答道。「说说你要我做什么。」

「你分明知晓！」凯瑟琳又说。

黑暗中，公主的语声兀自带着辛酸的快意，仿佛一个痛苦灵魂的断续笑声。

===Original===
“I will have you tell me, for it is a shameful tale that will shame you in the telling.”

The Queen paused to consider of her words.

“First, you shall be reconciled with, and speak pleasantly with, the King your father and my lord.”

“And is it not a shameful thing you bid me do, to bid me speak pleasant words to him that slew my mother and called me bastard?”

The Queen answered that she asked it in the name of Christ, His pitiful sake, and for the good of this suffering land.

“None the less, Queen, thou askest it in the darkness that thy face may not be seen. And what more askest thou?”

“That when the Duke of Orleans his ambassadors come asking your hand in marriage, you do show them a pleasant and acquiescent countenance.”

The sacredness of that dark place kept Mary from laughing aloud.

“That, too, you dare not ask in the light of day, Queen,” she said. “Ask on!”

“That when the Emperor’s ambassadors shall ask for your hand you shall profess yourself glad indeed.”

“Well, here is more shame, that I should be prayed to feign this gladness. I think the angels do laugh that hear you. Ask even more.”

===Chinese===
「我要你亲口告诉我，因为那是一桩可耻的故事，说出口便要教你蒙羞。」

王后顿了顿，斟酌自己的措辞。

「第一，你当与国王重归于好——你的父亲、我的夫君——与他好言相待。」

「你吩咐我做的岂不正是可耻之事么——吩咐我对那个杀了我母亲、骂我私生女的人说好听的话？」

王后答道，她是奉基督之名、看在祂悲悯的份上而求，也是为这片受苦国土的好处。

「然而，王后，你是在黑暗中求这事，好教你的脸不被人看见。你还求什么？」

「当奥尔良公爵（the Duke of Orleans）的使臣前来向你求亲时，你要以和悦而顺从的颜色接待他们。」

那幽暗之地的圣洁，使玛丽不至于放声大笑。

「这一条，你也不敢在光天化日之下开口，王后，」她说。「接着求！」

「当皇帝的使臣前来求亲时，你要显出真心实意的欢喜。」

「好啊，又添一桩羞耻：竟要人家恳求我来假装欢喜。我想听见这话的天使们都在笑呢。再多求些。」

===Original===
Katharine said patiently—

“That, having in reward of these favours, been set again on high, having honours shown you and a Court appointed round you, you shall gladly play the part of a princess royal to these realms, never gibing, nor sneering upon this King your father, nor calling upon the memory of the wronged Queen your mother.”

“Queen,” the Lady Mary said, “I had thought that even in the darkness you had not dared to ask me this.”

“I will ask it you again,” the Queen said, “in your room where the light of the candles shines upon my face.”

“Why, you shall,” the Lady Mary said. “Let us presently go there.”

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳耐心地说——

「这些殷勤既得了报偿，你重登高位，尊荣加于你身，一整套宫廷环侍于你左右；此后你便当欣然以这邦国王家长公主的身份行事，永不讥诮、永不冷嘲你的父王，也永不把你那位蒙冤的王后母亲挂在嘴上。」

「王后，」玛丽公主说，「我原以为，纵然是在黑暗里，你也不敢向我求这个。」

「我要再求你一次，」王后说，「在你那烛光照在我脸上的房间里。」

「好啊，你求定了，」玛丽公主说。「我们这就到那里去。」

---

===Original===
They went down the dark and winding stair. At the foot the procession of the *coucher de la royne* awaited them, first being two trumpeters in black and gold, then four pikemen with lanthorns, then the marshal of the Queen’s household and five or seven lords, then the Queen’s ladies, the Lady Rochford that slept with her, the Lady Cicely Rochford; the Queen’s tiring-women, leaving a space between them for the Queen and the Lady Mary to walk in, then four young pages in scarlet and with the Queen’s favours in their caps, and then the guard of the Queen’s door, and four pikemen with torches whose light, falling from behind, illumined the path for the Queen’s steps. The trumpeters blew four shrill blasts and then four with their fists in the trumpet mouths to muffle them. The brazen cries wound down the dark corridors, fathoms and fathoms down, to let men know that the Queen had done her prayers and was going to her bed. This great state was especially devised by the King to do honour to the new Queen that he loved better than any he had had. The purpose of it was to let all men know what she did that she might be the more imitated.

But the Queen bade them guide her to the Lady Mary’s door, and in the doorway she dismissed them all, save only her women and her door guard and pikemen who awaited her without, some on stools and some against the wall, ladies and men alike.

The Lady Mary looked into the Queen’s face very close and laughed at her when they were in the fair room and the light of the candles.

“Now you shall say your litany over again,” she sneered; “I will sit me down and listen.” And in her chair at the table, with her face averted, she dug with little stabs into the covering rug the stiletto with which she was wont to mend her pens.

Standing by her, her face fully lit by the many candles that were upon the mantel, the Queen, dressed all in black and with the tail of her hood falling down behind to her feet, went patiently through the list of her prayers—that the Lady Mary should be reconciled with her father, that she should show at first favour to the ambassadors that sued for her hand for the Duke of Orleans, and afterwards give a glad consent to her marriage with the Prince Philip, the Emperor’s son; and then, having been reinstated as a princess of the royal house of England, she should bear herself as such, and no more cry out upon the memory of Katharine of Aragon that had been put away from the King’s side.

===Chinese===
她们走下黑暗而盘旋的楼梯。梯脚下，*coucher de la royne*（王后就寝仪注）的行列正候着她们：走在最前的是两名黑金号衣的号手，继而是四名提着提灯的长枪手，再后是王后府的典礼官与五七位勋贵；然后是王后的女官们——与她同寝的罗奇福德夫人、西西莉·罗奇福德；再后是王后的梳妆侍女们，当中留出空当，给王后与玛丽公主行走；然后是四名猩红衣衫的少年侍从，帽上缀着王后所赐的饰记；再后是王后的门卫，最后是四名执火把的长枪手，火光自身后照来，为王后的脚步照亮路径。号手们吹了四声尖厉的号，又用拳头塞住号口，闷声吹了四下。铜号声沿着黑暗的走廊盘旋而下，一英寻又一英寻地传下去，让众人知道王后已祈祷完毕，正要去就寝。这一套盛大的仪制是国王特意创设的，用以荣耀这位他爱之胜过历来任何一位的新王后。其用意在于让所有世人知晓她的行止，好教人们愈发效法于她。

但王后吩咐他们把她送到玛丽公主的门前；到了门道里，她把众人尽数遣散，只留下她的女官、门卫与长枪手在外面等候，有的坐在小凳上，有的倚着墙壁，女官与男仆一例如此。

进了那间明丽的房间、到了烛光之下，玛丽公主凑得极近地端详王后的脸，冲着她笑。

「现在你把你的连祷再念一遍吧，」她讥笑道，「我且坐下听着。」她在桌旁自己的椅中坐定，脸偏向一旁，用她惯来修削羽毛笔的那柄小匕首，一下一下戳着覆在桌上的绒毯。

王后立在她身旁，壁炉台上众烛把她的脸照得通亮；她通身黑衣，兜帽的垂纱从背后直垂到脚边，耐心地把她的祷愿一条条数过——愿玛丽公主与她的父亲和好；愿她起初善待那些为奥尔良公爵向她求亲的使节，其后欣然应允她与皇帝之子菲利普亲王（Prince Philip）的婚事；然后，既已复为英格兰王室的公主，便当举止如其身份，不再为阿拉贡的凯瑟琳——那位被从国王身边废去的王后——鸣冤叫屈。

===Original===
The Queen spoke these words with a serious patience and a level voice; but when she came to the end of them she stretched out her hand and her voice grew full.

“And oh,” she said, her face being set and earnest in entreaty towards the girl’s back, “if you have any love for the green and fertile land that gave birth both to you and to me—”

“But to me a bastard,” the Lady Mary said.

“If you would have the dishoused saints to return home to their loved pastures; if you would have the Mother of God and of us all to rejoice again in her dowry; if you would see a great multitude of souls, gentle and simple reconducted again towards Heaven—”

“Well, well!” the Lady Mary said; “grovel! grovel! I had thought you would have been shamed thus to crawl upon your belly before me.”

“I would crawl in the dust,” Katharine said. “I would kiss the mire from the shoon of the vilest man there is if in that way I might win for the Church of God—”

“Well, well!” the Lady Mary said.

“You will not let me finish my speech about our Saviour and His mother,” the Queen said. “You are afraid I should move you.”

===Chinese===
王后说这些话时，神情郑重而耐心，语声平缓；但说到末尾，她伸出一只手，声音渐渐饱满起来。

「啊，」她说，那张脸正对着姑娘的背影，恳切而凝重，「倘若你对这片生养了你我二人的青葱沃土还有半分爱恋——」

「于我不过是私生女罢了，」玛丽公主说。

「倘若你愿让流离失所的圣徒们重返他们眷恋的牧场；倘若你愿让天主之母、我们众人之母，重新欢享她的嫁妆；倘若你愿看到贵贱不论的一大群灵魂，重又被引回天国——」

「好呀，好呀！」玛丽公主说。「趴吧！趴吧！我原以为你会羞于这样肚皮贴地、趴在我面前爬呢。」

「我甘愿在尘土里爬，」凯瑟琳说。「我甘愿吻去世上最早贱之人鞋上的泥污，只要能以那方式为上帝的教会赢得——」

「好呀，好呀！」玛丽公主说。

「你不容我把关于我们救主与祂母亲的话说完，」王后说。「你是怕我打动你。」

===Original===
The Lady Mary turned suddenly round upon her in her chair. Her face was pallid, the skin upon her hollowed temples trembled—

“Queen,” she called out, “ye blaspheme when ye say that a few paltry speeches of yours about God and souls will make me fail my mother’s memory and the remembrances of the shames I have had.”

She closed her eyes; she swallowed in her throat and then, starting up, she overset her chair.

“To save souls!” she said. “To save a few craven English souls! What are they to me? Let them burn in the eternal fires! Who among them raised a hand or struck a blow for my mother or me? Let them go shivering to hell.”

“Lady,” the Queen said, “ye know well how many have gone to the stake over conspiracies for you in this realm.”

“Then they are dead and wear the martyr’s crown,” the Lady Mary said. “Let the rest that never aided me, nor struck blow for my mother, go rot in their heresies.”

“But the Church of God!” the Queen said. “The King’s Highness has promised me that upon the hour when you shall swear to do these things he will send the letter that ye wot of to our Father in Rome.”

===Chinese===
玛丽公主在椅中猛地朝她转过身来。她脸色灰白，凹陷的太阳穴上皮肤颤动——

「王后，」她喊道，「汝说，汝那几段关于上帝与灵魂的微末言语，便能教我辜负我母亲的记忆、忘却我受过的种种羞辱——汝这是渎神。」

她闭上眼睛；喉头一哽，随即霍然起身，把椅子掀翻了。

「救灵魂！」她说。「救几个怯懦的英格兰灵魂！他们于我算得了什么？让他们在永火里烧吧！他们中间有谁为我母亲或我抬过一只手、打过一拳？让他们哆哆嗦嗦地下地狱去。」

「公主，」王后说，「这王国里有多少人因你而起的密谋送了命、上了火刑柱，汝知道得很清楚。」

「那么他们已经死了，戴上了殉道者的冠冕，」玛丽公主说。「其余那些从未援助过我、也从未为我母亲打过一拳的，就让他们在自己的异端里烂掉吧。」

「可上帝的教会呢！」王后说。「国王陛下已经应许我：在你起誓行这些事的那一刻，他便把汝所知晓的那封信，发给我们在罗马的圣父。」

===Original===
The Lady Mary laughed aloud—

“Here is a fine woman,” she said. “This is ever the woman’s part to gloss over crimes of their men folk. What say you to the death of Lady Salisbury that died by the block a little since?”

She bent her body and poked her head forward into the Queen’s very face. Katharine stood still before her.

“God knows,” she said. “I might not stay it. There was much false witness—or some of it true—against her. I pray that the King my Lord may atone for it in the peace that shall come.”

“The peace that shall come!” the Lady Mary laughed. “Oh, God, what things we women are when a man rules us. The peace that shall come? By what means shall it have been brought on?”

“I will tell you,” she pursued after a moment. “All this is cogging and lying and feigning and chicaning. And you who are so upright will crawl before me to bring it about. Listen!”

And she closed her eyes the better to calm herself and to collect her thoughts, for she hated to appear moved.

===Chinese===
玛丽公主放声大笑——

「好一位贤德的妇人，」她说。「替自家男人的罪过涂脂抹粉，向来是女人的本分。索尔兹伯里夫人（Lady Salisbury）不久前命丧断头斧下，你对此又有何话说？」

她俯下身子，把头一直探到王后的脸前。凯瑟琳一动不动地立在她面前。

「天主知道，」她说。「我无力拦阻。告她的伪证太多——或者其中几分也是真的。我祈祷我的夫君国王，在那将要来临的和平里为此补赎。」

「那将要来临的和平！」玛丽公主笑道。「哦，天主，男人统治我们的时候，我们女人算是什么东西。将要来临的和平？它将凭什么样的手段被促成？」

「我告诉你，」她过了一会儿又接着说。「这一切全是出千、撒谎、装假、行诈。而你这样端正的人，却要趴在我面前促成它。听着！」

她闭上眼睛，好教自己平静下来、收拢思绪，因为她憎恨显得动情。

===Original===
“I am to feign a friendship to my father. That is a lie that you ask me to do, for I hate him as he were the devil. And why must I do this? To feign a smooth face to the world that his pride may not be humbled. I am to feign to receive the ambassadors of the Duke of Orleans. That is cogging that you ask of me. For it is not intended that ever I shall wed with a prince of the French house. But I must lead them on and on till the Emperor be affrighted lest your King make alliance with the French. What a foul tale! And you lend it your countenance!”

“I would well—” Katharine began.

“Oh, I know, I know,” Mary snickered. “Ye would well be chaste but that it must needs be other with you. It was the thief’s wife said that.

“Listen again,” she pursued, “anon there shall come the Emperor’s men, and there shall be more cogging and chicaning, and honours shall be given me that I may be bought dear, and petitioning that I should be set in the succession to make them eager. And then, perhaps, it shall all be cried off and a Schmalkaldner prince shall send ambassadors—”

“No, before God,” Katharine said.

“Oh, I know my father,” Mary laughed at her. “You will keep him tied to Rome if you can. But you could not save the venerable Lady of Salisbury, nor you shall not save him from trafficking with Schmalkaldners and Lutherans if it shall serve his monstrous passions and his vanities. And if he do not this yet he will do other villainies. And you will cosset him in them—to save his hoggish dignity and buttress up his heavy pride. All this you stand there and ask.”

===Chinese===
「我要假装对我的父亲怀有友爱。你要我行的是一桩谎言，因为我恨他，恨他如同恨魔鬼。而我为什么必须这样做？为了向世人装出一张和顺的脸，好教他的骄矜不至受挫。我要假装殷勤接待奥尔良公爵的使节。你要我做的是出千的勾当。因为根本无意让我嫁与法兰西王室的亲王。可我必须牵着他们一程又一程地往前走，直到皇帝心生恐慌，生怕你的国王与法兰西人结了盟。多么污秽的算计！而你竟给它撑腰！」

「我本愿——」凯瑟琳开口道。

「哦，我知道，我知道，」玛丽嗤笑道。「汝本愿守贞，只是汝身的事注定由不得汝。这话原是那贼婆娘说的。」

「再听着，」她继续道，「不久皇帝的人马便要来了，又会有更多的出千与行诈；要给我尊荣，好教我卖得昂贵；又要请愿立我于继承之位，好教他们心切。然后，或许这一切又会尽数取消，换一位施马尔卡尔登（Schmalkaldner）诸侯遣使前来——」

「不，凭天主起誓，」凯瑟琳说。

「哦，我了解我的父亲，」玛丽冲她笑道。「你若办得到，要把他拴在罗马。可你救不了那位可敬的索尔兹伯里夫人，也救不了他、拦不住他与施马尔卡尔登人和路德派做交易——只要那能伺候他那畸怪的情欲与虚荣。即便这桩他还不做，他也会做别的恶行。而你还要在这些事上娇宠他——为的是保全他那猪一般的体面，撑住他那沉重的傲慢。这一切，你都站在那里一一开口相求。」

===Original===
“In the name of God I ask it,” Katharine said. “There is no other way.”

“Well then,” the Lady Mary said, “you shall ask it many times. I will have you shamed.”

“Day and night I will ask it,” Katharine said.

The Lady Mary sniffed.

“It is very well,” she said. “You are a proud and virtuous piece. I will humble you. It were nothing to my father to crawl on his belly and humble himself and slaver. He would do it with joy, weeping with a feigned penitence, making huge promises, foaming at the mouth with oaths that he repented, calling me his ever loved child—”

She stayed and then added—

“That would cost him nothing. But that you that are his pride, that you should do it who are in yourself proud—that is somewhat to pay oneself with for shamed nights and days despised. If you will have this thing you shall do some praying for it.”

“Even as Jacob served so will I,” Katharine said.

“Seven years!” the Lady Mary mocked at her. “God forbid that I should suffer you for so long. I will get me gone with an Orleans, a Kaiserlik, or a Schmalkaldner leaguer before that. So much comfort I will give you.” She stopped, lifted her head and said, “One knocks!”

They said from the door that a gentleman was come from the Archbishop with a letter to the Queen’s Grace.

===Chinese===
「我奉天主之名求，」凯瑟琳说。「此外再无别路。」

「那么好，」玛丽公主说，「你要一求再求。我要教你蒙羞。」

「日日夜夜我都会求，」凯瑟琳说。

玛丽公主嗤了嗤鼻。

「很好，」她说。「你是一件又骄傲又贞洁的物事。我要贬抑你。教我父亲肚皮贴地爬行、自贬身份、淌着口水乞怜，于他算不得什么。他会欢天喜地地做，淌着假悔改的泪，许下天大的宏愿，口沫四溅地起誓说他已悔悟，唤我做他始终挚爱的孩子——」

她顿了顿，又补上一句——

「那于他毫不足惜。可你——你是他的骄傲——偏偏是你这个自身心高气傲的人要这样做，这才算得一份抵偿，抵得过那些蒙羞的夜、受贱的日。你若要这事成，就须得为它做些祈祷。」

「雅各（Jacob）怎样服事，我也必怎样服事，」凯瑟琳说。

「七年哪！」玛丽公主讥嘲她。「天主也不容我容你这么久。不等那七年，我便要跟一个奥尔良家的、一个皇帝党的（Kaiserlik）、或一个施马尔卡尔登同盟里的人走掉。这点安慰，我便赏给你。」她停住，抬起头说：「有人叩门！」

门外的人回禀：大主教座下来了一位侍从绅士，带一封信呈王后陛下。

### VIII

===Original===
There came in the shaven Lascelles and fell upon his knees, holding up the sheets of the letter he had copied.

The Queen took them from him and laid them upon the great table, being minded later to read them to the Lady Mary, in proof that the King very truly would make his submission to Rome, supposing only that his daughter would make submission to her.

When she turned, Lascelles was still kneeling before the doorway, his eyes upon the ground.

“Why, I thank you,” she said. “Gentleman, you may get you gone back to the Archbishop.”

She was thinking of returning to her duel of patience with the Lady Mary. But looking upon his blond and agreeable features she stayed for a minute.

===Chinese===
剃净了脸的拉塞尔斯走了进来，双膝跪倒，捧呈着他誊抄下的那几页信笺。

王后从他手里接过，搁在大案上，心里想着稍后念给玛丽公主听，以证明确凿：国王是真愿臣服罗马的，只消他的女儿肯向她俯首称臣。

待她转过身来，拉塞尔斯仍跪在门前，双眼望着地面。

「啊，我谢谢你，」她说。「侍从，你自去罢，回大主教那里去。」

她本想着回去继续同玛丽公主那场耐心的较量。可是望着他那金发白皙、和蔼可亲的面容，她又停留了片刻。

===Original===
“I know your face,” she said. “Where have I seen you?”

He looked up at her; his eyes were blue and noticeable, because at times of emotion he was so wide-lidded that the whites showed round the pupils of them.

“Certainly I have seen you,” the Queen said.

“It is a royal gift,” he said, “the memory of faces. I am the Archbishop’s poor gentleman, Lascelles.”

The Queen said—

“Lascelles? Lascelles?” and searched her memory.

“I have a sister, the spit and twin of me,” he answered; “and her name is Mary.”

===Chinese===
「我认得你这张脸，」她说。「我在哪里见过你？」

他抬眼望她；那双眼睛是蓝色的，惹人注目，因为每逢情绪起伏，他的眼睑便睁得极开，眼白在瞳仁四周显露出来。

「我确乎见过你，」王后说。

「认人的记性，」他说，「是一种王者之赋。我不过是大主教手下微贱的侍从，拉塞尔斯。」

王后说——

「拉塞尔斯？拉塞尔斯？」一边搜索着自己的记忆。

「我有一位姊妹，同我一个模子里刻出来似的，宛如孪生，」他答道，「她的名字是玛丽。」

===Original===
The Queen said—

“Ah! ah!” and then, “Your sister was my bedfellow in the maid’s room at my grandmother’s.”

He answered gravely—

“Even so!”

And she—

“Stand up and tell me how your sister fares. I had some kindnesses of her when I was a child. I remember when I had cold feet she would heat a brick in the fire to lay to them, and such tricks. How fares she? Will you not stand up?”

===Chinese===
王后说——

「啊！啊！」随即又道，「你的姊妹，当年在我祖母府上，是侍女房中与我同榻的人。」

他神色庄重地答道——

「正是如此！」

于是她——

「起来罢，告诉我你姊妹过得怎样。我小时候得过她的许多好处。我记得脚冷的时候，她会在火里烧热一块砖来给我焐脚，还有诸般这样的体贴。她过得如何？你还不肯起来么？」

===Original===
“Because she fares very ill I will not stand upon my feet,” he answered.

“Well, you will beg a boon of me,” she said. “If it is for your sister I will do what I may with a good conscience.”

He answered, remaining kneeling, that he would fain see his sister. But she was very poor, having married an esquire called Hall of these parts, and he was dead, leaving her but one little farm where, too, his old father and mother dwelt.

“I will pay for her visit here,” she said; “and she shall have lodging.”

“Safe-conduct she must have too,” he answered; “for none cometh within seven miles of this court without your permit and approval.”

“Well, I will send horses of my own, and men to safeguard her,” the Queen said. “For, sure, I am beholden to her in many little things. I think she sewed the first round gown that ever I had.”

===Chinese===
「正因为她过得极苦，我不愿起身站直，」他答道。

「那么，你这是要向我讨一项恩典了，」她说。「若是为了你姊妹，凡是我凭良心办得到的，无不为力。」

他仍跪着回禀，说他极愿见一见他的姊妹。但她穷得很，嫁了此地一位名叫霍尔的乡绅，乡绅已死，只留给她一座小小的田庄，庄上还住着霍尔的年迈爹娘。

「她来此一行的花费，由我承担，」她说，「还要给她安顿住处。」

「她还须有安全通行的凭照，」他答道，「因为未经您的允准与核准，任何人不得行近这宫廷七英里之内。」

「好，我派我自己的马去，再派人一路护卫她，」王后说。「因为，说真的，我在许多小事上受过她的恩。我想，我平生第一件圆裙，就是她缝的。」

===Original===
He remained kneeling, his eyes still upon the floor.

“We are your very good servants, my sister and I,” he said. “For she did marry one—that Esquire Hall—that was done to death upon the gallows for the old faith’s sake. And it was I that wrote the English of most of this letter to his Holiness, the Archbishop being ill and keeping his bed.”

“Well, you have served me very well, it is true,” the Queen answered. “What would you have of me?”

“Your Highness,” he answered, “I do well love my sister and she me. I would have her given a place here at the Court. I do not ask a great one; not one so high as about your person. For I am sure that you are well attended, and places few there are to spare about you.”

And then, even as he willed it, she bethought her that Margot Poins was to go to a nunnery. That afternoon she had decided that Mary Trelyon, who was her second maid, should become her first, and others be moved up in a rote.

“Why,” she said, “it may be that I shall find her an occupation. I will not have it said—nor yet do it—that I have ever recompensed them that did me favours in the old times, for there are a many that have served well in the Court that then I was outside of, and those it is fitting first to reward. Yet, since, as you say you have writ the English of this letter, that is a very great service to the Republic, and if by rewarding her I may recompense thee, I will think how I may come to do it.”

===Chinese===
他仍旧跪着，双眼依旧望着地板。

「我姊妹与我，都是您万分忠实的仆人，」他说。「因为她嫁的那一个人——那位霍尔乡绅——是为着旧教的缘故，被送上绞架处死的。而写给教皇陛下的这封信，其中大半的英文，是大主教抱恙卧床时由我代笔的。」

「那么，你确乎为我效力甚勤，这是真的，」王后答道。「你要我给你什么？」

「陛下，」他答道，「我真心爱我的姊妹，她也真心爱我。我愿她在这宫廷里得一个位置。我不求显位；不求近侍御前的那等高位。因为我确信您身边的侍奉已然齐备，您左右能空出的位置寥寥无几。」

而这时，恰如他所愿，她想起玛戈·普因斯将要入女修道院。那天下午她已定下：由她的次席侍女玛丽·特雷利昂（Mary Trelyon）升为首席，其余诸人依次递升。

「啊，」她说，「兴许我能为她寻得一份差事。我不愿让人说起——自己也决不做——我曾酬报旧日施恩于我的人；因为有许多人在宫廷里效力甚勤，而我那时还在宫廷之外，理当先赏的是那些人。然而，既然如你所说，这封信的英文出自你的手笔，这于邦国是一桩极大的功勋；倘若酬报了她便算酬报了你，我会思量怎样才能把这事办成。」

===Original===
He stood up upon his feet.

“It may be,” he said, “that my sister is rustic and unsuited. I have not seen her in many years. Therefore, I will not pray too high a place for her, but only that she and I may be near, the one to the other, upon occasions, and that she be housed and fed and clothed.”

“Why, that is very well said,” the Queen answered him. “I will bid my men to make inquiries into her demeanour and behaviour in the place where she bides, and if she is well fitted and modest, she shall have a place about me. If she be too rustic she shall have another place. Get you gone, gentleman, and a good night to ye.”

He bent himself half double, in the then newest courtly way, and still bent, pivoted through the door. The Queen stayed a little while musing.

“Why,” she said, “when I was a little child I fared very ill, if now I think of it; but then it seemed a little thing.”

“Y’had best forget it,” the Lady Mary answered.

===Chinese===
他站直了身子。

「也许，」他说，「我姊妹乡气粗陋，不堪选用。我已多年不曾见她。因此，我不为她求太高的位置，只求她与我彼此间或能够相近相见，只求她有屋住、有饭吃、有衣穿。」

「呵，这话说得很好，」王后答他。「我将吩咐我的人去查访她居留之地的品行举止；她若端庄合宜、谦逊知礼，便让她到我身边当差。她若乡气太重，便另安插一个去处。去罢，侍从，祝汝晚安。」

他按当时最时新的宫廷礼数，把身子深深躬下，几成对折，就这样躬着，侧身旋出门去。王后独自留了片刻，陷入沉思。

「唉，」她说，「如今想来，我做小孩子的时候过得实在很苦；可那时却只当是小事一桩。」

「你顶好忘了它，」玛丽公主答道。

---

===Original===
She was thinking of the maids’ dormitory at her grandmother’s, the old Duchess.

“I am climbed very high,” she said; “but to think—”

She was such a poor man’s child and held of only the littlest account, herding with the maids and the servingmen’s children. At eight by the clock her grandmother locked her and all the maids—at times there were but ten, at times as many as a score—into that great dormitory that was, in fact, nothing but one long attic or grange beneath the bare roof. And sometimes the maids told tales or slept soon, and sometimes their gallants, grooms and others, came climbing through the windows with rope ladders. They would bring pasties and wines and lights, and coarsely they would revel.

“Why,” she said, “I had a gallant myself. He was a musician, but I have forgot his name. Aye, and then there was another, Dearham, I think; but I have heard he is since dead. He may have been my cousin; we were so many in family, I have a little forgot.”

She stood still, searching her memory, with her eyes distant. The Lady Mary surveyed her face with a curious irony.

===Chinese===
她心里想的，是祖母、老诺福克公爵夫人府中侍女们的那间大寝房。

「我攀得极高了，」她说，「可是想想——」

她原是个穷汉的女儿，无足轻重之至，同侍女与仆役的孩子们厮混一处。一到晚钟敲过八点，祖母便把她和全体侍女——有时不过十人，有时多达二十来人——锁进那间大寝房；那其实不过是光秃秃的屋架下一溜长阁楼，或者说一间顶仓。有时侍女们讲讲闲话便早早睡了；有时她们的情郎——马夫之流——攀着绳梯从窗户里爬进来。他们带上馅饼、美酒和灯烛，粗鄙地纵情作乐。

「唉，」她说，「我自己也有过一位情郎。他是个乐师，名字我可忘了。是，后来还有一位，叫迪尔姆（Dearham），我想；可听说他后来死了。他兴许还是我表亲；我们家族人丁太众，我有些记不清了。」

她兀立不动，目光悠远，搜索着记忆。玛丽公主带着一种奇异的讥诮，端详她的脸。

===Original===
“Why, what a simple Queen you are!” she said. “This is something rustic.”

The Queen joined her hands together before her, as if she caught at a clue.

“I do remember me,” she said. “It was a make of a comedy. This Dearham, calling himself my cousin, beat this music musician for calling himself my gallant. Then goes the musicker to my grandam, bidding the old Duchess rise up again one hour after she had sought her bed. So comes my grandam and turns the key in the padlock and looketh in over all the gallimaufry of lights and pasties and revels.

“Why,” she continued. “I think I was beaten upon that occasion, but I could not well tell why. And I was put to sleep in another room. And later came my father home from some war. And he was angry that I had consorted so with false minions, and had me away to his own poor house. And there I had Udal for my Magister and evil fare and many beatings. But this Mary Lascelles was my bedfellow.”

“Why, forget it,” the Lady Mary said again.

“Other teachers would bid me remember it that I might remain humble,” Katharine answered.

“Y’are humble enow and to spare,” the Lady Mary said. “And these are not good memories for such a place as this. Y’had best keep this Mary Lascelles at a great distance.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，你是个多么质朴的王后啊！」她说，「这可真有几分乡气。」

王后双手在身前合拢，仿佛抓住了一条头绪。

「我想起来了，」她说。「那简直成了一出喜剧。这位迪尔姆，自称是我的表亲，把这位自称是我情郎的、弄音乐的乐师打了一顿。于是那乐师便去寻我的老祖母，硬请老公爵夫人在就寝一个钟头之后重新起身。老祖母来了，在挂锁里转动了钥匙，朝那一片灯火、馅饼与狂欢的大杂烩里张望。

「唉，」她接着说，「我想那一回我挨了打，却说不清究竟为的什么。我被挪到另一间房里去睡。后来我父亲从哪场战事里回了家。他恼恨我竟同那些虚妄的轻薄之徒这般厮混，便把我带往他自己清贫的宅子。在那里，尤达尔做了我的先生，我吃着粗恶的饭食，挨了许多顿打。而这位玛丽·拉塞尔斯，便是与我同榻的人。」

「唉，忘了罢，」玛丽公主又说。

「换了别的先生，倒会吩咐我记住它，好教我保持谦卑呢，」凯瑟琳答道。

「你谦卑得已足足有余了，」玛丽公主说。「而且，在这样一处所在，这些算不得什么好回忆。你顶好让这位玛丽·拉塞尔斯离得远远的。」

===Original===
Katharine said—

“No; for I have passed my word.”

“Then reward her very fully,” the Lady Mary commended, and the Queen answered—

“No, for that is against my conscience. What have I to fear now that I be Queen?”

Mary shrugged her squared shoulders.

“Where is your Latin,” she said, “with its *nulla dies felix*—call no day fortunate till it be ended.”

“I will set another text against that,” she said, “and that from holy sayings—that *justus ab aestimatione non timebit*.”

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳说——

「不；因为我已许下了话。」

「那就重重地酬赏她，」玛丽公主进言道。王后答——

「不，那有违我的良心。我如今既已是王后，还有什么可惧怕的？」

玛丽耸了耸她那方正的肩膀。

「你的拉丁文到哪里去了，」她说，「连同那句 *nulla dies felix*——一日未终，莫呼吉日。」

「我要拿另一句经文来抵它，」她说，「而且出自圣言之列——*justus ab aestimatione non timebit*（大意：义人必不惧审判）。」

===Original===
“Well,” Mary answered, “you will make your bed how you will. But I think you would better have learned of these maids how to steer a course than of your Magister and the Signor Plutarchus.”

The Queen did not answer her, save by begging her to read the King’s letter to his Holiness.

“And surely,” she said, “if I had never read in the noble Romans I had never had the trick of tongue to gar the King do so much of what I will.”

“Why, God help you,” her stepdaughter said. “Pray you may never come to repent it.”

===Chinese===
「唉，」玛丽答道，「你的床要怎么铺，悉听尊便。可我想，你与其向你的先生和那位普鲁塔克先生（the Signor Plutarchus）领教，倒不如向那些侍女学学怎样掌舵择路。」

王后没有答她的话，只恳请她读一读国王致教皇陛下的那封信。

「千真万确，」她说，「倘若我从未读过那些高贵的罗马人的著述，我断不会有这套舌辩的本事，哄得国王依我行了这许多事。」

「唉，愿天主垂怜于你，」她的继女说。「但愿你永远不至于为此抱悔。」

## Part II. The Threatened Rift / 第二部 濒临的裂痕

### I

===Original===
In these summer days there was much faring abroad in the broad lands to north and to south of the Pontefract Castle. The sunlight lay across moors and uplands. The King was come with all his many to Newcastle; but no Scots King was there to meet him. So he went farther to northwards. His butchers drove before him herds of cattle that they slew some of each night: their hooves made a broad and beaten way before the King’s horses. Behind came an army of tent men: cooks, servers, and sutlers. For, since they went where new castles were few, at times they must sleep on moorsides, and they had tents all of gold cloth and black, with gilded tent-poles and cords of silk and silver wire. The lords and principal men of those parts came out to meet him with green boughs, and music, and slain deer, and fair wooden kegs filled with milk. But when he was come near to Berwick there was still no Scots King to meet him, and it became manifest that the King’s nephew would fail that tryst. Henry, riding among his people, swore a mighty oath that he would take way even into Edinburgh town and there act as he listed, for he had with him nigh on seven thousand men of all arms and some cannon which he had been minded to display for the instruction of his nephew. But he had, in real truth, little stomach for this feat. For, if he would go into Scotland armed, he must wait till he got together all the men that the Council of the North had under arms. These were scattered over the whole of the Border country, and it must be many days before he had them all there together. And already the summer was well advanced, and if he delayed much longer his return, the after progress from Pontefract to London must draw them to late in the winter. And he was little minded that either Katharine or his son should bear the winter travel. Indeed, he sent a messenger back to Pontefract with orders that the Prince should be sent forthwith with a great guard to Hampton Court, so that he should reach that place before the nights grew cold.

And, having stayed in camp four days near the Scots border﻿—for he loved well to live in a tent, since it re-awoke in him the ardour of his youth and made him think himself not so old a man﻿—he delivered over to the Earl Marshal forty Scots borderers and cattle thieves that had been taken that summer. These men he had meant to have handed, pardoned, to the Scots King when he met him. But the Earl Marshal set up, along the road into Scotland, from where the stone marks the border, a row of forty gallows, all high, but some higher than others; for some of the prisoners were men of condition. And, within sight of a waiting crowd of Scots that had come down to the boundaries of their land to view the King of England, Norfolk hanged on these trees the forty men.

And, laughing over their shoulders at this fine harvest of fruit, gibbering and dangling against the heavens on high, the King and his host rode back into the Border country. It was pleasant to ride in the summer weather, and they hunted and rendered justice by the way, and heard tales of battle that there had been before in the north country.

===Chinese===
在这些夏日里，庞蒂弗拉克特城堡南北两面的广袤土地上，到处是四出巡行的人马。阳光铺洒在荒原与丘陵之上。国王携全部扈从到了纽卡斯尔，却不见苏格兰国王前来相会。于是他继续北行。他的屠夫们赶着成群的牛在御驾之前走，每晚宰杀几头：牛蹄在国王的马队前面，踏出一条宽阔的熟路。后面跟着一支扎营的人马：厨子、侍膳与随军贩夫。因为所到之处新城堡寥寥，他们有时只得露宿荒坡，于是备下了通体金布与黑布的营帐，帐杆镀金，帐索用丝线与银丝绞成。当地的领主与头面人物纷纷出迎，献上青枝、音乐、猎杀的鹿，还有盛满奶的漂亮木桶。然而待他行近贝里克，仍不见苏格兰国王来会，显然国王的那位外甥是要爽约了。亨利骑在臣仆中间，发下一声重大的誓言：他索性要一路打进爱丁堡城去，在那里随心行事——因为他麾下带着将近七千名各色兵种的人马，还有几门炮，原打算陈列一番，教他那位外甥开开眼界。但说实在的，对这番壮举他并无多少胃口。因为若要武装进入苏格兰，他必须等北境枢密院辖下所有在役兵员尽数集结。这些人散布在整个边境地带，要将其聚齐，非许多天不可。而夏季已过大半，倘若他再迟迟不启程回銮，其后自庞蒂弗拉克特往伦敦的巡幸，便要拖他们到深冬了。他不情愿让凯瑟琳或他的儿子经受冬日的跋涉。果然，他遣一名信使回庞蒂弗拉克特传令：着王子即刻起程，以重兵护卫送往汉普顿宫，好教他在夜寒降临之前抵达彼处。

他在苏格兰边境附近的营地里驻了四日——因为他极爱住帐篷，那会重新唤起他年轻时的热忱，令他自觉并不那么老迈——随后，他把那年夏天拿获的四十名苏格兰边民与盗牛贼，交付给王室典礼官。这些人他原打算赦免了，等与苏格兰国王会面时移交给他。但王室典礼官沿着通往苏格兰的大路，从界石标出国界的地方起，竖起一排四十座绞架，座座都高，有几座尤高——因为囚犯中有几位是有身份的人。一大群苏格兰人下到他们国土的边界上，等着瞻仰英格兰国王；诺福克当着这群人的面，把四十个人绞死在这排架子上。

国王一行回身望着这茬丰硕的「果实」——那些身影对着高天呜呜作声、摇曳悬荡——不由朗声而笑，纵马折返边境地带。夏日天时行路甚是惬意，他们一路行猎、听讼断案，听人讲述从前北国发生过的战事。

---

===Original===
But there was one man, Thomas Culpepper, in the town of Edinburgh to whom this return was grievous. He had been in these outlandish parts now for more than nineteen months. The Scots were odious to him, the town was odious; he had no stomach for his food, and such clothes as he had were ragged, for he would wear nothing that had there been woven. He was even a sort of prisoner. For he had been appointed to wait on the King’s Ambassador to the King of Scots, and the last thing that Throckmorton, the notable spy, had done before he had left the Court had been to write to Edinburgh that T. Culpepper, the Queen’s cousin, who was a dangerous man, was to be kept very close and given no leave of absence.

And one thing very much had aided this: for, upon receiving news, or the rumour of news, that his cousin Katharine Howard﻿—he was her mother’s brother’s son﻿—had wedded the King, or had been shown for Queen at Hampton Court, he had suddenly become seized with such a rage that, incontinently, he had run his sword through an old fishwife in the fishmarket where he was who had given him the news, newly come by sea, thinking that because he was an Englishman this marriage of his King might gladden him. The fishwife died among her fish, and Culpepper with his sword fell upon all that were near him in the market, till, his heel slipping upon a haddock, he fell, and was fallen upon by a great many men.

He must stay in jail for this till he had compounded with the old woman’s heirs and had paid for a great many cuts and bruises. And Sir Nicholas Hoby, happening to be in Edinburgh at that time, understood well what ailed Thomas Culpepper, and that he was mad for love of the Queen his cousin﻿—for was it not this Culpepper that had brought her to the court, and, as it was said, had aforetime sold farms to buy her food and gowns when, her father being a poor man, she was well-nigh starving? Therefore Sir Nicholas begged alike the Ambassador and the King of Scots that they would keep this madman clapped up till they were very certain that the fit was off him. And, what with the charges of blood ransom and jailing for nine months, Culpepper had no money at all when at last he was enlarged, but must eat his meals at the Ambassador’s table, so that he could not in any way come away into England till he had written for more money and had earned a further salary. And that again was a matter of many months, and later he spent more in drinking and with Scots women till he persuaded himself that he had forgotten his cousin that was now a Queen. Moreover, it was made clear to him by those about him that it was death to leave his post unpermitted.

===Chinese===
然而在爱丁堡城里，有一个人——托马斯·卡尔佩珀——对这趟回师痛心不已。他滞留在这化外之地，至今已十九个月有余。苏格兰人令他憎厌，这座城令他憎厌；他食不甘味，身上的衣裳褴褛不堪——因为当地织出的东西他一概不肯上身。他甚至形同一名囚徒。因为他被派去随侍驻苏格兰国王处的国王大使，而著名的密探斯罗克莫顿离开宫廷前办的最后一件事，便是致信爱丁堡：王后的表兄托马斯·卡尔佩珀是个危险人物，须严加看管，不得给予离任的许可。

而有一桩事大大促成了这番看管：因为他一得到消息——或者说只是消息的风声——说他的表妹凯瑟琳·霍华德（他乃她母亲兄弟之子）嫁与了国王，或已在汉普顿宫受册立示众为王后，登时被一股狂怒攫住，就在他当时置身其中的鱼市上，一剑捅穿了一个老鱼贩妇——那刚由海路传来的消息正是她说与他听的，她还想着，他既是个英格兰人，国王的这桩婚事总该教他欢喜。老鱼贩妇死在她的鱼摊中间，卡尔佩珀提剑扑向集市上所有靠近他的人，直到脚跟在一条黑线鳕上一滑，摔倒在地，反被许许多多人扑住痛打。

为此他蹲了牢，直到与老妇的继承人私了妥帖、又为许许多多刀伤淤伤付足了赔金，方才出来。尼古拉斯·霍比爵士恰于其时身在爱丁堡，对托马斯·卡尔佩珀害的是什么病了然于胸，知道他是为他表妹王后害了疯狂的相思——可不是么，当年把凯瑟琳带进宫廷的正是这个卡尔佩珀；而且据人说，从前她父亲是个穷人、她几乎饿死之时，他还变卖过田庄，给她买食物和裙裳。于是霍比爵士恳请大使与苏格兰国王双方，把这疯子严严关押，直到十分确信他这阵疯病业已过去。血偿金的重负，加上九个月的牢狱，到终于获释之时，卡尔佩珀已一文不名，只能在大使的餐桌上用饭，以致非得写信再讨些钱来、再挣得一份薪俸，否则断然无法脱身回到英格兰。而这一等又是好几个月；后来他更多的钱花在了饮酒和苏格兰女人身上，直到他终于说服了自己：他已忘掉那位如今贵为王后的表妹。况且，身边的人还向他挑明：未经许可擅离职守，即是死罪。

---

===Original===
But, with the coming of the Court up into the north parts, his impatience grew again, so that he could no longer eat but only drink and fight. It was rumoured that the Queen was riding with the King, and he swore a mighty oath that he would beg of her or of the King leave at last to be gone from that hateful city; and the nearer came the King the more his ardour grew. So that, when the news came that the King was turned back, Culpepper could no longer compound it with himself. He had then a plenty of money, having kept his room for seven days, and the night before that he had won half a barony at dice from a Scots archer. But he had no passport into England; therefore, because he was afraid to ask for one, being certain of a refusal, he blacked his face and hands with coal and then took refuge on a coble, leaving the port of Leith for Durham. He had well bribed the master of this ship to take him as one of his crew. In Durham he stayed neither to wash nor to eat, but, having bought himself a horse, he rode after the King’s progress that was then two days’ journey to the south, and came up with them. He had no wits left more than to ask of the sutlers at the tail of the host where the Queen was. They laughed at this apparition upon a haggard horse, and one of them that was a notable cutpurse took all the gold that he had, only giving him in exchange the news that the Queen was at Pontefract, from which place she had never stirred. With a little silver that he had in another bag he bought himself a provision of food, a store of drink, and a poor Kern to guide him, running at his saddlebow.

He saw neither hills nor valleys, neither heather nor ling: he had no thoughts but only that of finding the Queen his cousin. At times the tears ran down his begrimed face, at times he waved his sword in the air and, spurring his horse, he swore great oaths. How he fared, where he rested, by what roads he went over the hills, that he never knew. Without a doubt the Kern guided him faithfully.

===Chinese===
但随着宫廷车驾北来，他的焦灼又滋长起来，以致饭也吃不下，只剩下饮酒和斗殴。有风声说王后正随国王同行，他便发下一声重誓：定要恳求她或国王，终归恩准他离开那座可憎的城；国王来得越近，他的热望便烧得越旺。于是，国王折返的消息一到，卡尔佩珀再也无法与自己讲和。那时他手头正宽裕——他闭门不出已七日，前一夜又从一名苏格兰弓手处掷骰赢来了半个男爵领的产业。但他没有进入英格兰的通行证；又因自料讨也必遭拒绝，他不敢开口，便用煤烟把脸和手涂黑，躲上一条平底小艇，自利思港（Leith）启程往达勒姆（Durham）去。他重重贿通了那条船的船主，把自己收作船上的一名水手。在达勒姆他不作停留，既不洗濯也不用餐，给自己买了一匹马，便去追赶国王的巡幸队伍——那时队伍在南面两天的路程之外——终于赶上了。他心神耗竭，只剩得一个念头：向队伍尾上的随军贩夫打听王后何在。贩夫们望着这骑一匹瘦马、形同鬼魅的人物哄笑，其中一个出了名的割包惯贼摸走了他所有的金子，只拿一条消息与他交换：王后在庞蒂弗拉克特——她从没有离开过那地方一步。他用另一只袋里剩下的少许银钱，置备了一份口粮、一批酒，又雇了一个穷苦的克恩仆卒（Kern，盖尔轻装步卒）为他引路，那人跟在他鞍旁奔走。

他看不见山，看不见谷，看不见石南，也看不见帚石南：心里再无别的念头，只有寻到他表妹王后这一件事。时而泪水淌下他涂黑的脸，时而他向空挥剑，踢马疾驰，发下一声声重誓。他走的是怎样的路、在哪里歇脚、循着哪些道翻过山岭，他自己一概不知。毫无疑问，那克恩仆卒忠实地引着他走。

---

===Original===
For the Queen, having news that the King was nearly come within a day’s journey, rode out towards the north to meet him. And as she went along the road, she saw, upon a hillside not very far away, a man that sat upon a dead horse, beating it and tugging at its bridle. Beside him stood a countryman, in a garment of furs and pelts, with rawhide boots. She had a great many men and ladies riding behind her, and she had come as far as she was minded to go. So she reined in her horse and sent two prickers to ask who these men were.

And when she heard that this was a traveller, robbed of all his money and insensate, and his poor guide who knew nothing of who he might be, she turned her cavalcade back and commanded that the traveller should be borne to the castle on a litter of boughs and there attended to and comforted until again he could take the road. And she made occasion upon this to comment how ill it was for travellers that the old monasteries were done away with. For in the old time there were seven monasteries between there and Durham, wherein poor travellers might lodge. Then, if a merchant were robbed upon the highways, he could be housed at convenient stages on his road home, and might afterwards send recompense to the good fathers or not as he pleased or was able. Now, there was no harbourage left on all that long road, and, but for the grace of God, that pitiful traveller might have lain there till the ravens picked out his eyes.

And some commended the Queen’s words and actions, and some few, behind their hands, laughed at her for her soft heart. And the more Lutheran sort said that it was God’s mercy that the old monasteries were gone; for they had, they said, been the nests for lowsels, idle wayfarers, palmers, pilgrims, and the like. And, praise God, since that clearance fourteen thousand of these had been hanged by the waysides for sturdy rogues, to the great purging of the land.

===Chinese===
原来，王后得到消息，说国王已近在一日路程之内，便策马向北出迎。行到路上，她望见不很远的山坡上，有个人坐在一匹倒毙的马身上，捶打着它，拽扯着它的辔头。他身旁立着一个乡下人，穿一身毛皮兽裘的衣裳，蹬一双生皮靴子。她身后有大队男子与贵妇随行，而她也已行到自己打算走到的尽头。于是她勒住坐骑，派两名轻骑扈从前去问这两个人是谁。

待她听说这是一个钱财被劫掠一空、神志昏迷的旅人，以及他那可怜的向导——那向导全然不知自己主子可能是何等人物——她便拨转骑队回程，传令用树枝扎的担架把那旅人抬回城堡，在那里好生照料抚慰，直到他能重新上路。她还借这因头议论：旧日修道院尽数废去，于旅人是何等不幸的事。因为旧时从那里到达勒姆之间原有七座修道院，穷苦旅人尽可在其中落脚安身。那时节，商人若在大路上遭了劫，归途中便可一站一站地在沿途得到安顿，事后是否酬报那些好心的神父，全凭他乐意与否、力所能及与否。如今，那条漫漫长路上再无一处宿处；若非天主的恩典，那可怜的旅人只怕要一直躺在那里，直到渡鸦啄出他的双眼。

有人称许王后的这番言语行事，也有少数人掩着嘴，笑她心肠太软。而路德派中更激烈的一流则说，旧修道院之废正是天主的慈悲；因为据他们说，那些修道院向来是无赖闲汉、游惰过客、行脚朝圣者与香客之流的巢穴。而且，赞美天主，自那番廓清以来，此辈已有一万四千人被当作顽健的匪徒吊死在道路旁，大大涤荡了这片土地。

### II

===Original===
In the part of Lincolnshire that is a little to the northeastward of Stamford was a tract of country that had been granted to the monks of St. Radigund’s at Dover by William the Conqueror. These monks had drained this land many centuries before, leaving the superintendence of the work at first to priors by them appointed, and afterwards, when the dykes, ditches, and flood walls were all made, to knights and poor gentlemen, their tenants, who farmed the land and kept up the defences against inundations, paying scot and lot to a bailiff and water-wardens and jurats, just as was done on the Romney marshes by the bailiff and jurats of that level.

And one of these tenants, holding two hundred acres in a simple fee from St. Radigund’s for a hundred and fifty years back, had been always a man of the name of Hall. It was an Edward Hall that Mary Lascelles had married when she was a maid at the Duchess of Norfolk’s. This Edward Hall was then a squire, a little above the condition of a groom, in the Duchess’s service. His parents dwelled still on the farm which was called Neot’s End, because it was in the angle of the great dyke called St. Neot’s and the little sewer where St. Radigund’s land had its boundary stone.

But in the troublesome days of the late Privy Seal, Edward Hall had informed Throckmorton the spy of a conspiracy and rising that was hatching amongst the Radigund’s men a little before the Pilgrimage of Grace, when all the north parts rose. For the Radigund’s men cried out and murmured amongst themselves that if the Priory was done away with there would be an end of their easy and comfortable tenancy. Their rents had been estimated and appointed a great number of years before, when all goods and the produce of the earth were very low priced. And the tenants said that if now the King took their lands to himself or gave them to some great lord, very heavy burdens would be laid upon them and exacted; whereas in some years under easy priors the monks forgot their distant territory, and in bad seasons they took no rents at all. And even under hard and exacting priors the monks could take no more than their rentals, which were so small. They said, too, that the King and Thomas Cromwell would make them into heathen Greeks and turn their children to be Saracens. So these Radigund’s men meditated a rising and conspiracy.

===Chinese===
林肯郡境内、斯坦福德东北少许的地方，有一片土地，是征服者威廉（William the Conqueror）赐给多佛（Dover）圣拉德贡德修道院（St. Radigund’s）修士们的。这些修士早在许多个世纪之前便垦排了这片土地：起初，工程的督理之责交由他们委派的修院院长；待到堤坝、沟渠、防洪墙一应筑成，便转交给他们的佃户——骑士与清寒的士绅——由这些人耕种田地、维护防涝的工事，并向总管、水务员与评事们缴纳例行的捐份，一如罗姆尼沼泽（the Romney marshes）上由那水利区的总管和评事们照章经管的成例。

这些佃户中有一家，以无条件世袭的地权（simple fee）向圣拉德贡德修道院保有两百英亩田地，一百五十年来，当家的始终是姓霍尔（Hall）的人。玛丽·拉塞尔斯当年在诺福克公爵夫人府中做侍女时嫁的，正是这家的一位爱德华·霍尔。那时的爱德华·霍尔在公爵夫人府中当差，是个乡绅侍从，地位比马夫高不了多少。他的父母仍旧住在名叫尼茨恩德（Neot’s End）的田庄上——庄子坐落在大堤圣尼茨（St. Neot’s）与那条小排水沟相夹的角上，沟边立着圣拉德贡德修道院地界的界石。

然而在已故御玺大臣当政的多事之秋，爱德华·霍尔曾向密探斯罗克莫顿告发一桩在圣拉德贡德会众中酝酿的阴谋与暴动——那桩事，就出在北方诸郡全数举事的恩典朝圣之前不久。因为圣拉德贡德会众彼此叫嚷怨叹，说修院一旦裁革，他们安逸舒适的租佃便到了头。他们的租额是许多年前估定核派的，那时无论百物还是田里的出产，价钱都贱得很。佃户们说，倘若如今国王把这些地收归己有、或转赐哪位大勋贵，沉重的负担便会压到他们头上、横加勒索；而遇上宽厚的院长，有些年头修士们索性忘了这片边远的领地，灾荒年景更分文租子不取。即便在苛刻诛求的院长治下，修士们所能征取的，也不能逾出租册所载——那租额本就微薄。他们还说，国王和托马斯·克伦威尔要把他们变成异教的希腊人，把他们的儿女变成萨拉森人（Saracens）。于是这些圣拉德贡德会众便密谋起一场暴动与阴谋。

===Original===
But, because Edward Hall informed Throckmorton of what was agate, a posse was sent into that country, and most of the men were hanged and their lands all taken from them. Those that survived from the jailing betook themselves to the road, and became sturdy beggars, so that many of them too came to the gallows tree.

Most of the land was granted to the Sieur Throckmorton with the abbey’s buildings and tithe barns. But the Halls’ farm and another of near three hundred acres were granted to Edward Hall. Then it was that Edward Hall could marry and take his wife, Mary Lascelles, down into Lincolnshire to Neot’s End. But when the Pilgrimage of Grace came, and the great risings all over Lincolnshire, very early the rioters came to Neot’s End, and they burned the farm and the byres, they killed all the beasts or drove them off, they trampled down the corn and laid waste the flax fields. And, between two willow trees along the great dyke, they set a pole, and from it they hanged Edward Hall over the waters, so that he dried and was cured like a ham in the smoke from his own stacks.

Then Mary Lascelles’ case was a very miserable one; for she had to fend for the aged father and bedridden mother of Edward Hall, and there were no beasts left but only a few geese and ducks that the rebels could not lay their hands on. And the only home that they had was the farmhouse that was upon Edward Hall’s other farm, and that they had let fall nearly into ruin. And for a long time no men would work for her.

===Chinese===
然而，因为爱德华·霍尔把正在酝酿的事报给了斯罗克莫顿，一支缉捕队开进了那片乡野，人大多被绞死，土地尽数夺走。那些下狱留得性命的流落道途，成了身强力壮的游丐，于是他们中不少人终究也上了绞架。

大部分土地，连同修道院的房舍与什一税谷仓，都封给了斯罗克莫顿老爷（the Sieur Throckmorton）。但霍尔家的田庄和另一处近三百英亩的地，封给了爱德华·霍尔。爱德华·霍尔这才娶得起亲，把妻子玛丽·拉塞尔斯接回林肯郡的尼茨恩德。然而恩典朝圣事起，林肯郡遍地举乱，乱民很早便打到尼茨恩德：他们烧了田庄和牛棚，牲畜或杀或赶，踩倒了庄稼，毁尽了亚麻田。又在大堤边两株柳树之间竖起一根长杆，把爱德华·霍尔吊在水面上空，让他像一只火腿那样，在自家谷堆飘出的烟里慢慢风干腌熟。

这一来，玛丽·拉塞尔斯的处境凄惨已极：她得养活爱德华·霍尔年迈的父亲和卧床不起的母亲；牲畜一头不剩，只剩几只叛党未能下手的鹅和鸭。一家人唯一的栖身之处，是爱德华·霍尔另一处田庄上的农舍，早已任其破败，几乎塌尽。好长一段日子，没有一个汉子肯替她做工。

---

===Original===
But at last, after the rebellion was pitifully ended, a few hinds came to her, and she made a shift. And it was better still after Privy Seal fell, for then came Throckmorton the spy into his lands, and he brought with him carpenters and masons and joiners to make his house fair, and some of these men he lent to Mary Hall. But it had been prophesied by a wise woman in those parts that no land that had been taken from the monks would prosper. And, because all the jurats, bailiffs, and water-wardens had been hanged either on the one part or the other and no more had been appointed, at about that time the sewers began to clog up, the lands to swamp, murrain and fluke to strike the beasts and the sheep, and night mists to blight the grain and the fruit blossoms. So that even Throckmorton had little good of his wealth and lands.

Thus one morning to Mary Hall, who stood before her door feeding her geese and ducks, there came a little boy running to say that men-at-arms stood on the other side of the dyke that was very swollen and grey and broad. And they shouted that they came from the Queen’s Highness, and would have a boat sent to ferry them over.

The colour came into Mary Hall’s pale face, for even there she had heard that her former bedfellow was come to be Queen. And at times even she had thought to write to the Queen to help her in her misery. But always she had been afraid, because she thought that the Queen might remember her only as one that had wronged her childish innocence. For she remembered that the maids’ dormitory at the old Duchess’s had been no cloister of pure nuns. So that, at best, she was afraid, and she sent her yard-worker and a shepherd a great way round to fetch the larger boat of two to ferry over the Queen’s men. Then she went indoors to redd up the houseplace and to attire herself.

===Chinese===
但终于，在那场叛乱凄凄惨惨收场之后，来了几个雇农，她便勉力撑持度日。御玺大臣倒台之后光景又好些，因为密探斯罗克莫顿来到自己的领地上，带着木匠、石匠、细木匠替他修整宅邸，还把其中一些人手借给了玛丽·霍尔（Mary Hall，即婚后改姓的玛丽·拉塞尔斯）。但那一带曾有一位智慧老妇下过谶语：凡从修士手里夺来的土地，必不得兴旺。又因为评事、总管、水务员们在两党相争中先后都上了绞架，再没有人补任，大约从那时起，沟渠渐渐淤塞，田地渐渐沦为泽国，畜疫与肝蛭侵击牛羊，夜雾摧坏谷粒和果树的花。连斯罗克莫顿本人，也没能从他的财富和土地上得到多少好处。

就这样，一天早晨，玛丽·霍尔正立在门前喂鹅喂鸭，一个小男孩跑来说：堤对岸站着些带兵器的人——堤间涨起的水又灰又宽。他们高喊，自己是王后陛下驾前差来的，要她遣一条船渡他们过水。

玛丽·霍尔苍白的脸上泛起了血色，因为即便在那等偏僻之地，她也听说了昔日同榻的姑娘竟做了王后。她甚至几番想写信给王后，求她解救自己的困厄。可她始终害怕，因为她想，王后若还记得她，恐怕只会记得她是玷污了她童稚清白的人。因为她记得，老公爵夫人府上侍女的寝阁，绝不是贞洁修女的净室。于是她到底还是怕，便打发场院工和一个牧羊人绕大老远的路，去取两条船里较大的那条，渡王后的人过水。随后她进屋去收拾堂屋，更衣打扮。

===Original===
To the old farmstead, that was made of wood hung over here and there with tilework with a base of bricks, she had added a houseplace for the old folk to sit all day. It was built of wattles that had had clay cast over them, and was whitened on the outside and thatched nearly down to the ground like any squatter’s hut; it had cupboards of wood nearly all round it, and beneath the cupboards were lockers worn smooth with men sitting upon them, after the Dutch fashion—for there in Lincolnshire they had much traffic with the Dutch. There was a great table made of one slab of a huge oak from near Boston. Here they all ate. And above the ingle was another slab of oak from the same tree. Her little old stepmother sat in a stuff chair covered with a sheepskin; she sat there night and day, shivering with the shaking palsy. At times she let out of her an eldritch shriek, very like the call of a hedgehog; but she never spoke, and she was fed with a spoon by a little misbegotten son of Edward Hall’s. The old stepfather sat always opposite her; he had no use of his legs, and his head was always stiffly screwed round towards the door as if he were peering, but that was the rheumatism. To atone for his wife’s dumbness, he chattered incessantly whenever anyone was on that floor; but because he spoke always in Lincolnshire, Mary Hall could scarce understand him, and indeed she had long ceased to listen. He spoke of forgotten floods and ploughings, ancient fairs, the boundaries of fields long since flooded over, of a visit to Boston that King Edward IV had made, and of how he, for his fair speech and old lineage, had been chosen of all the Radigund’s men to present into the King’s hands three silver horseshoes. Behind his back was a great dresser with railed shelves, having upon them a little pewter ware and many wooden bowls for the hinds’ feeding. A door on the right side, painted black, went down into the cellar beneath the old house. Another door, of bars of iron with huge locks from the old monastery, went into the old house where slept the maids and the hinds. This was always open by day but locked in the dark hours. For the hinds were accounted brutish lumps that went savage at night, like wild beasts, so that, if they spared the master’s throat, which was unlikely, it was certain that they would little spare the salted meat, the dried fish, the mead, metheglin, and cider that their poor cellar afforded. The floor was of stamped clay, wet and sweating but covered with rushes, so that the place had a mouldering smell. Behind the heavy door there were huge bolts and crossbars against robbers: the raftered ceiling was so low that it touched her hair when she walked across the floor. The windows had no glass but were filled with a thin reddish sheepskin like parchment. Before the stairway was a wicket gate to keep the dogs—of whom there were many, large and fierce, to protect them alike from robbers and the hinds—to keep the dogs from going into the upper room.

Each time that Mary Hall came into this home of hers her heart sank lower; for each day the corner posts gave sideways a little more, the cupboard bulged, the doors were loth to close or open. And more and more the fields outside were inundated, the lands grew sour, the sheep would not eat or died of the fluke.

“And surely,” she would cry out at times, “God created me for other guesswork than this!”

===Chinese===
那座老农舍是木构的，东一处西一处披挂着瓦，底下衬着砖基；她在旁边添盖了一间堂屋，好让老人家们整日安坐。堂屋编枝为壁，外敷泥土，外面刷得雪白，茅草几乎苫到地皮，同垦荒汉的窝棚没有两样；四壁几乎绕了一圈全是木橱，橱下设着坐柜，柜面教常坐的人磨得溜光——此乃荷兰式样，因为林肯郡那地方同荷兰人往来极多。屋里有一张巨桌，用的是波士顿（Boston）附近一株大橡树的整块板材，一家人都在这里用饭；炉膛上方，另悬着同一株橡树的又一块板材。她那瘦小年迈的继婆母坐在一把蒙着羊皮的绒椅里，日夜坐在那里，因震颤麻痹症浑身发抖；她时不时发出一声凄厉怪异的尖叫，活像刺猬的叫唤，却从不说话，由爱德华·霍尔的一个矮小畸陋的私生子用勺子喂食。老继公公总坐在她对面；他的两腿不听使唤，脑袋却永远僵直地拧向门口，仿佛在张望，其实那不过是风湿作祟。为了补上老伴的哑，只要这层屋里有个人影，他便絮叨个没完；可他说的永远是林肯郡土话，玛丽·霍尔几乎听不懂，说实话，她也早已不去听了。他讲那些被遗忘的洪水与耕作、古老的集市、早已没入水底的田界，讲爱德华四世到波士顿的那一回巡幸，讲自己如何因为口舌便给、家世古老，被圣拉德贡德会众众人推举出来，把三只银马掌捧到国王手里。他背后立着一座带围栏搁板的大碗橱，搁板上摆着少许白镴器皿和许多供雇农吃饭的木碗。右手一扇漆成黑色的门，下去通到老宅底下的地窖。另一扇门由铁条做成，挂着从老修道院拆来的大锁，通进老宅，丫头们和雇农们睡在里面。这门白日总敞着，天黑便落锁。因为雇农们被看作畜生般的莽汉，一到夜里便野性发作，如同野兽；所以哪怕他们肯饶过主人的喉咙——这多半靠不住——那寒碜地窖里的腌肉、干鱼、蜂蜜酒、蜜白酒和苹果酒，他们断然是不会放过的。地面是夯实的黏土，湿漉漉地渗着潮气，上头铺了灯芯草，满屋一股霉烂气味。厚重的门后是防贼的粗大门闩与横杠；椽木的顶棚低得她走过屋地时头发便扫着它。窗上没有玻璃，绷着薄薄的发红羊皮，像羊皮纸似的。楼梯口立着一道小栅门，为的是拦狗——家里狗多，又大又凶，防强盗也防雇农——不让它们上楼进屋。

玛丽·霍尔每回走进自己这个家，心便再往下沉一分：屋角的柱子一日比一日歪斜，木橱鼓胀，门懒怠得不肯关也不肯开。屋外的田地越淹越广，土性越种越酸，羊不肯吃食，或是死于肝蛭。

「千真万确，」她有时会失声喊道，「上帝造我，原为的是另一种营生，断不是这般光景！」

---

===Original===
At nights she was afraid, and shivered at the thought of the fens and the black and trackless worlds all round her; and the ravens croaked, nighthawks screamed, the dog-foxes cried out, and the flames danced over the swampy grounds. Her mirror was broken on the night that they hanged her husband: she had never had another but the water in her buckets, so that she could not tell whether she had much aged or whether she were still brown-haired and pink-cheeked, and she had forgotten how to laugh, and was sure that there were crow’s-feet about her eyelids.

Her best gown was all damp and mouldy in the attic that was her bower. She made it meet as best she could, and indeed she had had so little fat living, sitting at the head of her table with a whip for unruly hinds and louts before her—so little fat living that she could well get into her wedding-gown of yellow cramosyn. She smoothed her hair back into her cord hood that for so long had not come out of its press. She washed her face in a bucket of water: that and the press and her bed with grey woollen curtains were all the furnishing her room had. The straw of the roof caught in her hood when she moved, and she heard her old father-in-law cackling to the serving-maids through the cracks of the floor.

When she came down there were approaching, across the field before the door, six men in scarlet and one in black, having all the six halberds and swords, and one a little banner, but the man in black had a sword only. Their horses were tethered in a clump on the farther side of the dyke. Within the room the serving-maids were throwing knives and pewter dishes with a great din on to the table slab. They dropped drinking-horns and the saltcellar itself all of a heap into the rushes. The grandfather was cackling from his chair; a hen and its chickens ran screaming between the maids’ feet. Then Lascelles came in at the doorway.

===Chinese===
一到夜里她就害怕，想到四周的沼泽和四面八方黑沉沉、无径可循的天地，便浑身发抖；渡鸦嘎嘎地叫，夜鹰尖啸，公狐狸哀嗥，鬼火在湿漉漉的沼地上明灭起舞。她丈夫被绞死的那一夜，她的镜子碎了：此后再不曾有第二面，只有桶里的水给她照影，于是她无从知道自己究竟老了多少，还是依旧棕发粉颊；她已忘了怎么笑，却确信眼角早爬满了鱼尾纹。

她那条最好的裙袍，在充作闺阁的阁楼里潮得发了霉。她尽力把它拾掇齐整；说真的，她素日的饮食少荤缺油——坐在自家桌首，面前横着对付不驯的雇农粗汉的鞭子——油水这样少，竟致那条黄绛色的婚裙她依然穿得进去。她把头发向后抿平，塞进那顶久不曾出过衣橱的粗线软帽。她用一桶水洗了脸：那桶水、那口衣橱，加上挂着灰色呢帷的床，便是她房中全部的陈设。她一动，屋顶的茅草便勾住她的帽子；她听见老公公隔着地板的缝隙，正同丫头们咯咯地说笑。

她下楼出来时，六名猩红衣的人和一名黑衣人正穿过门前的田野走近前来：六个人个个持戟佩剑，其中一个还擎着一面小旗，唯独那黑衣人只佩一柄剑。他们的马成群拴在堤的远岸。屋里，丫头们正叮叮哐哐把刀子和白镴盘子撂上桌板，饮酒的角杯连盐罐一起，一股脑儿丢进灯芯草里。老公公在椅上咯咯地笑；一只母鸡领着小鸡，在丫头们脚边尖叫着乱窜。随后，拉塞尔斯走进了门道。

### III

===Original===
The Sieur Lascelles looked round him in that dim cave.
“Ho!” he said, “this place stinks,” and he pulled from his pocket a dried and shrivelled orange-peel purse stuffed with cloves and ginger. “Ho!” he said to the cornet that was come behind him with the Queen’s horsemen. “Come not in here. This will breed a plague amongst your men!” and he added—
“Did I not tell you my sister was ill-housed?”
“Well, I was not prepared against this,” the cornet said. He was a man with a grizzling beard that had little patience away from the Court, where he had a bottle that he loved and a crony or two that he played all day at chequers with, except when the Queen rode out; then he was of her train. He did not come over the sill, but spoke sharply to his men.
“Ungird not here,” he said. “We will go farther.” For some of them were for setting their pikes against the mud wall and casting their swords and heavy bottle-belts on to the table before the door. The old man in the armchair began suddenly to prattle to them all—of a horse-thief that had been dismembered and then hanged in pieces thirty years before. The cornet looked at him for a moment and said—
“Sir, you are this woman’s father-in-law, I do think. Have you aught to report against her?” He bent in at the door, holding his nose. The old man babbled of one Pease-Cod Noll that had no history to speak of but a swivel eye.

===Chinese===
拉塞尔斯老爷环顾这幽暗如穴的屋子。
「嗬！」他说，「这地方臭气熏天。」说着他从口袋里掏出一只干瘪皱缩的橘皮香囊，里头塞满了丁香与生姜。「嗬！」他对跟在身后、与王后骑兵同来的那名旗官（cornet）说，「休要进来。这要在你部下中间养出瘟疫来的！」他又补上一句——
「我不是早说过，我妹妹住的地方有多不堪么？」
「唉，这光景倒出乎我的意料，」旗官说。他是个灰白胡须的汉子，一离开宫廷便没什么耐性；在宫里，他有心爱的一瓶酒，还有一两个能整日陪他下棋的老伙计——除非王后骑马出行，那时他便在她扈从之列。他没跨过门槛，只厉声吩咐手下。
「不许在这儿解卸行装，」他说，「我们再往前头去。」原来有几个人正要把长枪靠上泥墙，把佩剑和沉甸甸的挂酒瓶腰带扔到门前那张桌上。扶手椅里的老头儿忽然对着众人絮叨起来——讲一个马贼，三十年前先被肢解，然后一块一块吊死。旗官打量了他片刻，说道——
「老丈，我想您是这位妇人的公公吧。您可有要告发她的言语？」他捏着鼻子，在门口俯身探问。老头儿却咿咿呀呀，说起一个叫「豆荚诺尔」（Pease-Cod Noll）的人——那人除了一只斜眼，说不上有什么事迹可讲。

===Original===
“Well,” the grizzled cornet said, “I shall get little sense here.” He turned upon Mary Hall.
“Mistress,” he said, “I have a letter here from the Queen’s High Grace,” and, whilst he fumbled in his belt to find a little wallet that held the letter, he spoke on: “But I misdoubt you cannot read. Therefore I shall tell you the Queen’s High Grace commandeth you to come into her service—or not, as the report of your character shall be. But at any rate you shall come to the castle.”
Mary Hall could find no words for men of condition, so long she had been out of the places where such are found. She swallowed in her throat and held her breast over her heart.
“Where is the village here?” the cornet said, “or what justice is there that can write you a character under his seal?”
She made out to say that there was no village, all the neighbourhood having been hanged. A half-mile from there there was the house of Sir Nicholas Throckmorton, a justice. From the house-end he might see it, or he might have a hind to guide him. But he would have no guide; he would have no man nor maid nor child to go from there to the justice’s house. He set one soldier to guard the back door and one the front, that none came out nor went beyond the dyke-end.
“Neither shall you go, Sir Lascelles,” he said.

===Chinese===
「罢了，」灰髯旗官说，「在这儿问不出什么名堂。」他转向玛丽·霍尔（Mary Hall）。
「夫人，」他说，「我这里有王后陛下的一封信。」他一面在腰带上摸索着找那只装信的小皮夹，一面往下说：「不过我料想您不识字。所以我念给您听：王后陛下命您入她帐下听用——或是不用，全凭对您品行文书的呈报而定。但无论如何，您总须先随我们到城堡里去。」
玛丽·霍尔一时找不出话来应付体面人——她离开有这等人物的地方已经太久了。她喉头一哽，一只手按住胸口的心。
「这里的村落在哪儿？」旗官问，「又有什么治安官，能盖着印鉴给你写品行文书？」
她勉力说出：这里没有村落，左近一带的乡邻都被绞死了。半英里之外是尼古拉斯·斯罗克莫顿爵士（Sir Nicholas Throckmorton）的宅子，他是位治安官；从这屋子的山墙尽头便望得见它，再不然可以叫个雇工引路。可他不要向导；他不许任何男人、女人或孩子从这里往治安官家去。他派了一名士兵守后门，一名守前门，任何人不许出来，也不许走出沟渠的尽头。
「拉塞尔斯爵士，您也不能去。」他说。

===Original===
“Well, give me leave with my sister to walk this knoll,” Lascelles said good-humouredly. “We shall not corrupt the grass blades to bear false witness of my sister’s chastity.”
“Ay, you may walk upon this mound,” the cornet answered. Having got out the packet of the Queen’s letter, he girded up his belt again.
“You will get you ready to ride with me,” he said to Mary Hall. “For I will not be in these marshes after nightfall, but will sleep at Shrimpton Inn.”
He looked around him and added—
“I will have three of your geese to take with us,” he said. “Kill me them presently.”
Lascelles looked after him as he strode away round the house with the long paces of a stiff horseman.

===Chinese===
「那么，请许我带着妹妹到这土丘上走走，」拉塞尔斯好脾气地说。「我们总不至于教这些草叶片儿作伪证，毁我妹妹的清白名声。」
「行，你们可以在土丘上走走，」旗官答道。他取出装着王后信函的小包，重新束紧了腰带。
「你去收拾停当，随我骑行，」他对玛丽·霍尔说。「因为入夜之后我不愿待在这片泽地里，我要宿在施林普顿客栈（Shrimpton Inn）。」
他环顾四周，又补上一句——
「我要你三只鹅带走，」他说。「这便给我宰了。」
旗官迈着僵直骑手的大步绕屋而去，拉塞尔斯目送他的背影。

===Original===
“Before God,” he laughed, “that is one way to have information about a quean. Now are we prisoners whilst he inquires after your character.”
“Oh, alack!” Mary Hall said, and she cast up her hands.
“Well, we are prisoners till he come again,” her brother said good-humouredly. “But this is a foul hole. Come out into the sunlight.”
She said—
“If you are with them, they cannot come to take me prisoner.”
He looked her full in the eyes with his own that twinkled inscrutably. He said very slowly—

===Chinese===
「上帝在上，」他笑道，「这倒是打听一个轻狂婆娘底细的一个法子。如今咱们成了囚徒，只等他去查你的品行。」
「唉，苦也！」玛丽·霍尔说着，双手向天一扬。
「罢了，在他回来之前，咱们就是囚徒，」她哥哥好脾气地说。「不过这可是个腌臜洞。出来晒晒太阳吧。」
她说——
「有你同他们在一起，他们便不能来拿我当囚徒了。」
他直直望进她的眼睛，自己的双目闪着不可测的光。他说得极慢——

===Original===
“Were your mar-locks and prinking-prankings so very evil at the old Duchess’s?”
She grew white: she shrank away as if he had threatened her with his fist.
“The Queen’s Highness was such a child,” she said. “She cannot remember. I have lived very godly since.”
“I will do what I can to save you,” he said. “Let me hear about it, as, being prisoners, we may never come off.”
“You!” she cried out. “You who stole my wedding portion!”

===Chinese===
「你在老公爵夫人府里那些轻狂把戏、浪荡勾当，果真就有那等见不得人么？」
她脸白了：她直往后缩，仿佛他抡拳威胁了她一般。
「王后陛下那时还是个孩子，」她说，「她记不得的。打那以后，我过日子向来虔敬端正。」
「我必尽我所能救你，」他说。「讲给我听吧——你我既成了囚徒之身，兴许永远脱不了身呢。」
「你！」她嚷起来。「你这偷了我嫁妆的人！」

===Original===
He laughed deviously.
“Why, I have laid it up so well for you that you may wed a knight now if you do my bidding. I was ever against your wedding Hall.”
“You lie!” she said. “You gar’d me do it.”
The maids were peeping out of the cellar, whither they had fled.
“Come upon the grass,” he said. “I will not be heard to say more than this: that you and I stand and fall together like good sister and goodly brother.”
Their faces differed only in that hers was afraid and his smiling as he thought of new lies to tell her. Her face in her hood, pale beneath its weathering, approached the colour of his that shewed the pink and white of indoors. She came very slowly near him, for she was dazed. But when she was almost at the sill he caught her hand and drew it beneath his elbow.

===Chinese===
他拐着弯儿笑了。
「嘿，我替你把那份嫁妆存得妥妥帖帖，如今你只要听我的吩咐，连嫁个爵士也尽够了。你嫁霍尔这件事，我本来就是一向反对的。」
「你胡说！」她说。「是你要我嫁的。」
家里的女仆们先前逃进了地窖，这会儿正从窖口向外窥望。
「到草地上来，」他说。「我不肯让人听见我多说一句话，只这一句：你我存亡与共，好比一对好妹妹、好兄长。」
兄妹俩的脸唯一的差别，是一张惶恐、一张含笑——他笑着，心里正盘算要对她说的新谎话。她兜在头巾里的那张脸，风霜色下透出苍白，颜色竟渐渐赶得上他那养在深室的红润白皙。她恍恍惚惚，挨近他挨得极慢。可待她快到门槛时，他一把攥住她的手，塞进自己臂弯底下。

===Original===
“Tell me truly,” she said, “shall I see the Court or a prison? … But you cannot speak truth, nor ever could when we were tiny twins. God help me: last Sunday I had the mind to wed my yardman. I would become such a liar as thou to come away from here.”
“Sister,” he said, “this I tell you most truly: that this shall fall out according as you obey me and inform me”; and, because he was a little the taller, he leaned over her as they walked away together.

===Chinese===
「跟我说句真话，」她说，「我此去见的是宫廷，还是牢狱？……可你说不了真话，打我们还是小小一对双生儿的时候起便是如此。天主可怜我：上个礼拜日，我都动了念头要嫁给我家的长工。为了能离开这里，我都甘愿变得同你这谎话精一样。」
「妹妹，」他说，「这一句我说得最真：事情的结局如何，全看你顺不顺我、肯不肯把实情告诉我。」因他身材略高些，两人一同走开时，他朝她微微俯着身。

---

===Original===
On the fourth day from then they were come to the great wood that is to south and east of the castle of Pontefract. Here Lascelles, who had ridden much with his sister, forsook her and went ahead of the slow and heavy horses of that troop of men. The road was broadened out to forty yards of green turf between the trees, for this was a precaution against ambushes of robbers. Across the road, after he had ridden alone for an hour and a half, there was a guard of four men placed. And here, whilst he searched for his pass to come within the limits of the Court, he asked what news, and where the King was.
It was told him that the King lay still at the Fivefold Vents, two days’ progress from the castle, and as it chanced that a verderer’s pricker came out of the wood where he had been to mark where the deer lay for tomorrow’s killing, Lascelles bade this man come along with him for a guide.
“Sir, ye cannot miss the way,” the pricker said surlily. “I have my deer to watch.”
“I will have you to guide me,” Lascelles said, “for I little know these parts.”
“Well,” the pricker answered him, “it is true that I have not often seen you ride a-hawking.”
Whilst they went along the straight road, Lascelles, who unloosened the woodman’s tongue with a great drink of sherry-sack, learned that it was said that only very unwillingly did the King lie so long at the Fivefold Vents. For on the morrow there was to be driven by, up there, a great herd of moor stags and maybe a wolf or two. The King would be home with his wife, it was reported, but the younger lords had been so importunate with him to stay and abide this gallant chase and great slaughter that, they having ridden loyally with him, he had yielded to their prayers and stayed there—twenty-four hours, it was said.

===Chinese===
自那日起第四天，他们到了庞蒂弗拉克特城堡东南面的大林子里。拉塞尔斯一路多与妹妹同行，到了这里便撇下她，赶到那队人马的前头——那些马匹迟缓而沉重。路面在林间展宽成四十码的绿草带，这是防备盗贼埋伏的措置。他独自骑行了一个半钟头之后，道路对面设着四名哨卫。就在这里，他一面翻找进入王室行在地界的通行证，一面打听消息，问国王在哪里。
有人告诉他，国王仍驻跸在五岔风口（Fivefold Vents），离城堡还有两日行程。恰巧一名猎苑官手下的骑巡手（verderer’s pricker）从林子里出来——他刚去探看明天要宰的鹿群卧在何处——拉塞尔斯便叫这人随他同行，权充向导。
「老爷，这条路您丢不了，」骑巡手没好气地说。「俺还有俺的鹿要看管。」
「我要你给我引路，」拉塞尔斯说，「这一带我实在生疏。」
「也罢，」骑巡手答道，「俺不常见您出来放鹰行猎，这倒是不假。」
一路走去，拉塞尔斯用一大口雪利萨克酒（sherry-sack）撬开了这林中人的话匣子，得知据说国王极不情愿在五岔风口久驻。因为明日那边要围赶一大群荒原雄鹿经过，兴许还有一两匹狼。据报国王本要回王后身边去，无奈一班年轻勋贵苦苦缠着他留下，等这场豪迈的围猎与盛大屠宰——他们既忠心随驾至此，他便依了他们的恳求留了下来——据说留上二十四小时。

===Original===
“Why, you know a great deal,” Lascelles answered.
“We who stand and wait had needs have knowledge,” the woodman said, “for we have little else.”
“Aye, ’tis a hard service,” Lascelles said. “Did you see the Queen’s Highness o’ Thursday week borrow a handkerchief of Sir Roger Pelham to lure her falcon back?”
“That did not I,” the woodman answered, “for o’ Thursday week it was a frost and the Queen rode not out.”
“Well, it was o’ Saturday,” Lascelles said.
“Nor was it yet o’ Saturday,” the woodman cried; “I will swear it. For o’ Saturday the Queen’s Highness shot with the bow, and Sir Roger Pelham, as all men know, fell with his horse on Friday, and lies up still.”

===Chinese===
「嘿，你知道的倒不少。」拉塞尔斯答道。
「俺们这些站着干等的人，不多知道些事儿可不行，」林中人说，「因为俺们旁的什么都缺。」
「是啊，这是桩苦差，」拉塞尔斯说。「上礼拜四，你可曾见王后陛下向罗杰·佩勒姆爵士（Sir Roger Pelham）借手帕，好把她放的隼引回来？」
「俺没见着，」林中人答道，「因为上礼拜四下了霜，王后没有出巡。」
「那么，是上礼拜六。」拉塞尔斯说。
「上礼拜六也没有，」林中人嚷道，「俺敢起誓。因为上礼拜六王后陛下是拉弓射箭的，而罗杰·佩勒姆爵士，人人皆知，礼拜五连人带马摔了，至今还卧床不起。」

===Original===
“Then it was Sir Nicholas Rochford,” Lascelles persisted.
“Sir,” the woodman said, “you have a very wrong tale, and patent it is that little you ride a-hunting.”
“Well, I mind my book,” Lascelles said. “But wherefore?”
“Sir,” the woodman answered, “it is thus: The Queen when she rides a-hawking has always behind her her page Toussaint, a little boy. And this little boy holdeth ever the separate lures for each hawk that the Queen setteth up. And the falcon or hawk or genette or tiercel having stooped, the Queen will call upon that eyass for the lure appropriated to each bird as it chances. And very carefully the Queen’s Highness observeth the laws of the chase, of venery and hawking. For the which I honour her.”
Lascelles said, “Well, well!”
“As for the borrowing of a handkerchief,” the woodman pursued, “that is a very idle tale. For, let me tell you, a lady might borrow a jewelled feather or a scarlet pouch or whatnot that is bright and shall take a bird’s eye—a little mirror upon a cord were a good thing. But a handkerchief! Why, Sir Bookman, that a lady can only do if she will signify to all the world: ‘This knight is my servant and I his mistress.’ Those very words it signifieth—and that the better for it showeth that that lady is minded to let her hawk go, luring the gentleman to her with that favour of his.”

===Chinese===
「那便是老罗奇福德爵士了。」拉塞尔斯还不死心。
「老爷，」林中人说，「您这话错得到家了，明摆着您少出来行猎。」
「唉，我是照着记事本说的，」拉塞尔斯说。「可这是什么缘故？」
「老爷，」林中人答道，「事情是这样的：王后出外放鹰时，身后总跟着她的侍童图森特（Toussaint），一个小小孩儿。这小孩儿手里始终捧着各不相同的诱饵（lure），王后放起哪只鹰，便有哪只鹰的份。那放起的无论是隼、是鹰、是鹞、还是雄鹞，一俟俯冲下来，王后便唤那小雏儿，照着碰巧是哪只鸟，递上它自己那枚诱饵。而王后陛下恪守狩猎之法——兽猎与鹰猎的规约——一丝不苟。为着这个，俺敬重她。」
拉塞尔斯说：「嗯，嗯！」
「至于借手帕这桩事，」林中人接着说，「那是个顶没影儿的闲话。您听俺说：一位夫人要借，尽可以借一根缀宝的翎毛、一只猩红的小香囊，或随便什么鲜亮、能惹鸟儿入眼的东西——拴在绳上的一面小镜子就是好东西。可手帕！嘿，书虫爵士，一位夫人借手帕，那就只有一个缘故：她要昭告天下——『这位骑士是奴家的侍从情人，奴家是他的主母。』手帕说的正是这个意思——而且这意思再明白不过：那位夫人是存心要放她那只鹰高飞，再拿那位骑士的这件信物，把那位绅士引到自己跟前来。」

===Original===
“Well, well,” Lascelles said, “I am not so ignorant that I did not know that. Therefore I asked you, for it seemed a very strange thing.”
“It is a very foolish tale and very evil,” the man answered. “For this I will swear: that the Queen’s Highness—and I and her honour for it—observeth very jealously the laws of wood and moorland and chase.”
“So I have heard,” Lascelles said. “But I see the castle. I will not take you farther, but will let you go back to the goodly deer.”
“Pray God they be not wandered fore,” the woodman said. “You could have found this way without me.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，嗯，」拉塞尔斯说，「我还不至于无知到连这个都不懂。我正因如此才问你，因为这事看起来太离奇了。」
「那是个顶愚蠢、顶恶毒的闲话，」那人答道。「这一点俺敢起誓：王后陛下——俺拿自己和她两人的名誉担保——守着林木、荒原与围猎的法度，戒慎之至。」
「我也这么听说，」拉塞尔斯说。「不过我望见城堡了。不劳你再送，放你回去照看你那些好鹿吧。」
「求天主保佑它们没走散，」林中人说。「没有俺，您这条路也找得着。」

---

===Original===
There was but one road into the castle, and that from the south, up a steep green bank. Up the roadway Lascelles must ride his horse past four men that bore a litter made of two pikes wattled with green boughs and covered with a horse-cloth. As Lascelles passed by the very head of it, the man that lay there sprang off it to his feet, and cried out—
“I be the Queen’s cousin and servant. I brought her to the Court.” Lascelles’ horse sprang sideways, a great bound up the bank. He galloped ten paces ahead before the rider could stay him and turn round. The man, all rags and with a black face, had fallen into the dust of the road, and still cried out outrageously. The bearers set down the litter, wiped their brows, and then, falling all four upon Culpepper, made to carry him by his legs and arms, for they were weary of laying him upon the litter from which incessantly he sprang.
But before them upon his horse was Lascelles and impeded their way. Culpepper drew in and pushed out his legs and arms, so that they all four staggered, and—
“For God’s sake, master,” one of them grunted out, “stand aside that we may pass. We have toil enow in bearing him.”
“Why, set the poor gentleman down upon the litter,” Lascelles said, “and let us talk a little.”
The men set Culpepper on the horse-cloth, and one of them knelt down to hold him there.

===Chinese===
进城堡只有一条路，在南边，上一道陡峭的绿坡。拉塞尔斯沿路骑行，必须从四个抬担架的人身边经过——那担架是用两根长枪绑上青枝编成的，上头盖着一块马衣。拉塞尔斯刚从担架头边擦过，躺在上头的那人便一跃而起，直喊——
「俺是王后的表兄和仆人。是俺把她带进宫廷的。」拉塞尔斯的马惊得横里一蹿，朝坡上高高跃起。它一口气跑出十来步，骑的人才勒得住它、回过头来。那人衣衫褴褛，一脸黑灰，已跌进路面的尘土里，兀自声嘶力竭地叫骂。抬担架的把担架放下，抹了抹额头上的汗，随即四个人一齐扑上卡尔佩珀，抓腿的抓腿、抓胳膊的抓胳膊，把他架起来——他们已经烦透了他一次次从担架上蹦起来。
可拉塞尔斯骑马挡在他们面前的路上。卡尔佩珀一收一蹬他的手脚，四个人被晃得东倒西歪，只听——
「看在上帝份上，老爷，」其中一个呼哧呼哧地喊，「劳驾闪开，让俺们过去。抬这厮，俺们吃的苦头够啦。」
「唉，把这位可怜的先生放回担架上吧，」拉塞尔斯说，「咱们说会儿话。」
几人把卡尔佩珀放回马衣上，其中一个跪下来按住他。

===Original===
“If you will lend us your horse to lay him across, we may come more easily up,” one said. In these days the position and trade of a spy was so little esteemed—it had been far other with the great informers of Privy Seal’s day—that these men, being of the Queen’s guard, would talk roughly to Lascelles, who was a mere poor gentleman of the Archbishop’s if his other vocation could be neglected. Lascelles sat, his hand upon his chin.
“You use him very roughly if this be the Queen’s cousin,” he said.
The bearer set back his beard and laughed at the sky.
“This is a coif—a poor rag of a merchant,” he cried out. “If this were the Queen’s cousin should we bear him thus on a clout?”
“I am the Queen’s cousin, T. Culpepper,” Culpepper shouted at the sky. “Who be you that stay me from her?”
“Why, you may hear plainly,” the bearer said. “He is mazed, doited, starved, thirsted, and a seer of visions.”

===Chinese===
「您若肯把马借俺们，让他横在马背上，俺们上坡就轻省多了，」一个说。想当年御玺大臣当政时，那些大告密人何等风光，如今密探的身份和行当已这般不为人看重——这几个人既是王后卫队的人，便敢对拉塞尔斯说话粗声粗气；倘若撇开他另一重营生不提，他不过是大主教手下的一名穷绅士。拉塞尔斯坐在马上，手托着下巴。
「这位若真是王后的表兄，你们待他可太粗鲁了。」他说。
那抬担架的把脑袋一昂，朝天大笑。
「这位是个戴便帽的——一个商贩穷措大罢咧，」他喊道。「他若是王后的表兄，俺们会拿块破布片儿这么抬着他？」
「俺是王后的表兄，托马斯·卡尔佩珀，」卡尔佩珀冲着天喊。「你们是什么东西，敢拦着俺不叫俺见她？」
「喏，您这不是听得明明白白，」抬担架的说。「他迷了心窍、痴傻了、饿脱了形、渴疯了，还是个满眼幻象的。」

===Original===
Lascelles pondered, his elbow upon his saddle-peak, his chin caught in his hand.
“How came ye by him?” he asked.
One with another they told him the tale, how, the Queen being ridden towards the north parts, at the extreme end of her ride had seen the man, at a distance, among the heather, flogging a dead horse with a moorland kern beside him. He was a robbed, parched, fevered, and amazed traveller. The Queen’s Highness, compassionating, had bidden bear him to the castle and comfort and cure him, not having looked upon his face or heard his tongue. For, for sure then, she had let him die where he was; since, no sooner were these four, his new bearers, nearly come up among the knee-deep heather, than this man had started up, his eyes upon the Queen’s cavalcade and many at a distance. And, with his sword drawn and screaming, he had cried out that, if that was the Queen, he was the Queen’s cousin. They had tripped up his heels in a bed of ling and quieted him with a clout on the poll from an axe end.
“But now we have him here,” the eldest said; “where we shall bestow him we know not.”
Lascelles had his eyes upon the sick man’s face as if it fascinated him, and, slowly, he got down from his horse. Culpepper then lay very still with his eyes closed, but his breast heaved as though against tight and strong ropes that bound him.
“I think I do know this gentleman for one John Robb,” he said. “Are you very certain the Queen’s Highness did not know his face?”

===Chinese===
拉塞尔斯肘支鞍头，手托下巴，沉吟起来。
「你们是怎么得着这个人的？」他问。
你一句我一句，他们把事情始末讲给他听：王后北巡，行到行程的尽头，远远望见石楠丛里有这么一个人，正抽打一匹死马，旁边立着个荒原上的克恩仆卒。他是个遭了劫、渴得冒烟、发着热、神志迷乱的行路人。王后陛下动了怜悯，吩咐把他抬回城堡将养医治——并未看清他的脸，也没听见他开口。因为倘使看清了、听见了，她准会让他就地死在那里；这不，这四个新抬他的人刚蹚进没膝的石楠丛、快要走到他跟前，那人便一跃而起，眼睛盯着远处的王后仪仗大队人马。他拔出剑，嘶声喊叫，说那若是王后，他便是王后的表兄。他们绊翻他，让他跌进一丛石楠里，又用斧头背在他脑壳上敲了一记，才算让他安静下来。
「可如今人到了俺们手里，」年长的那个说，「往哪儿安置他，俺们也不知道。」
拉塞尔斯的眼睛盯住那张病脸，仿佛被它摄住了魂，随后他慢慢下了马。卡尔佩珀这时一动不动地躺着，双眼紧闭，胸口却起伏不定，像在挣脱捆住他的紧绷绷的粗绳。
「我想我认得这位先生——他叫约翰·罗布（John Robb），」他说。「你们能拿得稳，王后陛下没认出他的脸么？」

===Original===
“Why, she came not ever within a quarter mile of him,” the bearer said.
“Then it is a great charity of the Queen to show mercy to a man she hath never seen,” Lascelles answered absently. He was closely casting his eyes over Culpepper. Culpepper lay very still, his begrimed face to the sky, his hands abroad above his head. But when Lascelles bent over him it was as if he shuddered, and then he wept.
Lascelles bent down, his hands upon his knees. He was afraid—he was very afraid. Thomas Culpepper, the Queen’s cousin, he had never seen in his life. But he had heard it reported that he had red hair and beard, and went always dressed in green with stockings of red. And this man’s hair was red, and his beard, beneath coal grime, was a curly red, and his coat, beneath a crust of black filth, was Lincoln green and of a good cloth. And, beneath the black, his stockings were of red silk. He reflected slowly, whilst the bearers laughed amongst themselves at this Queen’s kinsman in rags and filth.
Lascelles gave them his bottle of sack to drink empty among them, that he might have the longer time to think.
If this were indeed the Queen’s cousin, come unknown to the Queen and mazed and muddled in himself to Pontefract, what might not Lascelles make of him? For all the world knew that he loved her with a mad love—he had sold farms to buy her gowns. It was he that had brought her to Court, upon an ass, at Greenwich, when her mule—as all men knew—had stumbled upon the threshold. Once before, it was said, Culpepper had burst in with his sword drawn upon the King and Kate Howard when they sat together. And Lascelles trembled with eagerness at the thought of what use he might not make of this mad and insolent lover of the Queen’s!
But did he dare?

===Chinese===
「嗐，她离他从来没有进过四分之一英里，」抬担架的说。
「那么，王后对一位从未谋面的人大发慈悲，真是莫大的善举，」拉塞尔斯心不在焉地答道。他的目光正细细扫过卡尔佩珀的周身。卡尔佩珀一动不动地躺着，污黑的脸朝着天，双手摊开在头顶上方。可拉塞尔斯俯身看他时，他似乎打了个哆嗦，接着便哭了。
拉塞尔斯弯下腰去，双手撑在膝上。他害怕——他非常害怕。王后的表兄托马斯·卡尔佩珀，他此生从未见过。但他听人说起过：此人生着红头发、红胡须，总是一身绿衣裳、配红袜子。而这人的头发是红的；他的胡须，煤灰底下，是卷曲的红色；那件外衣，在一层黑垢的硬壳底下，是上好料子的林肯绿（Lincoln green）呢；黑垢之下，长袜是红丝线的。他缓缓思量着，抬担架的人们则在一边彼此哄笑，笑这个衣衫褴褛、满身垢污的王室姻亲。
拉塞尔斯把自己那瓶萨克酒（sack）拿给他们分着喝干，好多挣得一点盘算的工夫。
倘若这真是王后的表兄——王后并不知情，他本人疯疯癫癫、糊里糊涂地流落到了庞蒂弗拉克特——拉塞尔斯还有什么不能拿他派用场的？因为天下无人不知，他爱她爱得发疯——他卖过田庄给她买裙裳。当年把她送进宫廷的正是他：在格林尼治（Greenwich），牵着一头驴——因为众所周知，她的骡子在门槛上失了蹄。据说更早还有一回，国王与凯特·霍华德同坐时，卡尔佩珀曾拔剑闯到他们跟前。一想到能拿这位王后的疯癫狂徒情人派上什么用场，拉塞尔斯便兴奋得浑身发颤！
可他敢么？

===Original===
Culpepper had been sent into Scotland to secure him up, away at the farthest limits of the realm. Then, if he was come back? This grime was the grime of a sea-coal ship! He knew that men without passports, outlaws and the like, escaped from Scotland on the Durham ships that went to Leith with coal. And this man came on the Durham road. Then. …
If it were Culpepper he had come unpermitted. He was an outlaw. Dare Lascelles have trade with—dare he harbour—an outlaw? It would be unbeknown to the Queen’s Highness! He kicked his heels with impatience to come to a resolution.
He reflected swiftly:
What hitherto he had were: some tales spread abroad about the Queen’s lewd Court—tales in London Town. He had, too, the keeper of the Queen’s door bribed and talked into his service and interest. And he had his sister. …
His sister would, with threatening, tell tales of the Queen before marriage. And she would find him other maids and grooms, some no doubt more willing still than Mary Hall. But the keeper of the Queen’s door! And, in addition, the Queen’s cousin mad of love for her! What might he not do with these two?
The prickly sweat came to his forehead. Four horsemen were issuing from the gate of the castle above. He must come to a decision. His fingers trembled as if they were a pickpocket’s near a purse of gold.

===Chinese===
卡尔佩珀原是被打发到苏格兰圈禁起来的，丢在王国最远的边陲。那么，倘若他回来了呢？这一身垢，分明是运海煤船上的垢！他知道，那些没有通行证的人、亡命徒之流，都是搭达勒姆那些往利思港运煤的船逃出苏格兰的。而这个人，走的是达勒姆大道。这么说来……
若这真是卡尔佩珀，那他就是未经许可回来的。他是个亡命之徒。拉塞尔斯敢与亡命之徒打交道么——敢窝藏亡命之徒么？这事，王后陛下将一无所知！他急不可耐地磕着脚跟，要赶快拿定主意。
他飞快地权衡着：
他迄今为止手中有的是：几桩关于王后宫廷淫乱的流言——伦敦城里的流言。他还收买了王后宫门的守门人，连劝带哄，说得那人入了他的门下、为他图谋效力。再有，便是他的妹妹……
他妹妹吃不住吓唬，会讲出王后婚前那些事来。她还会替他物色别的侍女和仆从，有几个无疑会比玛丽·霍尔更肯出力。可王后宫门的守门人！再加上这位爱王后爱得发疯的表兄！有这两个人在手里，还有什么干不得？
一层刺痒的汗沁上他的额头。四名骑手正从上方城堡的大门里出来。他必须拿定主意了。他的手指颤着，活像扒手凑近了一袋金子。

===Original===
He straightened his back and stood erect.
“Yes,” he said very calmly, “this is my friend John Robb.”
He added that this man had been in Edinburgh where the Queen’s cousin was. He had had letters from him that told how they were sib and rib. Thus this fancy had doubtless come into his brain at sight of the Queen in his madness.
He breathed calmly, having got out these words, for now the doubt was ended. He would have both the Queen’s doorkeeper and the Queen’s mad lover.
He bade the bearers set Culpepper upon his horse and, supporting him, lead him to a room that he would hire of the Archbishop’s chamberlain, near his own in the dark entrails of the castle. And there John Robb should live at his expenses.

===Chinese===
他挺直脊背，站得笔直。
「是，」他十分平静地说，「这位是我的朋友约翰·罗布。」
他又说，这人先前在爱丁堡待过，王后的表兄也在那里。这人收到过他的信，信里说他们是嫡亲的骨肉亲眷（sib and rib）。于是，他疯癫之中一见到王后，这个妄想便钻进了他的脑子。
说罢这几句，他呼吸平顺下来，因为疑虑已经了断。王后宫门的守门人与王后的疯情人，他要一双都收入彀中。
他吩咐抬担架的人把卡尔佩珀扶上他的马，由他们搀着，领到一间屋子里去——那屋子他要向大主教府的总管（chamberlain）租下，就在他自己那间的近旁，在城堡幽暗的内腹深处。约翰·罗布便要在那里吃他的、住他的。

===Original===
And when the men protested that, though this was very Christian of Lascelles, yet they would have recompense of the Queen for their toils, he said that he himself would give them a crown apiece, and they might get in addition what recompense from the Queen’s steward that they could. He asked them each their names and wrote them down, pretending that it was that he might send each man his crown piece.
So, when the four horsemen were ridden past, the men hoisted Culpepper into Lascelles’ horse and went all together up into the castle.
But, that night, when Culpepper lay in a stupor, Lascelles went to the Archbishop’s chamberlain and begged that four men, whose names he had written down, might be chosen to go in the Archbishop’s paritor’s guard that went next dawn to Ireland over the sea to bring back tithes from Dublin. And, next day, he had Culpepper moved to another room; and, in three days’ time, he set it about in the castle that the Queen’s cousin was come from Scotland. By that time most of the liquor had come down out of Culpepper’s brain, but he was still muddled and raved at times.

===Chinese===
那几人口出怨言，说拉塞尔斯这样行事固然是十足的基督徒慈悲，可他们这一路辛苦，总该从王后那里讨些酬报。他便说，他自己会给他们每人一枚克朗（crown），此外能从王后府管家那里讨到多少赏钱，也尽他们去讨。他问了他们各人的名字，一一记下，只推说是好把克朗银币送到每人手里。
于是，等那四名骑手过去之后，几人便把卡尔佩珀架上拉塞尔斯的马，一行人径直进了城堡。
