他又猛然跨到那少年上方，仿佛要把他踩成一摊不成形状的烂泥。可他站在那里发着颤，终于退了回去。

「起来，野种！」他喝道。「跑出汝生平没有过的脚力来。去把德斯潘勋爵和坎特伯雷大主教给朕传来，这些事原该由他们整饬。」

===Original===
The boy stumbled to his knees, and then, a flash of scarlet, ran, his head down, as if eagles were tearing at his hair.

The King turned upon his guard.

“Ho!” he said, “you, Jenkins, stay here with this my knight cousin. You, Cale and Richards, run to fetch a launderer that shall set a mattress in the antechamber for this my cousin to lie on. For this my cousin is the Queen’s chamber-ward, and shall there lie when I am here, if so be I have occasion for a messenger at night.”

The two guards ran off, striking upon the ground before them as they ran the heavy staves of their pikes. This noise was intended to warn all to make way for his Highness’ errand-bearers.

“Why,” the King said pleasantly to Jenkins, a guard with a blond and shaven face whom he liked well, “let us set this gentleman against the wall in the anteroom till his bed be come. He hath earned gentle usage, since he hasted much, bringing my message from Scotland to the Queen, and is very ill.”

===Chinese===
少年踉踉跄跄挣起到膝上，随后便是一道绯红的影子，埋着头飞奔而去，仿佛有鹰隼正在撕扯他的头发。

国王转向他的卫士。

「嚯！」他说，「你，詹金斯（Jenkins），留在这里，陪着朕这位骑士表亲。你们，凯尔（Cale）和理查兹（Richards），跑去叫一名洗衣仆役来，在前厅里铺一张床垫，好让朕这位表亲躺卧。因为朕这位表亲就是王后的寝宫值守，朕在此驻跸时，夜里万一要用信使，他就睡在那儿。」

两名卫士奔跑而去，一路把长枪沉重的枪杆顿在身前地上，山响。这声响是有意惊动众人，叫大家闪开道路，让陛下当差跑腿的人过。

「哟，」国王和颜悦色地对詹金斯说——这名卫士生着一张白净无须的脸，他素来喜爱——「咱们把这位先生靠墙安顿到前厅里去，等他的床铺送来。他理当受些温存款待，他替朕从苏格兰给王后送信，一路星驰，如今病得不轻。」

===Original===
So, helping his guard gently to conduct the drunkard into his wife’s dark anteroom, the King came out again to his wife.

“Is it well done?” he asked.

“Marvellous well done,” she answered.

“I am the man for these difficult times!” he answered, and was glad.

The Queen sighed a little. For if she admired and wondered at her lord’s power skilfully to have his way, it made her sad to think﻿—as she must think﻿—that so devious was man’s work.

===Chinese===
就这样，国王帮着卫士，轻手轻脚把醉汉搀进他妻子幽暗的前厅，然后又走出来，回到妻子身边。

「办得妥当么？」他问。

「妥当得出奇，」她答道。

「朕正是应付这艰难时世的人才！」他答道，满心欢喜。

王后轻轻叹了口气。夫君有本领、有手腕，能干练地凡事遂愿，她固然叹服称奇；可是一想到——她不能不想到——人做的事业竟是这般迂曲邪僻，又使她悲伤。

===Original===
“I would,” she said, “that it was not to such an occasion that I spurred thee.”

Her eyes, being cast downwards, fell upon the Lady Rochford, by the table.

“Ho, get up,” she cried. “You have feigned fainting long enough. But for you all this had been more easy. I would have you relieve mine eyes of the sight of your face.” She moved to aid the old woman to rise, but before she was upon her knees there stood without the door both the Lord d’Espahn and the Archbishop. They had waited just beyond the corridor-end with a great many of the other lords, all afraid of mysteries they knew not what, and thus it was that they came so soon upon the young Poins’ summoning.

===Chinese===
「我但愿，」她说，「我扬鞭策动您的，原不是为了这样一场变故。」

她垂下眼睛，落在桌边的罗奇福德夫人身上。

「嚯，起来，」她喊道。「你这场晕也装得够久了。若不是有你，这一切本会容易得多。我请你行个好，别再让你的脸来碍我的眼。」她上前去扶那老妇人起身，可还没等她挣到膝上立起，门外已经站着德斯潘勋爵和大主教两位了。他们方才同许多别的勋贵一起，候在走廊尽头之外，个个提心吊胆，怕着他们不知底细的种种玄秘；正因如此，小普因斯一传召，他们才来得这样快。

### II

===Original===
The King thought fit to change his mood, so that it was with uplifted brows and a quizzing smile at the corners of his mouth that for a minute he greeted these frightened lords in the doorway. They stood there silent, the Archbishop very dejected, the Lord d’Espahn, with his grey beard, very erect and ruddy featured.

“Why, God help me,” the King said, “what make of Court is this of mine where a King may not send a messenger to his wife?”

The Archbishop swallowed in his throat; the Lord d’Espahn did not speak but gazed before him.

“You shall tell me what befell, for I am ignorant,” the King said; “but first I will tell you what I do know.

“Why, come out with me into the corridor, wife,” he cried over his shoulder. “For it is not fitting that these lords come into thy apartment. I will walk with them and talk.”

He took the Archbishop by the elbow and the Lord d’Espahn by the upper arm, and, leaning upon them, propelled them gently before him.

===Chinese===
国王觉得该换一换心境了。于是，有一分钟工夫，他扬着眉毛、嘴角挂一丝戏谑的笑意，招呼站在门口的那几位惊惶的勋贵。他们默然立在那里：大主教十分沮丧；德斯潘勋爵灰须红面，站得笔挺。

「哟，天主助朕，」国王说，「朕这算个什么朝廷，一个国王竟不能差个信使去见自己的妻子？」

大主教喉头哽了一下；德斯潘勋爵不作声，只望着前方。

「你们须把出了什么事告诉朕，因为朕一无所知，」国王说；「不过，朕先说一说我所知道的。

「哟，随朕到走廊里来，夫人，」他回头喊道。「让这些卿家进汝的卧房，不合适。朕要同他们边走边谈。」

他一手挽住大主教的臂肘，一手抓住德斯潘勋爵的上臂，倚靠着他们，轻轻推着他们走在自己前头。

===Original===
“Thus it was,” he said; “this cousin of my wife’s was in the King o’ Scots’ good town of Edinboro’. And, being there, he was much upon my conscience—for I would not have a cousin of my wife’s be there in exile, he being one that formerly much fended for her. …”

He spoke out his words and repeated these things for his own purposes, the Queen following behind. When they were come to the corridor-end, there he found, as he had thought, a knot of lords and gentlemen, babbling with their ears pricked up.

“Nay, stay,” he said, “this is a matter that all may hear.”

There were there the Duke of Norfolk and his son, young Surrey with the vacant mouth, Sir Henry Wriothesley with the great yellow beard, the Lord Dacre of the North, the old knight Sir N. Rochford, Sir Henry Peel of these parts, with a many of their servants, amongst them Lascelles. Most of them were in scarlet or purple, but many were in black. The Earl of Surrey had the Queen’s favour of a crowned rose in his bonnet, for he was of her party. The gallery opened out there till it was as big as a large room, broad and low-ceiled, and lit with torches in irons at the angles of it. On rainy days the Queen’s maids were here accustomed to play at stool-ball.

“This is a matter that all may hear,” the King said, “and some shall render account.” He let the Lord d’Espahn and the Archbishop go, so that they faced him. The Queen looked over his shoulder.

“As thus …” he said.

===Chinese===
「事情原是这样，」他说；「我夫人的这位表兄，先前身在苏格兰国王治下的好城爱丁堡。他在那里时，朕心里时常惦着——朕不愿夫人的表兄流落在外，况且他往日曾多方护持过她。……」

他朗声说着这些话，又为了自己的目的把这些事反复申说，王后跟在后面。一行人到了走廊尽头，果然如他所料，他在那里撞见一小群勋贵与绅士，正竖着耳朵喋喋私语。

「别走，」他说，「这事人人都可以听。」

在场的是诺福克公爵和他的儿子、嘴呆呆张着的年轻萨里，有大黄胡子的亨利·赖奥思利爵士，有北方达克雷勋爵、老骑士N·罗奇福德爵士、本地的亨利·皮尔爵士，还有他们的许多仆从，拉塞尔斯也在其中。他们多半穿绯红或紫衣，也有不少一身黑。萨里伯爵的帽上缀着王后所赐的加冕玫瑰，因为他是她一党的人。画廊长廊在那里豁然放宽，宽敞得同一间大屋子一般，顶棚低矮，四角的铁架上燃着火把。雨天里，王后的侍女们惯在这里玩凳球戏。

「这事人人都可以听，」国王说，「而且有些人要交账。」他放开德斯潘勋爵和大主教，让他们面对自己站定。王后从他肩后望过去。

「如此这般……」他说。

===Original===
And he repeated how it had lain upon his conscience and near his heart that the Queen’s good cousin languished in the town of Edinburgh.

“And how near we came to Edinboro’ those of ye that were with me can make account.”

And, lying there, he had taken occasion to send a messenger with others that went to the King o’ Scots—to send a messenger with letters unto this T. Culpepper. One letter was to bid him hasten home unto the Queen, and one was a letter that he should bear.

“For,” said the King, “we thought thus—as ye wist—that the King o’ Scots would come obedient to our summoning and that there we should lie some days awaiting and entertaining him. Thus did I wish to send my Queen swift message of our faring, and I was willing that this, her cousin and mine, should be my postman and messenger. For he should—I bade him—set sail in a swift ship for these coasts and so come quicker than ever a man might by land.”

He paused to observe the effect of his words, but no lord spoke though some whispered amongst themselves.

===Chinese===
他又反复申说道：王后的好表兄困顿在爱丁堡城里，这件事如何压在他的良心上、悬在他的心头。

「朕等的行在离爱丁堡何等近，随朕同行的你们自会计算。」

他说，驻跸在那里时，他便趁便差了一名信使，同其他前往苏格兰国王处的人一道——差这信使带了几封书信，送给这位T·卡尔佩珀。一封信是命他从速回国来见王后；另一封，是要他随身携呈的信。

「因为，」国王说，「朕等原是这样打算的——汝等并非不知——以为苏格兰国王会奉召恭顺前来，朕等便将在那里驻上几日，等候他、款待他。因此朕愿把朕等的行程尽快传信给朕的王后，也乐意让这位她的也是朕的表兄，充当朕的信差与使者。因为朕命他——朕吩咐过他——乘一艘快船扬帆驶向此间的海岸，比任何人由陆路赶来都快。」

他顿了一顿，观察这番话的效验；但没有一个勋贵开口，虽则有人彼此窃窃私语。

===Original===
“Now,” he said, “what stood within my letter to the Queen was this, after salutations, that she should reward this her cousin that in the aforetime had much fended for her when she was a child. For I was aware how, out of a great delicacy and fear of nepotism, such as was shown by certain of the Popes now dead, she raised up none of her relations and blood, nor none that before had aided her when she was a child and poor. But I was willing that this should be otherwise, and they be much helped that before had helped her since now she helpeth me and assuageth my many and fell labours.”

He paused and went a step back that he might stand beside the Queen, and there, before them all, Katharine was most glad that she had again set on all her jewels and was queenlike. She had composed her features, and gazed before her over their heads, her hands being folded in the lap of her gown.

“Now,” the King said, “this letter of mine was a little thing—but great maybe, since it bore my will. Yet”—and he made his voice minatory—“in these evil and tickle times well it might have been that that letter held delicate news. Then all my plots had gone to ruin. How came it that some of ye—I know not whom!—thus letted and hindered my messenger?”

He had raised his voice very high. He stayed it suddenly, and some there shivered.

He uttered balefully, “Anan!”

===Chinese===
「现在，」他说，「朕那封写给王后的信，说的是这个：叙礼之后，朕写道，她应当酬谢这位表兄——在她幼年之时，他曾在从前多方护持过她。因为朕深知，她出于极细腻的用心、又深惧任人唯亲——如几位已故教皇所显露的那样——从不拔擢她的亲族血脉，连她幼年贫贱时出过力的人也不拔擢。但朕却愿意事情换个做法：从前帮过她的人，如今该多多得她提携，因为她如今正辅助着朕，宽解着朕繁多而严酷的劳瘁。」

他顿住，退后一步，好站到王后身旁。当着众人的面，凯瑟琳深自庆幸自己重新戴齐了全部珠玉、恢复了王后的仪态。她端凝了面容，目光越过众人头顶望向前方，双手交叠在裙兜里。

「现在，」国王说，「朕这封信本是件小东西——或许又重大，因为它载着朕的旨意。然而」——他把嗓音放得咄咄逼人——「在这邪恶而危殆的时世，那封信里尽可以装着紧要的消息。倘若那样，朕的全盘谋划便都毁了。怎么搞的，你们当中有些人——朕不知是谁！——竟这般阻挠、留难朕的信使？」

他把嗓门提得很高很高，又猛地收住；人丛里有几个人打了个寒噤。

他阴狠狠地吐出一个字眼：「怎么说！」

===Original===
“As Christ is my Saviour,” the Lord d’Espahn said, “I, since I am the Queen’s Marshal, am answerable in this, as well I know. Yet never saw I this man till tonight at supper. He would have my seat then, and I gave it him. Ne let ne hindrance had he of me, but went his way where and when he would.”

“You did very well,” the King said. “Who else speaks?”

The Archbishop looked over his shoulder, and with a dry mouth uttered, “Lascelles!”

Lascelles, deft and blond and gay, shouldered his way through that unwilling crowd, and fell upon his knees.

“Of this I know something,” he said; “and if any have offended, doubtless it is I, though with good will.”

“Well, speak!” the King said.

===Chinese===
「既以基督为我的救主起誓，」德斯潘勋爵说，「我身为王后府典礼官，此事难辞其咎，这我心里明白。然而直到今夜晚膳，我从未见过此人。当时他要我的座席，我便让给了他。我不曾拦他一回、不曾碍他一下，他爱去哪里、爱几时走，随他的便。」

「你做得很好，」国王说。「还有谁说话？」

大主教回头望了一眼，口干舌燥地吐出：「拉塞尔斯！」

拉塞尔斯身手伶俐，金发，一脸快活，从那不情愿的人丛里挤身而出，扑通跪倒在地。

「这事我知道一点，」他说；「若说有人获了罪，那必定是我，虽则我出于好意。」

「好，说吧！」国王说。

===Original===
Lascelles recounted how the Queen, riding out, had seen afar this gentleman lying amid the heather.

“And if she should not know him who was her cousin, how should we who are servants?” he said. But, having heard that the Queen would have this poor, robbed wayfarer tended and comforted, he, Lascelles, out of the love and loyalty he owed her Grace, had so tended and so comforted him that he had given up to him his own bed and board. But it was not till that day that, Culpepper being washed and apparelled—not till that day a little before supper, had he known him for Culpepper, the Queen’s cousin. So he had gone with him that night to the banquet-hall, and there had served him, and, after, had attended him with some lords and gentles. But, at the last, Culpepper had shaken them off and bidden them leave him.

“And who were we, what warrants had we, to restrain the Queen’s noble cousin?” he finished. “And, as for letters, I never saw one, though all his apparel, in rags, was in my hands. I think he must have lost this letter amongst the robbers he fell in with. But what I could do, I did for love of the Queen’s Grace, who much hath favoured me.”

The King studied his words. He looked at the Queen’s face and then at those of the lords before him.

===Chinese===
拉塞尔斯陈说道，王后某日出游骑行，远远望见这位老爷躺在石楠丛中。

「她既是他的表妹，尚且认不出他来，我们做仆从的又怎么认得？」他说。但他既听说王后愿人把这位遭了劫的落难行路人好好照料、好好宽慰，他拉塞尔斯便出于对王后陛下应尽的爱戴与忠诚，如此照料、如此宽慰了他，连自己的床铺饭食都让给了他。但直到那一天——待卡尔佩珀洗净更衣之后——直到那天晚膳前不久，他才知道他就是王后的表兄卡尔佩珀。于是当夜他便随他去了宴厅，在那里侍奉他，随后又同几位勋贵绅士陪随他。可到末了，卡尔佩珀甩开了他们，吩咐他们别再跟着他。

「我们算什么人，有什么凭据，敢约束王后高贵的表兄？」他收尾道。「至于书信，我一封也没见过，尽管他那身褴褛衣裳全经过我的手。我想他必定是落进强盗手里时，把那封信丢在他们中间了。但凡我力所能及的，我都做了——出于对王后陛下的爱，她素来厚待我。」

国王掂量着他的话。他看看王后的脸，又看看面前众勋贵的脸。

===Original===
“Why, this tale hath a better shewing,” he said. “Herein appeareth that none, save the Queen’s door-ward, came ever against this good knight and cousin of mine. And, since this knight was in liquor, and not overwise sensible—as well he might be after supping in moors and deserts—maybe that door-ward had his reasonable reasonings.”

He paused again, and looking upon the Queen’s face for a sign:

“If it be thus, it is well,” he said, “I will pardon and assoil you all, if later it shall appear that this is the true truth.”

Lascelles whispered in the Archbishop’s ear, and Cranmer uttered—

“The witnesses be here to prove it, if your Highness will.”

“Why,” the King said, “it is late enough,” and he leered at Cranmer, for whom he had an affection. He looked again upon the Queen to see how fair she was and how bravely she bore herself, upright and without emotion. “This wife of mine,” he said, “is ever of the pardoning side. If ye had so injured me I had been among ye with fines and amercements. But she, I perceive, will not have it so, and I am too glad to be smiled upon now to cross her will. So, get you gone and sleep well. But, before you go, I will have you listen to some words. …”

===Chinese===
「哟，这番话听着就体面多了，」他说。「由此可见，除了王后宫门的守门人，从未有人同朕这位好骑士、好表兄作难。这位骑士既醉了酒，神志又不甚清明——在荒原野地里进过晚餐之后，这也难怪——那么那守门人自有他讲得出的道理。」

他又顿了顿，望着王后的脸，等一个示意：

「倘若果真如此，那也就罢了，」他说，「朕便赦免你们众人、解除你们的罪愆——只要日后果然查明，这是真而又真的实情。」

拉塞尔斯对着大主教的耳朵低语，克兰默便道——

「证人都在这里，可以作证，只要陛下愿意。」

「哟，」国王说，「时候也够晚了。」他朝克兰默斜睨一笑——他对克兰默素有几分喜爱。他又看了看王后，看她何等姣好，何等刚毅地自持：身姿挺直，不动声色。「朕这位夫人，」他说，「向来站在宽赦的一边。你们若这样欺犯朕，朕早到你们中间收罚金、课罚金去了。可我看出，她不愿如此；朕此刻正喜获她的嫣然一笑，也不忍违拗她的心意。都去吧，好好睡。不过走之前，朕要你们听几句话。……」

===Original===
He cleared his throat, and in his left hand took the Queen’s.

“Know ye,” he said, “that I am as proud of this my Queen as was ever mother of her firstborn child. For lo, even as the Latin poet saith, that, upon bearing a child, many evil women are led to repentance and right paths, so have I, your King, been led towards righteousness by wedding of this lady. For I tell you that, but for certain small hindrances—and mostly this treacherous disloyalty of the King o’ Scots that thus with his craven marrow hath featorously dallied to look upon my face—but for that and other small things there had gone forth this night through the dark to the Bishop of Rome certain tidings that, please God, had made you and me and all this land the gladdest that be in Christendom. And this I tell you, too, that though by this misadventure and fear of the King o’ Scots, these tidings have been delayed, yet is it only for a little space and, full surely, that day cometh. And for this you shall give thanks first to God and then to this royal lady here. For she, before all things, having the love of God in her heart, hath brought about this desired consummation. And this I say, to her greater praise, here in the midmost of you all, that it be noised unto the utmost corners of the world how good a Queen the King hath taken to wife.”

The Queen had stood very motionless in the bright illuminations and dancings of the torches. But at the news of delay, through the King of Scots, a spasm of pain and concern came into her face. So that, if her features did not again move they had in them a savour of anguish, her eyebrows drooping, and the corners of her mouth.

“And now, good night!” the King pursued with raised tones. “If ever ye slept well since these troublous times began, now ye may sleep well in the drowsy night. For now, in this my reign, are come the shortening years like autumn days. Now I will have such peace in land as cometh to the husbandman. He hath ingarnered his grain; he hath barned his fodder and straw; his sheep are in the byres and in the stalls his oxen. So, sitteth he by his fireside with wife and child, and hath no fear of winter. Such a man am I, your King, who in the years to come shall rest in peace.”

===Chinese===
他清了清喉咙，左手握住王后的手。

「汝等要知道，」他说，「朕对朕这位王后引以为豪，一如天下做母亲的初得头生子女时的自豪。因为，看哪，正如那位拉丁诗人所言，许多恶妇一朝生子，便被引向悔改与正路；朕，你们的国王，也因聘娶这位夫人而被引向了正义。朕告诉你们：若不是有些小小的阻碍——头一桩，便是苏格兰国王那背信弃义的负义，他凭着那怯懦的骨血，奸滑地迁延躲闪，不肯来觐见朕的圣颜——若非为此及旁的些许小事，今夜早已有消息穿过黑暗发往罗马主教那里；那些消息，若蒙天主允准，本已使朕与你们、使这片国土成为基督世界最欢喜的君民。朕再告诉你们：虽因这场横逆、因对苏格兰国王的忌惮，这些消息延误了下来，但这不过是一时之隔，那一日必定到来，确然无疑。为此，你们当先感谢天主，再感谢这位尊贵的夫人。因为她把对天主的爱放在万事之先，成就了这期盼已久的圆满。朕又当着你们众人的面，为她更大的荣耀添一句：要让这事传扬到世界最远的角落——国王娶了一位何等贤德的王后。」

王后在火把明亮的照映与焰光的跳荡中，一直站得纹丝不动。可一听到消息因苏格兰国王而延误，一阵痛楚与忧切的痉挛掠上她的脸。她的眉梢垂下来，嘴角也垂下来；面容纵然不再牵动，那里面已含着一缕苦楚。

「现在，晚安！」国王提高声调继续说。「自从这多难的时世开场以来，你们若有过安稳的睡眠，如今在这昏昏欲睡的夜里，尽可安睡了。因为在朕这个朝代，如今已到来那渐短的岁月，如同秋日一天天短下去。如今朕要这国土享农人享的那份太平：他已把谷粒收进仓；已把草料秸秆堆入棚；他的羊进了圈，牛进了栏。于是他携妻抱子坐在炉边，不惧严冬。朕，你们的国王，便是这样一个人：在未来的岁月里，将安然休息。」

===Original===
The lords and gentlemen made their reverences, bows and knees; they swept round in their coloured assembly, and the Queen stood very tall and straight, watching their departure with saddened eyes.

The King was very gay and caught her by the waist.

“God help me, it is very late,” he said. “Hearken!”

From above the corridor there came the drowsy sound of the clock.

===Chinese===
众勋贵与绅士行礼如仪，或躬身，或屈膝；五颜六色的一群人回旋着散去。王后站得颀长笔直，眼里含着黯然，目送他们离去。

国王兴高采烈，一把揽住她的腰。

「天主助我，很晚了，」他说。「听！」

走廊上方传来时钟困倦的报时声。

===Original===
“Thy daughter hath made her submission,” the Queen said. “I had thought this was the gladdest day in my life.”

“Why, so it is,” he said, “as now day passeth to day.” The clock ceased. “Every day shall be glad,” he said, “and gladder than the rest.”

At her chamber door he made a bustle. He would have the Queen’s women come to untire her, a leech to see to Culpepper’s recovery. He was willing to drink mulled wine before he slept. He was afraid to talk with his wife of delaying his letter to Rome. That was why he had told the news before her to his lords.

He fell upon the Lady Rochford that stood, not daring to go, within the Queen’s room. He bade her sit all night by the bedside of T. Culpepper; he reviled her for a craven coward that had discountenanced the Queen. She should pay for it by watching all night, and woe betide her if any had speech with T. Culpepper before the King rose.

===Chinese===
「您的女儿已经臣服了，」王后说。「我原以为，这是我一生中最欢喜的一天。」

「哟，正是这样，」他说，「如今正是日子接着日子过。」时钟停了。「每一天都会是欢喜的，」他说，「而且一天比一天更欢喜。」

到了她卧房门口，他忙乱了一阵。他要王后的侍女们来替她卸装，要一名医师照看卡尔佩珀的复原。他睡前乐意喝一盅烫热的香料酒。他不敢同妻子谈他致罗马的信如何延误——所以他才当着她的面，把那消息先告诉了卿家们。

他对罗奇福德夫人发作了一顿——她不敢走开，正待在王后房里。他命她整夜守在T·卡尔佩珀的床边；他骂她是个怯懦的懦妇，扫了王后的脸面。她该拿整夜的看守来抵这罪过；天亮国王起身之前，若有人同T·卡尔佩珀说过半句话，灾祸就落在她头上。

### III

===Original===
Down in the lower castle, the Archbishop was accustomed, when he undressed, to have with him neither priest nor page, but only, when he desired to converse of public matters﻿—as now he did﻿—his gentleman, Lascelles. He knelt above his kneeling-stool of black wood; he was telling his beads before a great crucifix with an ivory Son of God upon it. His chamber had bare white walls, his bed no curtains, and all the other furnishing of the room was a great black lectern whereto there was chained a huge Book of the Holy Writ that had his Preface. The tears were in his eyes as he muttered his prayers; he glanced upwards at the face of his Saviour, who looked down with a pallid, uncoloured face of ivory, the features shewing a great agony so that the mouth was opened. It was said that this image, that came from Italy, had had a face serene, before the Queen Katharine of Aragon had been put away. Then it had cried out once, and so remained ever lachrymose and in agony.

“God help me, I cannot well pray,” the Archbishop said. “The peril that we have been in stays with me still.”

“Why, thank God that we are come out of it very well,” Lascelles said. “You may pray and then sleep more calm than ever you have done this sennight.”

He leant back against the reading-pulpit, and had his arm across the Bible as if it had been the shoulder of a friend.

===Chinese===
在城堡下层，大主教夜里卸衣就寝时，惯来身边既不要神父，也不要侍童；只当他有意谈论公务——此刻正是如此——才单留他的绅士侍从拉塞尔斯在侧。他跪在黑木跪凳前，正对着一座巨大的十字架数念珠，架上是一尊象牙的天主之子。他的居室四壁皆白，空无饰物，卧榻不设帐幔；屋里其余的全部陈设，唯有一座黑色大读经台，台上一册以铁链锁住的圣经巨著，书前载着他亲撰的序文。他喃喃祷告时，眼中噙着泪；他抬眼仰望救主的面容——救主垂视着他，一张苍白无色的象牙脸，五官现出极大的痛楚，以致嘴张开着。据说这尊来自意大利的圣像，在阿拉贡的凯瑟琳王后被废之前，原有一副安详的面容；后来它呐喊过一声，从此便永远涕泪涟涟，长带痛苦。

「天主助我，我祷告不进去了，」大主教说。「我们方才身历的危难，至今还缠着我。」

「哟，感谢天主，我们总算平平安安脱了身，」拉塞尔斯说。「大人尽可祷告，然后安睡——比这七日来任何一夜都睡得安稳。」

他往后靠在读经台上，一条手臂横搭在那部圣经上，仿佛搭在友人的肩头。

===Original===
“Why,” the Archbishop said, “this is the worst day ever I have been through since Cromwell fell.”

“Please it your Grace,” his confidant said, “it shall yet turn out the best.”

The Archbishop faced round upon his knees; he had taken off the jewel from before his breast, and, with his chain of Chaplain of the George, it dangled across the corner of the fald-stool. His coat was unbuttoned at the neck, his robe open, and it was manifest that his sleeves of lawn were but sleeves, for in the opening was visible, harsh and grey, the shirt of hair that night and day he wore.

“I am weary of this talk of the world,” he said. “Pray you begone and leave me to my prayers.”

===Chinese===
「唉，」大主教说，「这是自克伦威尔倒台以来，我经历过最凶险的一日。」

「大人，」他这位心腹说，「这日子到头来，反倒会成为最好的一日。」

大主教就着跪姿转过脸来；他已摘下胸前那枚宝石，连同圣乔治项章随堂神父的链子，一齐垂挂在跪凳一角。他的外衣领口解开了纽扣，长袍敞开着，显而易见，那两条细麻布袖子只是空垂的袖子而已，因为开口处露出他日夜贴身穿着的毛发苦衣，粗硬而灰暗。

「这尘世的言语，我已倦了，」他说。「请汝去吧，留我独自祷告。」

===Original===
“Please it your Grace to let me stay and hearten you,” Lascelles said, and he was aware that the Archbishop was afraid to be alone with the white Christ. “All your other gentry are in bed. I shall watch your sleep, to wake you if you cry out.”

And in his fear of Cromwell’s ghost that came to him in his dreams, the Archbishop sighed﻿—

“Why stay, but speak not. Y’are over bold.”

He turned again to the wall; his beads clicked; he sighed and remained still for a long time, a black shadow, huddled together in a black gown, sighing before the white and lamenting image that hung above him.

===Chinese===
「求大人容我留下，也好给大人添些胆气，」拉塞尔斯说。他心里明白，大主教是怕独自与那白色的基督相对。「大人的其余侍从都已睡下了。我会守着大人入睡，大人若喊出声来，我便唤醒大人。」

而他惧怕克伦威尔的鬼魂入梦相寻，遂叹了一声——

「既留便留着，只是别作声。汝忒大胆了些。」

他又转向墙壁；念珠嗒嗒地捻动；他叹了口气，久久呆然不动——一团黑影，裹在黑色长袍里瑟缩着，在那悬于头顶、白色的哀恸圣像前长叹。

===Original===
“God help me,” he said at last. “Tell me why you say this is *dies felix*?”

Lascelles, who smiled forever and without mirth, said﻿—

“For two things: firstly, because this letter and its sending are put off. And secondly, because the Queen is﻿—patently and to all people﻿—proved lewd.”

The Archbishop swung his head round upon his shoulders.

===Chinese===
「天主助我，」他终于开口道。「告诉我，汝为何说今日是 *dies felix*（吉日）？」

拉塞尔斯脸上永远挂着笑，笑中却无欢意，说道——

「为着两件事：其一，这封信连同发信之事，都已暂缓。其二，王后已——昭然于天下人眼前——被证实淫荡。」

大主教霍地转过头来。

===Original===
“You dare not say it!” he said.

“Why, the late Queen Katharine from Aragon was accounted a model of piety, yet all men know she was over fond with her confessor,” Lascelles smiled.

“It is an approved lie and slander,” the Archbishop said.

“It served mightily well in pulling down that Katharine,” his confidant answered.

===Chinese===
「汝决不敢说这话的！」他道。

「哟，已故的王后、阿拉贡来的凯瑟琳，当初不也被视作虔敬的楷模么？可普天之下谁人不知，她同她的告解神父过分亲昵，」拉塞尔斯含笑道。

「那是一桩查明坐实的谎言与诽谤，」大主教说。

「可它用来扳倒那位凯瑟琳，着实大见功效，」他这位心腹答道。

===Original===
“One day”﻿—the Archbishop shivered within his robes﻿—“the account and retribution for these lies shall be to be paid. For well we know, you, I, and all of us, that these be falsities and cozenings.”

“Marry,” Lascelles said, “of this Queen it is now sufficiently proved true.”

The Archbishop made as if he washed his hands.

“Why,” Lascelles said, “what man shall believe it was by chance and accident that she met her cousin on these moors? She is not a compass that pointeth, of miraculous power, true North.”

===Chinese===
「总有一日」——大主教在长袍里打了个寒噤——「这些谎言的账、连同它们的报应，是必得偿付的。因为咱们心中了然——汝、我，连同咱们众人——这些尽属虚妄，尽属欺诈。」

「嗳，」拉塞尔斯说，「至于这位王后的事，如今可是查实得够充分了。」

大主教做出一副洗手的模样。

「哟，」拉塞尔斯说，「哪个人会相信，她在这片荒原上遇见她那位表兄，竟是出之于偶然、碰巧？她又不是一具罗盘，会凭神迹之力，恒指正北。」

===Original===
“No good man shall believe what you do say,” the Archbishop cried out.

“But a multitude of indifferent will,” Lascelles answered.

“God help me,” the Archbishop said, “what a devil you are that thus hold out and hold out forever hopes.”

“Why,” Lascelles said, “I think you were well helped that day that I came into your service. It was the Great Privy Seal that bade me serve you and commended me.”

===Chinese===
「良善之人决不会信汝这话，」大主教高声道。

「可那一大批不好不坏的庸众，却会信的，」拉塞尔斯答道。

「天主助我，」大主教说，「汝是何等的一个魔鬼，就这样把希望一悬再悬、永远悬着不放。」

「哟，」拉塞尔斯说，「我倒想，我进大人门下效力那一日，大人实在得了好大的助力。正是那位大御玺大臣吩咐我来侍奉大人，又把我荐举给大人的。」

===Original===
The Archbishop shivered at that name.

“What an end had Thomas Cromwell!” he said.

“Why, such an end shall not be yours whilst this King lives, so well he loves you,” Lascelles answered.

The Archbishop stood upon his feet; he raised his hands above his head.

===Chinese===
大主教一听这名字，便打了个寒噤。

「托马斯·克伦威尔落了个何等的下场啊！」他说。

「哟，只要当今这位国王在世一日，大人便决计不会落那样的下场——国王是这样地爱重大人，」拉塞尔斯答道。

大主教站起身来；他把双手高举过了头顶。

===Original===
“Begone! Begone!” he cried. “I will not be of your evil schemes.”

“Your Grace shall not,” Lascelles said very softly, “if they miscarry. But when it is proven to the hilt that this Queen is a very lewd woman﻿—and proven it shall be﻿—your Grace may carry an accusation to the King﻿—”

Cranmer said﻿—

“Never! never! Shall I come between the lion and his food?”

===Chinese===
「去罢！去罢！」他喊道。「汝等的恶计，我决不与闻。」

「大人诚然可以不与闻，」拉塞尔斯声音极轻地说，「倘若这些计策败露的话。可待到查得铁证，证明这位王后委实是个失德之妇——而这铁证是必会查得的——大人便可向国王呈递这纸控状——」

克兰默道——

「决不！决不！我岂能横身拦在狮子与其食物之间？」

===Original===
“It were better if your Grace would carry the accusation,” Lascelles uttered nonchalantly, “for the King will better hearken to you than to any other. But another man will do it too.”

“I will not be of this plotting,” the Archbishop cried out. “It is a very wicked thing!” He looked round at the white Christ that, upon the dark cross, bent anguished brows upon him. “Give me strength,” he said.

“Why, your Grace shall not be of it,” Lascelles answered, “until it is proven in the eyes of your Grace﻿—ay, and in the eyes of some of the Papist Lords﻿—as, for instance, her very uncle﻿—that this Queen was evil in her life before the King took her, and that she hath acted very suspicious in the aftertime.”

“You shall not prove it to the Papist Lords,” Cranmer said. “It is a folly.”

===Chinese===
「这控状若由大人亲递，原是更好，」拉塞尔斯淡然说道，「因为国王听大人进言，总比听旁人的更肯入耳。可换了别人，这桩事也照样办得。」

「这等密谋我决不与闻，」大主教喊道。「这是大大的恶事！」他回望那白色的基督——基督在暗色十字架上，正向他紧蹙着愁苦的双眉。「求您赐我力量，」他说。

「哟，大人尽可不与闻，」拉塞尔斯答道，「只等这桩事在大人眼中得了证明——然，还须在几位教皇派勋贵眼中——譬如她那位亲叔祖——证明这位王后在国王娶她之前便已平生失德，其后行事又极为可疑。」

「汝等决计无法把这证明给教皇派勋贵看，」克兰默说。「这是愚妄。」

===Original===
He added vehemently﻿—

“It is a wicked plot. It is a folly too. I will not be of it.”

“This is a very fortunate day,” Lascelles said. “I think it is proven to all discerning men that that letter to him of Rome shall never be sent.”

“Why, it is as plain as the truths of the Six Articles,” Cranmer remonstrated, “that it shall be sent tomorrow or the next day. Get you gone! This King hath but the will of the Queen to guide him, and all her will turns upon that letter. Get you gone!”

===Chinese===
他又激愤地说道——

「这是恶毒的阴谋。也是愚妄。我决不与闻。」

「今日真是大吉大利的日子，」拉塞尔斯说。「我想，凡有眼力的人此刻都已看清：那封致罗马主教的信，是永远不会发出的了。」

「嗬，明日或后日那信便要发出，这同六条信条的真理一般明白，」克兰默抗辩道。「去罢！这位国王行事，唯以王后之意志为依归，而王后的全部意志，都系于那一封信上。去罢！」

===Original===
“Please it your Grace,” the spy said, “it is very manifest that with the Queen so it is. But with the King it is otherwise. He will pleasure the Queen if he may. But﻿—mark me well﻿—for this is a subtle matter﻿—”

“I will not mark you,” the Archbishop said. “Get you gone and find another master. I will not hear you. This is the very end.”

Lascelles moved his arm from the Bible. He bent his form to a bow﻿—he moved till his hand was on the latch of the door.

“Why, continue,” the Archbishop said. “If you have awakened my fears, you shall slake them if you can﻿—for this night I shall not sleep.”

===Chinese===
「大人，」这密探说，「王后那边，事情确然如此，这是显而易见的。可国王那边，却另当别论。他若能遂王后之意，自然肯遂。只是——大人务要听仔细——因这内中情由幽微——」

「我决不听汝，」大主教说。「去罢，另寻一个主子去。我决不听汝说话。这事就此完了。」

拉塞尔斯把手臂从那部圣经上挪开。他俯身行了一礼——他一路挪步，直挪到手已搭上门闩。

「唉，接着讲罢，」大主教说。「汝既已唤醒了我的恐惧，便须尽力替我平息——横竖今夜我是睡不成了。」

===Original===

And so, very lengthily, Lascelles unfolded his view of the King’s nature. For, said he, if this alliance with the Pope should come, it must be an alliance with the Pope and the Emperor Charles. For the King of France was an atheist, as all men knew. And an alliance with the Pope and the Emperor must be an alliance against France. But the King o’ Scots was the closest ally that Francis had, and never should the King dare to wage war upon Francis till the King o’ Scots was placated or wooed by treachery to be a prisoner, as the King would have made him if James had come into England to the meeting. Well would the King, to save his soul, placate and cosset his wife. But that he never dare do whilst James was potent at his back.

And again, Lascelles said, well knew the Archbishop that the Duke of Norfolk and his following were the ancient friends of France. If the Queen should force the King to this Imperial League, it must turn Norfolk and the Bishop of Winchester forever to her bitter foes in that land. And along with them all the Protestant nobles and all the Papists too that had lands of the Church.

The Archbishop had been marking his words very eagerly. But suddenly he cried out﻿—

“But the King! The King! What shall it boot if all these be against her so the King be but for her?”

===Chinese===

于是，拉塞尔斯便絮絮不休，长篇铺陈起他对国王性情的见解。他说，倘若与教皇结盟一事果成，那必是与教皇并皇帝卡尔两造的盟约。因法兰西国王是个无神论者，此乃尽人皆知。而与教皇并皇帝结盟，便必是与法兰西为敌的盟约。然苏格兰国王又是法兰西斯最亲密的盟友；不先将苏格兰国王安抚帖然，或施诡计诱他就擒、收作阶下之囚——当日詹姆士倘若果然入境英格兰赴会，国王原是要这般摆布他的——国王便决不敢对法兰西斯兴兵。为救自己的灵魂，国王尽肯百般抚慰、娇宠王后。然詹姆士一日雄踞其背，此事他便一日决不敢行。

拉塞尔斯又说，大人岂不了然：诺福克公爵及其一党，本是法兰西的旧交。王后若强逼国王缔结这皇帝同盟，便势必要使诺福克与温彻斯特主教在这片国土上永远沦为她的死敌。而附从于他们的，还有所有的新教勋贵，以及一切领有教会田产的教皇派。

大主教一直极切地留意着他这番话。可他忽然失声喊了出来——

「可是国王！国王！只要国王向着王后，纵使这班人个个与她为敌，于她又何损分毫？」

===Original===

“Why,” Lascelles said, “this King is not a very stable man. Still, man he is, a man very jealous and afraid of fleers and flouts. If we can show him﻿—I do accede to it that after what he hath done tonight it shall not be easy, but we may accomplish it﻿—if before this letter is sent we may show him that all his land cries out at him and mocks him with a great laughter because of his wife’s evil ways﻿—why then, though in his heart he may believe her as innocent as you or I do now, it shall not be long before he shall put her away from him. Maybe he shall send her to the block.”

“God help me,” Cranmer said. “What a hellish scheme is this.”

He pondered for a while, standing upright and frailly thrusting his hand into his bosom.

===Chinese===

「哟，」拉塞尔斯说，「这位国王本不是个心志坚定之人。不过他终归是个男人，一个极重颜面、深怕讥嘲奚落的男人。倘若我们能使他见到——我承认，经他今夜那番作为，此事殊非易办，然我们仍旧办得到——倘若在那封信发出之前，我们能教他看见：因着王后的失德，举国上下正对他高声斥骂、纵声哄笑——那么，纵使他心中或仍信她无辜，一如你我此刻所信，也管保不出多久，他便会将她废去。说不定，还会把她送上断头台。」

「天主助我，」克兰默说。「这是何等阴毒的计谋。」

他直立不动，孱弱地将一只手探入怀中，沉吟了片刻。

===Original===

“You shall never get the King so to believe,” he said; “this is an idle invention. I will none of it.”

“Why, it may be done, I do believe,” Lascelles said, “and greatly it shall help us.”

“No, I will none of it,” the Archbishop said. “It is a foul scheme. Besides, you must have many witnesses.”

“I have some already,” Lascelles said, “and when we come to London Town I shall have many more. It was not for nothing that the Great Privy Seal commended me.”

===Chinese===

「汝断然劝不动国王来相信这话，」他说；「这不过是虚妄的杜撰。我决不与闻。」

「哟，此事未必办不成，我确信办得成，」拉塞尔斯说，「况且一旦办成，于我们大有裨益。」

「不，我决不与闻，」大主教说。「这是个卑污的计谋。再者，汝总须有众多见证之人。」

「我手里已有了几个，」拉塞尔斯说，「待我们进了伦敦城，还会多得许多。已故御玺大臣那番抬举，原不是无缘无故的。」

===Original===

“But to make the King,” Cranmer uttered, as if he were aghast and amazed, “to make the King﻿—this King who knoweth that his wife hath done no wrong﻿—who knoweth it so well as tonight he hath proven﻿—to make *him*, him, to put her away﻿ ﻿… why, the tiger is not so fell, nor the Egyptian worm preyeth not on its kind. This is an imagination so horrible﻿—”

“Please it your Grace,” Lascelles said softly, “what beast or brute hath your Grace ever seen to betray its kind as man will betray brother, son, father, or consort?”

The Archbishop raised his hands above his head.

“What lesser bull of the herd, or lesser ram, ever so played traitor to his leader as Brutus played to Caesar Julius? And these be times less noble.”

===Chinese===

「可是要教国王，」克兰默说道，仿佛惊骇交集，「要教国王——教这位明知王后未曾失德、且明之又明、今夜更已亲自证实的国王——要教*他*，教他，亲手把她废去……哟，猛虎尚不这般凶残，埃及的毒虫也从不吞噬同类。这念头实在可怖至极——」

「大人，」拉塞尔斯轻声说，「大人可曾见过何种畜生野兽，竟像人出卖兄弟、儿子、父亲或配偶那般，出卖自己的同类？」

大主教将双手举过了头顶。

「牛群之中可有次一等的公牛、羊群之中可有次一等的公羊，曾像布鲁图斯背叛恺撒那般，背叛自己的首领？何况当今之世，犹不及彼时高尚。」

## Part IV. The End of the Song / 第四部 歌之终章

### I

===Original===
The Queen was at Hampton, and it was the late autumn. She had been sad since they came from Pontefract, for it had seemed more than ever apparent that the King’s letter to Rome must be ever delayed in the sending. Daily, at night, the King swore with great oaths that the letter must be sent and his soul saved. He trembled to think that if then he died in his bed he must be eternally damned, and she added her persuasions, such as that each soul that died in his realms before that letter was sent went before the Throne of Mercy unshriven and unhouselled, so that their burden of souls grew very great. And in the midnights, the King would start up and cry that all was lost and himself accursed.

And it appeared that he and his house were accursed in these days, for when they were come back to Hampton, they found the small Prince Edward was very ill. He was swollen all over his little body, so that the doctors said it was a dropsy. But how, the King cried, could it be a dropsy in so young a child and one so grave and so nurtured and tended? Assuredly it must be some marvel wrought by the saints to punish him, or by the Fiend to tempt him. And so he would rave, and cast tremulous hands above his head. And he would say that God, to punish him, would have of him his dearest and best.

And when the Queen urged him, therefore, to make his peace with God, he would cry out that it was too late. God would make no peace with him. For if God were minded to have him at peace, wherefore would He not smoothe the way to this reconciliation with His vicegerent that sat at Rome in Peter’s chair? There was no smoothing of that way﻿—for every day there arose new difficulties and torments.

===Chinese===
王后驻跸在汉普顿宫，时节已是暮秋。自打从庞蒂弗拉克特回来，她便一直郁郁寡欢，因为国王那封致罗马的信看来比以往任何时候都更加注定要一拖再拖、发不出去。每天夜里，国王都指天誓日，说这封信必须发出去、他的灵魂必须得救。他一想到自己若在信未发出之前便死在床上、就此永堕地狱，便不寒而栗；她也从旁加添她的劝谏，譬如说，信未发出之前，他治下每有一个亡者，那灵魂便不曾忏悔、不曾领圣餐，就那样到仁慈的宝座前去了——于是他们两人灵魂上的重负愈积愈大。到了夜半，国王还会惊跳起来，喊叫说一切全完了、他自己受了诅咒。

这些时日，他和他的一族看起来也当真受了诅咒，因为回到汉普顿宫后，他们发现幼小的爱德华王子病得很重。他小小的浑身都肿了起来，御医们便说是水肿。可是，国王嚷道，这样年幼的孩子、教养看护得这样郑重周至，怎么会是水肿？这必定是诸圣施行灵异，来惩罚他；不然，便是恶魔作祟，来试探他。于是他呓语谵狂，把颤巍巍的双手举过头顶。他说，天主要为罚他，要向他索取他最亲爱、最宝贵的人。

王后劝他不如就此与天主修好，他却喊叫说太迟了。天主不会同他修好了。因为天主若真有意赐他平安，为何不肯为他与那位坐在罗马圣彼得宝座上的代理人修平这条和好之路？那条路是修不平的——因为每一天都有新的磨难与苦恼冒出来。

===Original===
The King o’ Scots would come into no alliance with him; the King of France would make no bid for the hand of his daughter Mary; it went ill with the Emperor in his fighting with the Princes of Almain and the Schmalkaldners, so that the Emperor would be of the less use as an ally against France and the Scots.

“Why!” he would cry to the Queen, “if God in His Heaven would have me make a peace with Rome, wherefore will He not give victory over a parcel of Lutheran knaves and swine? Wherefore will He not deliver into my hands these beggarly Scots and these atheists of France?”

At night the Queen would bring him round to vowing that first he would make peace with God and trust in His great mercy for a prosperous issue. But each morning he would be afraid for his sovereignty; a new letter would come from Norfolk, who had gone on an embassy to his French friends, believing fully that the King was minded to marry to one of them his daughter. But the French King was not ready to believe this. And the King’s eyes grew red and enraged; he looked no man in the face, not even the Queen, but glanced aside into corners, uttered blasphemies, and said that he﻿—he!﻿—was the head of the Church and would have no overlord.

===Chinese===
苏格兰国王不肯与他缔结任何同盟；法兰西国王不肯来求娶他的女儿玛丽公主；皇帝与阿尔迈因诸侯及施马尔卡尔登诸侯交战又战得不利，这样一来，皇帝作为对抗法兰西与苏格兰的盟友，用处便要大打折扣。

「哟！」他会朝王后喊道，「天上的天主若真要朕同罗马修好，为何不肯赐朕胜利，去降服那一伙路德派的宵小与猪猡？为何不肯把那些乞儿似的苏格兰人、把法兰西那班无神之徒，交到朕的手里？」

到了夜里，王后会劝得他回心转意，立誓说他要先与天主修好、再仰赖天主的大慈悲，等候事情顺遂有成。可每到清晨，他又为自己的君权担起忧来；诺福克新有书信到来——诺福克已出使他的法兰西朋友处，满心相信国王有意把女儿嫁与其中之一。可法兰西国王还不肯轻信此事。国王的眼睛愈来愈红、愈来愈怒；他不正眼看任何人，连王后也不，只斜睨着屋子角落，口出渎神之语，说他——他！——是教会的最高领袖，绝不受任何人的辖制。

===Original===
The Bishop Gardiner came up from his See in Winchester. But though he was the head of the Papist party in the realm, the Queen had little comfort in him. For he was a dark and masterful prelate, and never ceased to urge her to cast out Cranmer from his archbishopric and to give it to him. And with him the Lady Mary sided, for she would have Cranmer’s head before all things, since Cranmer it was that most had injured her mother. Moreover, he was so incessant in his urging the King to make an alliance with the Catholic Emperor that at last, about the time that Norfolk came back from France, the King was mightily enraged, so that he struck the Bishop of Winchester in the face, and swore that his friend the Kaiser was a rotten plank, since he could not rid himself of a few small knaves of Lutheran princes.

Thus for long the Queen was sad; the little Prince very sick; and the King ate no food, but sat gazing at the victuals, though the Queen cooked some messes for him with her own hand.

===Chinese===
温彻斯特主教加德纳从他的教区上来。他虽是国中教皇派一党的党魁，王后却从他身上得不到多少慰藉。因为他是个阴鸷而专横的主教，喋喋不休地撺掇王后把克兰默从大主教任上撵走，把那职位授给他。玛丽公主也站在他一边，因为她恨不得先取克兰默的脑袋，别的事都在其次——伤她母亲最深的，正是克兰默。再者，他一味纠缠不休，撺掇国王与旧教皇帝结盟，到末了，约莫在诺福克从法兰西回来的前后，国王勃然大怒，一巴掌打在温彻斯特主教脸上，赌咒说他的朋友皇帝是一块朽木板——连路德派诸侯里几个小小的宵小结伙，他都摆脱不掉。

就这样，长久以来王后郁郁不乐；小王子病得沉重；国王则饮食不进，只呆坐着瞪视盘中的肴馔，尽管王后亲手给他做了几样羹汤。

---

===Original===
One Sunday after evensong, at which Cranmer himself had read prayers, the King came nearly merrily to his supper.

“Ho, chuck,” he said, “you have your enemies. Here hath been Cranmer weeping to me with a parcel of tales writ on paper.”

He offered it to her to read, but she would not; for, she said, she knew well that she had many enemies, only, very safely she could trust her fame in her Lord’s hands.

“Why, you may,” he said, and sat him down at the table to eat, with the paper stuck in his belt. “Body o’ God!” he said. “If it had been any but Cranmer he had eaten bread in Hell this night. ’A wept and trembled! Body o’ God! Body o’ God!”

===Chinese===
一个礼拜日，晚祷方毕——当日的经文便是克兰默亲诵的——国王几乎是欢欢喜喜地来进晚餐。

「嗬，小乖乖，」他说，「你有你的仇人呢。克兰默方才到朕这里来，哭了一场，带着一叠写在纸上的鬼话。」

他递给她看，她却不肯看；她说，她很清楚自己仇人不少，只是她的声名尽可安然托付在她夫君的手里。

「哟，你尽可以放心，」他说，便在桌边坐下用饭，那张纸插在腰带里。「天主的圣体！」他说。「若是换了别人，今夜他早下地狱吃面包去了。他又是哭、又是抖！天主的圣体！天主的圣体！」

===Original===
And that night he was more merry before the fire than he had been for many weeks. He had in the music to play a song of his own writing, and afterwards he swore that next day he would ride to London, and then at his council send that which she would have sent to Rome.

“For, for sure,” he said, “there is no peace in this world for me save when I hear you pray. And how shall you pray well for me save in the old form and fashion?”

He lolled back in his chair and gazed at her.

“Why,” he said, “it is a proof of the great mercy of the Saviour that He sent you on earth in so fair a guise. For if you had not been so fair, assuredly I had not noticed you. Then would my soul have gone straightway to Hell.”

===Chinese===
那天夜里，他在炉火前的欢畅，是许多个星期以来所未有的。他召乐师进来，奏了一支他自己填词的歌；随后他起誓说，明日便骑马去伦敦，到枢密院把那件她要送往罗马的东西发出去。

「因为，确然，」他说，「这世上朕不得安宁，唯有听你祈祷的时候。可你若不用旧日的形式仪文，又怎能好好为朕祈祷呢？」

他懒洋洋地向后靠在椅中，凝望她。

「哟，」他说，「救主的大慈悲，从他把你送到世上来时生得这般标致，便可见一斑了。你若不这样标致，朕必定不曾留意到你。那朕的灵魂便径直下地狱去了。」

===Original===
And he called that the letter to Rome might be brought to him, and read it over in the firelight. He set it in his belt alongside the other paper, that next day when he came to London he might lay it in the hands of Sir Thomas Carter, that should carry it to Rome.

The Queen said: “Praise God!”

For though she was not set to believe that next day that letter would be sent, or for many days more, yet it seemed to her that by little and little she was winning him to her will.

===Chinese===
他又吩咐人把那封致罗马的信取来，就着火光通读了一遍。他把信插在腰带里，同那张纸放在一处，预备明日到了伦敦，便交到托马斯·卡特爵士（Sir Thomas Carter）手里，由他赍送往罗马。

王后说：「赞美天主！」

因为她固然还不肯相信那封信明日便会发出，也不肯相信往后多少天内便会发出，然而在她看来，她正一点一点地把他争取到她的意愿这边来。

### II

===Original===
Gardiner, Bishop of Winchester, had builded him a new tennis court in where his stables had been before poverty had caused him to sell the major part of his horseflesh. He called to him the Duke of Norfolk, who was of the Papist cause, and Sir Henry Wriothesley who was always betwixt and between, according as the cat jumped, to see this new building of his that was made of a roofed-in quadrangle where the stable doors were bricked up or barred to make the grille.

But though Norfolk and Wriothesley came very early in the afternoon, while it was yet light, to his house, they wasted most of the daylight hours in talking of things indifferent before they went to their inspection of this court. They stood talking in a long gallery beneath very high windows, and there were several chaplains and young priests and young gentlemen with them, and most of the talk was of a bear-baiting that there should be in Smithfield come Saturday. Sir Henry Wriothesley matched seven of his dogs against the seven best of the Duke’s, that they should the longer hold to the bear once they were on him, and most of the young gentlemen wagered for Sir Henry’s dogs that he had bred from a mastiff out of Portugal.

But when this talk had mostly died down, and when already twilight had long fallen, the Bishop said﻿—
===Chinese===
温彻斯特主教加德纳给自己新修了一座网球场，就造在他的马厩原先所在之处——自从穷困迫得他卖掉大半马匹，那地方便空了出来。他把归于教皇派一党的诺福克公爵、连同那位永远脚踩两头、专看风色行事的亨利·赖奥思利爵士，召来参观他这座新起的建筑：那是一座四面围拢、上了顶棚的院子，旧日马厩的门或用砖砌死、或装上栅条，便做成了格栅。

不过，诺福克与赖奥思利虽趁天色尚明、午后很早便到了他府上，却把大半白昼都消磨在闲谈些不相干的题面上，才去察看那座球场。他们站在一条长廊里、极高的大窗底下谈讲，身边随侍着几位司铎、年轻教士和年轻绅士，谈的大半是到了星期六，史密斯菲尔德（Smithfield）将要有的一场纵犬斗熊。亨利·赖奥思利爵士拿自己的七条狗对上公爵最好的七条，赌的是哪边的狗一旦扑上熊身，能咬住得更久；年轻绅士们大多把赌注押在赖奥思利爵士的狗上——那狗是他用一条葡萄牙獒犬配育出来的。

可等这场谈论大体平息、暮色落下已久，主教才说道——

===Original===
“Come, let us visit this new tennis place of mine. I think I shall show you somewhat that you have not before seen.”

He bade, however, his gentlemen and priests to stay where they were, for they had all many times seen the court or building. When he led the way, prelatical and black, for the Duke and Wriothesley, into the lower corridors of his house, the priests and young gentlemen bowed behind his back, one at the other.

In the courtyard there were four hounds of a heavy and stocky breed that came bounding and baying all round them, so that it was only by vigilance that Gardiner could save Wriothesley’s shins, for he was a man that all dogs and children hated.
===Chinese===
「来，去看看我这座新网球场。我想我能给汝等看一样先前不曾见过的东西。」

然而他却吩咐自己的绅士与教士们留在原地，因为这球场也罢、建筑也罢，他们早已见过许多回了。当他一身黑衣、十足的主教气派，为公爵和赖奥思利在前引路，走进宅子下层的回廊时，教士与年轻绅士们便在他背后一躬接一躬地行礼。

庭院里养着四条身躯笨重、腿脚粗壮的猎犬，蹦跳着、吠叫着将他们团团围住，害得加德纳唯有处处留神，才保得住赖奥思利的小腿——因为这个人，天下的狗和孩童见了都要憎恨。

===Original===
“Sirs,” the Bishop said, “these dogs that ye see and hear will let no man but me﻿—not even my grooms or stablemen﻿—pass this yard. I have bred them to that so I may be secret when I will.”

He set the key in the door that was in the bottom wall of the court.

“There is no other door here save that which goes into the stable where the grille is. There I have a door to enter and fetch out the balls that pass there.”

In the court itself it was absolute blackness.
===Chinese===
「列位，」主教说，「汝等眼见耳闻的这些狗，除了我，不放任何一个人过这院子——连我的马夫马童也在内。我把它们教养成了这样，为的是我何时想隐秘，便能隐秘。」

他把钥匙插进球场底端墙上那扇门的锁眼里。

「这里再没有别的门，只有通进马厩的那扇，格栅便设在那里。我在那边留了一个进去的门，好把打过去的球取出来。」

球场本身是彻头彻尾的漆黑。

===Original===
“I trow we may talk very well without lights,” he said. “Come into this far corner.”

Yet, though there was no fear of being overheard, each of these three stole almost on tiptoe and held his breath, and in the dark and shadowy place they made a more dark and more shadowy patch with their heads all close together.

Suddenly it was as if the Bishop dropped the veil that covered his passions.
===Chinese===
「我确信，不用灯火咱们也尽谈得成，」他说。「到这个远角上来。」

然而，虽则全无隔墙有耳之虞，这三人却个个几乎踮起脚尖、屏住呼吸，在这幽晦的所在，把脑袋凑作一堆，凑成了一团更黑、更暗的影子。

霎时间，仿佛主教把蒙在他激愤之上的那幅面纱甩落了下来。

===Original===
“I may well build tennis courts,” he said, and his voice had a ring of wild and malignant passion. “I may well build courts for tennis play. Nothing else is left for me to do.”

In the blackness no word came from his listeners.

“You too may do the like,” the Bishop said. “But I would you do it quickly, for soon neither the one nor the other of you but will be stripped so bare that you shall not have enough to buy balls with.”

The Duke made an impatient sound like a drawing in of his breath, but still he spoke no word.

“I tell you, both of you,” the Bishop’s voice came, “that all of us have been fooled. Who was it that helped to set on high this one that now presses us down? I did! I!﻿ ﻿…
===Chinese===
「我大可以修修网球场，」他说，嗓音里带着一股狂乱而恶毒的激愤。「我大可以修些球场来打网球。除此之外，再没有什么事留给我做了。」

黑暗里，听他说话的人没有一个出声。

「汝等两位也尽可以照此行事，」主教说。「不过我劝汝等快快动手，因为用不了多久，汝等两位谁都逃不过被剥得精光，连买球的钱也不够了。」

公爵发出一声不耐烦的声响，像倒抽了一口气，却仍不发一言。

「我告诉汝等二人，」主教的声音传来，「咱们全都被愚弄了。是谁帮着把如今压在咱们头上的这一个捧上高位的？是我干的！是我！……

===Original===
“It was I that called the masque at my house where first the King did see her. It was I that advised her how to bear herself. And what gratitude has been shown me? I have been sent to sequester myself in my see; I have been set to gnaw my fingers as they had been old bones thrown to a dog. Truly, no juicy meats have been my share. Yet it was I set this woman where she sits.﻿ ﻿…”

“I too have my griefs,” the Duke of Norfolk’s voice came.

“And I, God wot,” came Wriothesley’s.

“Why, you have been fooled,” Gardiner’s voice; “and well you know it. For who was it that sent you both, one after the other, into France thinking that you might make a match between the Lady Royal and the Duke of Orleans?﻿—Who but the Queen?﻿—For well she knew that ye loved the French and their King as they had been your brothers. And well we know now that never in the mind of her, nor in that of the King whom she bewitches and enslaves, was there any thought save that the Lady Royal should be wedded to Spain. So ye are fooled.”
===Chinese===
「是我，在我府上张罗起那场假面剧，国王才头一回见到她。是我，教她该如何自处进退。可我得了什么报答？我被打发回自己的主教辖区去幽居；我被人安置下来啃自己的手指头，仿佛它们是扔给狗的老骨头。说真的，多汁的肉从来轮不到我一份。可把这个女人安置到她如今这个座位上的，是我。……」

「我也有我的苦楚，」诺福克公爵的声音传来。

「我也有，天知道，」这是赖奥思利的声音。

「哟，汝等受愚弄了，」这是加德纳的声音；「而且汝等自己心里也清楚。是谁把汝等二人一个接一个派往法兰西，叫汝等以为能为玛丽公主与奥尔良公爵结下一门亲事？——不是王后又是谁？——因为她深知汝等爱法兰西人、爱他们的国王，爱得如同自家兄弟。而咱们如今也看得分明：在她心里、在她以妖术蛊惑役使着的那位国王心里，除让玛丽公主嫁往西班牙之外，从来不曾有过半点别的念头。所以汝等是受愚弄了。」

===Original===
He let his voice sink low; then he raised it again﻿—

“Fooled! Fooled! Fooled! You two and I. For who of your friends the French shall ever believe again word that you utter. And all your goods and lands this Queen will have for the Church, so that she may have utter power with a parcel of new shavelings, that will not withstand her. So all the land will come in to her leash.﻿ ﻿… We are fooled and ruined, ye and I alike.”

“Well, we know this,” the Duke’s voice said distastefully. “You have no need to rehearse griefs that too well we feel. There is no lord, either of our part or of the other, that would not have her down.”

“But what will ye do?” Gardiner said.
===Chinese===
他让声音沉低下去；随后又扬了起来——

「愚弄了！愚弄了！愚弄了！汝等两个，加上我。因为汝等那些法兰西朋友，往后谁还肯再信汝等吐出的一个字。汝等的全部财物田产，这王后都要拿去归给教会，好让她挟着一帮新剃度的毛头神父独揽大权——那帮人是不敢违拗她的。这样，整片国土都要被她收上同一条皮带。……咱们受愚弄了、毁掉了，汝等和我一个样。」

「这个咱们知道，」公爵的声音带着嫌恶说。「这些苦楚咱们受得太清楚了，用不着汝来一一数说。无论咱们这一边的还是那一边的勋贵，没有一个不想把她拉下马来。」

「可汝等究竟打算怎么办？」加德纳说。

===Original===
“Nothing may we do!” the voice of Wriothesley with its dismal terror came to their ears. “The King is too firmly her Highness’s man.”

“Her ‘Highness,’ ” the Bishop mocked him with a bitter scorn. “I believe you would yet curry favour with this Queen of straw.”

“It is a man’s province to be favourable in the eyes of his Prince,” the buried voice came again. “If I could win her favour I would. But well ye know there is no way.”

“Ye ha’ mingled too much with Lutheran swine,” the Bishop said. “Now it is too late for you.”

“So it is,” Wriothesley said. “I think you, Bishop, would have done it too had you been able to make your account of it.”
===Chinese===
「咱们什么也办不成！」赖奥思利的声音带着凄惨的恐惧，传进他们耳中。「国王太死心塌地，做定了她那位陛下的人。」

「她『陛下』，」主教带着刻毒的轻蔑讥诮他。「我看汝到如今，还想向这个稻草扎的王后讨好献媚呢。」

「在自家君王眼中得宠，本是做臣子的本分，」那闷在喉咙里的声音又响起来。「我若能博得她的恩宠，自然肯博。可汝等清楚得很，没有那条路。」

「汝同路德派的猪猡厮混得太多了，」主教说。「如今再想回头，太迟了。」

「正是如此，」赖奥思利说。「我看汝，主教，若当初能盘算出个划算来，也是会照样行事的。」

===Original===
The Bishop snarled invisibly.

But the voice of Norfolk came malignantly upon them.

“This is all of a piece with your silly schemings. Did I come here to hear ye wrangle? It is peril enow to come here. What will ye do?”

“I will make a pact with him of the other side?” the Bishop said.

“Misery!” the Duke said; “did I come here to hear this madness? You and Cranmer have sought each other’s heads this ten years. Will you seek his aid now? What may he do? He is as rotten a reed as thou or Wriothesley.”
===Chinese===
主教在黑暗里无形地狺狺低吼了一声。

可诺福克的声音已怀着恶意压到他们头上。

「这同汝等那套愚蠢的计谋正是一路货色。我到这儿来，是听汝等拌嘴的吗？光是到这儿来就够凶险的了。汝等究竟干是不干？」

「我要同对面那一边的人订一份盟约？」主教说。

「作孽！」公爵说；「我到这儿来，是听这种疯话的吗？汝同克兰默互索对方的脑袋，已经索了这十年了。如今汝倒要向他求援？他能成什么事？他同汝、同赖奥思利一样，是根烂芦苇。」

===Original===
The Bishop cried suddenly with a loud voice﻿—

“Ho, there! Come you out!”

Norfolk set his hand to his sword and so did Wriothesley. It was in both their minds, as it were one thought, that if this was a treason of the Bishop’s he should there die.

From the blackness of the wall sides where the grille was there came the sound of a terroring lock and a creaking door.

“God!” Norfolk said; “who is this?”

There came the sound of breathing of one man who walked with noiseless shoes.
===Chinese===
主教陡然放开嗓子喊道——

「嚯，那边！汝给我出来！」

诺福克把手按上剑柄，赖奥思利也照样做了。两人心里仿佛共着同一个念头：倘若这是主教设下的一场叛逆，他就该当场死在这里。

从装着格栅的那几面墙边的黑暗里，传来一声骇人的锁响和一扇门的吱呀声。

「天主！」诺福克说；「这是谁？」

随即传来一个人的呼吸声，那人穿着不出声的鞋，一路走来。

===Original===
“Have you heard enow to make you believe that these lords’ hearts are true to the endeavour of casting the Queen down?”

“I have heard enow,” a smooth voice said. “I never thought it had been otherwise.”

“Who is this?” Wriothesley said. “I will know who this is that has heard us.”

“You fool,” Gardiner said; “this man is of the other side.”

“They have come to you!” Norfolk said.

“To whom else should we come,” the voice answered.
===Chinese===
「汝听够了没有，足以叫汝相信，这几位大人的心当真系在推倒王后这桩事业上？」

「我听够了，」一个圆滑的声音说。「我从不曾以为会是别的情形。」

「这是谁？」赖奥思利说。「这个听了咱们说话的人究竟是谁，我必要知道。」

「汝这蠢货，」加德纳说；「这人是对面那一边的。」

「他们竟寻到汝这里来了！」诺福克说。

「不寻到您这里，我们还能寻谁去，」那声音答道。

===Original===
A subtler silence of agitation and thought was between these two men. At last Gardiner said﻿—

“Tell these lords what you would have of us?”

“We would have these promises,” the voice said; “first, of you, my Lord Duke, that if by our endeavours your brother’s child be brought to a trial for unchastity you will in no wise aid her at that trial with your voice or your encouragement.”

“A trial!” and “Unchastity!” the Duke said. “This is a winter madness. Ye know that my niece﻿—St. Kevin curse her for it﻿—is as chaste as the snow.”

“So was your other niece, Anne Boleyn, for all you knew, yet you dogged her to death,” Gardiner said. “Then you plotted with Papists; now it is the turn of the Lutherans. It is all one, so we are rid of this pest.”
===Chinese===
一种更为微妙的、翻腾着思虑的沉默，横在这两人之间。终于，加德纳说——

「告诉这几位大人，汝等要从我们这里要什么？」

「我们要的是这几项承诺，」那声音说。「第一项，向您，公爵大人：倘若经我们的努力，您兄弟的女儿被以不贞之罪付诸审判，在那审判上，您决计不以声援或鼓舞相助。」

「审判！」「不贞！」公爵说。「这是冬天里发的癫狂。汝等知道我的侄女——愿圣凯文（St. Kevin）为这个咒她——贞洁得如同白雪。」

「就汝所知，汝那另一个侄女安妮·博林又何尝不贞洁，可汝照样穷追不舍，把她逼到了死路上，」加德纳说。「那时候汝等同教皇派合谋；如今轮到路德派了。横竖是一回事，只要咱们除掉这个祸害。」

===Original===
“Well, I will promise it,” the Duke said. “Ye knew I would. It was not worth while to ask me.”

“Secondly,” the voice said, “of you, my Lord Duke, we would have this service: that you should swear your niece is a much older woman than she looks. Say, for instance, that she was in truth not the eleventh but the second child of your brother Edmund. Say that, out of vanity, to make herself seem more forward with the learned tongues when she was a child, she would call herself her younger sister that died in childbed.”

“But wherefore?” the Duke said.

“Why,” Gardiner answered, “this is a very subtle scheme of this gentleman’s devising. He will prove against her certain lewdnesses when she was a child in your mother’s house. If then she was a child of ten or so, knowing not evil from good, this might not undo her. But if you can make her seem then eighteen or twenty it will be enough to hang her.”
===Chinese===
「好吧，我答应，」公爵说。「汝等原就知道我会答应的。问我一声都是多余。」

「第二项，」那声音说，「还要求您效一臂之力，公爵大人：您须起誓说，您的侄女是个比她相貌老得多的女人。譬如说，她其实并非您兄弟埃德蒙（Edmund）的第十一个孩子，而是第二个。又说，她出于虚荣，为叫自己幼年时在学问语言上显得更为早慧，惯常自称是那个死于产褥的妹妹。」

「可这是为何？」公爵说。

「哟，」加德纳答道，「这是这位先生构想出来的一条十分精微的计策。他要证明她幼年在汝母亲府上时，曾有过几桩淫行。倘若她那时不过十岁上下，善恶尚且不辨，这未必毁得掉她。可汝若能叫她在那时显得已有十八岁、二十岁，那就尽够把她送上绞架了。」

===Original===
Norfolk reflected.

“Well, I will say I heard that of her age,” he said; “but ye had best get nurses and women to swear to these things.”

“We have them now,” the voice said. “And it will suffice if your Grace will say that you heard these things of old of your brother. For your Grace will judge this woman.”

“Very willingly I will,” Norfolk said; “for if I do not soon, she will utterly undo both me and all my friends.”
===Chinese===
诺福克沉吟起来。

「好吧，我就说我曾听说过她年纪的这些话，」他说；「不过汝等顶好去寻些乳母妇人来，叫她们对这些事起誓作证。」

「我们如今已经有了，」那声音说。「只要大人肯说一句，这些事您早年便听您兄弟讲过，也就尽够了。因为将来审判这女人的，正是大人。」

「我万般情愿，」诺福克说；「因为我不赶紧行事，她就要把我和我所有的朋友一齐毁个干净。」

===Original===
He reflected again.

“Those things will I do and more yet, if you will.”

“Why, that will suffice,” the voice said. It took a new tone in the darkness.

“Now for you, Sir Henry Wriothesley,” it said. “These simple things you shall promise. Firstly, since you have the ear of the Mayor of London you shall advise him in no way to hinder certain meetings of Lutherans that I shall tell you of later. And, though it is your province so to do, you shall in no wise hinder a certain master printer from printing what broadsides and libels he will against the Queen. For it is essential, if this project is to grow and flourish, that it shall be spread abroad that the Queen did bewitch the King to her will on that night at Pontefract that you remember, when she had her cousin in her bedroom. So broadsides shall be made alleging that by sorcery she induced the King to countenance his own shame. And we have witnesses to swear that it was by appointment, not by chance, that she met with Culpepper upon the moorside. But all that we will have of you is that you will promise these two things﻿—that the Lutherans may hold certain meetings and the broadsides be printed.”
===Chinese===
他又沉吟了一番。

「这些事我都肯做，还肯做得更多，只要汝等愿意。」

「哟，这就尽够了，」那声音说。它在黑暗里换上了一副新的腔调。

「如今轮到您了，亨利·赖奥思利爵士，」它说。「您要应承下这几件简单的事。第一，既然伦敦市长肯听您的话，您须劝他，无论如何不可去阻挠某些路德派的集会——是哪些集会，我日后再告知您。第二，纵然干预本是您的职分，您也决计不可去阻挠某一位印书坊主，任他印什么攻讦王后的传单与谤文。因为，这桩大计要生长兴旺，就必须把这样一层意思播扬开去：王后在庞蒂弗拉克特的那一晚——你们记得的那一晚，她把表兄留在卧房里的那一晚——曾用妖术把国王迷得唯她的意愿是从。所以要造出传单来，宣称她以妖术诱使国王甘忍自身的奇耻大辱。我们还有证人可以起誓：她同卡尔佩珀在荒坡上的相会，是有约在先，并非出于偶遇。可我们要从您这里得到的，无非是您应承这两件事——路德派可以举行那几场集会，传单可以付印。」

===Original===
“Those I will promise,” came in Wriothesley’s buried voice.

“Then I will no more of you,” the other’s words came. They heard his hands feeling along the wall till he came to the door by which he had entered. The Bishop followed him, to let him out by a little door he had had opened for that one night, into the street.

When he came back to the other two and unfolded to them what was the scheme of the Archbishop’s man, they agreed that it was a very good plan. Then they fell to considering whether it should not serve their turn to betray this plan at once to the Queen. But they agreed that, if they preserved the Queen, they would be utterly ruined, as they were like to be now, whereas, if it succeeded, they would be much the better off. And, even if it failed, they lost nothing, for it would not readily be believed that they had aided Lutherans, and there were no letters or writings.

So they agreed to abide honourably by their promises﻿—and very certain they were that if clamour enough could be raised against the Queen, the King would be bound into putting her away, though it were against his will.
===Chinese===
「这几件我答应，」这是赖奥思利那闷在喉咙里的声音。

「那么，我不再向您多要什么了，」那人的话音传来。他们听见他的双手沿着墙壁一路摸索，直到他走到进来的那扇门跟前。主教跟着他，把他送出一扇小门——那扇门是他专为这一夜叫人打开的——门外便是街道。

等他回到另外两人跟前，把大主教手下那人的计策一一摊开讲明，他们同声称许这是一条极好的计。随后他们又盘算起来：把这计策即刻出首给王后，是否反倒更合他们的用处。可他们一致认定：若保全了王后，他们便会像眼下多半难逃的那样彻底倾覆；而若这计策得成，他们的境遇便会大大改观。纵然计策败了，他们也没有半点亏损，因为世人不会轻易相信他们曾援助过路德派，何况又没有书信字据。

于是他们约定，堂堂正正地信守各自的诺言——而且他们深信不疑：只要能鼓起足够的喧腾，群起攻讦王后，国王便不得不将她休弃，哪怕这有违他自己的心意。

### III

===Original===
In the Master Printer Badge’s house﻿—and he was the uncle of Margot and of the young Poins﻿—there was a great and solemn dissertation towards. For word had been brought that certain strangers come on an embassy from the Duke of Cleves were minded to hear how the citizens of London﻿—or at any rate those of them that held German doctrines﻿—bore themselves towards Schmalkaldnerism and the doctrines of Luther.

It was understood that these strangers were of very high degree﻿—of a degree so high that they might scarce be spoken to by the meaner sort. And for many days messengers had been going between the house of the Archbishop at Lambeth and that of the Master Printer, to school him how this meeting must be conducted.

His old father was by that time dead﻿—having died shortly after his granddaughter Margot had been put away from the Queen’s Court﻿—so that the houseplace was clear. And of all the old furnishings none remained. There were presses all round the wall, and lockers for men to sit upon. The table had been cleared away into the printer’s chapel; a lectern stood a-midmost of the room, and before the hearth-place, in the very ingle, there was set the great chair in which aforetimes the old man had sat so long.

Early that evening, though already it was dusk, the body of citizens were assembled. Most of them had haggard faces, for the times were evil for men of their persuasion, and nearly all of them were draped in black after the German fashion among Lutherans of that day. They ranged themselves on the lockers along the wall, and with set faces, in a funereal row, they awaited the coming of this great stranger. There were no Germans amongst them, for so, it was given out, he would have it﻿—either because he would not be known by name or for some other reason.

===Chinese===
在印书坊主巴奇——玛戈与小普因斯的叔父——的宅子里，一场盛大而庄严的讲论正在酝酿。因为已有话传来：几位自克莱沃公爵处奉使而来的外邦客人，有意听一听伦敦的市民——或者至少是其中信奉日耳曼教义的那部分人——对施马尔卡尔登主义与路德的教义作何观感。

众人都晓得，这几位外邦客人身份极高——高到微末之辈几乎连向他们搭话也不配。许多天来，信使一直往返于兰贝斯的大主教宅邸与印书坊主的宅子之间，教导他这场会面必须如何张罗。

他的老父那时已经去世——就在孙女玛戈被逐出王后宫中之后不久亡故——正屋因此腾了出来。旧日的家具陈设一件也不剩了。四壁沿墙摆满印刷机，还有供人坐的长柜。餐桌已被搬进印书房；一座读经台立在屋子正中，火塘前、恰恰在炉隅最深处，安放着老人昔年久久枯坐的那把大椅。

那晚时候还早，虽则天色已暮，市民们便已齐聚一堂。他们大多面容枯槁，因为时世对他们这般信仰的人甚为险恶；他们几乎个个遵当日路德派中的日耳曼风俗，通身裹着黑衣。他们在沿墙的长柜上依次坐定，板着面孔，排成送葬一般的一列，等候那位贵客驾临。他们中间没有一个日耳曼人，因为——据传出的说法——这是他本人的意思：或因他不愿名姓为人所知，或另有别的缘故。

===Original===
The Master Printer, in the pride of his craft, wore his apron. He stood in the centre of the room facing the hearth-place; his huge arms were bare﻿—for bare-armed he always worked﻿—his black beard was knotted into little curls, his face was so broad that you hardly remarked that his nose was hooked like an owl’s beak. And about the man there was an air of sombreness and mystery. He had certain papers on his lectern, and several sheets of the great Bible that he was then printing by the Archbishop’s license and command. They sang all together and with loud voices the canticle called “A Refuge fast is God the Lord.”

Then, with huge gestures of his hands, he uttered the words﻿—

“This is the very word of God,” and began to read from the pages of his Bible. He read first the story of David and Saul, his great voice trembling with ecstasy.

“This David is our King,” he said. “This Saul that he slew is the Beast of Rome. The Solomon that cometh after shall be the gracious princeling that ye wot of, for already he is wise beyond his years and beyond most grown men.”

===Chinese===
印书坊主怀着对本行当的自豪，系着他的围裙。他站在屋子正中，面朝火塘；一双巨臂袒露着——他做工向来赤臂——黑胡须挽成一个个小卷，脸盘阔大得叫人几乎不曾留意他的鼻子弯得像枭鸟的喙。而这人周身笼着一股阴沉而神秘的气派。他的读经台上放着几份文书，还有几页大《圣经》——那部大《圣经》，他当时正奉大主教的特许与谕令赶印。他们齐声高唱了那首名为《天主是我坚固避难所》的圣歌。

随后，他双手做出阔大的姿势，出言道——

「这正是天主的圣言，」说罢便开始诵读他《圣经》的书页。他先读了大卫与扫罗的故事，洪大的嗓音因狂喜而颤动。

「这大卫，便是咱们的国王，」他说。「他所杀的这扫罗，便是罗马的兽。日后要来的那位所罗门，便是汝等知晓的那位天纵小亲王，因为他年纪虽幼，智慧已超出他的年岁、更超出大多数成人了。」

===Original===
The citizens around the walls cried “Amen.” And because the strangers tarried to come, he called to his journeymen that stood in the inner doorway to bring him the sheets of the Bible whereon he had printed the story of Ehud and Eglon.

“This king that ye shall hear of as being slain,” he cried out, “is that foul bird the Kaiser Carl, that harries the faithful in Almain. This good man that shall slay him is some German lord. Who he shall be we know not yet; maybe it shall be this very stranger that tonight shall sit to hear us.”

His brethren muttered a low, deep, and uniform prayer that soon, soon the Lord should send them this boon.

But he had not got beyond the eleventh verse of this history before there came from without a sound of trumpets, and through the windows the light of torches and the scarlet of the guard that, it was said, the King had sent to do honour to this stranger.

===Chinese===
沿墙围坐的市民们高呼「阿门」。因为贵客迟迟未至，他便唤那立在里间门口的伙计，把他印着以笏（Ehud）与伊矶伦（Eglon）故事的那几页《圣经》取来。

「汝等将要听到的这位被弑之王，」他高声道，「便是那只恶鸟皇帝卡尔，他在阿尔迈因苛虐信众。将来杀他的那位义士，是某位日耳曼贵胄。究竟是谁，咱们眼下还不知道；兴许就是今夜要坐在这里听咱们说话的这位贵客本人。」

他的会中弟兄们低低地、深沉而整齐地咕哝着一句祷告，求主早早地、早早地把这个恩赐降给他们。

可这段史事他才念到第十一节，外头便传来了号角之声，窗上映出火炬的光和卫队的猩红衣色——据说，那卫队是国王派来向这位贵客致敬的。

===Original===
“Come in, be ye who ye may!” the printer cried to the knockers at his door.

There entered the hugest masked man that they ever had seen. All in black he was, and horrifying and portentous he strode in. His sleeves and shoulders were ballooned after the German fashion, his sword clanked on the tiles. He was a vision of black, for his mask that appeared as big as another man’s garment covered all his face, though they could see he had a grey beard when sitting down. He gazed at the fire askance.

He said﻿—his voice was heavy and husky﻿—

“*Gruesset Gott*,” and those of the citizens that had painfully attained to so much of that tongue answered him with﻿—

===Chinese===
「进来，随汝是何等人物！」印书坊主朝门上的叩门人喊道。

进来的是他们生平所见最魁伟的一个戴面具的人。他一身黑衣，阔步而入，骇人而森然，仿佛携着凶兆。他的双袖与双肩照日耳曼式样鼓胀如球，佩剑在方砖地上铿然作响。他整个人便是一团黑影，因为那副瞧着足有旁人一件衣裳那么大的面具罩住了他整张脸——只是待他坐下时，他们看得出他蓄着一部灰白胡须。他斜睨着炉火。

他说——嗓音沉重而沙哑——

「*Gruesset Gott*（愿天主赐安），」市民中那些费了大劲才学得这几句该种语言的人，便以下面一句应答他——

===Original===
“*Lobet den Herr im Himmels Reich!*”

He had with him one older man that wore a half-mask, and was trembling and clean-shaven, and one younger, that was English, to act as interpreter when it was needed. He was clean-shaven, too, and in the English habit he appeared thin and tenuous. They said he was a gentleman of the Archbishop’s, and that his name was Lascelles.

He opened the meeting with saying that these great strangers were come from beyond the seas, and would hear answers to certain questions. He took a paper from his pouch and said that, in order that he might stick to the points that these strangers would know of, he had written down those questions on that paper.

“How say ye, masters?” he finished. “Will ye give answers to these questions truly, and of your knowledge?”

===Chinese===
「*Lobet den Herr im Himmels Reich!*（当在天国称颂主！）」

他随身带着一个年长些的人，戴着半截面具，身子发颤，脸剃得干干净净；又带着一个年轻些的，是英格兰人，必要时充任通译。这年轻人也剃得脸净，一身英格兰装束，显得瘦削单薄。据说他是大主教手下的一位绅士，名叫拉塞尔斯。

会议由他开场：这几位贵客来自海外，要听若干问题的答话。他从随身皮袋里取出一纸，说为使自己紧扣这几位贵客想要知悉的各要点，他已把那些问题写在纸上了。

「诸位师傅意下如何？」他末了说。「可愿就这些问题据实作答、答以各人所知？」

===Original===
“Aye will we,” the printer said, “for to that end we are gathered here. Is it not so, my masters?”

And the assembly answered﻿—

“Aye, so it is.”

Lascelles read from his paper:

===Chinese===
「愿意，」印书坊主说，「咱们正是为此聚集在这里。可不是么，列位师傅？」

众人齐声应道——

「是，正是如此。」

拉塞尔斯照着纸念道：

===Original===
“How is it with this realm of England?”

The printer glanced at the paper that was upon his lectern. He made answer﻿—

“Well! But not over well!”

And at these words Lascelles feigned surprise, lifting his well-shapen and white hand in the air.

===Chinese===
「英格兰这个国度，光景如何？」

印书坊主朝读经台上摊着的那张纸瞥了一眼。他答道——

「好！只是算不得十分好！」

一听这话，拉塞尔斯便佯作惊讶，把一只形态好看的白手举到半空。

===Original===
“How is this that ye say?” he uttered. “Are ye all of this tale?”

A deep “Aye!” came from all these chests. There was one old man that could never keep still. He had huge limbs, a great ruffled poll of grizzling hair, and his legs that were in jerkins of red leather kicked continuously in little convulsions. He peered every minute at some new thing, very closely, holding first his tablets so near that he could see only with one eye, then the whistle that hung round his neck, then a little piece of paper that he took from his poke. He cried out in a deep voice﻿—“Aye! aye! Not over well. Witchcraft and foul weather and rocks, my mates and masters all!” so that he appeared to be a seaman﻿—and indeed he traded to the port of Antwerp, in the Low Countries, where he had learned of some of the Faith.

“Why,” Lascelles said, “be ye not contented with our goodly King?”

“Never was a better since Solomon ruled in Jewry,” the shipman cried out.

===Chinese===
「汝等这话从何说起？」他道。「汝等众口一词，都是这般说法么？」

一声深沉的「是！」从所有人的胸膛里发出来。有一个老头儿怎么也安坐不住。他四肢魁伟，一头蓬乱的花白头发，裹在红皮裤里的两条腿不住地小抽小颤、踢个不停。他每一分钟都要凑得极近去细看一样新鲜东西：先是那块举得只剩一只眼看得见的写字板，接着是挂在脖颈上的哨子，再是一张从随身小袋里掏出来的纸片。他扯着深嗓门喊道——「是！是！算不得十分好。妖术、坏天气，还有礁石，我的伙伴们、列位师傅！」听这口气他像个跑海的——他也确实往安特卫普（Antwerp）港做买卖，那港在尼德兰地界，他在那里学得了几分旧教的道理。

「哟，」拉塞尔斯说，「咱们这位贤明的国王，汝等还不称心么？」

「自所罗门王君临犹太地以来，再没出过比他更好的国王，」那海客高声道。

===Original===
“Is it, then, the Lords of the King’s Council that ye are discontented with?”

“Nay, they are goodly men, for they are of the King’s choosing,” one answered﻿—a little man with a black pill-hat.

“Why, speak through your leader,” the stranger said heavily from the hearth-place. “Here is too much skimble-skamble.” The old man beside him leaned over his chair-back and whispered in his ear. But the stranger shook his head heavily. He sat and gazed at the brands. His great hands were upon his knees, pressed down, but now and again they moved as if he were in some agony.

“It is well that ye do as the Lord commandeth,” Lascelles said; “for in Almain, whence he cometh, there is wont to be a great order and observance.” He held his paper up again to the light. “Master Printer, answer now to this question: Find ye aught amiss with the judges and justices of this realm?”

===Chinese===
「那么，汝等心怀不满的，莫非是国王枢密院的诸勋？」

「不是，诸勋都是贤良人等，因为他们出自国王的拣选，」一个人答道——那是个戴黑色小圆帽的矮小汉子。

「哟，说话须经由汝等的首领，」那贵客在火塘边沉沉地说。「这里头乱七八糟、没个章法。」他身旁那个老人俯在他椅背后，凑着他耳朵低语。可那贵客沉重地摇了摇头。他坐着，凝望炉中的柴薪。一双大手按在膝上，却时不时挪动，仿佛正受着某种痛楚的煎熬。

「汝等遵主的吩咐行事，很好，」拉塞尔斯说；「因为在他所自来的阿尔迈因，向来讲究极大的秩序与仪节。」他又把那纸举向亮处。「印书坊主，现在回答这个问题：汝觉得这国度的法官与治安官，有什么不妥么？」

===Original===
“Nay; they do judge indifferent well betwixt cause and cause,” the printer answered from his paper.

“Or with the serjeants, the apparitors, the collectors of taxes, or the Parliament men?”

“These, too, perform indifferent well their appointed tasks,” the printer said gloomily.

“Or is it with the Church of this realm that ye find fault?”

===Chinese===
「没有；他们一桩桩案子断下来，断得还算公允，」印书坊主照着他那张纸答道。

「那么，状师、执达吏、税吏们，或是国会里那班人，可有可指摘的？」

「这些人也都把他们分内的差事办得还算过得去，」印书坊主阴郁地说。

「抑或，汝等挑错的，乃是这国度的教会？」

===Original===
“Body of God!” the stranger said heavily.

“Nay!” the printer answered, “for the supreme head of that Church is the King, a man learned before all others in the law of God; such a King as speaketh as though he were that mouthpiece of the Most High that the Antichrist at Rome claimeth to be.”

“Is it, then, with the worshipful the little Prince of Wales that ye are discontented?” Lascelles read, and the printer answered that there was not such another Prince of his years for promise and for performance, too, in all Christendom.

The stranger said from the hearth-place﻿—

===Chinese===
「天主的圣体！」那贵客沉沉地说。

「不是！」印书坊主答道，「因为这教会的最高领袖便是国王——一个在天主律法上学问超乎众人的君主；这样一位国王，出言吐语，俨然便是那至高者的喉舌——正是罗马的敌基督所自称充当的那个。」

「那么，汝等不满的，莫非是那位可敬的威尔士小亲王？」拉塞尔斯念道；印书坊主答说，纵观整个基督世界，论前程、也论实绩，与他同龄的亲王里再寻不出第二个。

那贵客在火塘边说道——

===Original===
“Well! we are commended,” and his voice was bitter and ironical.

“How is it, then,” Lascelles read on, “that ye say all is not over well in the land?”

The printer’s gloomy and black features glared with a sudden rage.

“How should all be well with a land,” he cried, “where in high places reigns harlotry?” He raised his clenched fist on high and glared round upon his audience. “Corruption that reacheth round and about and down till it hath found a seedbed even in this poor house of my father’s? Or if it is well with this land now, how shall it continue well when witchcraft rules near the King himself, and the Devil of Rome hath there his emissaries.”

===Chinese===
「好！咱们倒蒙了一番夸奖，」他的嗓音苦涩而讥诮。

「既然如此，」拉塞尔斯接着念道，「汝等又说国内诸事算不得十分好，这却是怎么说？」

印书坊主那张阴沉黝黑的面孔骤然迸出怒火。

「一个国度，」他喊道，「高官显位之上竟由淫乱当道，怎还能指望它诸事皆好？」他高举攥紧的拳头，怒目环视他的听众。「那腐坏四处蔓延、层层向下，直到连我可怜的亡父这座宅子里都寻着了一块苗床，是不是？这国土眼下纵然还好，可一旦妖术在国王本人近旁执掌权柄、罗马的魔鬼在那里安插下他的密使，它又怎能好得长久？」

===Original===
A chitter of sound came from his audience, so that it appeared that they were all of a strain. They moved in their seats; the shipman cried out﻿—

“Ay! witchcraft! witchcraft!”

The huge bulk of the stranger, black and like a bull’s, half rose from its chair.

“Body of God!” he cried out. “This I will not bear.”

===Chinese===
听众席间响起一片嘁嘁喳喳，可见他们原是同一副心肠。他们在座位上挪动身子；那海客高声喊道——

「是！妖术！妖术！」

那贵客魁伟的身躯，黑得像一头公牛，从椅子里半直起身来。

「天主的圣体！」他高声喊道。「这个我决计忍受不得。」

===Original===
Again the older man leaned solicitously above him and whispered, pleading with his hands, and Lascelles said hastily﻿—

“Speak of your own knowledge. How should you know of what passes in high places?”

“Why!” the printer cried out, “is it not the common report? Do not all men know it? Do not the butchers sing of it in the shambles, and the botflies buzz of it one to the other? I tell you it is spread from here into Almain, where the very horse-sellers are abuzz with it.”

In his chair the stranger cried out﻿—

===Chinese===
那年长的人又关切地俯在他身上，凑耳低语，双手做出恳求的姿态；拉塞尔斯急忙说道——

「请说自家所知。深宫高位里的事，汝又何从知晓？」

「哟！」印书坊主喊道，「这不是尽人皆知的传闻么？难道不是人人皆知？屠户们在肉案旁唱着它，马蝇们彼此嗡嗡地传着它，难道不是么？我告诉汝等，这话已经从这里传进了阿尔迈因，连那边的马贩子都为它嗡嗡不休。」

那贵客在椅中喊道——

===Original===
“Ah! ah!” as if he were in great pain. He struggled with his feet and then sat still.

“I have heard witnesses that will testify to these things,” the printer said. “I will bring them here into this room before ye.” He turned upon the stranger. “Master,” he said, “if ye know not of this, you are the only man in England that is ignorant!”

The stranger said with a bitter despair﻿—

“Well, I am come to hear what ye do say!”

===Chinese===
「啊！啊！」仿佛痛楚已极。他的双脚挣动了一阵，随后又坐定不动。

「我这里已听过肯就这些事作证的证人，」印书坊主说。「我要把他们带到这间屋里，带到汝等面前。」他转身朝向那贵客。「大人，」他说，「这些事汝若不知，汝便是英格兰独一无二的一个蒙在鼓里的人了！」

那贵客带着刻骨的绝望说道——

「好罢，我正是来听汝等说些什么的！」

===Original===
So he heard tales from all the sewers of London, and it was plain to him that all the commonalty cried shame upon their King. He screamed and twisted there in his chair at the last, and when he was come out into the darkness he fell upon his companion, and beat him so that he screamed out.

He might have died﻿—for, though the King’s guard with their torches and halberds were within a bowshot of them, they stirred no limb. And it was a party of fellows batfowling along the hedges of that field that came through the dark, attracted by the glare of the torches, the blaze of the scarlet clothes, and the outcry.

And when they came, asking why that great man belaboured this thin and fragile one, black shadows both against the light, the big man answered, howling﻿—

“This man hath made me bounden to slay my wife.”

===Chinese===
于是他听遍了从伦敦各条阴沟里倒出来的故事，他也看得明白：全体平民百姓都在呼喝他们国王的耻辱。到末了，他在那椅子里尖叫扭动起来；待走进黑暗之中，他便扑向自己的同伴，把他打得尖叫出声。

他说不定真会送命——因为，虽说国王的卫队举着火炬与长戟，距他们不过一箭之地，却连一条肢体也不肯挪动。倒是一伙沿着那片田边树篱捕打夜鸟的汉子，被火炬的光焰、猩红衣装的火色和那阵叫喊引动，穿过黑暗赶了过来。

他们走上前来，问那大汉为何毒打这瘦弱单薄的一个——两人衬着火光，都成了黑影——那大汉嚎着答道——

「这个人害得我不得不杀我自己的妻子。」

===Original===
They said that that was a thing some of them would have been glad of.

But the great figure cast itself on the ground at the foot of a tree that stretched up like nerves and tentacles into the black sky. He tore the wet earth with his fingers, and the men stood round him till the Duke of Norfolk, coming with his sword drawn, hunted them afar off, and they fell again to beating the hedges to drive small birds into their nets.

For, they said, these were evidently of the quality whose griefs were none of theirs.

===Chinese===
他们说，这样的事，他们中有几位本是求之不得的呢。

可那魁伟的身影却扑倒在一株树的脚下，那树的枝干如神经与触手一般，直伸进漆黑的天空。他用手指撕挖湿泥，那班汉子围着他站立，直到诺福克公爵提着出鞘的剑赶到，把他们轰得远远的，他们才又回身去敲打树篱，把小鸟赶进网里。

因为，他们说，这分明是那种身份的人，他们的悲苦原与他们无干。

### IV

===Original===
The Queen was walking in the long gallery of Hampton Court. The afternoon was still new, but rain was falling very fast, so that through the windows all trees were blurred with mist, and all alleys ran with water, and it was very grey in the gallery. The Lady Mary was with her, and sat in a window-seat reading in a book. The Queen, as she walked, was netting a silken purse of a purple colour; her gown was very richly embroidered of gold thread worked into black velvet, and the heavy day pressed heavily on her senses, so that she sought that silence more willingly. For three days she had had no news of her lord, but that morning he was come back to Hampton, though she had not yet seen him, for it was ever his custom to put off all work of the day before he came to the Queen. Thus, if she were sad, she was tranquil; and, considering only that her work of bringing him to God must begin again that night, she let her thoughts rest upon the netting of her purse. The King, she had heard, was with his council. Her uncle was come to Court, and Gardiner of Winchester, and Cranmer of Canterbury, along with Sir A. Wriothesley, and many other lords, so that she augured it would be a very full council, and that night there would be a great banquet if she was not mistaken.

She remembered that it was now many months since she had been shown for Queen from that very gallery in the window that opened upon the Cardinal’s garden. The King had led her by the hand. There had been a great crying out of many people of the lower sort that crowded the terrace before the garden. Now the rain fell, and all was desolation. A yeoman in brown fustian ran bending his head before the tempestuous rain. A rook, blown impotently backwards, essayed slowly to cross towards the western trees. Her eyes followed him until a great gust blew him in a wider curve, backwards and up, and when again he steadied himself he was no more than a blot on the wet greyness of the heavens.

There was an outcry at the door, and a woman ran in. She was crying out still: she was all in grey, with the white coif of the Queen’s service. She fell down upon her knees, her hands held out.
===Chinese===
王后正在汉普顿宫的长廊里踱步。午后才开始不久，雨却下得极急，隔着窗子，所有的树都被水雾滤作一片模糊，所有的园径都淌着水，长廊里一派灰暗。玛丽公主陪在她身边，坐在一处窗座上读书。王后一面走，一面编结一只紫色丝线钱袋；她的裙袍以金线绣入黑天鹅绒，绣工极尽华丽。这沉重的天色沉沉压在她的感官上，使她愈发甘愿求得这份静寂。她已三天没有夫君的消息，可那天早晨他回到了汉普顿，她却还不曾见到他——因为他向来有这样的习惯：总要把当日的一切事务料理停当，才到王后来。这样，她纵然忧伤，心境却安然；她只想着今夜引他归向天主的功业又须从头做起，便让心思安歇在那只钱袋的编结上。她听说国王正与枢密院议事。她的叔祖进了宫，还有温彻斯特的加德纳、坎特伯雷的克兰默，连同A·赖奥思利爵士和许多别的勋贵，因此她料想这场枢密院会议必定济济一堂，若她没有料错，当夜还要有一场大宴。

她记起，距今已有多月，她正是从这条长廊上、从那扇开向红衣主教花园的窗前，被册立示众为王后的。那天国王牵着她的手。花园前平台上挤聚的许多下层百姓曾发出一片高声的欢呼。如今雨落着，四下尽是荒凉。一个穿棕色粗呢的庄稼汉顶着狂骤的暴雨，埋头奔走。一只白嘴鸦被吹得有气无力、直往后退，仍缓缓试着朝西边的树上飞去。她的目光一路追着它，直到一阵大风把它吹得画出一道更宽的弧线，向后、向上；待它重新稳住身形，它已不过是湿漉漉的灰色天幕上的一个墨点。

门口起了一阵喧嚷，一个女人奔了进来。她还在喊叫：她通身灰色装束，头戴王后宫中当差的白帽巾。她扑身跪倒，双手前伸。

===Original===
“Pardon!” she cried. “Pardon! Let not my brother come in. He prowls at the door.”

It was Mary Hall, she that had been Mary Lascelles. The Queen came over to raise her up, and to ask what it was she sought. But the woman wept so loud, and so continually cried out that her brother was the fiend incarnate, that the Queen could ask no questions. The Lady Mary looked up over her book without stirring her body. Her eyes were awakened and sardonic.

The waiting-maid looked affrightedly over her shoulders at the door.

“Well, your brother shall not come in here,” the Queen said. “What would he have done to you?”
===Chinese===
「开恩！」她喊道。「开恩！别让我兄弟进来。他在门边转来转去。」

来的是玛丽·霍尔，就是从前那位玛丽·拉塞尔斯。王后走过去扶她起来，问她求的是什么。可那妇人哭得那样响，又一迭连声地喊她兄弟是恶魔的化身，王后竟无从问起。玛丽公主隔着书抬起眼来，身子却不动。她的眼神醒了过来，带着讥诮。

那位侍女怯生生地回头看了一眼门口。

「好了，汝的兄弟进不到这里来，」王后说。「他要拿汝怎么样？」

===Original===
“Pardon!” the woman cried out. “Pardon!”

“Why, tell me of your fault,” the Queen said.

“I have given false witness!” Mary Hall blubbered out. “I would not do it. But you do not know how they confuse a body. And they threaten with cords and thumbscrews.” She shuddered with her whole body. “Pardon!” she cried out. “Pardon!”

And then suddenly she poured forth a babble of lamentations, wringing her hands, and rubbing her lips together. She was a woman passed of thirty, but thin still and fair like her brother in the face, for she was his twin.

“Ah,” she cried, “he threated that if I would not give evidence I must go back to Lincolnshire. You do not know what it is to go back to Lincolnshire. Ah, God! the old father, the old house, the wet. My clothes were all mouldered. I was willing to give true evidence to save myself, but they twisted it to false. It was the Duke of Norfolk﻿ ﻿…”
===Chinese===
「开恩！」那妇人喊道。「开恩！」

「那么，把汝的罪过说与我听，」王后说。

「我作了伪证！」玛丽·霍尔哭喊出来。「我本不肯的。可您不知道他们是怎样把人搅得晕头转向。他们拿绳索和夹指的刑具吓唬人。」她浑身打了个寒噤。「开恩！」她喊道。「开恩！」

随后她忽然倾泻出一串呜咽哀诉，绞着双手，两片嘴唇相互磨蹭。她是个年过三十的女人，却依然清瘦，脸庞和她兄弟一样俊秀，因为她与他是一胎孪生。

「啊，」她哭道，「他吓唬我，说我不肯作证，就得回林肯郡去。您不知道回林肯郡是什么滋味。啊，天主！那老神父，那老宅子，那湿气。我的衣裳全霉烂了。我原情愿说真话救自己，可他们把真话拧成了假话。是诺福克公爵……」

===Original===
The Lady Mary came slowly over the floor.

“Against whom did you give your evidence?” she said, and her voice was cold, hard, and commanding.

Mary Hall covered her face with her hands, and wailed desolately in a high note, like a wolf’s howl, that reverberated in that dim gallery.

The Lady Mary struck her a hard blow with the cover of her book upon the hands and the side of her head.

“Against whom did you give your evidence?” she said again.

The woman fell over upon one hand, the other she raised to shield herself. Her eyes were flooded with great teardrops; her mouth was open in an agony. The Lady Mary raised her book to strike again: its covers were of wood, and its angles bound with silver work. The woman screamed out, and then uttered﻿—
===Chinese===
玛丽公主从那边缓缓走了过来。

「汝作的证，是对着谁？」她说，声音又冷又硬，不容分说。

玛丽·霍尔双手掩面，发出一声高亢的哀嚎，像狼嗥一般，在那昏暗的长廊里回荡。

玛丽公主用书壳朝她的双手和头侧狠狠打了一记。

「汝作的证，是对着谁？」她又问了一遍。

那妇人伏倒在一只手上，另一只手举起护住自己。她眼里涌满大颗大颗的泪珠；嘴痛苦地张着。玛丽公主举起书又要打：那书的壳子是木制的，四角包着银活。那妇人尖叫一声，随后吐出——

===Original===
“Against Dearham and one Mopock first. And then against Sir T. Culpepper.”

The Queen stood up to her height; her hand went over her heart; the netted purse dropped to the floor soundlessly.

“God help me!” Mary Hall cried out. “Dearham and Culpepper are both dead!”

The Queen sprang back three paces.

“How dead!” she cried. “They were not even ill.”

“Upon the block,” the maid said. “Last night, in the dark, in their gaols.”
===Chinese===
「先是对迪尔姆和一个莫波克（Mopock）。后来是对托马斯·卡尔佩珀爵士。」

王后直挺挺立起身来；一只手掩上心口；编结的钱袋无声地落在地板上。

「天主救我！」玛丽·霍尔喊道。「迪尔姆和卡尔佩珀都死了！」

王后向后猛退了三步。

「怎么个死法！」她喊道。「他们连病都不曾病过。」

「上了断头台，」侍女说。「就在昨夜，黑地里，在他们的牢里。」

===Original===
The Queen let her hands fall slowly to her sides.

“Who did this?” she said, and Mary Hall answered﻿—

“It was the King!”

The Lady Mary set her book under her arm.

“Ye might have known it was the King,” she said harshly. The Queen was as still as a pillar of ebony and ivory, so black her dress was, and so white her face and pendant hands.
===Chinese===
王后让双手缓缓垂落身侧。

「这是谁下的手？」她问，玛丽·霍尔回答——

「是国王！」

玛丽公主把书夹进臂弯。

「汝等本该想到是国王，」她声色俱厉地说。王后静立不动，像一根乌木与象牙砌成的柱子——她的衣裙那样黑，她的脸和低垂的手那样白。

===Original===
